Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 The world this week 这周的世界 Politics 政治 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 Ron DeSantis launched his campaign for the Republican presidential nomination. The governor of Florida made his announcement in an audio conversation with Elon Musk on Twitter, though the event was marred by technical glitches for the first 20 minutes. 罗恩 · 德桑蒂斯开始了他的共和党总统候选人提名竞选活动。这位佛罗里达州州长是在与埃隆 · 马斯克(Elon Musk)在 Twitter 上的语音对话中宣布这一消息的，尽管在开始的20分钟里，这一活动因技术故障而受到了影响。 Mr DeSantis said he would revitalise America and criticised Joe Biden for being woke; as governor Mr DeSantis has made a point of clashing with the left on gay issues and identity politics. 德桑蒂斯先生说，他将重振美国，并批评乔 · 拜登被唤醒; 作为州长，德桑蒂斯先生在同性恋问题和身份政治上与左派发生冲突。 He didn’t mention Donald Trump, who holds a big lead in the Republican race at this early stage. 他没有提到唐纳德 · 特朗普，他在共和党初选中遥遥领先。 Swimming with the current 顺流而下 Seven states in the American west reached an agreement to protect the sustainability of the Colorado river , which provides water and electricity to 40m people. Described as “historic” by the White House, the deal will see Arizona, California and Nevada reduce their intake of water from the Colorado river system. 美国西部的七个州达成了一项协议，保护科罗拉多河的可持续发展。科罗拉多河为4000万人口提供水电。该协议被白宫形容为“历史性的”，它将使亚利桑那州、加利福尼亚州和内华达州减少从科罗拉多河流系统吸收的水量。 America signed a security pact with Papua New Guinea , a deal that seeks to counter the rise of Chinese influence in the Pacific. The prime minister of PNG, James Marape, tried to reassure domestic critics that America would not be allowed to launch “offensive military operations” from his country. The pact was signed at the US Pacific Islands Forum. Joe Biden had to miss the event, as he continued haggling with Republicans in Washington over the federal-debt ceiling. 美国与巴布亚新几内亚签署了一项安全协议，该协议旨在遏制中国在太平洋地区影响力的上升。巴布亚新几内亚总理詹姆斯•马拉佩(James Marape)试图安抚国内批评人士，称美国不会被允许从巴布亚新几内亚发动“进攻性军事行动”。该协议是在美国太平洋岛屿论坛上签署的。乔•拜登(Joe Biden)不得不错过这次活动，因为他在华盛顿继续与共和党人就联邦债务上限问题讨价还价。 In separate negotiations America signed agreements with Micronesia to expand economic assistance to the Pacific island nation. The State Department said the deal would “support freedom, security, and prosperity in the Indo-Pacific”. 在单独的谈判中，美国与密克罗尼西亚签署协议，扩大对这个太平洋岛国的经济援助。美国国务院表示，该协议将“支持印度洋-太平洋地区的自由、安全和繁荣”。 The opposition won a general election in East Timor . According to preliminary results the National Congress for Timorese Reconstruction took 42% of the vote, increasing the chances that Xanana Gusmão, its leader and a war hero during the country’s resistance to Indonesian rule, will return as prime minister. 反对派在东帝汶赢得了大选。初步结果显示，东帝汶重建国民大会获得了42% 的选票，增加了其领导人夏纳纳•古斯芒(Xanana Gusmão)重新担任总理的可能性。古斯芒在东帝汶抵抗印度尼西亚统治期间是一位战争英雄。 The legislature makes that appointment. 立法机关做出了这个任命。 It emerged that Alejandro Encinas, a Mexican official close to President Andrés Manuel López Obrador, had his phone hacked by Pegasus, a type of spyware. Pegasus has been deployed in Mexico before, but Mr López Obrador had said he would stop its use. Yet the armed forces continue to spy on human-rights campaigners. 接近总统洛佩斯·奥布拉多尔的墨西哥官员亚历杭德罗•恩西纳斯(Alejandro Encinas)的手机被一种名为 Pegasus 的间谍软件黑客入侵。Pegasus 之前已经部署在墨西哥，但是 López Obrador 先生说他将停止使用它。然而，武装部队仍在继续监视人权活动人士。 Mr Encinas was hacked last year while investigating the armed forces’ role in the disappearance of 43 students in 2014. 恩西纳斯先生去年在调查武装部队在2014年43名学生失踪事件中所扮演的角色时被黑客攻击。 An internationally monitored ceasefire in Sudan , due to last a week, lessened the intensity of the civil war, though violence persisted here and there. The UN said that 60,000-90,000 people had fled into Chad from Sudan’s western region of Darfur, where fighting has been especially fierce. 由国际社会监督的苏丹停火协议定于上周生效，这降低了内战的激烈程度，尽管暴力事件时有发生。联合国表示，已有6万至9万人从苏丹西部达尔富尔地区逃入乍得，那里的战斗尤其激烈。 The secretary-general of the ruling African National Congress said that South Africa risks becoming a “ failed state ”, causing business leaders to lament the state of the economy more forcefully than ever. 执政党非洲人国民大会(African National Congress)的秘书长表示，南非面临成为“失败国家”的风险，这使得商界领袖比以往任何时候都更加强烈地哀叹南非的经济状况。 The trial of Ousmane Sonko, a Senegalese opposition leader accused of rape, resumed. He was absent, denouncing the trial as politically motivated. If found guilty, he would almost certainly be ruled out of running in the presidential election due next February. 塞内加尔反对派领导人奥斯曼•松科(Ousmane Sonko)被控强奸，对他的审判重新开始。他缺席了，谴责审判是出于政治动机。如果被判有罪，他几乎肯定会被排除在明年2月举行的总统选举之外。 Over a hundred green-minded members of the European Parliament and the American Congress called for the designation of Sultan al-Jaberas as head of the coming COP28 climate conference in the United Arab Emirates to be rejected. He is CEO of the Abu Dhabi National Oil Company. 欧洲议会和美国国会的一百多名环保人士呼吁，不要指定苏丹阿尔贾贝拉斯为即将召开的联合国气候变化框架公约第28次缔约方大会的阿拉伯联合酋长国。他是阿布扎比国家石油公司的首席执行官。 Net migration exceeded 600,000 in Britain last year, a record figure. The number was boosted in part by arrivals from Ukraine and Hong Kong. The news is a big embarrassment for the Conservative government, which for years has been promising to 去年，英国的净移民人数超过了60万，这是一个创纪录的数字。来自乌克兰和香港的游客增加了这一数字。这个消息让保守党政府非常尴尬，多年来保守党政府一直承诺要这么做 reduce migration. 减少移民。 Sinn Féin, the main republican party in Northern Ireland , claimed victory in local elections, and now has the most council seats. Michelle O’Neill, Sinn Féin’s provincial leader, called on the Democratic Unionists to return to the Stormont Assembly, so that the power-sharing executive can start again. 北爱尔兰主要共和党新芬党(Sinn Féin)宣称在地方选举中获胜，目前在议会中的席位最多。新芬党省级领导人米歇尔•奥尼尔(Michelle O’Neill)呼吁民主联盟党人重返斯托蒙特议会(Stormont Assembly) ，以便这位权力分享高管能够重新开始工作。 Stormont has been suspended for over a year. 斯托蒙特已经停职一年多了。 The G7 held a summit in Hiroshima , which was dominated by a collective effort on how to tackle China and Russia. The forum criticised China over Taiwan, nuclear arms, human-rights violations and economic coercion. It adopted a strategy of “de-risking” the West’s trade and financial links with China. 七国集团(G7)在广岛举行了一次峰会，会议主要讨论了如何对付中国和俄罗斯的问题。论坛在台湾、核武器、侵犯人权和经济胁迫等问题上批评中国。它采取了“降低”西方与中国贸易和金融联系风险的战略。 Chinese media denounced the meeting as an “anti-China workshop”. 中国媒体谴责此次会议为“反华研讨会”。 Volodymyr Zelensky attended the G7 summit to discuss his plan to end the war in Ukraine , which includes a demand that Russia restore Ukraine’s territorial integrity. The G7 announced new sanctions on Russia, notably on its diamond business, and said it would support Ukraine for “as long as it takes”. America reversed its position, and will now support a plan to train Ukrainian pilots to fly F-16 fighter jets , but it has not yet decided whether to send F-16 s directly to Ukraine. 弗拉基米尔•泽伦斯基(Volodymyr Zelensky)出席了七国集团(g 7)峰会，讨论他结束乌克兰战争的计划，其中包括要求俄罗斯恢复乌克兰的领土完整。七国集团(G7)宣布了对俄罗斯的新制裁，尤其是对俄罗斯钻石业务的制裁，并表示将支持乌克兰“需要多久就多久”。美国改变了立场，现在将支持一项训练乌克兰飞行员驾驶 F-16战斗机的计划，但尚未决定是否直接向乌克兰派遣 F-16战斗机。 Russia claimed to have repelled an attack on its Belgorod region that came from across the Ukrainian border. It was the biggest incursion into Russian territory since the start of the war. Russia blamed Ukrainian “militants”. 俄罗斯声称击退了来自乌克兰边境对其别尔哥罗德地区的攻击。这是自战争开始以来对俄罗斯领土的最大一次入侵。俄罗斯指责乌克兰是“武装分子”。 Ukraine said the fighters belonged to pro-Ukrainian Russian groups that want to topple Vladimir Putin as president. 乌克兰表示，这些武装分子属于亲乌克兰的俄罗斯团体，他们希望推翻弗拉基米尔•普京(Vladimir Putin)的总统职位。 Responding to Russia’s assertion that it destroyed American-made weapons in the skirmish, the State Department said “it is up to Ukraine to decide how to conduct this war.” 针对俄罗斯声称在冲突中销毁了美国制造的武器，美国国务院表示，“如何开展这场战争取决于乌克兰。” Greek revival period 希腊复兴时期 The centre-right governing New Democracy party was the biggest winner in the Greek general election . It took 41% of the vote, double that of its closest rival, the left-wing Syriza party. But New Democracy fell just short of gaining an outright majority of seats. 中右翼执政的民主政体政党是希腊大选的最大赢家。它获得了41% 的选票，是最接近的竞争对手——左翼的 Syriza 政党的两倍。但是民主政体并没有获得绝大多数席位。 Rather than form a coalition, Kyriakos Mitsotakis, the prime minister, wants to hold another round of voting, probably on June 25th. 基里亚科斯·米佐塔基斯总理希望在6月25日举行另一轮投票，而不是组建联合政府。 At least 75,000 people attended a rally in Moldova calling for the country to join the European Union. It was organised by the government, which again accused Russia of meddling in its affairs. 至少75,000人参加了摩尔多瓦的一次集会，呼吁该国加入欧盟。它是由政府组织的，政府再次指责俄罗斯干涉其事务。 Belarus’s dictator, Alexander Lukashenko, popped up on state TV to scotch rumours that he is dying. Speculation mounted that he is seriously ill after he appeared in poor health at Russia’s Victory Day parade. 白俄罗斯的独裁者，亚历山大·卢卡申科，突然出现在国家电视台上，来澄清他即将死亡的谣言。在参加俄罗斯胜利日游行时，他的健康状况不佳，因此有越来越多的猜测认为他病得很重。 Unfortunately for the people of Belarus, Mr Lukashenko is seen telling officials, “You’ll have to struggle with me for a very long time.” 不幸的是，白俄罗斯人民看到卢卡申科先生告诉官员，“你们将不得不与我斗争很长一段时间。” This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/the-world-this-week/2023/05/25/politics 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/the-world-this-week/2023/05/25/politics 下载的 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 The world this week 这周的世界 Business 生意 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 Britain’s headline rate of annual inflation fell sharply in April, to 8.7%. But the fall was less than expected, given that last April’s rise in energy prices dropped out of the annual comparison. The core rate of inflation, stripping out energy and food, rose again to 6.8%; food prices were up by 19.1%. 英国四月份的年度通货膨胀率急剧下降到8.7% 。但这一降幅低于预期，因为去年4月的能源价格上涨没有进行年度对比。剔除能源和食品的核心通货膨胀率再次上升至6.8% ，食品价格上涨了19.1% 。 The yield on British government bonds jumped as markets bet that interest rates would increase further. 由于市场押注利率将进一步上升，英国国债收益率大幅上升。 Earlier, Andrew Bailey, the governor of the Bank of England, admitted that there are “big lessons to learn” from the failure of the bank’s economic models to predict the persistence of high inflation. 此前，英国央行行长安德鲁•贝利(Andrew Bailey)承认，从英国央行经济模型未能预测高通胀持续存在的失败中，我们可以“学到很多教训”。 An updated estimate of Germany’s GDP in the first quarter showed the economy shrinking by 0.3% over the previous three months. That means that Germany is technically in a recession, as the economy contracted by 0.5% in the fourth quarter of 2022. 对德国第一季度 GDP 的最新估计显示，德国经济在过去三个月里萎缩了0.3% 。这意味着从技术上讲，德国正处于衰退之中，2022年第四季度德国经济收缩了0.5% 。 The European Union slapped a €1.2bn ($1.3bn) fine on Meta , the owner of Facebook, for breaching rules on the transfer of personal data from the EU to America. It is the biggest penalty imposed by the EU on a company for privacy violations. The decision gives Meta six months to stop processing data in America that it holds on EU citizens. Meta described the actions as “unjustified” and said it would appeal against the fine. 欧盟(EU)对 Facebook 所有者梅塔(Meta)处以12亿欧元(合13亿美元)罚款，理由是后者违反了欧盟向美国转移个人数据的规定。这是欧盟对侵犯隐私的公司施加的最大惩罚。这个决定给了 Meta 六个月的时间来停止处理其掌握的欧盟公民在美国的数据。梅塔称这些行动是“不合理的”，并表示将对罚款提起上诉。 China banned memory chips made by Micron , an American company, from being used in the country’s infrastructure development, a tit-for-tat move in response to America’s prohibition on Chinese technology. China claimed Micron’s chips “posed significant security risks”. America has reportedly asked South Korea to dissuade Samsung and SK Hynix, which make similar chips to Micron, from filling the gap in the Chinese market that will be left by Micron’s absence. 中国禁止美国公司美光(Micron)生产的存储芯片用于中国的基础设施开发，这是针对美国禁止中国技术的一个针锋相对的举动。中国声称美光的芯片“构成重大安全风险”。据报道，美国已要求韩国劝阻三星和 SK 海力士(SK Hynix)填补美光退出后留下的中国市场缺口。三星和 SK 海力士生产与美光类似的芯片。 TikTok filed a lawsuit against Montana for its decision to ban the social-media app, the first statewide prohibition of TikTok in America. TikTok 对蒙大拿州提起诉讼，因为该州决定禁止这款社交媒体应用，这是美国第一次在全州范围内禁止 TikTok。 The Chinese firm argues that the ban is unconstitutional, and that Montana’s claim that TikTok shares data with the Chinese government is based on “unfounded speculation”. 这家中国公司认为这项禁令是违宪的，蒙大拿州声称 TikTok 与中国政府共享数据是基于“毫无根据的猜测”。 Apple announced an agreement in which Broadcom , a big American maker of semiconductors, will supply 5G components for the iPhone. Sensitive to claims that it relies on China for the assembly of its devices, Tim Cook, Apple’s chief executive, said that all “of Apple’s products depend on technology engineered and built here” in the United States. 苹果公司宣布了一项协议，美国大型半导体制造商 Broadcom 将为 iPhone 提供5G 组件。苹果首席执行官蒂姆•库克(Tim Cook)对于苹果依赖中国组装设备的说法很敏感，他表示，“苹果的所有产品都依赖在美国设计和制造的技术”。 China overtook Japan as the world’s biggest exporter of cars in the first quarter, mostly because of its expanding production of electric vehicles. China exported more than 1m vehicles in total during the three months, compared with Japan’s 950,000. 今年第一季度，中国超过日本，成为全球最大的汽车出口国，主要原因是中国电动汽车产量不断扩大。在这三个月里，中国总共出口了逾100万辆汽车，而日本为95万辆。 Lady Luck smiles again 幸运女神又笑了 There was more evidence that Macau’s gambling industry is on the rebound from covid and a crackdown on betting by the authorities. Galaxy Entertainment, which owns hotels and casinos in the Chinese territory, said its net revenues were 72% higher in the first quarter than a year earlier. 更多证据表明，澳门博彩业正从冠状病毒疾病和当局打击赌博的行动中反弹。在香港拥有酒店和赌场的银河娱乐(Galaxy Entertainment)表示，第一季度净收入同比增长72% 。 Other resort operators have reported similar results. Macau’s casinos are now focused on catering to ordinary punters, rather than the high rollers who attracted the ire of officials. 其它度假村运营商也报告了类似的结果。澳门的赌场现在专注于迎合普通赌客，而不是那些引起官员愤怒的豪赌客。 Citigroup abandoned plans to find a buyer for Banamex , a bank chain in Mexico that it bought in 2001, and will instead spin off the business in an IPO, probably in 2025. 花旗集团(Citigroup)放弃了为2001年收购的墨西哥连锁银行 Banamex 寻找买家的计划，转而将该业务进行首次公开发行(IPO) ，可能在2025年进行。 Shell’s annual general meeting was seriously disrupted by climate-change protesters. Led by green investors, 20% of the shareholders present rejected the company’s energy-transition plan, claiming its timetable for reducing fossil-fuel production is too slow. 壳牌的年度股东大会被气候变化抗议者严重扰乱。在绿色投资者的带领下，20% 的出席股东拒绝了该公司的能源转型计划，声称其减少化石燃料生产的时间表过于缓慢。 Facing what it described as “volatile circumstances”, Target removed some products related to LGBT pride from its stores. The retailer said that in some cases customers have confronted staff over the merchandise. The pride range includes books aimed at young children with titles such as “I’m Not a Girl” and “The Pronoun Book”. 面对它所描述的“不稳定的环境”，塔吉特将一些与 LGBT 自豪感相关的产品从其商店中撤下。这家零售商表示，在某些情况下，顾客会与员工就商品发生冲突。自豪系列包括针对幼儿的书籍，如“我不是女孩”和“代词书”。 Virgin Orbit was wound down, less than two months after filing for bankruptcy protection. A sale of the rocket-launch company’s assets fetched just $36m, around 1% of the $3.7bn it was valued at when it floated on the Nasdaq stock exchange in 2021. 在申请破产保护后不到两个月，维珍轨道公司就进行了清盘。这家火箭发射公司的资产出售仅获得3600万美元，约占其2021年在纳斯达克(Nasdaq)上市时估值37亿美元的1% 。 The terminator 终结者 Netflix began its long-trailed crackdown on sharing passwords. The streaming giant sent emails to subscribers in 100 countries reminding them that only one household can watch Netflix on a single account. Netflix 开始了长期以来对共享密码的打击。这家流媒体巨头向100个国家的用户发送电子邮件，提醒他们只有一个家庭可以用一个账户观看 Netflix。 Viewers will get prompts when they tune in; those who share passwords outside the household will be booted off. The decision to get tough now has nothing to do with the company’s PR naming Arnold Schwarzenegger as a new “chief action officer”. 查看者在收看时会得到提示; 那些在家庭以外共享密码的人将被关闭。现在采取强硬态度的决定，与该公司任命新“首席行动官”的公关阿诺·施瓦辛格毫无关系。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/the-world-this-week/2023/05/25/business 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/the-world-this-week/2023/05/25/business 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 The Economist 经济学人 This week’s covers 这周的封面 How we saw the world 我们如何看待这个世界 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 I N MOST OF the world this week our cover shows a familiar face. The race to become the Republican nominee for the presidential election in America next year is properly under way. And Donald Trump has a huge, perhaps insurmountable, lead. 本周我们的封面在世界大部分地区展示了一张熟悉的面孔。明年美国总统大选共和党提名人的角逐正在进行之中。唐纳德•特朗普(Donald Trump)拥有一个巨大的、或许不可逾越的领先优势。 The difficulties of co-ordinating the opposition to the former president are daunting. 协调反对这位前总统的力量的困难令人生畏。 Anyone who cares about America, about democracy, about conservatism or about decency should hope that Ron DeSantis or one of the other non-Trump Republican candidates can defy the odds and beat him. 任何关心美国、关心民主政体、关心保守主义或关心体面的人，都应该希望罗恩•德桑蒂斯(Ron DeSantis)或其他非特朗普的共和党候选人能够不顾一切击败他。 Leader : Donald Trump is very likely to be the Republican nominee 领导人: 唐纳德 · 特朗普很有可能成为共和党提名人 Briefing : Ron DeSantis has little chance of beating Donald Trump 简报: 罗恩 · 德桑蒂斯几乎没有机会击败唐纳德 · 特朗普 United States : DeSantis is a truer believer, if a lesser politician, than Trump 美国: 德桑蒂斯是一个比特朗普更忠实的信徒，如果说他是一个比特朗普更低级的政治家的话 In Britain, however, readers will see something different. A traumatised ambulance sums up the state of the country’s National Health Service. 然而，在英国，读者会看到一些不同的东西。一辆受伤的救护车概括了国家卫生服务的状况。 The recipe for saving it requires radicalism: it must be turned from what it has become—a sickness service—into what its name promises—a health service. This will mean spending more money. 拯救它的秘诀需要激进主义: 它必须从现在的ーー一个疾病服务机构ーー转变为它的名字所承诺的ーー一个医疗服务机构。这意味着要花更多的钱。 But to spend it productively requires a shift in focus: away from hospitals to the community, from treatment to prevention, from incentivising inputs to encouraging better outcomes. 但要有效地使用这些资金，就需要转移重点: 从医院转向社区，从治疗转向预防，从激励投入转向鼓励更好的结果。 Leaders : How to fix the NHS 领导者: 如何修复 NHS Britain : To survive, Britain’s NHS must stop fixating on hospital care 英国: 为了生存，英国的 NHS 必须停止专注于医院护理 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/the-world-this-week/2023/05/25/this-weeks-covers 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/the-world-this-week/2023/05/25/this-weeks-covers 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

The world this week 这周的世界 Sections 部分 Letters 信件 Leaders 领袖 How to fix the NHS 如何修复 NHS The sick factor :: Money will help. But a radical shift in focus is more important 病态因素: 金钱会有所帮助，但更重要的是重心的彻底转移 Donald Trump is very likely to be the Republican nominee 唐纳德 · 特朗普很有可能成为共和党提名人 America’s presidential race :: So his chances of re-entering the Oval Office are uncomfortably high 美国总统竞选: 因此，他重新进入椭圆形办公室的可能性高得令人不安。 A stunning election result for Greece’s prime minister 希腊总理选举结果惊人 Carry on Kyriakos :: Kyriakos Mitsotakis deserves his unexpected triumph 基里亚科斯·米佐塔基斯应该得到意想不到的胜利 How to get more bang for the buck in Western defence budgets 如何使西方国防预算更加物有所值 End of the peace dividend :: Lessons from the war in Ukraine 结束和平红利: 乌克兰战争的教训 Activist investors are needed more than ever 现在比以往任何时候都更需要维权投资者 Seize the day (and the board) :: Low rates, passive investing and ESG have left opportunities for active shareholders 抓住今天(和董事会) : 低利率、被动投资和 ESG 为主动股东留下了机会 The world this week 这周的世界 Sections 部分 Letters 信件

Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 The sick factor 病态的因素 How to fix the NHS 如何修复 NHS Money will help. But a radical shift in focus is more important 资金会有所帮助，但更重要的是彻底转变关注焦点 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 B RITONS ARE prouder of their health-care system than they are of the monarchy. But when the English National Health Service ( NHS ) turns 75 in July, the mood will not be celebratory. Hospital waiting lists in England spiral beyond 7m, forcing many to wait months or even years for treatment. Almost 300,000 adults are waiting for a social-care assessment. 比起君主制，英国人更为他们的医疗保健系统感到自豪。但是当英国国民健康服务(NHS)七月份满75岁的时候，人们的心情将不会是庆祝的。英格兰的医院等待名单螺旋式上升，超过700万，迫使许多人等待数月甚至数年的治疗。近30万成年人正在等待社会关怀评估。 A record 2.5m Britons are out of work because they are sick. NHS staff are leaving the workforce in droves. On basic measures of health, Britain suffers by comparison with its rich-world peers. Its people barely live any longer than they did a decade ago, and have some of the worst survival rates for diseases such as cancer. 创纪录的250万英国人因为生病而失业。英国国民医疗服务体系(NHS)的工作人员正成群结队地离开工作岗位。在基本的健康指标方面，英国与其他发达国家相比处于劣势。与十年前相比，人们的寿命几乎没有延长，而且癌症等疾病的存活率也是最低的。 During the pandemic the public clapped for the NHS . Now they are more likely to throw up their hands in frustration. 在大流行期间，公众为 NHS 鼓掌。现在，他们更有可能沮丧地举手投降。 When something is broken, the boldest reforms can often seem the most tempting. Some want to overhaul the NHS ’s funding model, switching from a system funded by taxation to one based on social insurance, as in France or Germany. Others mull the case for much wider use of means-tested charges. 当一些事情出现问题时，最大胆的改革往往看起来最具诱惑力。一些人希望彻底改革 NHS 的融资模式，从税收融资体系转向基于社会保险的体系，就像法国或德国那样。其他人则考虑更广泛地使用经济状况调查收费。 But Britons will not easily ditch what Nigel Lawson, a former chancellor, once called their “national religion” of health care funded by taxes and free at the point of use. And the country’s recent record of revolutionary change does not inspire confidence. 但英国人不会轻易放弃前财政大臣奈杰尔•劳森(Nigel Lawson)曾称之为“国教”的医疗保健服务，这种服务的资金来源是税收，使用时免费。这个国家最近革命性变革的记录并不能激发人们的信心。 It is also unnecessary . The recipe for saving the NHS requires radicalism, but of a simpler sort: turning the NHS from what it has become—a sickness service—into what its name promises—a health service. That will mean spending more money. 这也是没有必要的。拯救 NHS 的方法需要激进主义，但要简单一些: 把 NHS 从现在的“疾病服务”变成它名字所承诺的“健康服务”。这意味着要花更多的钱。 But to spend it productively requires a shift in focus: away from hospitals to the community, from treatment to prevention, from incentivising inputs to encouraging better outcomes. 但要有效地使用这些资金，就需要转移重点: 从医院转向社区，从治疗转向预防，从激励投入转向鼓励更好的结果。 Health already absorbs the biggest single chunk of government spending. Of every pound the state spends on public services, 38p goes on the NHS . But Britain spends less on health care than countries like France and Germany as a share of GDP . It especially skimps on capital spending: no OECD country invests less on a per-person basis. And the demands on the health service are only going to go up. In the next 25 years the number of Britons aged 85 and older is set to double. The NHS is the largest single employer in Europe; the phenomenon of “cost disease” means that the pay of nurses and doctors needs to keep rising to compete with wages elsewhere in the labour market. 医疗卫生已经占据了政府开支的最大一块。政府在公共服务上的每一英镑支出中，有38便士花在了国民保健服务上。但英国的医疗支出占 GDP 的比例低于法国和德国等国。它特别节省资本支出: 没有一个经合组织国家的人均投资比它少。对医疗服务的需求只会越来越大。在未来25年内，85岁及以上的英国人数量将翻番。NHS 是欧洲最大的单一雇主; “成本疾病”现象意味着护士和医生的工资需要不断上涨，以与劳动力市场其他地方的工资竞争。 The critical question is where the money is spent. At the moment, the answer to that question can be boiled down to one word: hospitals. Spending on public health (covid-19 prevention aside) and social care has fallen in real terms over the past decade. The share of total NHS spending allocated to primary and community care was falling even before the pandemic; the share doled out to hospitals had risen to almost two-thirds. As a share of GDP spent by rich-world governments and compulsory insurance schemes, only America spends more on hospitals. 关键问题是钱花在哪里。目前，这个问题的答案可以归结为一个词: 医院。在过去十年里，公共卫生(2019冠状病毒疾病预防除外)和社会保健的实际支出有所下降。甚至在大流行之前，分配给初级和社区护理的国民保健服务总支出份额就已经下降; 分配给医院的份额已经上升到近三分之二。作为发达国家政府和强制保险计划在国内生产总值中所占的份额，只有美国在医院上的花费更多。 This makes no sense. A system focused on hospitals is one designed to treat people only after they have become really sick. That is the equivalent of buying more fire extinguishers while dismantling the smoke alarms. The majority of health and social-care spending now goes on treating long-term conditions like diabetes, high blood pressure and arthritis. 这说不通啊。一个以医院为重点的系统是一个设计用来治疗人们只有在他们真的生病之后。这相当于在拆除烟雾报警器的同时购买更多的灭火器。现在，大部分的医疗和社会保健开支都用于治疗糖尿病、高血压和关节炎等长期疾病。 Such conditions are managed best by patients themselves, in their own homes and with the support of networks of general practitioners and local specialists. The share of money going to primary care should be restored from 8% of the NHS budget to the 11% proportion it was two decades ago. Social care needs more money, too, and a proper long-term funding plan. 这种情况最好由病人自己、在他们自己的家中以及在全科医生和当地专家网络的支持下进行管理。用于初级保健的资金份额应该从国民保健服务预算的8% 恢复到20年前的11% 。社会保障也需要更多的资金，以及一个合适的长期资助计划。 The corollary of moving care out of hospitals and into communities is to focus on prevention: keeping people healthy for as long as possible. That means widening the lens on health care. 将医疗服务从医院转移到社区的必然结果是将重点放在预防上: 尽可能长时间地保持人们的健康。这意味着要扩大对医疗保健的关注。 For all the headlines it grabs, medical care contributes comparatively little to the state of the nation’s health: socio-economic factors, genetics and individual behaviour matter more. One obvious example is obesity. 尽管医疗保健占据了所有的头条新闻，但它对国家健康状况的贡献相对较小: 社会经济因素、遗传和个人行为更为重要。一个明显的例子就是肥胖。 Britain is the third-fattest country in Europe; an obese patient costs the NHS twice as much to treat as one who is not. The government needs a more muscular strategy to tackle this problem before it turns up in waiting rooms and hospital beds, for example by making effective use of promising new anti-obesity drugs. 英国是欧洲第三胖的国家，肥胖病人的治疗费用是非肥胖病人的两倍。政府需要一个更强有力的战略来解决这个问题，在它出现在候诊室和医院病床之前，例如，通过有效利用有前途的新型抗肥胖药物。 The way that the NHS measures and motivates performance also needs to change. At the moment the system is geared towards inputs. Politicians conventionally compete to make promises about the number of new hospitals or the ranks of new doctors. NHS 衡量和激励绩效的方式也需要改变。目前，该系统面向输入。政客们通常竞相承诺新医院的数量或新医生的级别。 Top-down metrics based on “activity” encourage hospitals to spend billions on unnecessary and unwanted treatments towards the end of life. 基于“活动”的自上而下的衡量标准鼓励医院在临终前花费数十亿美元进行不必要和不想要的治疗。 Funding should be relentlessly focused on health outcomes; to encourage innovation, money saved by making people healthier should be made available to regional bodies to reinvest. 资金应坚持不懈地集中在卫生成果上; 为鼓励创新，应向区域机构提供通过使人们更健康而节省下来的资金，以便进行再投资。 Will any of these changes ever actually happen? The good news is that these ideas are neither new nor even particularly controversial: just this week Sir Keir Starmer, the leader of the Labour Party, laid out in a speech the need to shift focus from acute care to chronic care. 这些变化真的会发生吗？好消息是，这些想法既不新鲜，也不特别有争议: 就在本周，工党领袖凯尔 · 斯塔默爵士(Sir Keir Starmer)在一次演讲中提出，需要将重点从急性护理转向慢性护理。 The establishment in England of “integrated care systems”, a set of 42 regional partnerships between NHS providers and local bodies, paves the way for a more decentralised approach to health provision. The covid-19 pandemic vividly demonstrated the power of data and technology to reach people quicker, from carefully targeted vaccination campaigns to online consultations. 在英格兰建立的“综合医疗系统”(NHS 提供者和地方机构之间的42个区域合作伙伴关系) ，为更加分散的卫生服务提供方式铺平了道路。2019冠状病毒疾病大流行生动地展示了数据和技术的力量，从精心选定的疫苗接种运动到在线咨询，更快地惠及人们。 Faith healing 信仰治疗 But this refocusing of effort does require a radical shift in mindset, from politicians above all. Care workers and insulin pumps are less sexy during election campaigns than heart surgeons and new hospitals. 但是，这种努力的重新聚焦确实需要彻底改变思维方式，尤其是政治家的思维方式。在竞选活动中，护理人员和胰岛素泵不像心脏外科医生和新医院那样性感。 No crisis is more urgent for a politician to fix than one involving desperately ill people waiting for ambulances to arrive—one reason why technology and capital budgets are raided to cope with each new winter crisis. The NHS can be saved. But only if the people who run it see their job as keeping Britons healthy at home rather than treating them on the wards. ■ 对于政治家来说，最迫切需要解决的危机莫过于涉及等待救护车到来的重病患者的危机，这也是为什么科技和资本预算遭到突袭以应对每一场新的冬季危机的原因之一。英国国民医疗服务体系是可以被拯救的。但只有当管理者认为他们的工作是让英国人在家里保持健康，而不是在病房里治疗他们的时候。￭ For subscribers only: to see how we design each week’s cover, sign up to our weekly Cover Story newsletter. 仅限订阅者: 想知道我们是如何设计每周封面的，请注册我们的每周封面故事通讯。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/leaders/2023/05/25/how-to-fix-the-nhs 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/leaders/2023/05/25/how-to-fix-the-nhs 下载的 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 America’s presidential race 美国总统竞选 Donald Trump is very likely to be the Republican nominee 唐纳德 · 特朗普很有可能成为共和党提名人 So his chances of re-entering the Oval Office are uncomfortably high 因此，他重新进入椭圆形办公室的可能性很高，这令人不安 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 A GLITCH-PLAGUED chat with Elon Musk, live on Twitter, is an unconventional way to launch a presidential campaign. But with the entry of Florida’s governor, Ron DeSantis , the race for the Republican nomination is now properly under way. The first states will not vote until January. Primaries are hard to predict, because it is expensive to conduct enough high-quality polls of primary voters in the key states. 在 Twitter 上与埃隆•马斯克(Elon Musk)进行一次遭遇故障的聊天，是启动总统竞选活动的一种非传统方式。但随着佛罗里达州州长罗恩•德桑蒂斯(Ron DeSantis)的加入，共和党总统候选人提名的角逐正在顺利进行。第一批州要到一月份才会投票。初选很难预测，因为在关键州进行足够高质量的初选投票是昂贵的。 But, with that disclaimer over, one candidate has a huge, perhaps insurmountable, lead: Donald Trump. Mr Trump thus has a real chance of becoming America’s next president. Betting markets put his odds of returning to the White House at one in three. 但是，随着免责声明的结束，一位候选人拥有了巨大的、或许是不可逾越的领先优势: 唐纳德•特朗普(Donald Trump)。因此，特朗普先生有真正的机会成为美国的下一任总统。博彩市场认为他重返白宫的几率是三分之一。 If you decided to pay less attention to Mr Trump after he lost in 2020, to preserve your sanity, you may be wondering how this can be the case. Parties do not usually stick with losers. 如果在特朗普先生2020年落选后，你决定减少对他的关注，以保持你的理智，你可能想知道为什么会出现这种情况。政党通常不会和失败者在一起。 Mr Trump led the Republicans to defeats in the 2018 midterm and the 2020 presidential elections. After he encouraged his supporters to “stop the steal”, some of them broke into Congress, with the result that one police officer died of a stroke and four committed suicide. 特朗普先生带领共和党在2018年中期选举和2020年总统选举中失败。在他鼓励他的支持者“停止偷窃”之后，他们中的一些人闯入了国会，结果导致一名警官死于中风，四名警官自杀。 He has since been found liable for sexual assault, too. Would the Republican Party really nominate him again? 自那以后，他也被认定对性侵犯负有责任。共和党真的会再次提名他吗？ Yes, it probably would. In 2016 and in 2020 it made some sense to think of the Trump movement as a hostile takeover of the party. In 2023 it no longer does. He is the front-runner because a large proportion of Republicans really like him. 是的，很有可能。在2016年和2020年，将特朗普运动视为对共和党的敌意接管是有一定道理的。到了2023年，这种情况将不复存在。他是领先者，因为很大一部分共和党人真的喜欢他。 His supporters have had their hands on the Republican National Committee for six years now. More than half of Republicans in the House of Representatives were elected for the first time since 2016, and therefore under Mr Trump’s banner. 他的支持者已经控制共和党全国委员会六年了。自2016年以来，超过一半的共和党众议员首次当选，因此是在特朗普先生的旗帜下。 Almost all of those House and Senate Republicans who refused to make their peace with him have stood down or retired. Of the ten House members who voted to impeach Mr Trump in January 2021, only two are still there. They are outnumbered in their own caucus by more than 100 to 1. 几乎所有那些拒绝与他讲和的众议院和参议院共和党人都已经下台或退休。在2021年1月投票弹劾特朗普先生的10名众议院议员中，只有两人仍然在位。他们在自己的党团中的人数比例超过100比1。 Mr Trump’s campaign is also better organised than in either 2016 or 2020. Our analysis of the primaries shows how hard he will be to beat. He has a stunning lead: polling for The Economist by YouGov suggests Republican primary voters prefer Mr Trump to Mr DeSantis by 33 percentage points. He also has a big lead in endorsements from elected Republicans, which are usually a good predictor of what will happen. 特朗普先生的竞选也比2016年或2020年组织得更好。我们对初选的分析表明他很难被击败。他有着惊人的领先优势: YouGov 为《经济学人》所做的民意调查显示，共和党初选选民支持特朗普先生的人数比支持德桑蒂斯先生的人数多33个百分点。他在获得民选共和党人的支持方面也大大领先，这通常能很好地预测未来会发生什么。 In 2016, the last time Mr Trump contested a primary, he won the early primaries with much less support than he has now. 2016年，也就是特朗普先生最后一次参加初选的时候，他以比现在少得多的支持赢得了初选。 There are still Republican voters who would like an alternative—his 58% poll share means that close to half of primary voters must be open to choosing someone else. Yet the difficulties of co-ordinating the opposition to Mr Trump are daunting. 仍然有共和党选民希望有其他选择ーー他的58% 的民调支持率意味着，近一半的初选选民必须愿意选择其他人。然而，协调反对特朗普先生的力量的困难是令人生畏的。 People close to the Trump campaign say privately that the more candidates who enter the primary, dividing the field, the better for their candidate. 与特朗普竞选团队关系密切的人士私下表示，进入初选的候选人越多，对他们的候选人就越有利。 Some big donors are giving money to non-Trump candidates on the condition that they drop out after South Carolina, an early primary, if told to do so. 一些大型捐赠者向非特朗普候选人提供资金，条件是他们在南卡罗来纳州提前举行初选后退出竞选。 The idea is to engineer unity around a single non-Trump candidate, just as establishment Democrats united around Joe Biden in 2020 to stop Bernie Sanders, a leftist. 他们的想法是围绕一位非特朗普候选人团结起来，就像2020年建制派民主党人团结在乔•拜登(Joe Biden)周围阻止左翼人士伯尼•桑德斯(Bernie Sanders)一样。 Backroom manoeuvring by party bigwigs is less likely to work against Mr Trump, however, for the simple reason that he is the Republican establishment. 然而，党内大人物的幕后操纵不太可能对特朗普先生不利，原因很简单，他是共和党建制派。 The way the primary calendar and pending legal cases against Mr Trump intersect is nightmarish. His trial for falsifying records in New York will get under way shortly after Super Tuesday , when more than a dozen states vote. Neither this case nor any of the other investigations he faces are likely to be resolved by the time the primaries are over. 初选日程和悬而未决的针对特朗普先生的法律案件相互交织的方式简直就是噩梦。他在纽约伪造记录的审判将在超级星期二之后不久开始，届时将有十几个州投票。无论是这个案子还是他面临的其他任何调查，都不太可能在初选结束时得到解决。 It is therefore possible that the candidate of one of the two great parties could be subject to criminal charges when he is on the ballot. America has had badly behaved presidents before. It has never had one who is also the defendant in a criminal trial. 因此，两大政党之一的候选人在投票时可能受到刑事指控。美国以前也有过表现不好的总统。它从来没有一个谁也是被告在刑事审判。 You might think that, at this point, voters would abandon Mr Trump in large numbers. Maybe. But when, earlier this year, a jury found that he had sexually abused a woman 30 years ago, the verdict had no measurable effect on his poll numbers. 你可能认为，在这一点上，选民将抛弃特朗普先生在大量。也许吧。但是，今年早些时候，陪审团发现他在30年前对一名女性进行了性侵犯，这一判决对他的民意支持率没有明显的影响。 Mr Trump, it turns out, is adept at persuading Republican voters that he is the real victim. Democrats, and plenty of America’s allies, think Mr Trump is a threat to democracy (as does this newspaper). 事实证明，特朗普先生善于说服共和党选民他才是真正的受害者。民主党人和许多美国的盟友认为特朗普先生是对民主政体的威胁(本报也这么认为)。 His campaign is already turning this accusation back on the accuser: “The 2024 election”, a recent Trump campaign email announced, “will determine whether we can keep our Republic or whether America has succumbed to the dark forces of tyranny.” Those who accept that these are the stakes will probably overlook Mr Trump’s innumerable and obvious flaws. 特朗普的竞选团队已经开始将这种指责转向原告: “2024年的大选”，特朗普竞选团队最近的一封电子邮件宣布，“将决定我们是否能够保住我们的共和国，或者美国是否已经屈服于暴政的黑暗势力。”那些接受这些利害关系的人可能会忽视特朗普先生无数明显的缺点。 Imagine, then, that it is November 2024 and Mr Trump and President Biden are having a rematch—the first since Dwight Eisenhower beat Adlai Stevenson back in the 1950s. Could Mr Trump win? 那么，想象一下，现在是2024年11月，特朗普先生和拜登总统正在进行重赛ーー这是自上世纪50年代德怀特·艾森豪威尔击败阿德莱•史蒂文森(Adlai Stevenson)以来的首次重赛。Trump 先生能赢吗？ The general election will surely be close. The electoral college gives Republicans a slight edge. The most recent landslide was 40 years ago. America has since become evenly divided politically and calcified because voters seldom switch sides. 大选肯定势均力敌。选举人团给了共和党一个微弱的优势。最近的一次山体滑坡发生在40年前。自那以后，美国在政治上分歧均匀，因为选民很少改变立场而变得僵化。 Mr Biden has some under-appreciated strengths, but he is no one’s idea of formidable. Were the country to enter a recession, Mr Trump’s chances would go up. 拜登先生有一些被低估的优势，但没有人认为他令人敬畏。如果国家陷入衰退，特朗普先生的机会就会增加。 Some mooted post-primary tactics intended to stop him, such as running a third-party candidate, smack of desperation: they could easily backfire and boost him further. 一些酝酿中的初选后策略意在阻止他，比如竞选第三党候选人，带有绝望的味道: 这些策略很容易适得其反，进一步助长他的势头。 Prima Donald 首席唐纳德 All of which means that you should take seriously the possibility that America’s next president will be someone who would divide the West and delight Vladimir Putin; who accepts the results of elections only if he wins; who calls the thugs who broke into the Capitol on January 6th 2021 martyrs and wants to pardon them; who has proposed defaulting on the national debt to spite Mr Biden; and who is under multiple investigations for breaking criminal law, to add to his civil-law rap sheet for sexual assault. 所有这一切意味着，你应该认真对待下一任美国总统的可能性: 他会分裂西方，取悦弗拉基米尔 · 普京(Vladimir Putin) ; 他只有赢得选举才会接受选举结果; 他会给2021年1月6日闯入国会大厦的暴徒打电话，要求他们成为烈士，并希望赦免他们; 他曾提议拖欠国家债务，以对付拜登先生; 他还因违反刑法而接受多项调查，以便为他的性侵犯犯罪记录添砖加瓦。 Anyone who cares about America, about democracy, about conservatism or about decency should hope that Mr DeSantis or one of the other non-Trump Republican candidates can defy the odds and beat him. ■ 任何关心美国、关心民主政体、关心保守主义或关心体面的人都应该希望德桑蒂斯先生或其他非特朗普的共和党候选人能够不顾一切击败他。￭ Stay on top of American politics with Checks and Balance , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter, which examines the state of American democracy and the issues that matter to voters. You can read other articles about the elections of 2024 and follow along as we track shifts in Joe Biden’s approval rating . 通过《检查与平衡》(Checks and Balance)了解美国政治，这是我们的每周订阅时事通讯，探讨美国民主政体的状况以及对选民至关重要的问题。你可以阅读有关2024年大选的其他文章，并跟随我们跟踪乔•拜登(Joe Biden)支持率的变化。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/leaders/2023/05/25/donald-trump-is-very-likely-to-be-the-republican-nominee 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/leaders/2023/05/25/donald-trump-is-very-likely-to-be-the-republican-nominee 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Carry on Kyriakos 继续 Kyriakos A stunning election result for Greece’s prime minister 希腊总理选举结果惊人 Kyriakos Mitsotakis deserves his unexpected triumph 基里亚科斯·米佐塔基斯应该得到意想不到的胜利 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 T HERE IS BEATING your political opponent, there is trouncing him, and then there is what Kyriakos Mitsotakis, Greece’s prime minister, did on May 21st to Alexis Tsipras, the leader of the radical-left Syriza party. 有击败你的政治对手，有打败他，还有基里亚科斯·米佐塔基斯，希腊总理，在5月21日对激进左翼联盟党领导人亚历克西斯 · 齐普拉斯所做的事情。 Syriza ran the country from 2015 to 2019, a time when Greece came close to defaulting on its debts, crashing out of the euro and threatening the stability of the entire euro zone. Mr Mitsotakis then took over. 激进左翼联盟在2015年至2019年期间领导着希腊，当时希腊几乎无力偿还债务，退出欧元区，并威胁到整个欧元区的稳定。然后 Mitsotakis 先生接手了。 Now Greek voters have decided, by a whopping margin, that they prefer stability and technocratic competence to drama. 现在，希腊选民以惊人的优势决定，他们更喜欢稳定和技术官僚的能力，而不是戏剧。 Mr Mitsotakis picked up 41% of the vote to Syriza’s 20%, a stunning result, especially given that pollsters had predicted only a six- or seven-point gap. Mr Tsipras’s future, and that of his party, are now in doubt. Mitsotakis 先生获得了41% 的选票，激进左翼联盟获得了20% 的选票，这是一个令人震惊的结果，特别是考虑到民意测验专家预测只有6-7个百分点的差距。齐普拉斯先生的未来，以及他的政党的未来，现在都充满了疑问。 Yanis Varoufakis, the shaven-headed, leather-jacketed finance minister who propelled Syriza down the path of confrontation with Brussels but then quit and formed his own splinter party, is out of parliament. Mr Mitsotakis fell a few seats short of an overall majority. 这位雅尼斯·瓦鲁法克斯光头、身穿皮夹克的财政部长将激进左翼联盟推上了与布鲁塞尔对抗的道路，但随后辞职，组建了自己的分裂党派。Mitsotakis 先生的席位距离总体多数还有一些差距。 But he says he has no interest in forming a coalition; and no one else can. So a second election will be held, probably on June 25th; an interim prime minister will hold the reins until then. 但他表示，他对组建联合政府没有兴趣，其他人也没有兴趣。因此，第二次选举将于6月25日举行，届时将由一位临时总理掌权。 Mr Mitsotakis is sure to be back, because the next election will be held under a new electoral system that will reward the largest party, presumably his right-of-centre New Democracy, with up to 50 bonus seats, out of a parliamentary total of 300. 米佐塔基斯先生肯定会回来，因为下一次选举将在新的选举制度下进行，届时在议会300个席位中，将奖励最大的政党(可能是他的中间偏右的民主政体)最多50个额外席位。 Mr Mitsotakis has earned it. Over the past four years he has governed, for the most part, with energy and skill. Mitsotakis 先生应得的。在过去的四年里，他大部分时间都是以精力和技巧来统治国家的。 The economy has bounced back better than most from the disasters of covid-19 and the energy-price shock; its growth rate last year was around twice the euro-zone average, and is forecast to exceed it comfortably this year too. 经济从2019冠状病毒疾病和能源价格冲击的灾难中恢复得比大多数国家都要好，去年的经济增长率大约是欧元区平均水平的两倍，预计今年也将轻松超过欧元区。 Greece’s sovereign debt now trades at investment-grade interest rates; the spread between what it must pay on its ten-year bond and what Germany pays is only 1.4 percentage points. During the crisis of 2015, it hit a terrifying 18.7 percentage points. 希腊的主权债务目前的交易利率为投资级别; 希腊必须支付的10年期债券利差与德国支付的利差仅为1.4个百分点。在2015年的危机期间，这一数字达到了可怕的18.7个百分点。 The unemployment rate is still too high, at nearly 11%, but annual inflation is back down at only 3%. Greece, in short, has become a normal country, not a worry. 失业率仍然很高，接近11% ，但年通胀率已经回落到3% 。简而言之，希腊已经成为一个正常的国家，而不是一个令人担忧的国家。 Away from the economy, Mr Mitsotakis has done a reasonable job of managing tensions with Turkey, helped by his swift dispatch of aid to the earthquake-devastated Turkish south-east in February. 在经济之外，Mitsotakis 先生在处理与土耳其的紧张关系方面做得相当不错，这得益于他在二月份迅速向遭受地震破坏的土耳其东南部地区提供援助。 That is not to say that things are perfect. Mr Mitsotakis has three big blots on his record and, assuming he gets his second term, he will need to deal with them. 这并不是说一切都很完美。Mitsotakis 先生的履历上有三大污点，如果他连任，他将需要处理这些污点。 The first is a nasty scandal involving the phone-tapping, actual and attempted, of dozens of politicians, journalists and businesspeople by the security services. 第一个丑闻涉及安全部门对数十名政客、记者和商人的电话窃听，不管是实际的还是未遂的。 Mr Mitsotakis’s government has yet to come clean about exactly what happened; until a lot more is disclosed, trust in his administration will remain badly dented. 米佐塔基斯先生的政府还没有清楚到底发生了什么; 除非更多的信息被披露，否则对他的政府的信任将继续受到严重的打击。 The second is persistent reports of Greek border guards beating up and robbing migrants trying to enter from Turkey: something that may not much bother Mr Mitsotakis’s more nationalist-minded supporters, but ought to bother him. 第二是持续不断的关于希腊边防军殴打和抢劫试图从土耳其进入的移民的报道: 这些事情可能不会太困扰 Mitsotakis 先生更具民族主义思想的支持者，但应该困扰他。 And finally, far more needs to be done to reform Greece’s sclerotic and sometimes corrupt public services. The shortcomings of the state were demonstrated in February by a train crash which took 57 lives and revealed a litany of duff equipment and shoddy working practices. 最后，在改革希腊僵化、有时甚至腐败的公共服务方面，还有更多的工作要做。今年2月，一起造成57人死亡的火车相撞事故暴露了这个州的不足之处，事故中出现了一连串的设备和劣质工作方式。 It was the result of decades of mismanagement, but it sent Mr Mitsotakis’s polls reeling (they recovered in time for the election, which he put back by more than a month). 这是几十年来管理不善的结果，但是它使 Mitsotakis 先生的民意调查摇摇欲坠(他们及时地在选举前恢复了，他把选举推迟了一个多月)。 The mission of modernising Greek government has a long way to go—and Mr Mitsotakis will have his work cut out in a second term. But he clearly deserves one. ■ 希腊政府的现代化任务还有很长的路要走ーー米索塔基斯先生在第二个任期内将面临艰巨的任务。但显然他值得拥有一个。￭ This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/leaders/2023/05/25/a-stunning-election-result-for-greeces-prime-minister 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/leaders/2023/05/25/a-stunning-election-result-for-greeces-prime-minister 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 End of the peace dividend 和平红利的终结 How to get more bang for the buck in Western defence budgets 如何使西方国防预算更加物有所值 Lessons from the war in Ukraine 乌克兰战争的教训 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 T HE WORLD is tooling up. The peace dividend it has enjoyed since the end of the cold war—releasing wads of cash from defence to spend on other things—is ending. Now comes the new “war tax”. Our simulations suggest global defence spending may rise by $200bn-$700bn a year, or 9-32%. Blame fraught geopolitics—especially Russia’s invasion of Ukraine and China’s sabre-rattling at Taiwan. 这个世界正在改变。美国自冷战结束以来享受的和平红利——从国防中释放大量现金用于其它事情——即将结束。现在又出现了新的“战争税”。我们的模拟显示，全球国防开支每年可能增加2000亿至7000亿美元，增幅为9% 至32% 。这要归咎于充满忧虑的地缘政治，尤其是俄罗斯入侵乌克兰和中国对台湾的武力恫吓。 America and China are locked in a race for military ascendancy in Asia. European countries are scrambling to meet NATO ’s target of spending 2% of GDP on defence. Poland is aiming for 4% and wants to double its armed forces. Japan’s defence budget will rise by at least two-thirds by 2027, which may make it the world’s third-largest spender. Australia is developing pricey nuclear-powered submarines with America and Britain. 美国和中国正在亚洲争夺军事优势。欧洲国家正在争先恐后地实现北约国防开支占国内生产总值2% 的目标。波兰的目标是4% ，并希望将其武装力量增加一倍。到2027年，日本的国防预算将至少增长三分之二，这可能使日本成为世界第三大国。澳大利亚正在与美国和英国一起开发昂贵的核动力潜艇。 Yet for Western governments, finding money for arms will not be easy. They must pay interest on debts and cope with fiscal pressures that did not exist in the 1980s, such as the need to care for ageing populations and curb climate change. 然而，对于西方国家政府来说，筹措资金购买武器并不容易。它们必须支付债务利息，并应对上世纪80年代不存在的财政压力，比如照顾老龄化人口和遏制气候变化的必要性。 Furthermore, as in the cold-war era, there is a risk that cash is blown on good-but-exorbitant equipment, thanks to red tape and cronyism. How to get the best value from defence spending in the 2020s? 此外，与冷战时期一样，由于繁文缛节和任人唯亲，现金有可能被浪费在质量好但价格过高的设备上。如何在21世纪20年代从国防开支中获得最佳价值？ The war in Ukraine offers some pointers. The first priority for Western governments is to restock depleted arsenals and boost the factories that make shells and missiles. War eats up vast amounts of ammunition. 乌克兰战争提供了一些启示。西方政府的首要任务是补充已经耗尽的军火库，增加制造炮弹和导弹的工厂数量。战争消耗了大量的弹药。 Ukraine has been firing roughly as many 155mm artillery shells in a month as America can produce in a year. In a war with China over Taiwan, America could run out of vital anti-ship missiles within days. 乌克兰一个月发射的155毫米炮弹数量大约是美国一年的产量。在与中国的台湾战争中，美国可能会在几天内耗尽关键的反舰导弹。 Increasing output requires certainty for industry, in the form of multi-year contracts, and much work to find and clear production bottlenecks. 增加产量需要多年合同形式的行业确定性，需要大量工作来发现和清除生产瓶颈。 Next, governments should shake up procurement processes and disrupt the cosy structure of the defence industry. Western weapons have shown their value in Ukraine, and the war ought to be a “battle lab” for new ideas. Yet procurement is still woefully slow and costly. 其次，各国政府应改革采购流程，打破国防工业的舒适结构。西方的武器已经在乌克兰显示了它们的价值，这场战争应该成为新思想的“战斗实验室”。然而，采购仍然非常缓慢，而且成本高昂。 New entrants, notably dynamic tech firms, could end the oligopoly of the big contractors. 新进入者，尤其是充满活力的科技公司，可能终结大型承包商的寡头垄断。 SpaceX, a satellite firm, has broken the hold of big, stodgy rivals and slashed the cost of putting objects into orbit. Its Starlink constellation of communication satellites has proved invaluable to Ukrainian forces. 太空探索技术公司(SpaceX) ，一家卫星公司，已经打破了大型、乏味的竞争对手的控制，并大幅削减了将物体送入轨道的成本。它的星链通信卫星星座对乌克兰军队来说是无价之宝。 More Silicon Valley firms are helping with the fusion and analysis of data to create a “kill web”: a network of scattered “sensors” and “shooters” that is more powerful than any single weapon. 更多的硅谷公司正在帮助融合和分析数据，以创建一个“杀伤网络”: 一个由分散的“传感器”和“射手”组成的网络，比任何单一武器都更强大。 Governments should welcome the flow of venture capital into experimental defence startups, and accept that some failures are inevitable. 政府应该欢迎风险资本流入试验性国防初创企业，并承认一些失败是不可避免的。 Finally, Western countries need to create more of a single market for defence that boosts economies of scale and competition. Common standards, which NATO can help set, are one part of this. With its tutti-frutti arsenal of donated weapons, Ukraine knows all about the incompatibilities of Western kit: British tanks have rifled guns, so they cannot fire ammunition made for smooth-bored German and American ones. 最后，西方国家需要建立更多的单一国防市场，以促进规模经济和竞争。北约可以帮助制定的共同标准就是其中的一部分。乌克兰拥有大量捐赠的武器，它知道所有西方装备的不兼容性: 英国坦克有膛线枪，所以他们不能发射为平坦的德国和美国制造的弹药。 American tanks run on petrol; European ones on diesel. Given the growing importance of data in weaponry, open-architecture software that allows kit to “plug and play” should be helpful. 美国坦克靠汽油驱动，欧洲坦克靠柴油驱动。鉴于数据在武器装备中的重要性与日俱增，允许装备“即插即用”的开放式体系结构软件应该会有所帮助。 Creating an integrated market also means resisting protectionism. Europe got into a needless twist—and wasted time—over French attempts to exclude non-European firms from the EU ’s scheme to deliver 1m artillery shells to Ukraine in a year. Even mighty America could benefit from more co-operation. It has one supplier of rocket motors for many missiles; buying from trusted allies would make its supply chains more resilient. 创建一体化市场还意味着抵制保护主义。法国试图将非欧洲公司排除在欧盟一年内向乌克兰运送100万枚炮弹的计划之外，欧洲因此陷入了不必要的麻烦之中，并且浪费了时间。即使是强大的美国也可以从更多的合作中受益。它只有一个火箭发动机供应商，可以为许多导弹提供发动机; 从值得信赖的盟友那里购买可以增强其供应链的弹性。 In an unruly world, liberal democracies must figure out how to bolster their security despite other pressing demands on the public purse. The best way is to embrace innovation, and ruthlessly pursue efficiency and scale. ■ 在一个不受控制的世界里，自由民主国家必须想出办法来加强自己的安全，尽管其他国家对公共财政提出了紧迫的要求。最好的办法就是拥抱创新，无情地追求效率和规模。￭ This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/leaders/2023/05/25/how-to-get-more-bang-for-the-buck-in-western-defence-budgets 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/leaders/2023/05/25/how-to-get-more-bang-for-the-buck-in-western-defence-budgets 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Seize the day (and the board) 抓住今天(和董事会) Activist investors are needed more than ever 现在比以往任何时候都更需要维权投资者 Low rates, passive investing and ESG have left opportunities for active shareholders 低利率、被动投资和环境、社会和治理为主动股东留下了机会 May 24th 2023 | 2023年5月24日 L ITTLE SCARES the C -suite like shareholder activism. Bosses stay awake worrying about a call, a letter or a 100-page presentation in which a hedge fund outlines the depths of their ineptitude. At the start of the year executives were especially on edge. 小小的恐吓就像股东积极主义一样吓到了高层。老板们总是担心一个电话、一封信或者一份100页的报告，在这些报告中，对冲基金描述了他们的无能到了何种程度。今年年初，高管们尤其紧张。 During this year’s annual “proxy season”—a succession of shareholder meetings—they have mostly avoided votes on dissident nominees to their boards. 在今年一年一度的“代理季”(股东会议接连举行)期间，他们基本上避免对董事会异见人士的提名进行投票。 Nevertheless in recent months some of the world’s largest firms—including Alphabet, Bayer, Disney and Salesforce—have had to tussle with activists, who are increasingly focused on the biggest companies. 然而，最近几个月，一些全球最大的公司(包括 Alphabet、拜耳(Bayer)、迪士尼(Disney)和 Salesforce)不得不与维权人士发生冲突，后者越来越关注那些最大的公司。 On May 25th, as we published this article, the battle between Carl Icahn, a prominent activist, and Illumina, a genomics giant, was set to come to a head . 5月25日，当我们发表这篇文章时，著名活动家卡尔 · 伊坎(Carl Icahn)和基因组学巨头 Illumina 之间的斗争即将进入白热化阶段。 Activist hedge funds are often seen as villains who are nasty, brutish and focused on the short term. Sometimes the shoe fits. But more often activists are playing a role that is essential for shareholder capitalism. 维权对冲基金往往被视为恶棍，他们肮脏、野蛮，专注于短期目标。有时候就是这样。但更多时候，维权人士扮演的角色对股东资本主义至关重要。 For several reasons, their campaigns are increasingly important. 出于几个原因，他们的竞选活动变得越来越重要。 One is the rise of passive investing, which attempts to replicate the returns of an index rather than surpass them. Only one in three dollars invested by institutions in America’s thousand largest public companies is actively allocated, according to Man Group, an investment firm. 一个是被动投资的兴起，它试图复制一个指数的回报率，而不是超越它们。投资公司曼氏集团(Man Group)的数据显示，在美国最大的一千家上市公司中，只有三分之一的机构投资获得了积极配置。 The biggest passive asset-managers, such as BlackRock, charge low fees and run lean investment-stewardship teams which are not designed to spot empire-building bosses or lazy boards. The result is an increasingly idle corporate electorate. 贝莱德(BlackRock)等最大的被动型资产管理公司收费低廉，运营着精干的投资管理团队，这些团队的目的不是为了发现建立帝国的老板或懒惰的董事会。其结果是企业选民越来越无所事事。 Efforts to enfranchise the ultimate owners of funds are unlikely to solve the problem. They typically want to earn returns but leave the decision-making to somebody else. 赋予基金最终所有者权利的努力不太可能解决这个问题。他们通常希望获得回报，但把决策权留给了其他人。 There are other channels by which bosses’ feet might be held to the fire. Since the 1980s leveraged buy-outs by private-equity firms have been a persistent threat to underperforming executives. The best way to deter a hostile takeover is to raise your company’s share price. 还有其他渠道可以让老板们的脚受到严厉的惩罚。自上世纪80年代以来，私人股本公司的杠杆收购一直是业绩不佳的高管面临的一个持续威胁。阻止敌意收购的最好办法是提高你公司的股价。 Today, however, the buy-out industry is roiling from the effects of higher interest rates, and is unlikely to recover fully for some time. 然而，如今收购行业正受到利率上升的影响，在一段时间内不太可能完全复苏。 While the routes by which managers are held to account have shrunk, the need to boost profits by applying discipline has grown. When interest rates were low, large technology firms hired aggressively and expanded into peripheral lines of business. 尽管追究管理者责任的途径已经缩小，但通过实施纪律来提高利润的需求却在增长。当利率较低时，大型科技公司大举招聘，并向外围业务扩张。 Now profits are more important than growth. Over the past decade the demand for managers to respond to environmental, social and governance ( ESG ) concerns has also grown. Some shareholders campaign for ESG —as is their right—but the danger of firms losing focus and wasting money as they are drawn into politics has increased. 现在利润比增长更重要。在过去的十年中，对管理者回应环境、社会和治理(ESG)问题的需求也在增长。一些股东支持 ESG (这是他们的权利) ，但随着企业被卷入政治，它们失去焦点和浪费资金的风险已经增加。 In such an environment the presence of activists is a welcome reminder that it is owners, not managers, in whose interest firms should be run. 在这样的环境下，维权人士的出现是一个值得欢迎的提醒，他们提醒我们，公司应该为所有者而非管理者的利益而运营。 And because dealmaking is down, activists will be more likely to improve a firm’s operations than force it to sell itself in search of a quick buck. That should assuage the fears of those who see activists as corporate cowboys rather than drivers of efficiency. 而且，由于交易量下降，维权人士更有可能改善公司的运营，而不是迫使其出售自己，以寻求快速赚钱。这应该能够缓解那些将活动人士视为企业牛仔、而非效率推动者的人的担忧。 Get out the vote 投票吧 Fortunately, the job of activists is getting easier. New rules that came into force in America last September should make it simpler for them to obtain board seats by letting shareholders vote for candidates individually, rather than as a bloc. 幸运的是，积极分子的工作正变得越来越容易。去年9月在美国生效的新规定应该会让他们更容易获得董事会席位，让股东单独投票选举候选人，而不是作为一个集团。 The wave of nail-biting shareholder votes some expected to follow immediately has not yet materialised. But more battles between activists and complacent managers would be no bad thing. ■ 一些人预计将立即跟进的股东投票浪潮，迄今尚未成为现实。不过，激进分子与自满的管理者之间爆发更多争斗并非坏事。￭ This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/leaders/2023/05/24/activist-investors-are-needed-more-than-ever 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/leaders/2023/05/24/activist-investors-are-needed-more-than-ever 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Leaders 领袖 Sections 部分 By Invitation 邀请函 Letters 信件 Letters to the editor 给编辑的信 On international aid, inflation, Britain’s royal family, walking, bacteria, Liz Truss :: A selection of correspondence 关于国际援助，通货膨胀，英国皇室，步行，细菌，Liz Truss: : 一些信件的选择 Leaders 领袖 Sections 部分 By Invitation 邀请函

Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 On international aid, inflation, Britain’s royal family, walking, bacteria, Liz Truss 关于国际援助，通货膨胀，英国皇室，步行，细菌，利兹特拉斯 Letters to the editor 给编辑的信 A selection of correspondence 精选的信件 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 Letters are welcome via e-mail to letters@economist.com 欢迎通过电子邮件来信@economist.com Promoting local aid 促进当地援助 As you noted, risk aversion and bureaucracy get in the way of the American government effectively supporting locally led development and emergency relief (“ The effort to transform the aid business ”, May 6th). The same is true for most other government donors, UN agencies and international NGO s. After the earthquake in Syria and Turkey, just as on the front lines in Ukraine and in remote parts of Myanmar, local volunteer networks stayed and delivered life-saving help, whereas international agencies withdrew or never ventured. 正如你所指出的，风险规避和官僚作风阻碍了美国政府有效地支持地方主导的发展和紧急救援(“转变援助业务的努力”，5月6日)。大多数其他政府捐助者、联合国机构和国际非政府组织也是如此。在叙利亚和土耳其发生地震后，正如在乌克兰前线和缅甸偏远地区一样，当地志愿人员网络留了下来，提供救生帮助，而国际机构则撤出或从未冒险。 Sadly, the standard operating model has often been to sideline local agencies rather than reinforce them, or to sub-contract them into projects that do not adequately cover their overheads, or to poach their staff. Some aid agencies are taking a more responsible approach. 遗憾的是，标准的运作模式往往是将地方机构排除在外，而不是加强它们，或者将它们分包给不足以支付其间接费用的项目，或者挖走它们的工作人员。一些援助机构正在采取更负责任的做法。 The American government’s aid-reform agenda is already challenging other donors and big aid agencies to move from lip service on localisation towards more practical action. 美国政府的援助改革议程已经在挑战其他捐助者和大型援助机构，使其从口头上的本地化转向更实际的行动。 Donors hold the purse-strings. For meaningful change, we need to see them shift funding to local leadership. Over the past decade the aid industry scaled up cash assistance as a more effective and dignified way to help people in times of crisis than food aid. 捐赠者掌握着财政大权。要做出有意义的改变，我们需要看到他们将资金转移到地方领导层。在过去十年中，援助行业扩大了现金援助的规模，认为这是一种比粮食援助更有效、更有尊严的在危机时期帮助人民的方式。 The mantra was: “Why not cash?” Likewise, donors should be asking themselves and the partners they fund in every context: “Why not local?” 他们的口头禅是: “为什么不用现金?”同样，捐赠者应该在每个环境下都问自己和他们资助的合作伙伴: “为什么不是本地的?” HOWARD MOLLETT Head of humanitarian policy 人道主义政策主管 CAFOD London 伦敦 Your proposal to shift towards localisation in aid is commendable but naive. The staff of governments in host countries like Cambodia, Haiti, Jordan and Nigeria receive risible salaries that must be supplemented in order for them to gain a living wage. 你转向援助本地化的建议值得赞扬，但很天真。柬埔寨、海地、约旦和尼日利亚等东道国政府的工作人员得到的工资可笑至极，必须加以补充，才能获得维持生计的工资。 If large grants were disbursed to local agencies we could expect these government staff to seek “rents” from the agencies, and that the agencies, which depend on the government staff for licences to operate, will not be able to say no. 如果向地方机构发放大笔补助金，我们可以预期，这些政府工作人员会向这些机构索要“租金”，而这些依赖政府工作人员获得经营牌照的机构将无法拒绝。 In my 15 years working with USAID I have seen this scenario repeated countless times. Local agencies operate at the pleasure of the host government. International NGO s are somewhat insulated from similar pressures. 在我为美国国际开发署(USAID)工作的15年中，我看到这种情况重复了无数次。地方机构的运作取决于东道国政府。国际非政府组织在某种程度上不受类似压力的影响。 THOMAS D’AGNES THOMAS D’Agnes Kailua, Hawaii 凯卢阿 The factors behind inflation 通货膨胀背后的因素 “ Capital v labour ” (May 6th) set up a straw-man argument about capital’s responsibility for the surge in inflation. Corporate mark-ups have been rising in America since the early 1980s, but are not necessarily reflected in capital’s share of GDP. A growing body of economics research shows that capital share need not rise when labour share falls, and that the share of “factorless income” has been growing even as both labour and capital share have fallen. “资本 VS 劳动力”(5月6日)就资本对通货膨胀的责任提出了一个稻草人论点。自上世纪80年代初以来，美国企业的加价一直在上升，但并不一定反映在资本占 GDP 的比重上。越来越多的经济学研究表明，当劳动力份额下降时，资本份额不一定会上升，而“无因素收入”的份额一直在增长，尽管劳动力和资本份额都有所下降。 Much of this is explained by the growth of intangible capital, such as algorithms, but also by increased fixed costs, which for many firms includes the compensation to managers accounted for as part of sales and general administration costs. 这在很大程度上是由于算法等无形资本的增长，但也由于固定成本的增加，对许多公司来说，固定成本包括作为销售和一般管理成本一部分的经理薪酬。 The data show that over the past four decades, after-tax corporate profits in America have generally risen as a share of gross national income, falling in recessionary years, then rising again as the economy recovers. 数据显示，在过去40年里，美国税后企业利润占国民总收入的比例普遍上升，在衰退年份下降，然后随着经济复苏再次上升。 In the first year of the pandemic, their share shot up from 7.2% of national income to 8.4%. 在大流行的第一年，他们的份额从国民收入的7.2% 飙升到8.4% 。 Median wages have risen since mid-2014 and spiked briefly during the pandemic, but they have since settled back to their pre-pandemic levels. 工资中位数自2014年年中以来有所上升，并在大流行期间短暂飙升，但此后已回落至大流行前的水平。 Although we cannot ascribe the recent inflation to either higher capital or labour shares, these are neither necessarily inversely linked, nor the main reason for soaring income gains at the top. 尽管我们不能将近期的通胀归因于资本或劳动力份额的上升，但这两者既不一定是负相关的，也不是最高收入人群收入飙升的主要原因。 But there is no question that the share of corporate profits in national income has been increasing. 但毫无疑问，企业利润在国民收入中所占的份额一直在增加。 THOMAS REMINGTON Visiting professor of government 政府客座教授 Harvard University 哈佛大学 Cambridge, Massachusetts 马萨诸塞州，剑桥 Royal charities 皇家慈善机构 “ Crowning story ” (May 6th) could have mentioned some of the charities founded by Britain’s royal family. The Prince’s Trust has helped over 1m young people into employment. The Queen’s Green Canopy has planted more than 1m trees. “皇冠故事”(5月6日)可以提到一些由英国皇室成立的慈善机构。王子信托基金已经帮助100多万年轻人就业。女王的绿色天篷已经种植了超过100万棵树。 The Duke of Edinburgh’s Award sees over 300,000 young people volunteer each year, and as was the case with this reader, can inspire a lifelong commitment to volunteering. There is also the Earthshot Prize, the Royal Foundation and Heads Together. 爱丁堡公爵每年有超过30万名年轻人参加志愿者活动，正如这位读者的情况一样，这可以激励人们终生致力于志愿工作。此外，还有“地球拍摄奖”、“皇家基金会”和“团结首脑奖”。 The royal family fills an unfashionable gap engaging with communities that politicians can’t be bothered with and celebrities can’t bear to be seen with. Royal factory visits are not to be sniffed at. 皇室家族填补了一个不流行的空白，与政客们不愿意打扰的社区和名人们不愿意被看到的社区打交道。参观皇家工厂不容小觑。 If the media gave more focus to these endeavours it would change many more lives for the better. 如果媒体对这些努力给予更多的关注，将会改善更多的生活。 GAYATHRI FERNANDO GAYATHRI 费尔南多 London 伦敦 Taking the high road 走正道 Walking is a British thing, says Lexington (May 6th). One name missing from his list of famous walkers is Ben Jonson, an English poet and playwright and friend of William Shakespeare. Jonson set out on his 400-mile “foot voyage” from London to Edinburgh in July 1618. Despite the fact that he was 46 at the time and seriously overweight, he had a marvellous time en route being treated like the celebrity he was, and feted and housed by William Drummond of Hawthornden, his wealthy Scottish fan and fellow poet. 列克星敦(5月6日)说，步行是英国的事情。英国诗人、剧作家、威廉·莎士比亚的朋友本•琼森(Ben Jonson)没有出现在他的著名步行者名单上。1618年7月，琼森开始了他400英里的“徒步旅行”，从伦敦到爱丁堡。尽管当时他46岁，而且严重超重，但他在路上度过了一段美妙的时光，受到了他作为名人的待遇，受到了他富有的苏格兰粉丝、同为诗人的德拉蒙德的款待和款待。 JAMES HUNTER Toronto 多伦多 Divine organisms 神圣的有机体 Your review of Jonathan Kennedy’s book on bacteria says that he puts germs at the centre of human history (“ Bugs in the system ”, April 15th). Mark Twain went a step further. 你对乔纳森 · 肯尼迪(Jonathan Kennedy)关于细菌的书的评论说，他把细菌放在了人类历史的中心(《系统中的虫子》 ，4月15日)。马克吐温更进一步。 In “Letters from the Earth”, Twain claimed that microbes are God’s favourite creatures: “The microbes were by far the most important part of the Ark’s cargo and the part the Creator was most anxious about and most infatuated with…The large intestine was in effect their heaven.” 在《来自地球的信》一书中，吐温声称微生物是上帝最喜欢的生物: “到目前为止，微生物是方舟货物中最重要的部分，也是造物主最担心和最迷恋的部分... ... 大肠实际上是他们的天堂。” Elsewhere, Twain solved the age-old conundrum of theodicy, or why a benevolent God allows evil to exist. He said we falsely assume God is like us and that we are his favourites. Twain concluded that, based on which species thrive the best, God clearly must be a microbe. 在其他地方，吐温解决了古老的神学难题，也就是为什么仁慈的上帝允许邪恶存在。他说我们错误地认为上帝和我们一样，我们是他的宠儿。吐温的结论是，基于哪个物种繁衍得最好，上帝显然必须是一个微生物。 RICHARD WAUGAMAN Clinical professor of psychiatry 精神病学临床教授 Georgetown University 乔治城大学 Washington, DC 华盛顿特区 Bartleby wrote about employers looking for soft skills when they hire someone (May 13th). I was always keen on employing the very best people. The most effective way to hire the right match was for me to sweep the parking lot upon the candidate’s arrival, pushing a broom and looking busy. Bartleby 写到雇主在招聘员工时寻找软技能(5月13日)。我总是热衷于雇佣最优秀的人才。对我来说，雇佣合适的人选最有效的方法就是在候选人到达时清扫停车场，推着扫帚，看起来很忙。 I would say: “Hello”, wait and observe. It was much more effective than any of the usual techniques. 我会说: “你好”，等待和观察。这是比任何通常的技术更有效。 ANDRÉ MONCHEUR DE RIEUDOTTE Boise, Idaho 博伊西 Her salad days are over 她的黄金时代已经结束了 Bagehot is right (May 20th). Britain really “would rather forget about Liz Truss”. She just won’t lettuce. 白芝浩是对的(5月20日)。英国真的“宁愿忘记丽兹特拉斯”。她只是不会生菜。 JONATHAN ASPIN St Christophe en Brionnais, France 圣克里斯托弗在布里奥奈，法国 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/letters/2023/05/25/letters-to-the-editor 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/letters/2023/05/25/letters-to-the-editor 下载的 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Letters 信件 Sections 部分 Briefing 简报 By Invitation 邀请函 Don’t be fooled by Meta’s fine for data breaches, says Johnny Ryan 不要被梅塔的数据泄露罚款愚弄，约翰尼瑞恩说 Data protection :: Europe’s GDPR, once seen as a model, is an enforcement flop 数据保护: : 欧洲的 GDPR，曾经被视为一种模式，但是执行失败了 A forensic-accounting expert on how to treat the fraud epidemic 法务会计专家，专门研究如何处理欺诈问题 Financial crime :: Encourage more insiders to spill the beans, says Kelly Richmond Pope 金融犯罪: 凯利 · 里士满 · 波普说，鼓励更多的内部人士泄露秘密 Letters 信件 Sections 部分 Briefing 简报

Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Data protection 数据保护 Don’t be fooled by Meta’s fine for data breaches, says Johnny Ryan 不要被梅塔的数据泄露罚款愚弄，约翰尼瑞恩说 Europe’s GDPR, once seen as a model, is an enforcement flop 欧洲的 GDPR 一度被视为一种模式，但在执行方面却一败涂地 May 24th 2023 | 2023年5月24日 T HE EUROPEAN UNION’S best-known law is about to turn five. On May 25th 2018 the EU introduced the General Data Protection Regulation ( GDPR ). Spurred by the revelations of Edward Snowden, an American whistleblower, the bloc’s lawmakers gave Europe’s rights-based approach to data sharp new teeth. 欧盟最著名的法律即将迎来五岁生日。2018年5月25日，欧盟出台了《一般数据保护条例》(GDPR)。在美国泄密者爱德华•斯诺登(Edward Snowden)爆料的刺激下，欧盟议员们为欧洲基于权利的数据处理方式增添了新的锋芒。 Enforcers gained formidable powers, including the authority to raid companies like Google, Meta and Amazon, and to force them to change how they operate. This showpiece regulation was hailed as the new global standard for data protection and privacy. 执法者获得了强大的权力，包括突袭谷歌、梅塔和亚马逊等公司的权力，并迫使它们改变运营方式。这一标志性规定被誉为数据保护和隐私的新的全球标准。 Rarely has hype so contrasted with impact. As the GDPR marks its fifth birthday, Europe remains unable to police big tech’s use of people’s data, despite an enforcement budget of more than €330m ($355m). Most enforcement decisions confirmed at the EU level have been mere reprimands or “amicable settlements”. This is enforcement without tooth or claw. 很少有宣传与影响形成如此鲜明的对比。在 GDPR 诞生5周年之际，欧洲仍无法监管大型科技公司对人们数据的使用，尽管其执法预算超过3.3亿欧元(合3.55亿美元)。在欧盟层面确认的多数执行决定，只不过是谴责或“友好解决”。这是没有牙齿和爪子的强制执行。 Rather than tame big tech firms’ data free-for-alls, Europe’s weak enforcement has allowed them to steal a march on smaller competitors and betray users with data chicanery, while hiding behind legalese. Polling shows that people want tech giants to be regulated. 欧洲软弱的执法不仅没有驯服大型科技公司的数据自由竞争，反而使它们能够抢在规模较小的竞争对手之前，通过数据欺骗欺骗用户，同时躲在法律术语的后面。民意调查显示，人们希望科技巨头受到监管。 But they also have a fatalism about data misuse and are fatigued by consent spam. This offers two important lessons. 但是他们也对数据滥用抱有宿命论的态度，并且厌倦了同意的垃圾邮件，这提供了两个重要的教训。 The first is that Europe has a two-part enforcement crisis. The first part is national. The GDPR ’s “country of origin” principle obliges the country in which a company has its European headquarters to police its data use across the entire EU . Four of the world’s five biggest digital platforms, by market capitalisation, are based in Ireland. But Ireland delivers few major decisions as lead EU enforcer. 首先，欧洲的执法危机分为两部分。第一部分是全国性的。GDPR 的“起源国”原则要求公司欧洲总部所在国监督整个欧盟的数据使用情况。按市值计算，全球五大数字平台中，有四个位于爱尔兰。但作为欧盟的主要执行者，爱尔兰几乎没有做出什么重大决定。 Nor is there much effective co-operation between national enforcers. The animosity between Ireland’s Data Protection Commission and its counterparts elsewhere in the EU was highlighted in January, when the Irish body began legal action against all other EU supervisory authorities (collectively, the European Data Protection Board) at Europe’s highest court. 国家执法机构之间也没有多少有效的合作。今年1月，爱尔兰数据保护委员会(Data Protection Commission)开始在欧洲最高法院对所有其他欧盟监管机构(统称为欧洲数据保护委员会(European Data Protection Board))采取法律行动，突显出该委员会与欧盟其他国家数据保护委员会之间的敌意。 This week the Irish commission fined Meta €1.2bn for violating GDPR rules that require certain safeguards to be in place when data is transferred between Europe and America. The case made headlines around the world. 本周，爱尔兰委员会对 Meta 处以12亿欧元的罚款，因为它违反了 GDPR 规则，该规则要求在欧洲和美国之间传输数据时，必须采取某些保障措施。这个案子成了全世界的头条新闻。 But the truth is that even a penalty of this size is of little consequence to a company that made a net profit of more than $23bn last year. 但事实是，对于一家去年净利润超过230亿美元的公司而言，即便是这种规模的罚款也不会造成什么影响。 The other part of Europe’s enforcement crisis sits within the European Commission. The EU ’s justice commissioner, Didier Reynders, has the tools and legal duty to oversee the implementation of EU law in member states, and to sue them if they refuse to behave. But aside from a few cases about enforcer independence, this has not happened in the realm of big tech and data. 欧洲执法危机的另一部分在于欧盟委员会(European Commission)。欧盟司法专员迪迪埃 · 雷恩德斯(Didier Reynders)有工具和法律义务监督欧盟法律在成员国的执行情况，如果他们拒绝采取行动，他还可以起诉他们。但是，除了少数有关执法者独立性的案例外，这种情况在大型科技和数据领域并没有发生。 To the contrary, Mr Reynders resists any suggestion that there is a problem with how tech giants are supervised by national enforcers, and by Ireland in particular. 相反，雷恩德斯先生反对任何关于科技巨头如何受到国家执法机构，特别是爱尔兰监管的说法。 Rather than insisting on the proper enforcement of GDPR , the commission is instead introducing new legislation on competition, online content and services, and artificial intelligence. Much of this would not be necessary had the GDPR ’s myriad provisions been energetically enforced. 欧盟委员会没有坚持正确执行 GDPR，而是引入了有关竞争、在线内容和服务以及人工智能的新立法。如果 GDPR 的无数条款得到大力执行，那么其中的大部分就没有必要了。 If the GDPR is any guide, these new laws will also be neglected. The commission must start taking member states to task. New EU laws such as the Artificial Intelligence Act must offer less latitude to feckless enforcers. The Digital Markets Act ( DMA ), another sweeping set of rules giving the bloc more powers over large tech firms, must be policed adversarially to rein in “gatekeeper” firms. On competition, Europe should follow the example of America’s reinvigorated trustbusters. 如果 GDPR 是任何指导，这些新的法律也将被忽视。欧盟委员会必须开始责备成员国。新的欧盟法律，例如《人工智能法案》 ，必须为不负责任的执法者提供更少的自由。《数字市场法》(DMA)是另一套全面的规则，赋予欧盟对大型科技公司更大的权力，必须对其进行对抗性监管，以约束“看门人”公司。在竞争方面，欧洲应该效仿美国重振旗鼓的反垄断机构。 It is not clear that this will happen. Big tech has dusted off its GDPR playbook and applied it to these new laws. At a recent commission event a representative from Meta, Facebook’s owner, gave a lengthy oration about the difficulties of designing data-consent requests, which are required by the DMA . EU competition officials listened politely, perhaps unaware that Meta had used this same tactic to blunt the GDPR . 目前尚不清楚这种情况是否会发生。大型科技公司已经重拾 GDPR 战术，并将其应用于这些新法律。在最近的一次委托活动中，Facebook 的所有者 Meta 的一位代表就设计 DMA 要求的数据同意请求的困难发表了长篇大论。欧盟竞争事务官员礼貌地听取了意见，也许没有意识到梅塔使用了同样的策略来削弱 GDPR。 The second lesson from the EU ’s enforcement failure concerns Europe’s global role. Data flows through the world’s digital markets as ichor courses through the veins of the gods. In the first year or so of the GDPR , Europe served as a de-facto global dictator of data flows. Almost all major economies, including China, drafted a GDPR clone or otherwise attempted to accommodate the EU ’s law. (America was the big exception, although some states, including California, now have GDPR -like laws.) 欧盟执法失败的第二个教训与欧洲的全球角色有关。数据在世界数字市场中流动，就像在神的血管中流动一样。在 GDPR 的第一年左右，欧洲实际上成为了数据流动的全球独裁者。几乎所有的主要经济体，包括中国，都起草了一份克隆版的 GDPR，或者试图以其他方式适应欧盟的法律。(美国是一个很大的例外，尽管包括加利福尼亚在内的一些州现在已经有了类似 GDPR 的法律。) But in 2019, a year after the GDPR was first applied, a new European Commission took office and squandered the global position its predecessor had established, by going soft on enforcement. Other countries have since cooled on the idea of mimicking or aligning with the EU . India dumped its draft GDPR clone last year. Britain is watering down its version. American legislators look upon Europe’s plague of consent spam with horror. This growing scepticism matters: Europe will lack credibility to set the global agenda in other areas if it allows its showpiece data law to fade. 但在2019年，也就是 GDPR 首次实施一年后，一个新的欧盟委员会(European Commission)上任，却在执行方面表现软弱，浪费了其前身建立的全球地位。此后，其它国家对效仿欧盟或与欧盟结盟的想法冷却了下来。印度去年抛弃了克隆 GDPR 草案。英国正在淡化其版本。美国立法者对欧洲充斥着同意垃圾邮件的瘟疫感到恐惧。这种日益增长的怀疑态度至关重要: 如果欧洲允许其示范性的数据法律消失，那么它将缺乏在其它领域制定全球议程的可信度。 The current commission’s term ends in October 2024. If the next commission neglects the current one’s new tech laws the same way that Mr Reynders has ignored his predecessor’s, Europe’s regulatory soft power will erode. This would be bad for human rights in Europe and beyond. 目前的委员会任期将于2024年10月结束。如果下一届委员会像雷恩德斯先生忽视其前任那样忽视当前的新技术法律，欧洲的监管软实力将受到侵蚀。这将不利于欧洲及其他地区的人权。 The most important lesson of the first five years of the GDPR is this: if Europe really wants to be a regulatory superpower, it must stop laying down new sedimentary layers of digital law and start enforcing the laws it has. ■ GDPR 头五年最重要的教训是: 如果欧洲真的想成为一个监管超级大国，它必须停止制定新的沉淀层数字法律，并开始执行已有的法律。￭ _______________ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ Johnny Ryan is a senior fellow at the Irish Council for Civil Liberties and the Open Markets Institute. 约翰尼 · 瑞安是爱尔兰公民自由委员会和公开市场研究所的高级研究员。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/by-invitation/2023/05/24/dont-be-fooled-by-metas-fine-for-data-breaches-says-johnny-ryan 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/by-invitation/2023/05/24/dont-be-fooled-by-metas-fine-for-data-breaches-says-johnny-ryan 下载的 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Financial crime 金融犯罪 A forensic-accounting expert on how to treat the fraud epidemic 法务会计专家，专门研究如何处理欺诈问题 Encourage more insiders to spill the beans, says Kelly Richmond Pope Kelly Richmond Pope 说，鼓励更多的内部人士泄露秘密 May 19th 2023 | 2023年5月19日 I T MARKS A spectacular fall from grace for a one-time Silicon Valley star. This week a court in California ruled that, Hail Mary appeals notwithstanding, Elizabeth Holmes must report to prison on May 30th to begin serving an 11-year sentence for fraud. 这标志着一位曾经的硅谷明星的辉煌陨落。本周，加利福尼亚一家法院裁定，尽管万福玛利亚上诉，伊丽莎白 · 霍姆斯必须在5月30日到监狱报到，开始因欺诈罪服刑11年。 Theranos, the startup Ms Holmes had founded in 2003, was worth $9bn at its peak but crashed after its much-vaunted blood-testing technology was shown not to work, and she ended up in the dock for deceiving investors. Theranos 是赫尔姆斯女士在2003年创立的一家初创公司，最高时价值90亿美元，但在其大肆吹嘘的血液检测技术被证明不起作用后破产，她最终因欺骗投资者而被告上了法庭。 Theranos is one of a long list of financial scandals that have made headlines in recent years. Theranos 是近年来成为头条新闻的一长串金融丑闻之一。 Also among these are the frauds at Wirecard, a German payments processor, and Abraaj, a Dubai-based private-equity firm, various crypto-heists, and a bonanza of misappropriation of government handouts to businesses during the covid-19 pandemic. 其中还包括德国支付处理公司 Wirecard 和迪拜私募股权公司 Abraaj 的欺诈行为，各种加密抢劫，以及在2019冠状病毒疾病大流行期间大量挪用政府发放给企业的救济金。 So many frauds are there, and so big are the biggest, that pilfering a billion dollars does not guarantee a global headline. Chances are you haven’t heard of Outcome Health, a Chicago-based health-tech firm whose former CEO and president were recently convicted of defrauding clients, lenders and investors of roughly that amount of money. 这里有如此多的欺诈行为，其中规模最大的也是如此之大，以至于盗窃10亿美元并不能保证成为全球头条新闻。你可能没有听说过 Outcome Health，这是一家总部位于芝加哥的健康科技公司，其前首席执行官和总裁最近被判诈骗客户、贷款人和投资者的金额大致相当。 Beneath the blockbuster frauds in the billions of dollars is an alarmingly long tail of smaller financial scams. Taken together, these add up to a huge global problem. 在数十亿美元巨额欺诈案的背后，是一长串令人震惊的小型金融诈骗案。这些因素加在一起，构成了一个巨大的全球性问题。 Research by Crowe, a financial-advisory firm, and the University of Portsmouth, in England, suggests that fraud costs businesses and individuals across the world more than $5trn each year. That is nearly 60% of what the world spends annually on health care. 英国金融咨询公司 Crowe 和朴茨茅斯大学的研究表明，欺诈每年给全世界的企业和个人造成超过5万亿美元的损失。这几乎是世界卫生保健每年开支的60% 。 The drivers of fraud are many and complex. Sometimes it is down to pure greed. 造成欺诈的因素很多，也很复杂，有时候纯粹是贪婪的结果。 Sometimes it begins with a relatively innocuous attempt to paper over a small financial crack but spirals when that initial effort fails; some believe that’s how it started with Bernie Madoff’s giant Ponzi scheme. 有时，它开始于一个相对无害的试图掩盖一个小的金融裂缝，但螺旋式上升时，最初的努力失败了; 有些人认为，这就是如何开始与伯尼麦道夫的巨大庞氏骗局。 Market pressure and a desire to exceed analysts’ expectations can also play a part: after the global financial crisis of 2007-09, GE was fined $50m for artificially smoothing its profits to keep investors sweet. Accounting ruses like this, which fall in a grey area, are more common than outright fraud. 市场压力和超出分析师预期的愿望也可能起到一定作用: 在2007年至2009年的全球金融危机之后，通用电气因人为地平滑利润以讨好投资者而被罚款5000万美元。像这样的会计伎俩属于灰色地带，比彻头彻尾的欺诈更为常见。 Among tech startups there is even an established term for manipulating the numbers to buy you time to navigate the rocky road to financial respectability: “fake it till you make it.” 在科技初创企业中，甚至有一个既定的术语，用来操纵数字，为你赢得时间，在通往财务上体面的坎坷道路上前进: “假装它，直到你做到它。” Fraud is an all-weather pursuit. Economic booms help fraudsters conceal creative accounting, such as exaggerated revenues. Recessions expose some of this wrongdoing, but they also spawn fresh shenanigans. 诈骗是全天候的追捕。经济繁荣有助于欺诈者隐瞒创造性会计，比如夸大的收入。经济衰退暴露了其中一些不当行为，但它们也催生了新的恶作剧。 As funding dries up, some owners and managers cook the books to stay in business. When survival is at stake, the line between what is acceptable and unacceptable when disclosing information or booking sales can become blurred. 随着资金枯竭，一些所有者和管理者为了维持生意而做假账。当生存受到威胁时，在披露信息或预订销售时，可接受与不可接受之间的界限可能变得模糊。 World events can stoke fraud, too. At the height of the pandemic, an estimated $80bn of American taxpayer money handed out under the Paycheck Protection Programme, set up to assist struggling businesses, was stolen by fraudsters. 世界大事也会助长欺诈。在疫情最严重的时候，美国纳税人根据“薪酬支票保护计划”(Paycheck Protection Programme)发放的约800亿美元资金被欺诈者盗走。该计划是为帮助陷入困境的企业而设立的。 The covid-induced increase in remote working has created new opportunities for miscreants. The 2022 KPMG Fraud Outlook concludes that the surge in working from home has reduced businesses’ ability to monitor employees’ behaviour. Geopolitics affects fraud, too. NATO countries experienced four times as many email-phishing attacks from Russia in 2022 as they did in 2020. Cybercrimes such as ransomware attacks have already transferred a staggering amount of wealth to illicit actors. 由新冠疫情引起的远程工作的增加为恶棍创造了新的机会。《2022年毕马威欺诈展望》(2022 KPMG Fraud Outlook)总结称，在家工作的激增降低了企业监控员工行为的能力。地缘政治也会影响欺诈。2022年，北约国家遭受的来自俄罗斯的电子邮件钓鱼攻击是2020年的四倍。勒索软件攻击等网络犯罪已经将巨额财富转移给了非法行为者。 The costs to businesses range from the theft of data, intellectual property and money to post-attack disruption, lost productivity and systems upgrades. 企业的成本从数据、知识产权和资金被盗，到攻击后的中断、生产力下降和系统升级。 It is panglossian to think fraud can be eliminated, but more can be done to reduce it. Corporate boards and investors need to ask more questions. Investors are often too quick to take comfort from the presence of big names on the list of owners and directors. 认为欺诈可以被消除是盲目乐观的，但是还可以做更多的事情来减少欺诈。公司董事会和投资者需要提出更多问题。投资者往往过于迅速地从股东和董事名单上的大人物身上获得安慰。 Some were clearly wowed by Theranos’s star-studded board, whose members included two former US secretaries of state and the ex-boss of Wells Fargo, a big bank. 一些人显然对 Theranos 众星云集的董事会感到惊叹，其成员包括两位前美国国务卿和大银行富国银行(Wells Fargo)的前老板。 Regulators need to be more sceptical, too. America’s Securities and Exchange Commission brushed aside a detailed and devastating analysis of Madoff’s business provided by a concerned fund manager, Harry Markopolos. 监管机构也需要更加怀疑。美国证券交易委员会(Securities and Exchange Commission)对一位关心麦道夫的基金经理哈里•马科波洛斯(Harry Markopolos)提供的有关麦道夫业务的详尽而毁灭性的分析置之不理。 Germany’s financial-markets regulator was similarly dismissive of the short-sellers and journalists who called out Wirecard. 德国金融市场监管机构同样对呼吁使用 Wirecard 的卖空者和记者不屑一顾。 The most effective change would be to do more to encourage whistleblowers. Falsified financial statements must start with someone who notices fraudulent acts. When fraud happens, many people ask “Where were the auditors?”. 最有效的改变将是采取更多措施鼓励举报人。伪造的财务报表必须从注意到欺诈行为的人开始。当欺诈发生时，许多人会问“审计员在哪里?”. But the question should be “Where were the whistleblowers?” 但问题应该是“告密者在哪里?” As important as sceptical investors, regulators and journalists can be, much fraud would be undetectable without someone on the inside willing to spill the beans. Research shows that more than 40% of frauds are discovered by a whistleblower. 尽管持怀疑态度的投资者、监管机构和记者可能非常重要，但如果内部没有人愿意泄露秘密，很多欺诈行为将无法被发现。研究表明，超过40% 的欺诈行为是由告密者发现的。 The Wirecard scandal came to light largely because of the bravery of Pav Gill, one of the company’s lawyers, who went to the press with his concerns. The Theranos fraud was brought to the attention of the authorities and the Wall Street Journal by whistleblowing employees (one of whom was the grandson of a former political bigwig on the board).
