Wirecard 丑闻之所以曝光，很大程度上是因为该公司的律师之一帕夫•吉尔(Pav Gill)向媒体表达了自己的担忧。Theranos 欺诈案引起了当局和《华尔街日报》的注意，因为一些告密的雇员(其中一个是董事会前政治大人物的孙子)。 Too often, companies seek to silence whistleblowers, or portray them as mad, bad or both: Wirecard, for instance, fought back ferociously against Mr Gill’s allegations and the journalists who investigated them. 很多时候，公司试图让告密者闭嘴，或者把他们描绘成疯子、坏人或者两者兼而有之: 例如，Wirecard 公司对吉尔先生的指控和调查这些指控的记者进行了猛烈的反击。 Organisations need to create safe spaces where employees can voice their concerns about wrongdoing. Internal reporting channels need to be robust, and employees educated on how to use them. Creating an environment where whistleblowers are celebrated, not vilified, is critical. 企业需要创造一个安全的空间，让员工能够表达他们对不当行为的担忧。内部报告渠道需要健全，员工需要接受如何使用这些渠道的教育。创造一个举报人受到赞扬而不是中伤的环境是至关重要的。 Companies should worry more about anyone who can circumvent the controls, such as senior leaders or star employees, than about those inclined to raise concerns. 企业更应该担心的是那些能够规避管制的人，比如高管或明星员工，而不是那些倾向于提出担忧的人。 Governments, too, could do more. Protections for whistleblowers have been recognised as part of international law since 2003 when the United Nations adopted the Convention Against Corruption, and this has since been ratified by 137 countries. 政府也可以做得更多。自2003年联合国通过《反腐败公约》以来，对举报人的保护已被确认为国际法的一部分，此后有137个国家批准了该公约。 In reality, legal protections are patchy. They are strongest in America, which offers bounties to whistleblowers who provide information that leads to fines or imprisonment. 实际上，法律保护是零零碎碎的。他们在美国最为强大，对提供信息导致罚款或监禁的告密者给予奖励。 In much of Europe, and elsewhere, the law is still too soft on those who muzzle or retaliate against alarm-ringers. 在欧洲大部分地区和其他地区，法律对那些对警报响起者保持沉默或进行报复的人仍然过于宽松。 Fraud can be reduced. But first we must better understand who commits it, educate people on how to report it, and then ensure that policies protect those who choose to come forward. Until we do, financial crime will remain a multi-trillion-dollar scourge. ■ 可以减少欺诈。但首先，我们必须更好地了解是谁干的，教育人们如何报告，然后确保政策保护那些选择站出来的人。在此之前，金融犯罪仍将是一个数万亿美元的灾难。￭ Kelly Richmond Pope is the Barry Jay Epstein Endowed Professor of Forensic Accounting at DePaul University in Chicago, and the author of “Fool Me Once: Scams, Stories, and Secrets from the Trillion-Dollar Fraud Industry”. 凯利•里奇蒙德•波普是芝加哥德保罗大学法医会计学的巴里•杰•爱泼斯坦(Barry Jay Epstein)教授，著有《愚弄我一次: 来自万亿美元欺诈行业的骗局、故事和秘密》(Fool Me Once: sams，Stories，and secret from the 兆美元欺诈行业)一书。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/by-invitation/2023/05/19/a-forensic-accounting-expert-on-how-to-treat-the-fraud-epidemic 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/by-invitation/2023/05/19/a-forensic-accounting-expert-on-how-to-treat-the-fraud-epidemic 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

By Invitation 邀请函 Sections 部分 United States 美国 Briefing 简报 Ron DeSantis has little chance of beating Donald Trump ❗ 🔄 A bungled coup :: Hopes of depriving the former president of the Republican nomination are fading 失败的政变: 剥夺前总统共和党提名的希望正在消失 By Invitation 邀请函 Sections 部分 United States 美国

Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 A bungled coup 一场失败的政变 Ron DeSantis has little chance of beating Donald Trump ❗ 🔄 Hopes of depriving the former president of the Republican nomination are fading 剥夺这位前总统共和党总统候选人提名的希望越来越渺茫 May 24th 2023 | Washington, DC 2023年5月24日 | 华盛顿特区 B ELATEDLY AND nervously, the would-be assassins have been lining up. On May 22nd Tim Scott, a senator from South Carolina, became the latest Republican to announce a run for president. 迟来的，紧张的，潜在的刺客已经排队等候。5月22日，来自南卡罗来纳州的参议员蒂姆 · 斯科特成为最新一位宣布竞选总统的共和党人。 Greater fanfare accompanied the official declaration (on Twitter) on May 24th that Ron DeSantis, the governor of Florida, is joining the race for the Republican nomination. He has been widely heralded as the candidate with the best chance of defeating the favourite, Donald Trump. 5月24日，佛罗里达州州长罗恩 · 德桑蒂斯(Ron DeSantis)在 Twitter 上正式宣布将加入共和党总统候选人提名角逐。人们普遍认为，他是最有可能击败最受欢迎的候选人唐纳德•特朗普(Donald Trump)的候选人。 But even as more plotters step forward, the chances of a successful coup to overthrow Mr Trump are growing slimmer by the day. 但是，即使有更多的阴谋家站出来，成功推翻特朗普先生的政变的可能性一天比一天小。 Just a short while ago Mr DeSantis seemed successfully to have engineered a form of Trumpism without Trump. In November he won re-election in Florida by a resplendent 19-point margin. 就在不久前，德桑蒂斯先生似乎成功地创造了一种没有特朗普的特朗普主义。去年11月，他以19个百分点的优势在佛罗里达州赢得连任。 In damning contrast, Mr Trump’s attempts to play kingmaker in the midterm elections went disastrously wrong, with many of the candidates he endorsed losing races that the Republican Party had considered in the bag. 与此形成鲜明对比的是，特朗普先生试图在中期选举中扮演“国王制定者”的角色，结果却是灾难性的错误，他支持的许多候选人都输掉了共和党认为已经囊中羞涩的竞选。 Mr Trump appeared suitably deflated when he launched his presidential campaign on November 15th at Mar-a-Lago, his estate in Palm Beach, Florida. Back then some opinion polls put the two candidates neck-and-neck among primary voters. 特朗普先生11月15日在他位于佛罗里达州棕榈滩的庄园 Mar-a-Lago 开始他的总统竞选活动时，显得相当沮丧。当时的一些民意调查显示，两位候选人在初选选民中的支持率不相上下。 Six months later it is Mr DeSantis who has been stumbling. 六个月后，是德桑提斯先生跌跌撞撞。 He has been criticised as an isolationist for dismissing the war in Ukraine as a mere “territorial dispute”, as an anti-corporatist for picking fights with Disney and as an extremist for signing a ban on abortion beyond six weeks of pregnancy. 他被批评为孤立主义者，因为他将乌克兰战争斥为一场纯粹的“领土争端”; 他反社团主义，因为他挑起与迪士尼的争端; 他签署了一项禁止孕期超过6周的堕胎的禁令，因此他被批评为极端主义者。 Mr Trump, meanwhile, has recovered his step, making a triumphant return to the campaign trail. He and his team have spent months pummelling Mr DeSantis. 与此同时，特朗普先生已经收复失地，凯旋而归，重返竞选之路。他和他的团队花了几个月的时间来打击 DeSantis 先生。 In retrospect, the governor’s decision to wait until Florida’s legislature had completed its annual session earlier this month before announcing his candidacy looks like a miscalculation, although the delay was in part to allow lawmakers to pass a bill permitting him to run for president without resigning as governor. 现在回想起来，州长决定等到佛罗里达州立法机构本月早些时候结束年度会议后再宣布他的候选人资格，这看起来像是一个错误的估计，尽管这种延迟在一定程度上是为了让议员们能够通过一项法案，允许他在不辞去州长职务的情况下竞选总统。 During the long interlude he has endured a protracted beating on the airwaves without offering much defence. It is as if Brutus had overslept on the Ides of March, giving Julius Caesar a chance to put on his armour, but had tried to proceed with his hit job all the same. 在这漫长的间歇期间，他在电视广播中遭受了长时间的打击，却没有提供多少保护。这就好像布鲁图斯在选战风云上睡过头了，给了凯撒一个穿上盔甲的机会，但他还是试图继续他的刺杀任务。 The plot to overthrow Mr Trump, which once seemed plausible, now looks forlorn. 推翻特朗普先生的阴谋一度看似合理，但现在看来已经失败。 Lend me your cheers 让我听到你的欢呼 In 2016 Mr Trump ran as an insurgent. In 2020 he ran as the incumbent. For 2024, he is running as a hybrid of the two—insurrectionist and institutionalist all at once. It is proving to be a potent combination, which has helped to build a huge lead in the early polling. 2016年，特朗普先生以叛乱分子的身份参选。2020年，他以现任总统的身份参选。对于2024年，他将同时以两者的混合体——叛乱分子和制度主义者的身份参选。事实证明，这是一个强有力的组合，有助于在早期民调中建立巨大的领先优势。 Among likely voters in the primary, Mr Trump retains the stature of a former president, but is also seen as the put-upon underdog, owing to his claims that the election of 2020 was stolen, his bans from social media and his many legal troubles . Early stumbles, such as his lacklustre campaign launch and an embarrassing dinner with a white supremacist, have been righted by a new campaign operation that is more professional than the chaotic family affairs of Mr Trump’s past runs. 在可能参加初选的选民中，特朗普先生保持着前总统的地位，但也被视为受压迫者，因为他声称2020年的大选是被盗的，他被禁止使用社交媒体，以及他面临许多法律麻烦。早期的挫折，比如他平淡无奇的竞选启动和与白人至上主义者的尴尬晚宴，已经被一个新的竞选运作所纠正，这个运作比特朗普先生过去的混乱家庭事务更加专业。 What is more, the Republican Party has been thoroughly Trumpified. This is especially true at the state level, where most of the rules for primaries are set. Unlike 2016, when the party establishment shunned Mr Trump, today he dominates the race for elite endorsements. 更重要的是，共和党已经彻底被特朗普化了。在州级别上尤其如此，因为大多数主要规则都是在州级别上设置的。与2016年党内建制派回避特朗普先生不同，今天他主导了精英支持者的竞争。 Many big donors have defected to alternatives such as Mr DeSantis, but this is not as damaging as it sounds. 许多大的捐赠者已经转向了像德桑蒂斯先生这样的替代者，但是这并没有听起来那么有害。 Mr Trump will have no trouble raising money from his army of small-dollar donors and will again be able to portray himself as the authentic tribune of the people, assailed by the henchmen of the out-of-touch elite. 特朗普先生将毫不费力地从他的小额捐赠者军队中筹集资金，并且将再次能够把自己描绘成真正的人民护民官，受到那些与世隔绝的精英们的追随者的攻击。 Among the Republican base, he has universal name recognition and near-universal admiration. 在共和党基础选民中，他有着普遍的知名度和近乎普遍的钦佩。 This reverence for Mr Trump makes it difficult for his competitors to attack him. Most of them come not to bury Trumpism, but to praise it. But the resulting muddle blunts their appeal. 这种对特朗普先生的尊敬使得他的竞争对手很难攻击他。他们中的大多数人不是来埋葬特朗普主义，而是来赞美它。但由此产生的混乱削弱了它们的吸引力。 Jason Miller, a spokesman for the Trump campaign, puts it this way, “Why would you go to see a Rolling Stones tribute band, when the Rolling Stones themselves are still on tour?” 特朗普竞选团队的发言人杰森•米勒(Jason Miller)这样说道: “当滚石乐队们还在巡回演出的时候，你为什么要去看滚石乐队的致敬演出呢?” Are the primaries over before they have begun? Some think so. “Hope is not a strategy,” says Rick Wilson, a former Republican operative who now runs the Lincoln Project, an anyone-but-Trump outfit. He thinks Mr Trump would have to be incapacitated to lose. 初选还没开始就结束了吗？有人这么认为。“希望不是一种策略,”前共和党特工里克•威尔逊(Rick Wilson)表示。威尔逊目前负责林肯项目(Lincoln Project) ，这是一家只支持特朗普的机构。他认为特朗普先生必须丧失失败的能力。 “Donald Trump will start taking DeSantis’s head off in debates and kicking it around like a soccer ball. And the Republican base voters want that show,” he says. “唐纳德•特朗普(Donald Trump)将开始在辩论中砍下德桑蒂斯的头，像踢足球一样到处踢。共和党基层选民希望看到这样的表演。”。 Ask closeted anti-Trump Republicans around Washington how the primary will go, and many conjure scenes of brutality worthy of the Gallic wars. 问问华盛顿周围未出柜的反对特朗普的共和党人初选将如何进行，许多人会联想到配得上高卢战争的残暴场面。 More optimistic sorts, however, point out that there is still a long way to go, even if Mr Trump’s chariot is building speed. “We’re still months and months and months away from voting. 然而，更乐观的人指出，即使特朗普先生的战车正在加速，仍有很长的路要走。“我们离投票还有好几个月。 National polls are pretty meaningless—they are going to mean something once voting has started,” says David Kochel, a Republican consultant based in Iowa (the first state to vote) who previously worked with Mitt Romney and Jeb Bush. 全国性的民意调查毫无意义，一旦投票开始，它们就有了意义。”大卫•科切尔(David Kochel)是爱荷华州(第一个投票的州)的共和党顾问，曾与米特•罗姆尼(Mitt Romney)和杰布•布什(Jeb Bush)共事。 Consultants for struggling campaigns swap Cinderella stories about late-breaking candidates who went from 2% in Iowa to 20% in the space of a month. 苦苦挣扎的竞选顾问们交换着灰姑娘的故事，讲述着最后一批候选人在一个月内从爱荷华州的2% 上升到20% 的故事。 Early polls, it is true, do not predict the final results as well as later ones. But that does not mean they are useless. There is a correlation between early strength and the final results (see chart 1). 的确，早期的民意调查并不能像预测后期的结果那样预测最终的结果。但这并不意味着它们毫无用处。早期强度与最终结果之间存在相关性(见图表1)。 At present, Mr Trump commands the support of almost 55% of primary voters according to a polling average constructed by FiveThirtyEight, a data-journalism outfit. That is more than 30 percentage points more than Mr DeSantis’s average. 目前，根据数据新闻机构 FiveThirtyEight 的民调平均结果，特朗普先生赢得了近55% 的初选选民的支持。这比德桑蒂斯先生的平均水平高出了30个百分点。 In contested primaries over the past 40 years, six of the eight candidates who have polled above 30% this far ahead of the voting went on to capture their party’s nomination. 在过去40年竞争激烈的初选中，8位候选人中有6位在投票前就已经获得了超过30% 的选票，并获得了党内提名。 The two candidates who failed to capitalise on such big leads were Ted Kennedy, who ran against an incumbent president, Jimmy Carter, in 1980, and Hillary Clinton, who was eventually upstaged in 2008 by the charismatic Barack Obama. 未能利用如此巨大领先优势的两位候选人分别是泰德•肯尼迪(Ted Kennedy)和希拉里•克林顿(Hillary Clinton)。肯尼迪曾在1980年与现任总统吉米•卡特(Jimmy Carter)竞选总统，而希拉里最终在2008年被富有魅力的巴拉克•奥巴马(Barack Obama)抢了风头。 The Republican candidate who comes closest to both Carter-like incumbency and Obama-like magnetism this year is, of course, Mr Trump. 当然，今年最接近卡特式现任总统和奥巴马式魅力的共和党候选人是特朗普先生。 The problem is not simply one of a divided field. Although there are already six declared, halfway plausible candidates, with more to come in all likelihood, even a two-horse race would favour Mr Trump. Recent polling conducted for The Economist by YouGov finds that, in a head-to-head contest, Republican primary voters prefer Mr Trump to Mr DeSantis by 58% to 25%. And the former president’s support is not at all reluctant or half-hearted. Fully 64% of Republicans want Mr Trump to run again, despite the many alternatives. 这个问题不仅仅是一个被分割的领域。尽管已经有六位已经宣布的、半合理的候选人，而且很有可能还会有更多的候选人，但即使是两匹马的竞争也会有利于特朗普先生。YouGov 最近为《经济学人》进行的民意调查发现，在一场面对面的竞争中，共和党初选选民以58% 对25% 的比例支持特朗普先生，而不是德桑蒂斯先生。这位前总统的支持一点也不勉强或半心半意。整整64% 的共和党人希望特朗普先生再次竞选，尽管有很多选择。 Democrats, by contrast, are much less energised about Joe Biden, their presumptive nominee: only 53% of them want the president to run again. 相比之下，民主党人对他们的假定提名人乔•拜登(Joe Biden)则没有那么大的热情: 只有53% 的民主党人希望总统再次参选。 In 2016 Mr Trump’s opponents thought his outrageous statements and chaotic campaign would inevitably lead to political failure. That proved hopelessly Pollyannaish. Most now assume there is little that Mr Trump could say or do to alienate his base. 在2016年，特朗普先生的反对者认为他的无礼言论和混乱的竞选将不可避免地导致政治失败。这被证明是盲目的盲目乐观。现在大多数人认为，特朗普先生几乎不可能说什么或做什么来疏远他的基础选民。 But some opponents still hope that his campaign will implode because of the sort of messy infighting that characterised his entire time in the White House. 但一些反对者仍然希望他的竞选活动会因为他在白宫任职期间经常发生的那种混乱的内斗而崩溃。 The hitch is that the chaos candidate is a bit more orderly this time. In 2016 Mr Trump’s campaign attracted a fair number of charlatans. Current management is more stable. Susie Wiles, one of its leaders, ran Mr Trump’s successful operation in Florida in both 2016 and 2020. She also headed Mr DeSantis’s run for governor in 2018, when he was Mr Trump’s protégé. 问题是这次的混沌候选者更有序一些。2016年，特朗普先生的竞选活动吸引了相当数量的江湖骗子。目前的管理更加稳定。其领导人之一苏茜 · 怀尔斯(Susie Wiles)曾在2016年和2020年两次成功地负责特朗普先生在佛罗里达州的行动。她还领导了德桑蒂斯2018年的州长竞选，当时他是特朗普先生的门生。 There is little family involvement: Ivanka Trump and Jared Kushner, the former president’s daughter and son-in-law, who served as senior advisers when he was in the White House, are not expected to play any part in the campaign. 伊万卡 · 特朗普(Ivanka Trump)和前总统的女儿和女婿贾里德 · 库什纳(Jared Kushner)几乎没有家庭参与: 预计他们不会在竞选中发挥任何作用。库什纳在特朗普入主白宫期间担任高级顾问。 Mr Trump also has much more personal experience: this is his third run for president (fourth if you count his abortive campaign with the Reform Party in 2000). His Republican opponents are all running for the first time. 特朗普先生还有更多的个人经历: 这是他第三次竞选总统(如果算上他在2000年与改革党的竞选失败的话，是第四次)。他的共和党对手都是第一次参加竞选。 Mr Trump’s team is assiduous. “Shortly after I got elected, the next day or a couple of days later, I received a voicemail from President Trump congratulating me,” says Mike Brown, the chairman of the Republican Party in Kansas. Trump 先生的团队很勤奋。堪萨斯州共和党主席迈克•布朗(Mike Brown)表示: “我当选后不久，第二天或几天后，就收到了特朗普总统的语音邮件，祝贺我。”。 The Trump campaign has been “exceptional” in communicating about the primary in the state, he says. 他表示，特朗普的竞选团队在该州初选的沟通方面“非常出色”。 The rules that will translate the results of individual state primaries and caucuses (in effect, decentralised party conferences) into votes for contenders for the nomination at the party’s national convention are arcane and ever-changing. 将各州初选和党团会议(实际上是分散的党内会议)的结果转化为党内全国代表大会提名候选人投票的规则晦涩难懂，且不断变化。 Unlike the Democratic primaries, in which a state’s votes are typically split among the candidates in proportion to their share of the vote, many Republican primaries follow a winner-take-all system. 与民主党初选不同的是，许多共和党初选遵循的是赢家通吃的制度。在民主党初选中，一个州的选票通常按照候选人所占选票的比例分配给候选人。 That can turn a relatively narrow lead in terms of the share of the vote into a crushing dominion over delegates. While Mr Trump was president, he encouraged states to adopt such systems or other rules that help the front-runner. 这可能会使得在选票份额方面相对微弱的领先优势变成对代表的压倒性支配。在特朗普先生担任总统期间，他鼓励各州采用这样的制度或其他规则来帮助领先者。 And the dates of the primaries keep moving even further forward, giving insurgent candidates ever less time to build momentum (see chart 2). 而且初选的日期还在不断向前推进，给了反叛分子候选人更少的时间来建立势头(见图表2)。 Mr Trump’s team has been seizing a march on its adversaries in other respects, too, diligently soliciting endorsements from senior figures in the party with calls and invitations to the imperial court at Mar-a-Lago. 特朗普先生的团队也在其他方面赶超其对手，通过给马阿拉歌庄园的朝廷打电话和发出邀请，积极地争取党内高层人物的支持。 Such support was not forthcoming in 2016, but has been flooding in ahead of next year’s contest. Mr Trump has already racked up the endorsements of 51 of the 222 Republicans in the House of Representatives and ten of the 49 Republican senators. 这种支持在2016年没有出现，但在明年的角逐之前已经涌入。特朗普先生已经获得了众议院222名共和党议员中的51名和49名共和党参议员中的10名的支持。 His opponents have just seven representatives and two senators among them all. The Trump campaign takes particular pride in its success with Florida’s delegation to Congress, who have largely spurned their state’s governor. 他的对手只有七名众议员和两名参议员。特朗普的竞选团队尤为自豪的是，他们在佛罗里达州的国会代表团取得了成功，而这个代表团基本上对该州州长不屑一顾。 Eleven of Florida’s 20 Republican representatives have endorsed Mr Trump, compared with just one for Mr DeSantis. Mr DeSantis and Mr Scott could not expect many endorsements before entering the race, of course, but they have a lot of catching up to do. 佛罗里达州的20名共和党代表中有11人支持特朗普先生，而德桑蒂斯先生只有一人。当然，德桑蒂斯先生和斯科特先生在参加竞选之前不能指望得到很多支持，但他们还有很多事情要做。 Operationally, at least, Mr DeSantis can certainly hope to catch up. He is a prodigious fundraiser, taking in an astounding $217m during his gubernatorial run in 2022, much of which went unspent. 至少在操作上，德桑蒂斯先生肯定有希望赶上来。他是一个惊人的资金筹集者，在2022年竞选州长期间获得了惊人的2.17亿美元，其中大部分没有花掉。 Because Mr DeSantis is the favourite among deep-pocketed donors (though his stances on abortion, Disney and Ukraine have irked some), he will rely heavily on an organisation called “Never Back Down”, a so-called super PAC that can take unlimited donations as long as it does not co-ordinate with the official campaign. A person familiar with Mr DeSantis’s thinking says he could raise $250m between his official campaign and the super PAC . The mastermind of Never Back Down is Jeff Roe, the founder of a consultancy called Axiom Strategies, which worked on both the failed presidential campaign in 2016 of Ted Cruz, a Republican senator from Texas, and Glenn Youngkin’s successful bid for governor in Virginia in 2021. Never Back Down is expected to carpet-bomb television with advertisements and also pay for the canvassers and organisers who must traipse around early-voting states for months. 由于德桑蒂斯先生是财力雄厚的捐赠者中最受欢迎的(尽管他在堕胎、迪斯尼和乌克兰问题上的立场惹恼了一些人) ，他将严重依赖一个名为“永不退缩”的组织，这是一个所谓的超级政治行动委员会，只要不与官方活动协调，就可以接受无限制的捐赠。一位熟悉德桑蒂斯先生想法的人士表示，他可以在他的正式竞选活动和超级政治行动委员会之间筹集2.5亿美元。“永不退缩”的策划者是咨询公司 Axiom Strategies 的创始人杰夫 · 罗(Jeff Roe) ，该公司曾参与德克萨斯州共和党参议员特德 · 克鲁兹(Ted Cruz)2016年失败的总统竞选，以及格伦 · 扬金(Glenn Youngkin)2021年成功竞选弗吉尼亚州州长。预计《决不后退》将用广告对电视台进行地毯式轰炸，并为那些必须在提前投票的州游荡数月的游说者和组织者支付费用。 The official campaign will be run by members of Mr DeSantis’s inner circle in Florida. The likely campaign manager, Generra Peck, headed his most recent campaign for governor. His polling guru will probably be another homegrown talent, Ryan Tyson. 官方的竞选活动将由德桑蒂斯先生在佛罗里达州的核心集团成员进行。可能担任竞选经理的佩克将军(GenerraPeck)领导了他最近的州长竞选活动。他的民意调查专家很可能是另一位本土天才，瑞安 · 泰森。 Mr DeSantis is the favourite to win the arms race for donations. He will have plenty of campaign veterans who know how to spend his takings. 德桑蒂斯先生是赢得捐款军备竞赛的热门人选。他将有很多知道如何使用他的收入的竞选老兵。 I came, I saw, I squandered 我来了，我看见了，我挥霍了 If cash were all it took to become president, America would be midway through the first term of Michael Bloomberg, a tycoon and former mayor of New York who lavished $1.1bn on a failed campaign for the Democratic nomination in 2020. Persuading Republican voters to defect from Mr Trump, as Mr DeSantis must now do, is a difficult task. 如果只需要现金就能成为总统，那么美国迈克尔·布隆伯格的第一个任期将进行到一半。这位大亨、前纽约市长曾在2020年民主党总统候选人提名竞选中挥霍11亿美元，但最终以失败告终。说服共和党选民背叛特朗普先生，就像德桑蒂斯先生现在必须做的那样，是一项艰巨的任务。 Many obvious avenues of attack, the ones that might appeal to swing voters in a general election, are non-starters in the primary. 许多明显的攻击途径，那些可能在大选中吸引摇摆不定的选民的途径，在初选中都是不起作用的。 Mr Trump’s encouragement of the mob that stormed Congress on January 6th 2021 hardly imperils his standing in the party, most of which believes that the election of 2020 was stolen. 特朗普先生对2021年1月6日席卷国会的暴民的鼓励丝毫没有危及他在党内的地位，大多数党员认为2020年的选举是偷来的。 Nor can his opponents make hay from the inquiries, investigations and lawsuits that have dogged the president since he left office, from the FBI ’s search for classified documents at Mar-a-Lago to his recent indictment by Manhattan’s district attorney for fraudulent book-keeping. These indignities, far from diminishing Mr Trump in Republicans’ eyes, have turned him into a martyr. 他的反对者也无法从总统离任后一直困扰他的调查、调查和诉讼中获利，从联邦调查局(FBI)在马阿拉歌庄园(Mar-a-Lago)搜寻机密文件，到他最近被曼哈顿地区检察官以欺诈簿记的罪名起诉。在共和党人眼中，这些侮辱非但没有削弱特朗普先生的地位，反而把他变成了一名烈士。 Even political rivals like Mr DeSantis have felt obliged to come to his defence. 甚至像德桑蒂斯先生这样的政治对手也觉得有必要为他辩护。 The party firmly believes in Trumpism, the blend of populism, protectionism, nativism and disdain for institutions and norms. The old neoconservative and business-friendly order has been swept away. 党坚信 Trumpism 是民粹主义、保护主义、本土主义以及对制度和规范的蔑视的混合体。旧的新保守主义和有利于商业的秩序已经被扫除。 The other candidates’ failure to present a “value proposition”, says Mr Miller of the Trump campaign, “will impede their ability to build coalitions and actually go and challenge the president…Saying that you’re Trump without the drama, which is kind of where it seems Ron DeSantis is going—that just doesn’t work.” 特朗普竞选团队的米勒先生表示，其他候选人未能展现出“价值主张”，“将阻碍他们建立联盟的能力，并且实际上会去挑战总统... ... 说你是特朗普，但没有戏剧性，这似乎就是罗恩•德桑蒂斯(Ron DeSantis)要去的地方——这根本行不通。” Presidential primaries are often popularity contests more than they are policy debates—and this one will be little different. All the candidates agree that Mr Biden is intentionally wrecking the country, that wokeness has run amok and that the southern border must be sealed. 总统初选往往是民意测验，而不是政策辩论ーー这次也不会有什么不同。所有的候选人都认为拜登先生是在故意破坏这个国家，觉醒已经失控，南部边境必须被封锁。 There are only modest signs of disagreement. Mr Trump is attacking Mr DeSantis from the left on fiscal matters, arguing that he will endanger Social Security (the state pension) and Medicare (free health care for the elderly), two sacrosanct programmes among older voters. 只有少量迹象表明双方存在分歧。特朗普先生在财政问题上攻击德桑蒂斯先生，认为他将危及社会保障(国家养老金)和医疗保险(老年人免费医疗保健) ，这两个在老年选民中神圣不可侵犯的项目。 Mr Scott is calling for less confrontational politics. More internationalist candidates such as Nikki Haley, a former governor of South Carolina, argue that continuing to back Ukraine is vital for America’s national security. 斯科特先生呼吁减少政治对抗。更多的国际主义候选人，比如前南卡罗来纳州州长尼基•黑利(niki Haley)认为，继续支持乌克兰对美国的国家安全至关重要。 Others are more isolationist than Mr Trump. Vivek Ramaswamy, a wealthy Indian-American entrepreneur from Ohio, says, “We’ve got to be willing to ban most US businesses from doing business in China until the CCP [Chinese Communist Party] falls or until the CCP radically reforms itself.” That is a harder decoupling than the other candidates have proposed. He also wants to ban affirmative action, shut down the FBI and use the army to dismantle Mexican cartels that ship fentanyl across the border. Early polls show some success in peeling off Trump voters, but it is a delicate operation. “We rally behind America first for a reason. It’s not Trump first,” says Mr Ramaswamy. 其他人比特朗普先生更加孤立主义。来自俄亥俄州的富有的印裔美国企业家维韦克•拉马斯瓦米(Vivek Ramaswamy)表示: “我们必须愿意禁止大多数美国企业在中国做生意，直到中共(中国共产党)垮台，或者直到中共进行彻底改革。”这比其他候选人提出的“脱钩”难度更大。他还想禁止平权法案，关闭联邦调查局，利用军队瓦解运送芬太尼过境的墨西哥贩毒集团。早期的民意调查显示，在拉拢特朗普选民方面取得了一些成功，但这是一个微妙的操作。“我们首先支持美国是有原因的。它不是特朗普第一,”拉马斯瓦米先生说。 “I’m running against him, but to be clear, I still recoil at the idea that the nominee should somehow be the product of some plot of Republican donors.” “我正在与他竞选，但要明确的是，我仍然反对这样一种观点，即提名人应该是某些共和党捐助者阴谋的产物。” But the conspirators must at some point coalesce if they are to be rid of Caesar. In 2016 the fractured field, combined with winner-take-all primary rules, helped Mr Trump triumph. 但如果要除掉凯撒，阴谋者必须在某个时刻联合起来。2016年，支离破碎的领域，加上赢家通吃的初选规则，帮助特朗普先生取得了胜利。 Whereas Democrats united behind Mr Biden in 2020 when it became apparent that Bernie Sanders, a left-wing ideologue, might secure the nomination, Republicans may be hard to corral. At the moment, Mr DeSantis certainly looks like the strongest rival to Mr Trump. 2020年，当左翼理论家伯尼 · 桑德斯(Bernie Sanders)可能获得提名成为明显事实时，民主党人团结一致支持拜登先生，而共和党人可能很难说服。目前，德桑蒂斯先生看起来无疑是特朗普先生最有力的竞争对手。 But the other candidates seem much keener to attack him than the former president, for fear of alienating the base. Ms Haley has taken to denouncing Mr DeSantis for feuding with Disney, for instance. 但其他候选人似乎比前总统更热衷于攻击他，因为他们担心疏远基础选民。例如，黑利女士已经开始谴责德桑蒂斯先生与迪斯尼的不和。 Other prominent Republicans, such as Kari Lake, a failed candidate for governor of Arizona last year, and Kristi Noem, the governor of South Dakota, have taken to swiping at Mr DeSantis as well. 其他著名的共和党人，如去年竞选亚利桑那州州长失败的卡莉 · 雷克和南达科他州州长克里斯蒂 · 诺姆，也开始抨击德桑蒂斯先生。 They may be auditioning for the job of vice-president to Mr Trump, and see such attacks as a quick way into his good graces. 他们可能是在为特朗普先生的副总统职位试镜，并将此类攻击视为赢得他好感的捷径。 Despite his stumbles in recent months, Mr DeSantis is a clever and driven man. He may regain his lean and hungry look and let slip the dogs of war. 尽管他最近几个月跌跌撞撞，但德桑蒂斯先生是一个聪明而有上进心的人。他可能会恢复瘦削和饥饿的样子，让战争的恶犬溜走。 Many voters will pay close attention to the televised debates, the first of which will be held in August in Wisconsin (although the former president may not participate). 许多选民将密切关注电视辩论，第一场辩论将于8月在威斯康星州举行(尽管这位前总统可能不会参加)。 Mr DeSantis could point out that the national debt soared by $8trn during Mr Trump’s presidency; that crime and illegal immigration both increased despite all the former president’s big talk on both scores; that covid-19 was poorly contained and that another epidemic, of wokeness, became more rather than less contagious during his presidency. 德桑蒂斯先生可以指出，在特朗普总统任期内，国家债务飙升了8万亿美元; 犯罪和非法移民都有所增加，尽管这位前总统在这两方面都大谈特谈; 2019冠状病毒疾病控制不力; 另一种流行病——觉醒——在特朗普总统任期内变得更具传染性，而不是传染性更小。 It might help to point out that Mr Trump, a man famous for peremptory sackings, failed to dismiss Anthony Fauci, the public face of efforts to repress covid, who is about as popular in conservative circles as gun seizures and sharia law. 这或许有助于指出，以强制解雇闻名的特朗普先生未能解雇安东尼•福奇(Anthony Fauci)。福奇是镇压冠状病毒疾病的公众形象代言人，在保守派圈子中的受欢迎程度与枪支缴获和伊斯兰教法差不多。 Such barbs could draw some blood. If wielded with enough force and frequency, they might even fell a man as mighty as Caesar. Luckily for Mr Trump, he is facing a fractious, squabbling and timid bunch. ■ 这种冷嘲热讽可能会引起一些争议。如果使用足够的力量和频率，他们甚至可能打败一个像凯撒一样强大的人。对特朗普先生来说幸运的是，他面对的是一群脾气暴躁、争吵不休、胆小如鼠的家伙。￭ Editor’s note (May 25th 2023): This article was updated after Ron DeSantis’s announcement. 编者按(2023年5月25日) : 本文在 Ron DeSantis 发表声明后更新。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/briefing/2023/05/24/ron-desantis-has-little-chance-of-beating-donald-trump 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/briefing/2023/05/24/ron-desantis-has-little-chance-of-beating-donald-trump 下载的 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Briefing 简报 Sections 部分 The Americas 美国 United States 美国 How Donald Trump’s trials and the Republican primary will intersect 唐纳德•特朗普(Donald Trump)的审判与共和党初选将如何交叉 Tribulations and trials :: Where the rule of law clashes with the will of the people * 法治与人民意愿发生冲突的地方 Vasectomies rose by 29% in the three months after the end of Roe 在 Roe 结束后的三个月内，输精管切除术增加了29% Solidarity snips :: Brothers are doing it for themselves 团结一致: 兄弟们都是为了自己 Downtown San Francisco is at a tipping-point 旧金山市中心正处于一个临界点 The doom loop :: The city faces several crises, but one stands out 厄运循环: 这个城市面临几个危机，但有一个很突出 The push to bring insulin prices down in America 推动美国胰岛素价格下降 Drug pricing :: But not all patients are able to use the available schemes 药品定价: 但并非所有患者都能使用现有的方案 American policing has changed since George Floyd’s murder 自从乔治 · 弗洛伊德被杀后，美国的治安状况发生了变化 The too few and the less proud :: It needs to change further, and be better funded 它需要进一步改变，需要更好的资金 DeSantis is a truer believer, if a lesser politician, than Trump ❗ 🔄 Lexington :: The Florida governor aims to take Trump out of Trumpism 莱克星顿: 这位佛罗里达州州长的目标是让特朗普脱离特朗普主义。 Briefing 简报 Sections 部分 The Americas 美国

Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Tribulations and trials 苦难和考验 How Donald Trump’s trials and the Republican primary will intersect 唐纳德•特朗普(Donald Trump)的审判与共和党初选将如何交叉 Where the rule of law clashes with the will of the people 法治与人民意愿相冲突的地方 May 25th 2023 | NEW YORK 2023年5月25日 | 纽约 O NLY FOUR qualifications are necessary to become an American president. Article II of the constitution requires presidents to be natural-born citizens who have reached 35 years of age and lived in America for 14 years. 成为美国总统只需要四个条件。宪法第二条规定，总统必须是自然出生的公民，年满35岁，在美国生活了14年。 The 22nd Amendment adds that candidates must not have been elected twice to the job previously. Donald Trump satisfies those conditions, including the final hurdle—despite his insistence that he won both the 2016 and 2020 elections. 第22修正案补充说，候选人不得已经两次当选，以前的工作。唐纳德•特朗普(Donald Trump)满足了这些条件，包括最后一个障碍ーー尽管他坚称自己赢得了2016年和2020年的选举。 Nothing prevents candidates dogged by legal troubles, including criminal convictions, from running for president. Several presidential aspirants have even sought the office from behind bars. 没有什么能阻止那些被法律纠纷，包括刑事定罪所困扰的候选人竞选总统。一些有意竞选总统的人甚至在狱中寻求入主白宫。 The cases and the primaries will overlap in ways likely to set the rule of law against the will of the people. This month Mr Trump was found liable for sexually assaulting and defaming E. Jean Carroll (he is appealing against the judgment). 这些案件和初选将以可能使法治违背人民意愿的方式重叠。本月，特朗普先生因性侵犯和诽谤 E · 让 · 卡罗尔(他对判决提出上诉)而被判有罪。 He may soon owe more than the $5m the jury ordered him to pay, as Ms Carroll’s lawyer is asking for new damages stemming from Mr Trump’s doubling-down at an appearance on CNN the day after the verdict. 他可能很快就会欠下陪审团要求他支付的500万美元以上，因为卡罗尔女士的律师正在要求特朗普先生在判决后第二天出现在 CNN 上的双倍赔偿。 Jail time is a risk, too. Mr Trump became the first former president to be criminally indicted on April 4th when he was charged with 34 counts of falsifying business records in a Manhattan courtroom. 坐牢也很危险。4月4日，特朗普先生成为第一位被刑事起诉的前总统，他被指控在曼哈顿法庭上伪造34项商业记录。 The indictment involves an alleged cover-up, just before the 2016 election, of payments to hide alleged trysts with Stephanie Clifford, an adult-film actress (also known as Stormy Daniels) and Karen McDougal, a model. 起诉书涉及一项涉嫌在2016年大选之前隐瞒与成人电影女演员斯蒂芬妮 · 克利福德(Stephanie Clifford)和模特凯伦 · 麦克杜格尔(Karen McDougal)的所谓幽会费用的指控。 According to Alvin Bragg, the Manhattan district attorney, Mr Trump illegally concealed hush-money cheques to Mmes Clifford and McDougal as well as to Dino Sajudin, a former doorman at Trump Tower who allegedly knew about a child Mr Trump had fathered out of wedlock. 曼哈顿地区检察官阿尔文•布拉格(Alvin Bragg)表示，特朗普先生非法隐瞒了给克利福德女士和麦克杜格尔以及迪诺•萨朱丁(Dino Sajudin)的封口费支票。萨朱丁曾是特朗普大厦(Trump Tower)的门卫，据称他知道特朗普先生私生了一个孩子。 On May 23rd the judge in The People of the State of New York v Donald J Trump announced that the trial will begin on March 25th 2024, two months into primary season. 5月23日，纽约州人民诉唐纳德 · J · 特朗普一案的法官宣布，审判将于2024年3月25日开始，距离初选还有两个月。 The Republican front-runner will therefore be campaigning in 15 states for Super Tuesday delegates three weeks before standing trial in New York and, as proceedings begin, preparing to woo voters in about 20 more states. 因此，在纽约受审前三周，这位共和党领先者将在15个州竞选超级星期二代表，随着诉讼程序的开始，他还将准备在另外20个州争取选民。 Mr Trump could serve up to four years in prison for each of the 34 counts. But few first-time offenders on these charges are put behind bars. And some legal experts have doubts about Mr Bragg’s strategy. 特朗普先生可能会因这34项罪名中的每一项服刑4年。但这些罪名的初犯很少被关进监狱。一些法律专家对布拉格先生的策略表示怀疑。 Falsifying records is a misdemeanour under New York law; turning those charges into felonies requires piggybacking them on another crime. 根据纽约法律，伪造记录是一种轻罪; 将这些指控转化为重罪需要将它们与另一项罪行相提并论。 Mr Trump’s attention may be further divided in the primaries—and, if he prevails, in the general election—by indictments in other criminal matters. Georgia Republicans hold their primary on March 12th. 特朗普先生的注意力可能会在初选中进一步被其他刑事案件的起诉分散ーー如果他获胜的话，也可能在大选中被分散。乔治亚州的共和党人在3月12日举行初选。 By then, Mr Trump may be preparing for a trial focused on allegations that he sought to swing the Peach State in his favour after the 2020 election. 到那时，特朗普先生可能正在准备一场审判，这场审判的焦点是他试图在2020年大选后让桃州对他有利的指控。 A year ago, a special grand jury began considering evidence that Mr Trump and his allies may have made “unlawful attempts to disrupt the administration of the 2020 elections”. 一年前，一个特别大陪审团开始考虑特朗普先生及其盟友可能“非法企图破坏2020年大选管理”的证据。 The former president sought help from Brad Raffensperger, Georgia’s secretary of state, to “find” nearly 12,000 votes to reverse Joe Biden’s victory there. 这位前总统向格鲁吉亚国务卿布拉德•拉芬斯伯格(Brad Raffensperger)寻求帮助，以“找到”近1.2万张选票，逆转乔•拜登(Joe Biden)在格鲁吉亚的胜利。 The probe has also looked into the Trump team’s efforts to recruit a fake group of electors to present to the vice-president, Mike Pence, when he counted electoral votes on January 6th. 调查还调查了特朗普团队招募假选民的行为，以便在副总统迈克 · 彭斯(Mike Pence)1月6日清点选举人票时呈现给他。 Fani Willis, the Fulton County district attorney, appears ready to move forward with indictments over the summer. The head of the special grand jury said in February that she and her fellow jurors recommended charges for as many as a dozen people—including, she hinted, Mr Trump. 富尔顿县地方检察官法尼•威利斯(Fani wilis)似乎已准备好在今年夏天提出起诉。今年2月，特别大陪审团团长表示，她和其他陪审员建议对多达12人提出指控，其中包括她暗示的特朗普。 Ms Willis will submit evidence to a regular grand jury in late July. 威利斯女士将在七月下旬向常规大陪审团提交证据。 Indictments are “highly likely” to appear in August (which is when the first primary debate is scheduled), says Ryan Goodman, of New York University, but the case may be “tied up in pre-trial litigation” for some time. 纽约大学(New York University)的瑞安•古德曼(Ryan Goodman)表示，起诉书“极有可能”出现在8月份(预选辩论定于8月份举行) ，但此案可能会“在审前诉讼中搁置一段时间”。 The Department of Justice has been gathering evidence on two sets of similarly serious potential charges. Both are in the hands of Jack Smith, the special counsel appointed by Merrick Garland, the attorney-general, in November. 司法部一直在收集两组同样严重的潜在指控的证据。两者都掌握在总检察长梅里克•加兰(Merrick Garland)去年11月任命的特别检察官杰克•史密斯(Jack Smith)手中。 Mr Smith is investigating Mr Trump’s efforts to overturn the 2020 election and his role in the January 6th insurrection. Last month Mr Pence testified before a grand jury in this probe. 史密斯先生正在调查特朗普先生推翻2020年大选的努力以及他在1月6日起义中所扮演的角色。上个月，彭斯先生在大陪审团面前作证。 And Mr Smith appears poised to charge Mr Trump in connection with troves of classified documents the former president removed from the White House and brought to Mar-a-Lago, his Florida home. 史密斯似乎准备指控特朗普与这位前总统从白宫拿走的大量机密文件有关，这些文件被带到了他位于佛罗里达州的家乡 Mar-a-Lago。 After a few months’ delay from a federal district-court judge’s decision to appoint a “special master” to sift through the documents, in December an appeals court put an end to the hold-up and delivered all the files to Mr Smith. He seems to have broadened his sights. 联邦地区法院法官决定任命一位“特别法官”来筛选文件，这一决定推迟了几个月之后，12月上诉法院终止了拖延，并将所有文件交给了史密斯先生。他似乎开阔了视野。 He recently issued subpoenas looking into Mr Trump’s business dealings with seven foreign countries stretching back to 2017. The new line of inquiry could be spurred by an attempt to explain why Mr Trump took all those files with him when he left the White House. 他最近发出传票，调查特朗普先生从2017年开始与7个国家的商业往来。试图解释为什么特朗普先生离开白宫时带走了所有这些文件，这可能会激发新的调查路线。 Joyce Vance, a law professor at the University of Alabama and a former US attorney, predicts charges in the documents case “before the summer vacation season hits”. 阿拉巴马大学法学教授、前联邦检察官乔伊斯•万斯(Joyce Vance)预计，“在暑假季节到来之前”，文件案将面临指控。 At every turn, Mr Trump has condemned investigators for unfairly targeting him. In a letter to Mr Garland on May 23rd, his lawyers accused Mr Smith of bias. 特朗普先生每次都谴责调查人员不公平地针对他。在5月23日写给 Garland 先生的信中，他的律师指控 Smith 先生有偏见。 “No President of the United States has ever, in the history of our country, been baselessly investigated in such an outrageous and unlawful fashion,” they write, before requesting a meeting with the attorney-general to discuss the “injustice that is being perpetrated by your Special Counsel”. 他们写道，“在我国历史上，从未有任何一位美国总统以如此骇人听闻和非法的方式接受过毫无根据的调查。”之后，他们要求与司法部长会面，讨论“你们的特别顾问正在犯下的不公正行为”。 The accumulation of cases against Mr Trump could yet hurt him. But, so far, their impact among Republicans has been quite the opposite. It has fired them up, not led them to say: “You’re fired!” ■ 针对特朗普先生的案件的积累可能会伤害到他。但是，到目前为止，他们对共和党人的影响恰恰相反。这激怒了他们，而不是让他们说: “你被解雇了!”￭ 八点一刻杂志网 www.badianyike.com Stay on top of American politics with Checks and Balance , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter, which examines the state of American democracy and the issues that matter to voters. You can read other articles about the elections of 2024 and follow along as we track shifts in Joe Biden’s approval rating . 通过《检查与平衡》(Checks and Balance)了解美国政治，这是我们的每周订阅时事通讯，探讨美国民主政体的状况以及对选民至关重要的问题。你可以阅读有关2024年大选的其他文章，并跟随我们跟踪乔•拜登(Joe Biden)支持率的变化。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/united-states/2023/05/25/how-donald-trumps-trials-and-the-republican-primary-will-intersect 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/united-states/2023/05/25/how-donald-trumps-trials-and-the-republican-primary-will-intersect 下载的 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Solidarity snips 团结一致 Vasectomies rose by 29% in the three months after the end of Roe 在 Roe 结束后的三个月内，输精管切除术增加了29% Brothers are doing it for themselves 兄弟们都是为了自己 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 D R DOUG STEIN estimates that he has performed around 50,000 vasectomy procedures. He has been practising urology for 40 years, but still, achieving that impressive tally has meant “a lot of sunny Saturdays in windowless rooms hovering over scrotum”, he explains. DOUG STEIN 医生估计他已经做了大约50,000例输精管结扎手术。他从事泌尿外科已有40年，但他解释说，实现这一令人印象深刻的成绩意味着“在没有窗户的房间里度过大量阳光灿烂的周六，在阴囊上空盘旋”。 Dr Stein’s experience and reputation, built up over the decades, make him a popular choice for Floridian men looking to get the snip. But in the summer of 2022 demand for his services suddenly surged. 斯泰因博士的经验和声誉，建立了几十年，使他成为一个受欢迎的选择，佛罗里达州的男人希望得到剪刀。但在2022年夏天，对他的服务需求突然激增。 On June 24th last year the Supreme Court issued its ruling in the case of Dobbs v Jackson Women’s Health Organisation , reversing its decision in Roe v Wade , and allowing states to ban abortion. Dr Stein saw registrations from potential vasectomy patients almost triple the next week. 去年6月24日，最高法院对多布斯诉杰克逊妇女健康组织案作出裁决，推翻了罗伊诉韦德案的裁决，允许各州禁止堕胎。Stein 医生发现，潜在的输精管结扎患者的登记数在下周几乎增加了三倍。 It wasn’t just interest in the procedure that soared. Data on insurance claims from Komodo Health, a health-care-technology company, shows that in the second half of 2022 vasectomy rates across America were far higher than in previous years. Accounting for previous trends, The Economist calculates that the Dobbs ruling was associated with a 17% increase in procedures in the six months after the ruling, and a 29% increase between July and September. 不仅仅是对手术的兴趣激增。来自医疗保健技术公司科莫多健康公司的保险索赔数据显示，2022年下半年，美国输精管结扎率远远高于前几年。考虑到以前的趋势，《经济学人》计算出，多布斯案的裁决与裁决后六个月内程序增加17% 有关，与7月至9月间程序增加29% 有关。 The number of vasectomies has been on the rise. Between 2017 and 2021, the rate at which surgeries were performed increased by an average of 4% each year. 输精管切除术的数量一直在增加。从2017年到2021年，手术率以每年平均4% 的速度增长。 But the number of American men who report having had the procedure is lower than it was 20 years ago (and vasectomy rates still lag far behind rates of tubal ligation, the more invasive equivalent for women). 但是报告说做过输精管结扎手术的美国男性人数比20年前要少(输精管结扎手术的比率仍然远远落后于输卵管结扎手术，输卵管结扎手术对于女性来说更具侵入性)。 In 2002 national health surveys estimated that 6.9% of the male population aged 18-45 had been snipped. The most recent round of surveys, carried out between 2017 and 2019, put the figure at 5.4%. 2002年的全国健康调查估计，在18-45岁的男性人口中，有6.9% 的人被切除了阴茎。2017年至2019年进行的最新一轮调查显示，这一数字为5.4% 。 We estimate that around 20,000 extra men chose to undergo the short, mostly painless, surgery between July and December 2022. Normally, the number of procedures peaks towards the end of the year, when patients are more likely to have reached their insurance deductible (although this is probably overstated in our data, which do not capture vasectomies paid for in cash). 我们估计，在2022年7月至12月期间，约有20,000名额外的男性选择接受这种短暂的、大多无痛的手术。正常情况下，手术次数在年底达到峰值，那时患者更有可能达到他们的保险扣除额(尽管我们的数据可能夸大了这一点，这并不包括以现金支付的输精管切除术)。 Surgery rates also get a boost in March, which some urologists market as “vasectomy season”, a time when men can spend the day or two needed for recovery from the procedure watching March Madness basketball. 手术率在3月份也有所提高，一些泌尿科医生将3月份称为“输精管结扎季”，男性在这个季节可以花一两天时间观看“疯狂三月”(March Madness)篮球赛。 The bump in vasectomies following Dobbs could be seen in 46 states. The rise was larger in states with “trigger bans”, where abortion was severely limited right after the ruling. There, the average increase between July and September was 41%, compared with 26% elsewhere. 在46个州可以看到多布斯的输精管切除术后的肿块。在实行“触发禁令”的州，堕胎率的上升幅度更大，这些州在法院裁决后立即严格限制堕胎。在那里，7月至9月的平均增长率为41% ，而其它地区为26% 。 In Arizona, Florida, Georgia, Tennessee, Texas and Utah rates rose by more than 40%. 在亚利桑那州、佛罗里达州、乔治亚州、田纳西州、德克萨斯州和犹他州，这一比率上升了40% 以上。 As well as being clustered in more conservative states, these new post- Dobbs patients also tended to be younger than the normal vasectomy candidate. Data from Komodo show that, across the country, there was a small but consistent drop in the average age of patients in the second half of 2022. In Dr Stein’s practice, the number of childless men under 30 who opted for the procedure has increased by around 50% since the ruling. 除了聚集在更为保守的州，这些新的多布斯后患者也倾向于比正常的输精管结扎术候选人更年轻。来自科莫多的数据显示，在全国范围内，2022年下半年患者的平均年龄有小幅但持续的下降。在 Stein 博士的实践中，自从这项裁决以来，30岁以下选择手术的无子女男性的数量增加了50% 左右。 What is it about restricting abortion access that has driven men to the operating table? Among his patients who reported that they were motivated by Dobbs , Dr Stein says they tended to cite one of three reasons. Some worried that, without access to abortion, they lacked a genuine backup if their primary contraception method failed—vasectomy has a success rate of more than 99%. 为什么限制堕胎会把男人逼上手术台？Stein 博士说，在那些声称自己是被 Dobbs 激励的病人中，他们倾向于列举三个原因中的一个。一些人担心，由于没有堕胎的机会，如果他们的主要避孕方法失败，他们就会缺乏真正的后援ーー输精管结扎术的成功率超过99% 。 Others, who had been considering the procedure for a while, were apparently spurred by a concern that vasectomy could be outlawed next. A final set of men saw their surgery as an act of solidarity with women. 其他一些已经考虑了一段时间的人，显然是受到了输精管结扎术可能下一步被取缔的担忧的刺激。最后一组男性认为他们的手术是与女性团结一致的行为。 Vasectomy often represents a sacrifice. Like any surgery, it involves recovery time and risk of complications, however small. And for many men there are also misconceptions and concerns about how the procedure might affect their sense of masculinity. 输精管结扎通常意味着牺牲。像任何手术一样，它涉及恢复时间和并发症的风险，无论风险有多小。对于许多男人来说，他们也有误解和担忧，担心这个过程会影响他们的男子气概。 But making the sacrifice lifts the burden of responsibility for contraception from women. Studies have shown that high rates of vasectomy tend to go hand in hand with gender equality. Overturning Roe v Wade has been a brutal blow for women’s rights in America. The rise in vasectomies may be one very small consolation. ■ 但是做出这样的牺牲会减轻女性避孕的责任。研究表明，高比例的输精管结扎往往与性别平等齐头并进。推翻罗伊诉韦德案是对美国妇女权利的残酷打击。输精管切除术的增加可能是一个非常小的安慰。￭ Stay on top of American politics with Checks and Balance , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter, which examines the state of American democracy and the issues that matter to voters. 通过《检查与平衡》(Checks and Balance)了解美国政治，这是我们的每周订阅时事通讯，探讨美国民主政体的状况以及对选民至关重要的问题。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/united-states/2023/05/25/vasectomies-rose-by-29-in-the-three-months-after-the-end-of-roe 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/united-states/2023/05/25/vasectomies-rose-by-29-in-the-three-months-after-the-end-of-roe 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 The doom loop 末日循环 Downtown San Francisco is at a tipping-point 旧金山市中心正处于一个临界点 The city faces several crises, but one stands out 这个城市面临着几个危机，但有一个突出 May 25th 2023 | San Francisco 2023年5月25日 | 旧金山 M ANY IMAGES have symbolised San Francisco over the years. Fog enveloping the Golden Gate Bridge. Hippies tuning in and dropping out on Haight Street. Tents lining the pavement. These days, a “Retail for lease” sign in a vacant storefront seems appropriate. 多年来，许多图片都是旧金山的象征。雾笼罩着金门大桥。嬉皮士们在 Haight Street 打听消息。人行道两边是帐篷。如今，在一个空置的店面上挂一个“零售出租”的牌子似乎是合适的。 San Francisco itself has become a symbol, too, though what it represents depends on your politics. It is a hub of technological innovation or a bastion of inequality; a laboratory for the country’s most progressive policies or a fief of the radical left. 旧金山本身也已成为一个象征，尽管它代表什么取决于你的政治立场。它既是技术创新的中心，也是不平等的堡垒; 既是国家最进步政策的实验室，也是激进左派的领地。 No mid-size American city—San Francisco has fewer people than Indianapolis—has had a bigger effect on global culture or financial markets. 旧金山的人口比印第安纳波利斯少，没有一个美国中型城市对全球文化或金融市场产生过更大的影响。 But with stardom comes scrutiny. When you’re famous, everyone likes to kick you when you’re down, says Marisa Rodriguez, of the Union Square Alliance, the business-improvement district for downtown’s luxury shopping area. 但随着明星地位的提升，审查也随之而来。联合广场联盟(Union Square Alliance)的玛丽莎•罗德里格斯(Marisa Rodriguez)表示，当你出名时，每个人都喜欢在你情绪低落时踢你一脚。联合广场联盟是市中心奢侈品购物区的商业改善区。 Local and national media are publishing obituaries for the city. Local officials decry the coverage, but they also admit that something is deeply wrong. 当地和全国性的媒体都在为这座城市发布讣告。地方官员谴责这种报道，但他们也承认，有些事情是非常错误的。 “San Francisco has had what felt like an endless, year-after-year boom,” says Aaron Peskin, president of the Board of Supervisors, the city council. “And now the bubble really burst.” 旧金山市议会(city council)监事会（美国中西部及东部各州）主席亚伦•佩斯金(aaronpeskin)表示: “感觉上，旧金山经历了一场持续不断、年复一年的繁荣。”。“现在泡沫真的破灭了。” There are two groups of problems. The first is characterised by homelessness, drug overdoses and property crime. Nearly 7,800 people are homeless in San Francisco, slightly fewer than in 2019, but higher than at any other time the city has counted since at least 2005. 有两类问题。第一类是无家可归、吸毒过量和财产犯罪。旧金山有近7800人无家可归，比2019年略低，但比该市至少自2005年以来统计的任何时候都要高。 Local leaders point to Los Angeles or Seattle as proof that theirs is not the only pricey west-coast metro area unable to care for its vulnerable citizens. That is small consolation. All three cities have some of the highest rates of homelessness in the country. The difference is that San Francisco is more densely populated. Nearly 42,000 homeless people live in Los Angeles, but in neighbourhoods across the sprawling city, from Skid Row to Venice Beach. 当地领导人指出，洛杉矶或西雅图证明，他们不是唯一无法照顾弱势公民的昂贵的西海岸都市地区。这算是小小的安慰。这三个城市的无家可归率都是全国最高的。不同之处在于旧金山的人口密度更大。近42,000名无家可归者居住在洛杉矶，但是居住在从贫民窟到威尼斯海滩的城市周围。 A high share of San Francisco’s rough sleepers are in a single neighbourhood, the Tenderloin, next to downtown. 旧金山很大一部分露宿者都住在临近市中心的田德隆(Tenderloin)一带。 The second problem is financial. “When the pandemic happened, a good portion of San Francisco was able to literally pick up their laptops and go home,” says Rodney Fong of the city’s chamber of commerce. 第二个问题是财务方面的。旧金山商会的 Rodney Fong 说: “当流感大流行发生时，旧金山的大部分地区都能够拿起笔记本电脑回家了。”。 The University of Toronto has been measuring pandemic recovery in 63 American downtowns, since March 2020, by comparing mobile-phone use in city centres with pre-pandemic numbers. San Francisco’s recovery ranks last. The office-vacancy rate is nearing 30%, a record high. 自2020年3月以来，多伦多大学一直在测量美国63个市中心的大流行恢复情况，方法是将市中心的手机使用情况与大流行前的数据进行比较。旧金山的复苏排名最后。办公室空置率接近30% ，创历史新高。 These woes could spread beyond the city centre. Downtown generated at least 75% of the city’s GDP in 2021, and city revenues depend on downtown property and business taxes. As that pot of money shrinks—property taxes paid by offices could decline by up to 35% by 2028—the city will not be able to provide the same services. People and firms may leave, perpetuating the cycle. 这些灾难可能会蔓延到市中心以外。2021年，市中心至少创造了该市75% 的 GDP，而该市的财政收入依赖于市中心的房产税和营业税。随着这笔资金的减少(到2028年，写字楼缴纳的房产税可能下降至多35%) ，香港将无法提供同样的服务。人员和公司可能会离开，从而延续这种循环。 The San Francisco Chronicle calls this the city’s potential “doom loop”. 加拿大亚太邮报称之为这座城市潜在的“厄运循环”。 The doom loop is not inevitable, but avoiding it would take investment at a time when the city faces a $780m deficit, almost 6% of the annual budget, over the next two fiscal years. Local officials want the state to prop up the Bay Area subway system, BART , which ferries people downtown, but Gavin Newsom, California’s governor, has to plug a $32bn budget shortfall of his own. “It’s hard when your best-case scenario is bad,” says Ted Egan, the city’s chief economist. 厄运循环并非不可避免，但在未来两个财政年度，纽约将面临7.8亿美元的赤字，几乎相当于年度预算的6% ，避免厄运循环需要投资。地方官员希望加州支持湾区地铁系统(BART) ，该系统将人们送往市中心，但加州州长加文•纽森(Gavin Newsom)必须填补自己320亿美元的预算缺口。纽约市首席经济学家泰德•伊根(Ted Egan)表示: “当你的最佳情况是糟糕的时候，你很难做到这一点。”。 Some things may yet stop the bleeding. The city’s network effects are diminished by the rise of other tech hubs, but remain mighty. Several leading AI startups are based in San Francisco, including Open AI , which created Chat GPT . The Bay Area still gets more venture-capital funding than any other region, though its share of the pie is shrinking. Mr Peskin wants to incentivise firms to convert offices into apartments . But in the meantime, things look grim. Mr Egan says he recently visited a class of Stanford University students who were discussing their post-college plans. “How many of you are moving to San Francisco after you graduate?” he recalls their professor asking. 有些东西可能还会止血。随着其他科技中心的崛起，这座城市的网络效应有所减弱，但仍然强大。一些领先的人工智能初创公司总部设在旧金山，包括创建 ChatGPT 的 OpenAI。旧金山湾区仍然比其他任何地区获得更多的风险投资资金，尽管它在蛋糕中的份额正在缩小。Peskin 先生想要激励公司把办公室改造成公寓。但与此同时，形势看起来很严峻。伊根先生说他最近拜访了一个斯坦福大学的学生班级，他们正在讨论他们的大学毕业后计划。“你们中有多少人毕业后要搬去旧金山?”他记得他们的教授问。 Incredulous, they responded: “Why would anyone do that?” ■ 他们回答说: “为什么会有人这么做?”￭ Stay on top of American politics with Checks and Balance , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter, which examines the state of American democracy and the issues that matter to voters. 通过《检查与平衡》(Checks and Balance)了解美国政治，这是我们的每周订阅时事通讯，探讨美国民主政体的状况以及对选民至关重要的问题。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/united-states/2023/05/25/downtown-san-francisco-is-at-a-tipping-point 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/united-states/2023/05/25/downtown-san-francisco-is-at-a-tipping-point 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Drug pricing 药品定价 The push to bring insulin prices down in America 推动美国胰岛素价格下降 But not all patients are able to use the available schemes 但并非所有患者都能使用现有的方案 May 25th 2023 | WASHINGTON, DC 2023年5月25日 | 华盛顿特区 “M OVE TO THE back if you’ve lost your life savings to the drug companies,” shouts a woman walking by your correspondent. A spirited group follows her to the end of a long queue for a Senate hearing on insulin prices on May 10th. “如果你把毕生的积蓄都输给了制药公司，那就搬到后面去吧,”一位从记者身边走过的女士喊道。5月10日，参议院将举行关于胰岛素价格的听证会，一群精力充沛的人跟在她后面，排着长队等待听证会结束。 In the room David Ricks of Eli Lilly, a large American drug firm, testifies that insulin, used to regulate blood-sugar levels for diabetics, is cheap and even free in some instances. 美国大型制药公司礼来(Eli Lilly)的大卫 · 里克斯(David Ricks)在会议室里证实，用于调节糖尿病患者血糖水平的胰岛素很便宜，在某些情况下甚至是免费的。 “If you contact Lilly today, we will ship you a month’s supply at no cost with one question: ‘What’s your address?’” he says. Those dressed in gear with slogans like “Insulin for All” shake their heads in disbelief. “如果你今天与礼来公司联系，我们将免费为你运送一个月的货物，只有一个问题: ‘你的地址是什么？他说。那些身着带有“人人享有胰岛素”标语的服装的人摇头表示难以置信。 Drug companies may be offering cheap insulin, but it is not reaching all patients. 制药公司可能会提供廉价的胰岛素，但并不是所有的病人都能得到。 The high price of drugs is a long-running grievance for Americans—and insulin is a flashpoint, since more than 8m of them depend on it to survive. A standard unit that costs on average $9 in other rich countries sets Americans back $99. A study in 2021 in the Annals of Internal Medicine , a journal, found that 1.3m people skipped or reduced insulin doses due to cost. 高昂的药价是美国人长期以来的不满，而胰岛素是一个引爆点，因为超过800万美国人依赖胰岛素生存。在其他富裕国家，一个标准单位的平均价格为9美元，而美国人的平均价格为99美元。2021年发表在《内科学年鉴》(Annals of Internal Medicine)杂志上的一项研究发现，有130万人由于成本问题而放弃或减少了胰岛素剂量。 According to GoodRx, a company that helps customers find low drug prices, the average cash price of insulin rose by 54% between 2014 and 2019. The price then dropped by 11% between January 2020 and May 2023. Despite the recent dip, insulin prices remain high. 据一家帮助顾客寻找低价药物的公司 GoodRx 称，2014年至2019年间，胰岛素的平均现金价格上涨了54% 。从2020年1月到2023年5月，价格下降了11% 。尽管近期胰岛素价格有所下降，但仍居高不下。 For older patients, the cost is now coming down. The Inflation Reduction Act, one of President Biden’s landmark laws, allows Medicare to negotiate with manufacturers for lower drug prices. 对于老年患者，费用正在下降。作为拜登总统里程碑式的法律之一的《降低通货膨胀法案》 ，允许医疗保险与制造商谈判以降低药品价格。 It also requires companies to pay back Medicare if prices rise faster than inflation, and it capped insulin prices at $35 a month for Medicare. 该法案还要求，如果价格上涨快于通货膨胀，企业必须偿还医疗保险，而且该法案还将医疗保险的胰岛素价格上限定为每月35美元。 The new law seems to have nudged drug companies into offering lower prices for everyone. On March 1st Eli Lilly announced an automatic $35-a-month cap on all insulins for those with private insurance at select pharmacies, and a similar programme for the uninsured. 新法律似乎促使制药公司为所有人提供更低的价格。3月1日，礼来公司宣布，针对那些在特定药店拥有私人保险的人，每月自动限额35美元的所有胰岛素，以及针对没有保险的人的类似计划。 The firm also promised to sell one of its non-branded insulins for $25 a vial, from May 1st. Sanofi, a French drug company, followed suit with a similar deal for one of its insulins, starting next year. Novo Nordisk, a Danish firm, likewise announced lower prices for 2024. 该公司还承诺从5月1日起以每瓶25美元的价格出售一种非品牌胰岛素。法国制药公司赛诺菲(Sanofi)也紧随其后，从明年开始就其一种胰岛素进行了类似的交易。丹麦公司诺和诺德同样宣布2024年降价。 These deals are eye-catching, but when patients turn up at pharmacies, they may find they cannot grab a bargain. Shaina Kasper of T1International, an advocacy group for diabetes patients, is a type-1 diabetic. 这些交易是引人注目的，但当病人出现在药店，他们可能会发现他们不能抓住一个讨价还价。糖尿病患者宣传组织 T1International 的 ShainaKasper 是1型糖尿病患者。 She says she tried to buy Eli Lilly’s non-branded Insulin Lispro, the specific drug available from May 1st for $25. Her pharmacy in Vermont quoted her $90. 她说，她试图购买礼来公司的非品牌胰岛素 Lispro，特定的药物从5月1日起可用25美元。她在佛蒙特州的药房报价90美元。 Perhaps Eli Lilly’s programme needs time to work out the kinks, or the pharmacy charged more on top. But this is not the first time such a scheme has caused problems. For years pharma firms have offered a patchwork of plans to help customers afford insulin and other drugs. 也许礼来公司的项目需要时间来解决问题，或者药房需要收取更高的费用。但这并非该计划首次引发问题。多年来，制药公司为帮助消费者购买胰岛素和其他药物提供了一系列的计划。 Some are for almost anyone, others for the poor or uninsured. But they tend to be difficult for people to access, says Stacie Dusetzina, a drug-pricing expert at Vanderbilt University. 有些是为几乎所有人准备的，有些是为穷人或没有保险的人准备的。但是药品定价专家 Stacie Dusetzina 说，人们很难获得这些范德堡大学。 First patients must be aware that the scheme exists and that they are eligible, which is often unclear. Then they must jump through hoops. Some need applications, doctor’s notes, proof of residence, tax documents and more. 首先，患者必须意识到该计划的存在，并且他们是合格的，这往往是不清楚的。然后他们必须跳过圈。有些需要申请表、医生证明、居住证明、税务文件等等。 Pharmacies must also know about the programme and participate in it. It can be hard to know whom to blame—the drug companies for the faulty deals or the pharmacies for failing to implement them properly. 药房还必须了解这个项目并参与其中。人们可能很难知道该责怪谁——是制药公司造成了交易失误，还是药店未能妥善执行交易。 These programmes can also end at any time. “What the Lord giveth, the Lord can taketh away,” says David Mitchell of Patients For Affordable Drugs, an advocacy organisation for patients. 这些节目也可以随时结束。“上帝给予的，上帝也可以带走,”患者权益倡导组织“平价药物患者”的大卫 · 米切尔说。 That is why Mr Mitchell and others are pushing for laws to lower drug prices rather than relying on a mishmash of special schemes. 这就是为什么米切尔先生和其他人正在推动法律，以降低药物价格，而不是依靠一个混杂的特殊计划。 Some states have given up on waiting. Colorado was the first to pass a cap on insulin, at $100 a month, in 2019. Since then nearly half of states have introduced caps, but these cover only state-regulated health plans. 一些州已经放弃了等待。2019年，科罗拉多州首次通过了每月100美元的胰岛素上限。从那时起，将近一半的州都引入了上限，但这些上限只涵盖了州政府规定的医疗保险计划。 In March Gavin Newsom, the governor of California, announced that he had signed a contract with a manufacturer to make and distribute insulin to Californians for $30. Maine, Michigan and Washington may follow. 今年3月，加利福尼亚州州长加文•纽森(Gavin Newsom)宣布，他已经与一家制造商签署了一份合同，以30美元的价格生产并向加利福尼亚人分发胰岛素。缅因州、密歇根州和华盛顿可能也会效仿。 There are also bills in Congress to tackle insulin prices and increase access to generics. Perhaps insulin’s cost will eventually come down further and become more predictable. ■ 国会也有一些法案来解决胰岛素的价格问题和增加对非专利药的使用。也许胰岛素的成本最终会进一步下降，变得更可预测。￭ 八点一刻杂志网 www.badianyike.com Stay on top of American politics with Checks and Balance , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter, which examines the state of American democracy and the issues that matter to voters. 通过《检查与平衡》(Checks and Balance)了解美国政治，这是我们的每周订阅时事通讯，探讨美国民主政体的状况以及对选民至关重要的问题。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/united-states/2023/05/25/the-push-to-bring-insulin-prices-down-in-america 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/united-states/2023/05/25/the-push-to-bring-insulin-prices-down-in-america 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 The too few and the less proud 少数人和不那么骄傲的人 American policing has changed since George Floyd’s murder 自从乔治 · 弗洛伊德被杀后，美国的治安状况发生了变化 It needs to change further, and be better funded 它需要进一步改变，并获得更好的资金 May 25th 2023 | Minneapolis 2023年5月25日 | 明尼阿波利斯 T ALK WITH Chris Thomsen and Rick Zimmerman, two longtime homicide investigators with the Minneapolis Police Department ( MPD ), and they’ll tell you their job has changed markedly over the past three years. Restrictions now forbid officers from stopping drivers for expired tags or something dangling from a rear-view mirror; those stops often yielded guns, drugs or people evading arrest warrants. 与明尼阿波利斯警察局(MPD)的两名长期凶杀案调查员克里斯 · 汤姆森(Chris Thomsen)和里克 · 齐默尔曼(Rick Zimmerman)交谈，他们会告诉你，在过去三年里，他们的工作发生了显著变化。现在的限制措施禁止警察拦截过期的车牌或悬挂在倒后镜上的东西，这些拦截常常导致枪支、毒品或逃避逮捕令的人。 A chokehold ban and body-worn-camera footage of every interaction mean officers worry that accidental contact with a suspect’s neck during a physical altercation could be grounds for a lawsuit or dismissal. 禁止使用窒息式呼吸器，并且每次交流都有身穿的摄像机镜头，这意味着警官们担心，在发生肢体冲突时意外接触到嫌疑人的脖子，可能会导致诉讼或解雇。 Prosecutors and jurors used to defer to cops’ words in court; now they demand video or audio evidence. 过去，检察官和陪审员在法庭上听从警察的话，现在他们要求提供视频或音频证据。 But talk with Dave Bicking and Emma Pedersen—members of Communities United Against Police Brutality, an activist group—and they will tell you the opposite. The city council vowed to “defund the police”; instead the MPD ’s budget has grown. Police killings, Mr Bicking asserts, “go on at the same rate as always”. The MPD ’s culture, says Ms Pedersen, is “getting worse”. 但是，如果你与社区联合反对过度用武的成员戴夫•比金和艾玛•佩德森交谈，他们会告诉你相反的情况。市议会发誓要“取消对警察的资助”; 相反，市警察局的预算却增加了。比金先生断言，警察杀人事件“一如既往地继续发生”。佩德森表示，MPD 的文化正“变得越来越糟糕”。 These two poles define a roiling debate. Leftists argue that policing has changed far too little since a Minneapolis police officer murdered George Floyd three years ago. Police killings in America rose from 2020 to 2021, and again from 2021 to 2022. Politicians who promised reform have backtracked; changes have been cosmetic. 这两个极点定义了一场激烈的辩论。左派人士认为，自从三年前一名明尼阿波利斯警官谋杀了乔治 · 弗洛伊德以来，警务工作几乎没有什么变化。2020年至2021年，美国警察杀人事件上升，2021年至2022年再次上升。承诺改革的政治家们已经收回了他们的承诺; 改革只是表面上的。 Conservatives argue that public suspicion of police and policy changes driven by progressive politicians have hamstrung law enforcement, leading directly to a rise in violent crime. 保守派认为，公众对警察的怀疑以及进步派政治家推动的政策变化妨碍了执法，直接导致了暴力犯罪的上升。 The consensus that 30 years ago led politicians from both parties to espouse “tough on crime” rhetoric and policies has disappeared. 30年前，两党政治家一致赞成“严厉打击犯罪”的言论和政策，如今这种共识已不复存在。 As in so much else in American life, the centre has not held. 正如美国生活中的许多其他事情一样，中心并不存在。 What is beyond dispute, however, is that big-city departments across the country face shortages. Minneapolis had over 900 officers at the time of Floyd’s murder. As of May 6th it had just 583, well below the 731 required by the city’s charter. 然而，毋庸置疑的是，全国各大城市的部门都面临着供应短缺的问题。Floyd 被杀时，明尼阿波利斯有900多名警官。截至5月6日，只有583架，远低于该市宪章所要求的731架。 Baltimore, which in 2022 had more than 1,000 homicides and non-fatal shootings, lost nearly 157 officers that year, and is short more than 500. Between 2011 and late 2022, the size of Memphis’s police force shrank by nearly one-quarter; some wonder whether that contributed to the widely seen beating death of Tyre Nichols by lowering the department’s hiring and training standards. 巴尔的摩在2022年发生了1000多起谋杀和非致命枪击事件，那一年失去了近157名警官，目前还缺500多名警官。从2011年到2022年末，孟菲斯的警力缩减了近四分之一; 有人怀疑，这是否导致了泰尔 · 尼科尔斯(Tyre Nichols)被殴打致死的广为人知的事件，因为这降低了该部门的招聘和培训标准。 Nationally, between April 1st 2020 and March 31st 2021 (the most recent period for which data are available), resignations rose by 18% and retirements by 45% year-on-year. 在全国范围内，从2020年4月1日到2021年3月31日(有数据可查的最近时期) ，辞职人数同比增加了18% ，退休人数同比增加了45% 。 Some of those departures were expected: the Violent Crime Control and Law Enforcement Act, passed in 1994 and championed by then-Senator Joe Biden, funded the hiring of tens of thousands of officers, many of whom are now at retirement age. 其中一些人的离职是意料之中的: 1994年通过的《暴力犯罪控制和执法法案》 ，由当时的参议员乔 · 拜登(Joe Biden)倡导，为雇佣数万名警官提供了资金，其中许多人现在已经到了退休年龄。 Though critics now decry that bill for fuelling mass incarceration, at the time 49% of whites, 58% of African-Americans and a majority of the Congressional Black Caucus supported it. 尽管批评人士现在谴责该法案助长了大规模监禁，但当时49% 的白人、58% 的非洲裔美国人和国会黑人核心小组的大多数成员都支持该法案。 A related phenomenon has seen officers transfer from departments in big cities with high crime rates to agencies in wealthier, smaller cities with less crime, a lower cost of living and higher salaries. According to the New York Times , the New York Police Department saw more resignations last year than at any time in the past two decades. At least 14 of the department’s officers moved to Aurora, Colorado; others have accepted $5,000 signing bonuses to move to Florida. 一个相关的现象是，犯罪率较高的大城市的警官被调到犯罪率较低、生活成本较低、工资较高的较富裕、较小城市的机构。据《纽约时报》报道，纽约警察局去年的辞职人数超过了过去20年的任何时候。该部门至少有14名工作人员搬到了科罗拉多州的奥罗拉; 其他人则接受了5000美元的签约奖金，搬到了佛罗里达州。 This leaves departments short-staffed, which means officers have to work more overtime with less supervision; that combination, notes a veteran west-coast officer, leads to “more stress and bad decisions on the street”. 这导致各部门人手不足，这意味着警官们不得不在监督较少的情况下加班; 一位资深西海岸警官指出，这种组合会导致“街头压力更大，做出更糟糕的决定”。 And the more cases that pile up, the harder it gets to solve them: officers lack investigatory time, leads go cold, newer cases assume greater importance. 案件越多，破案就越困难: 警官缺乏调查时间，线索断了，新案子就显得更重要了。 Compounding the problem of mass departures is that hiring has not kept pace: from April 2020 through March 2021 the hiring rate actually declined by 5%. 使大规模离职问题更加复杂的是招聘没有跟上步伐: 从2020年4月到2021年3月，招聘率实际上下降了5% 。 A poll of 10,000 police officers taken in June 2020 found that just under 17% were either “extremely satisfied” or “pleased” to be in law enforcement, and only around 7% would recommend that their child go into it. 2020年6月对10000名警察进行的一项调查发现，只有不到17% 的警察“非常满意”或“满意”在执法部门工作，只有大约7% 的警察建议他们的孩子参加执法。 For that state of affairs many blame a changed national attitude towards police. “It used to be that policing was a job for helpers, it’s honourable,” says one veteran officer. 对于这种状况，许多人将其归咎于国民对警察态度的转变。“过去，维持治安是助手的工作，这是光荣的,”一位资深警官表示。 “Now the story is that policing is an inherently racist institution, and if you go into policing you’re complicit in supporting structures of white racial hegemony.” “现在的情况是，警务工作本身就是一种种族主义制度，如果你从事警务工作，你就是支持白人种族霸权结构的同谋。” Art Acevedo, who headed the Austin, Houston and Miami police departments and is now interim chief in Aurora, Colorado, said that “the push by some elected officials to defund the police…had an impact on recruitment and retention.” That shift in attitudes did not begin with Floyd’s murder: the shooting of Michael Brown, a young black man, by a police officer in Ferguson, Missouri, in August 2014 also sparked widespread protests, though Barack Obama’s Justice Department, after an extensive investigation, found the officer’s actions were not “objectively unreasonable”, and declined to press charges. 阿特 · 阿塞韦多(Art Acevedo)是奥斯汀、休斯顿和迈阿密警察局的负责人，现在是科罗拉多州奥罗拉市的临时警察局长。他说，“一些民选官员推动撤销对警察的资助... ... 对警察的招募和留用产生了影响。”这种态度的转变并非始于弗洛伊德被谋杀: 2014年8月，一名警察在密苏里州弗格森枪杀了年轻的黑人男子迈克尔 · 布朗(Michael Brown) ，这也引发了广泛的抗议，尽管巴拉克 · 奥巴马(Barack Obama)的司法部经过广泛调查后发现，这名警察的行为并非“客观上不合理”，并拒绝提出指控。 But Floyd’s death pushed previously fringe positions, such as police defunding and abolition, to the fore. 但是弗洛伊德的去世把之前的边缘立场，如警察撤资和废除，推到了前面。 Peter Moskos, a sociologist and former Baltimore beat cop who now teaches at John Jay College of Criminal Justice, said that “pre-George Floyd, reformers would work with the police.” He sees much of the post-Floyd legislation—such as limiting when police can stop drivers, as San Francisco has done, or eliminating most cash bail, as New York has done—as coming from activists who see policing as malign. 社会学家、前巴尔的摩巡警彼得 · 莫斯科斯(Peter Moskos)现在在约翰 · 杰伊刑事司法学院(John Jay College of Criminal Justice)任教，他说: “在乔治 · 弗洛伊德之前，改革者会与警方合作。”他认为，后弗洛伊德时代的许多立法ーー比如限制警察拦截司机的时间(旧金山就是这样做的) ，或者取消大部分的现金保释(纽约就是这样做的)ーー都来自那些认为维持治安是恶意行为的活动人士。 “The idea that you can reform police in opposition to the police is crazy.” “以改革警察来对抗警察的想法是疯狂的。” The public’s appetite for reform seems to be waning. San Francisco’s voters recalled Chesa Boudin, their reformist chief prosecutor; Seattle’s elected a tough-on-crime Republican prosecutor over a far-left candidate. 公众对改革的兴趣似乎正在减弱。旧金山的选民召回了他们的改革派首席检察官切萨•布丹(Chesa Boudin) ; 西雅图选出了一位严厉打击犯罪的共和党检察官，而非一位极左翼候选人。 In late February Ron DeSantis, Florida’s Republican governor, made a pre-presidential-campaign trip to Illinois, New York and Pennsylvania to talk up his tough-on-crime credentials. 今年2月底，佛罗里达州共和党州长罗恩•德桑蒂斯(Ron DeSantis)在总统竞选前访问了伊利诺伊州、纽约州和宾夕法尼亚州，宣传自己在打击犯罪方面的资历。 Perhaps sensing the shifting political winds, the following week Mr Biden disappointed progressives by refusing to veto Congress’s repeal of a crime bill that would have reduced sentences for some offences. 也许是感觉到了政治风向的转变，接下来的一周，拜登先生拒绝否决国会废除一项犯罪法案，该法案本可以减轻某些罪行的刑期，这让进步人士感到失望。 Yet the ground on which police operate has changed. 然而，警察行动的基础已经发生了变化。 Ten years ago, police facing a crime spike might have embarked on a “flood-the-zone” strategy: pour officers into a neighbourhood with a lot of violent crime, and start arresting people and writing tickets to show “zero tolerance”. That strategy will no longer work. 10年前，面临犯罪率飙升的警方可能已经开始采取“洪水淹没区域”战略: 将警察赶到暴力犯罪频发的社区，开始逮捕人们，并开罚单以表明“零容忍”。这种策略将不再奏效。 Some of those arrests will involve displays of force that, thanks to mobile-phone cameras, will end up on social media. 其中一些逮捕将涉及武力展示，由于手机摄像头，最终将出现在社交媒体上。 More importantly, ticketing and bothering law-abiding citizens for jaywalking or drinking a beer on a street corner will turn the community against police, and police need community trust and witnesses’ help to solve crimes. 更重要的是，违反交通规则或在街角喝啤酒而给守法公民开罚单或打扰他们会使社区反对警察，而警察需要社区的信任和证人的帮助来解决犯罪。 Goodness gracious that’s why it’s a mess 天啊，所以才这么乱 People in crime-ridden communities do not say they want to defund or abolish the police. They want better, more accountable policing. That starts with better training. 在犯罪猖獗的社区，人们并没有说他们想要撤销或废除警察。他们想要更好，更负责任的治安。这要从更好的训练开始。 Whereas military officers get four years of training and education in an institution such as West Point or the Naval Academy, police officers get an average of 20 weeks in their academy, often with a few months of field training afterwards. 军官们在西点军校(West Point)或海军学院(Naval Academy)等机构接受四年的培训和教育，而警官们平均在学院学习20周，之后往往还要接受几个月的实地培训。 Military tours in active duty generally comprise a minority of a career officer’s time; many patrol officers spend almost their entire career deployed. 服现役的军事旅行一般只占职业军官时间的一小部分; 许多巡逻军官几乎整个职业生涯都在部署。 In-career training is often cursory: California, for instance, requires officers to undertake just four hours of use-of-force training every two years. 职业培训通常是粗略的: 例如，加利福尼亚州要求军官每两年只接受4小时的使用武力培训。 National training standards are non-existent, largely because America’s police forces, unlike those of most other countries, are decentralised, run at the state and local level. 国家培训标准是不存在的，主要是因为美国的警察部队，不像其他大多数国家，是分散的，运行在州和地方一级。 But better training will require more money, both for its direct costs and to ensure that forces have enough active officers to cover those getting trained. 但是，更好的训练需要更多的资金，不仅要支付直接费用，还要确保部队有足够的现役军官来支付接受训练的军人的费用。 That may be a tough sell. Left-leaning politicians sceptical of police may be unwilling to teach officers how to use violence more effectively, even though, in the long run, it could save lives. 这可能很难让人接受。对警察持怀疑态度的左倾政客可能不愿意教警察如何更有效地使用暴力，尽管从长远来看，暴力可以挽救生命。 A recent study conducted in Cambridge (Massachusetts), Houston and Tucson found that well-trained officers reduced offences in crime hotspots better than officers who received standard police training. 最近在剑桥(马萨诸塞州)、休斯顿和图森进行的一项研究发现，训练有素的警官比接受标准警察培训的警官更能减少犯罪热点地区的犯罪。 But the most important thing police could do to bring down the violent-crime rate is to catch more violent criminals. Nationally, just over half of all murders, and less than half of other violent crimes, go unsolved (see chart). 但是警察能做的降低暴力犯罪率的最重要的事情是抓住更多的暴力罪犯。在全国范围内，超过一半的谋杀案和不到一半的其他暴力犯罪案件没有得到解决(见图表)。 By contrast, more than 95% of murders are solved in South Korea and Japan, as well as 80% in Sweden, 87% in Switzerland and 98% in Finland. 相比之下，超过95% 的谋杀案在韩国和日本破获，80% 在瑞典，87% 在瑞士和98% 在芬兰。 Some departments, recognising that solving murders often requires sifting through massive amounts of data, have begun hiring civilian data analysts, who trawl digital traces while homicide detectives talk to witnesses and chase down leads. 一些部门认识到解决谋杀案往往需要筛选大量的数据，已经开始聘请民用数据分析师，他们在凶杀案侦探与目击者交谈并追查线索时，搜寻数字痕迹。 Others have focused investigative resources on places—trouble tends to occur in remarkably small geographical areas—rather than simply reacting to crimes after they occur. 其他人则把调查资源集中在一些地方(麻烦往往发生在非常小的地理区域) ，而不是仅仅在犯罪发生后才作出反应。 Whatever path departments choose to follow, they will need to hire more people to walk it. That will require young folk who see policing as a desirable, honourable occupation. 无论部门选择走什么样的道路，他们都需要雇佣更多的人来走这条路。这就需要年轻人把维持治安看作是一项令人向往的光荣职业。 And politicians, especially in big, liberal cities, will need to recognise that police have an essential role in keeping the public safe. 政治家们，尤其是那些自由的大城市的政治家们，需要认识到警察在保护公众安全方面起着至关重要的作用。 That does not preclude holding police accountable. 但这并不妨碍追究警察的责任。 Officers, says Jacob Frey, mayor of Minneapolis, “need to be paid more, fired more and trained more”: the first to attract good recruits to a dangerous, necessary job; the second to ensure that those who abuse their badges can be got rid of; and the last to keep them effective. 明尼阿波利斯市市长雅各布•弗雷(Jacob Frey)表示，警官“需要获得更高的薪酬，解雇更多人，并接受更多培训”: 第一，吸引优秀的新人从事危险而必要的工作; 第二，确保那些滥用警徽的人能够被解雇; 最后，确保他们的工作效率。 Police must accept increased scrutiny and oversight. And politicians, argues Michael Shellenberger, an author and activist who ran for governor of California in 2022, need to do more than just promise to fund the police. 警方必须接受更严格的审查和监督。迈克尔 · 谢伦伯格(Michael Shellenberger)是一位作家和活动家，曾在2022年竞选加利福尼亚州州长。他认为，政治家们需要做的不仅仅是承诺资助警察。 They need to make it clear that they are valued, like teachers and firefighters. Yes, “there are some bad actors,” but “it’s a noble profession full of people who want to do the right thing.” ■ 他们需要清楚地表明自己的价值，就像老师和消防员一样。是的，“有一些坏演员”，但“这是一个崇高的职业，充满了想做正确事情的人。”￭ Stay on top of American politics with Checks and Balance , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter, which examines the state of American democracy and the issues that matter to voters. 通过《检查与平衡》(Checks and Balance)了解美国政治，这是我们的每周订阅时事通讯，探讨美国民主政体的状况以及对选民至关重要的问题。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/united-states/2023/05/25/american-policing-has-changed-since-george-floyds-murder 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/united-states/2023/05/25/american-policing-has-changed-since-george-floyds-murder 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Lexington 列克星敦 DeSantis is a truer believer, if a lesser politician, than Trump ❗ 🔄 The Florida governor aims to take Trump out of Trumpism 这位佛罗里达州州长的目标是让特朗普脱离特朗普主义 May 24th 2023 | 2023年5月24日 A S GIVEN VOICE by Donald Trump, American right-wing populism has sounded more like a howl of rage or a whine of self-pity than a rational plan for the country’s future. When he ran for re-election, the Republican Party could not even bring itself to write a platform. 作为唐纳德 · 特朗普的发言人，美国的右派民粹主义听起来更像是愤怒的嚎叫或自怜的哀鸣，而不是对国家未来的理性规划。当他竞选连任时，共和党甚至连一个政纲都写不出来。 “Trumpism” blurred boundaries between his policies and his needs and interests, distinctions that vanished as his obsession with his loss in 2020 consumed his message. Reactive and emotional, Mr Trump has reigned as the id of populism, and that has made him dangerous to democracy. “特朗普主义”模糊了他的政策与他的需求和利益之间的界限，随着他对2020年失利的执念消耗了他传递的信息，这种界限消失了。特朗普先生被动且情绪化，他一直是民粹主义的代表，这使他对民主政体构成了威胁。 Ron DeSantis, the chilly, cerebral governor of Florida, has an outside chance of becoming its superego, and thus dangerous to the Democrats. 罗恩•德桑蒂斯(Ron DeSantis) ，这位冷淡、理性的佛罗里达州州长，有一个极小的机会成为该州的超我，从而对民主党人构成危险。 Mr DeSantis may wind up as just another speed-bump under Mr Trump’s relentless wheels . But more than any other Republican, he has extracted a coherent agenda from the jumble of Trumpian fears and hostilities, pointing the way towards a Trumpism without Trump. 德桑蒂斯先生最终可能只是特朗普先生无情的车轮下的又一个减速带。但与其他任何共和党人相比，他从特朗普的恐惧和敌意中提取出了一个连贯的议程，为没有特朗普的特朗普主义指明了道路。 And if that has worked in Florida—a diverse state and once a political toss-up that has gone solidly Republican under Mr DeSantis—Democrats would be unwise to dismiss its appeal. Mr DeSantis’s zeal for culture war is not a sideline to this potential successor ideology. It is the unifying principle . 而且，如果这种做法在佛罗里达州(一个多元化的州，在德桑蒂斯先生领导下曾经是共和党坚定支持的政治难题)奏效的话，民主党人如果不理睬它的吸引力，将是不明智的。德桑蒂斯先生对文化战争的热情并不是这种潜在的继任者意识形态的副业。这是统一的原则。 The end of the cold war was hard on American conservatism. Anti-communism had served as what the writer William F. Buckley called the “harnessing bias” of the movement. With the Soviet Union gone, old divisions began widening again between libertarians and religious conservatives. 冷战的结束对美国的保守主义打击很大。反共产主义曾被作家威廉 · F · 巴克利(William F. Buckley)称为这场运动的“利用偏见”。随着苏联的解体，自由意志主义者和宗教保守主义者之间的旧的分歧又开始扩大。 Isolationism, protectionism and nativism, conservative strains that retreated at the outbreak of the second world war, began creeping back. 孤立主义、保护主义和本土主义，这些在二战爆发后退却的保守主义倾向，开始悄悄卷土重来。 George W. Bush’s war on terror held them in check for a time, dangling the prospect of another unifying struggle against an ideological foe. But it was not to be. 乔治•布什(George W. Bush)的反恐战争在一段时间内遏制了他们，让他们看到了另一场团结一致对抗意识形态敌人的前景。但事实并非如此。 “If the Vietnam war splintered the Democratic coalition, then the 2003 Iraq war fractured the Republican one,” writes Matthew Continetti in “The Right”, a history of the conservative movement. “如果说越南战争分裂了民主党联盟，那么2003年的伊拉克战争分裂了共和党联盟,”马修•康蒂内蒂(Matthew Continetti)在《右翼》(The Right)一书中写道。 “Conservatism was never the same after the first improvised explosive device detonated in Baghdad.” “在巴格达发生第一起简易爆炸装置爆炸后，保守主义从此彻底改变。” The financial crash further discredited establishment Republicans like the Bushes, with their contentment with trade, immigration and Wall Street. The ferocity of the Tea Party’s opposition to President Barack Obama obscured its comparable disdain for establishment Republicans. 金融危机进一步败坏了布什等共和党建制派的名声，他们满足于贸易、移民和华尔街。茶党对巴拉克•奥巴马(Barack Obama)总统的强烈反对，掩盖了它对共和党建制派同样的蔑视。 Then came Donald Trump. He rolled over Jeb Bush with a message of contempt for elites and the institutions they dominated. They were all corrupt, dispensing money and privilege to insiders, and only he could fix it. 然后是唐纳德 · 特朗普。他对杰布•布什(Jeb Bush)表达了蔑视精英阶层及其统治的机构的态度。他们都很腐败，向内部人员分发金钱和特权，只有他能解决这个问题。 Mr DeSantis shares Mr Trump’s lack of humility but not his lack of discipline and understanding of government. A Harvard lawyer who served in Iraq and then for three terms in Congress, Mr DeSantis is the thinking Republican’s populist. 德桑蒂斯先生和特朗普先生一样缺乏谦逊，但他并不缺乏纪律和对政府的理解。德桑蒂斯先生是一位哈佛大学的律师，曾在伊拉克服役，后来在国会任职三届，他是一位思维敏捷的共和党民粹主义者。 He shares with progressives a conviction about the primacy of “narrative” in entrenching power. 他与进步人士一样，坚信“叙事”在巩固权力中占据首要地位。 But he argues that the left has taken control of America’s core narratives through undemocratic means, by seizing cultural and corporate institutions, and is telling stories that warp young minds and curtail freedom. 但他认为，左派通过非民主的手段，通过夺取文化和企业机构，控制了美国的核心叙事，他们讲述的故事扭曲了年轻人的思想，限制了自由。 America’s institutions are not just corrupt; they are insidiously corrupting. 美国的机构不仅腐败，而且还在暗中腐败。 More clearly than Mr Trump, Mr DeSantis has defined an ideological foe to rally conservatives and provided them with a plan to fight back. 比特朗普先生更清楚的是，德桑蒂斯先生已经定义了一个意识形态上的敌人来团结保守派，并为他们提供了一个反击计划。 In his telling, leftist ideology has infiltrated the federal bureaucracy, public schools, universities, news media and major corporations in much the way conservatives once feared communism had. 按照他的说法，左翼意识形态已经渗透到联邦官僚机构、公立学校、大学、新闻媒体和大型企业中，就像保守派曾经担心的共产主义那样。 “Because most major institutions in American life have become thoroughly politicised, protecting people from the imposition of leftist ideology requires more than just defeating leftist measures in the legislative arena,” Mr DeSantis writes in his memoir, “The Courage to Be Free”. 德桑蒂斯先生在他的回忆录《获得自由的勇气》中写道: “因为美国生活中的大多数主要机构已经完全政治化，保护人们免受左派意识形态的影响需要的不仅仅是在立法领域击败左派措施。”。 Mr DeSantis appears to be channelling an adviser he cites elsewhere in his memoir, Christopher Rufo, a senior fellow at the Manhattan Institute who has called for “laying siege to the institutions”. 德桑蒂斯先生似乎正在引用他在回忆录其他地方引用的顾问克里斯托弗•鲁福(Christopher Rufo)的话。鲁福是曼哈顿研究所(Manhattan Institute)高级研究员，呼吁“围攻金融机构”。 In one display of this besieging mentality, Mr DeSantis appointed Mr Rufo to the board of trustees of New College , a progressive school in the Florida state system, to help change its leadership and curriculum. Mr Rufo quickly moved to eliminate diversity, equity and inclusion initiatives. Students have protested against the changes. 德桑蒂斯先生任命鲁福先生为新学院的董事会，这是佛罗里达州的一所进步学校，以帮助改变其领导层和课程。鲁福先生迅速采取行动消除多样性、公平性和包容性倡议。学生们对这些变化提出了抗议。 But they will graduate and move on, and in time Mr DeSantis’s preferred narratives will probably take hold. 但是他们将毕业并继续前进，到那时，德桑蒂斯先生偏爱的叙事方式可能会站稳脚跟。 Every day he rewrites the book 他每天都重写这本书 Opportunistic rather than ideological, Mr Trump has been attacking Mr DeSantis from the left on entitlements (saying that Mr DeSantis wanted to cut them), from the right on taxes (that he wanted to raise them) and from the left again on abortion (Mr DeSantis’s six-week ban is “too harsh”). Mr Trump has even risen to the defence of corporations that Mr DeSantis has accused of abetting leftist indoctrination. Mr DeSantis has been calling Mr Trump a loser, though without naming him. “We must reject the culture of losing that has infected our party in recent years,” the governor said in a recent speech in Iowa. 机会主义而非意识形态，特朗普先生一直从左翼攻击德桑蒂斯先生的福利(说德桑蒂斯先生想削减福利) ，从右翼攻击税收(说他想提高税收) ，再从左翼攻击堕胎(德桑蒂斯先生六周的禁令“太严厉”)。特朗普先生甚至站出来为被德桑蒂斯先生指责为教唆左翼思想灌输的公司辩护。德桑蒂斯先生一直称特朗普先生为失败者，尽管没有点名。“我们必须摒弃近年来影响我们党的失败文化,”这位州长最近在爱荷华州发表演讲时表示。 Yet this creates a box for Mr DeSantis: will he say outright that Mr Trump lost the 2020 election? 然而，这为德桑蒂斯先生创造了一个盒子: 他会直接说特朗普先生输掉了2020年的大选吗？ Mr DeSantis is a glowering glad-hander. He comes off as humourless. Should he win the nomination, Democrats think his bullying demeanour and culture-warring will repel suburban and independent voters. That may be right. 德桑蒂斯先生是个怒目而视的讨好者。他看起来很没幽默感。如果他赢得提名，民主党人认为他的恃强凌弱的行为和文化冲突将使郊区和独立选民感到厌恶。也许是这样。 But the governor, a quick study, may overcome the klutzy start to his campaign (snafus marred its launch on Twitter) and grow as a candidate.
