At 44, he would accentuate Joe Biden’s seniority. 但是州长，一个快速的研究，可能克服笨拙的开始，他的竞选(混乱破坏了它在 Twitter 上的启动) ，并成长为一个候选人。44岁时，他会强调乔•拜登(Joe Biden)的资历。 His high job-approval rating in Florida suggests voters there have not concluded he is an extremist. Given a microphone to voice his own narrative, Mr DeSantis has a knack for making his resistance to progressives on matters such as gender sound like common sense. 他在佛罗里达州的高支持率表明，那里的选民还没有得出他是一个极端分子的结论。德桑蒂斯先生拥有一个麦克风来表达他自己的叙述，他有一种诀窍，可以让他在性别等问题上对进步人士的抵制听起来像是常识。 For Democrats and their real influence over cultural institutions, the story of Ron DeSantis may not have a happy ending. ■ 对于民主党人和他们对文化机构的真正影响力来说，罗恩 · 德桑蒂斯的故事可能不会有一个圆满的结局。￭ Read more from Lexington, our columnist on American politics: 阅读更多来自列克星敦，我们的美国政治专栏作家: It turns out that Democrats bus migrants, too (May 18th) 事实证明，民主党人也开公共汽车接送移民(5月18日) Donald Trump has become more dangerous (May 11th) 唐纳德 · 特朗普变得更加危险(5月11日) What walking from Washington to New York reveals about America (May 4th) 从华盛顿步行到纽约揭示了美国(5月4日) Also: How the Lexington column got its name 还有: 列克星敦专栏是如何得名的 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/united-states/2023/05/24/desantis-is-a-truer-believer-if-a-lesser-politician-than-trump 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/united-states/2023/05/24/desantis-is-a-truer-believer-if-a-lesser-politician-than-trump 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

United States 美国 Sections 部分 Asia 亚洲 The Americas 美国 Mexico could elect its first female president next year 墨西哥明年可能选出首位女总统 The race to succeed AMLO :: Will Claudia Sheinbaum be overshadowed by Andrés Manuel López Obrador? 继任者的竞争: 克劳迪娅 · 辛鲍姆会被洛佩斯·奥布拉多尔盖过风头吗？ Lula wants to purge Brazil of Jair Bolsonaro’s influence ❗ 🔄 A power struggle :: He faces resistance from evangelicals, gun owners and big tech 权力斗争: 他面临来自福音派、枪支拥有者和大型科技公司的抵制。 United States 美国 Sections 部分 Asia 亚洲

Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 The race to succeed AMLO 继任反洗钱组织的竞赛 Mexico could elect its first female president next year 墨西哥明年可能选出首位女总统 Will Claudia Sheinbaum be overshadowed by Andrés Manuel López Obrador? ❗ 🔄 May 23rd 2023 | Mexico City 2023年5月23日 | 墨西哥城 O N JUNE 4TH voters in the state of Mexico, the most populous in the country of the same name, will elect a new governor. The vote is widely seen as a testing ground for general elections due next year. Delfina Gómez, a former education minister, is polling far ahead of her rival. 6月4日，墨西哥州的选民将选出一位新州长。墨西哥州是同名国家中人口最多的州。人们普遍认为，这次选举是明年大选的一个试验场。前教育部长德尔菲娜•戈麦斯(Delfina Gómez)的民调结果远远领先于她的竞争对手。 If she wins, it will suggest her party, Morena, is well positioned for next year’s polls. Morena was founded by Andrés Manuel López Obrador, Mexico’s leader. He cannot run again. 如果她获胜，这将表明她的政党莫雷纳(Morena)在明年的民调中处于有利地位。莫雷纳是由墨西哥领导人洛佩斯·奥布拉多尔创立的。他不能再跑了。 As a result the presidential vote next year could see another woman win big: Claudia Sheinbaum, the mayor of Mexico City. She would be the first female head of state. But she could struggle to shake off the legacy of Mr López Obrador, her mentor. 因此，明年的总统大选可能会看到另一位女性大获全胜: 墨西哥城市长克劳迪娅 · 辛鲍姆(Claudia Sheinbaum)。她将成为第一位女性国家元首。但她可能很难摆脱她的导师洛佩斯 · 奥夫拉多尔先生的遗产。 When Mr López Obrador won the election in 2018, with 53% of the vote, it appeared to mark a sea-change in Mexican politics. For decades the country had been dominated by the Institutional Revolutionary Party ( PRI ), a centre-right party that ruled Mexico for 71 years until 2000. Opposition had been provided by the National Action Party ( PAN ), a conservative group that held the presidency for two terms from 2000 to 2012, and the Party of the Democratic Revolution ( PRD ), a left-wing outfit. By 2018 many voters fed up with the status quo felt hopeful that Mr López Obrador could deliver change. He promised to end corruption, reduce inequality and tackle gang violence. 当 López Obrador 先生以53% 的得票率赢得2018年大选时，这似乎标志着墨西哥政治发生了翻天覆地的变化。几十年来，墨西哥一直由制度革命党(PRI)统治，这是一个中右翼政党，在2000年之前统治了墨西哥71年。国家行动党(PAN)和左翼组织民主革命党(PRD)提供了反对意见。前者是一个保守派团体，从2000年到2012年担任了两届总统。到2018年，许多厌倦了现状的选民对洛佩斯 · 奥夫拉多尔先生能够带来改变抱有希望。他承诺结束腐败，减少不平等，打击帮派暴力。 Once in power Mr López Obrador began executing his political project, a contradictory mix of policies he grandly calls the “Fourth Transformation”. They combine a penchant for 1970s-style nationalism with fiscal hawkishness. 洛佩斯 · 奥夫拉多尔先生一上台就开始执行他的政治计划，这是一个自相矛盾的政策组合，他盛大地称之为“第四次转变”。他们将上世纪70年代式的民族主义倾向与财政上的鹰派立场结合起来。 Although the president’s rhetoric sounds left-wing, his policies are a more complicated mix. He did away with Mexico’s health-care system for the uninsured, scrapped child-care subsidies for working mothers and canned a conditional cash programme for the poor. 尽管总统的言论听起来像是左翼，但他的政策是一个更复杂的组合。他废除了墨西哥为没有医疗保险的人提供的医疗保健系统，取消了对有工作的母亲的儿童保育补贴，并取消了针对穷人的有条件现金计划。 His main instinct is statism. The president boosted the state oil and electricity companies at the expense of clean energy, including by building a new oil refinery. He also wants to nationalise Mexico’s lithium. 他的主要本能是国家主义。总统以牺牲清洁能源为代价提振了国有石油和电力公司，包括建造了一座新的炼油厂。他还希望将墨西哥的锂矿国有化。 He handed sweeping power to the armed forces, and has tried to undermine the Supreme Court and the electoral body. On May 19th he expropriated a railway from Grupo México, a mining company. 他把大权交给武装部队，并试图破坏最高法院和选举机构。5月19日，他没收了墨西哥矿业公司 Grupo 的一条铁路。 On May 24th Citigroup, an American bank, announced it would abandon a deal to sell Banamex, a Mexican retail bank, to Grupo México. 5月24日，花旗集团，一家美国银行，宣布放弃将墨西哥零售银行 Banamex 出售给墨西哥集团的交易。 It was unclear if Grupo México had pulled out because of the expropriation, or if Citibank had decided not to go ahead because of frequent comments made by the president in public about the sale. 目前尚不清楚，墨西哥集团是因为此次收购而退出，还是因为花旗银行(Citibank)总裁经常公开发表关于此次出售的言论而决定不进行收购。 Despite this ideological ragbag of policies, the president is popular. Fully 65% of survey respondents approve of him. That is higher than any recent Mexican president at a similar point in their terms (see chart). Many investors are put off by Mr López Obrador. 尽管有这些意识形态上的政策，总统还是很受欢迎的。足足有65% 的受访者认可他。这一数字高于墨西哥任期内任何一位处于类似水平的近期总统(见图表)。许多投资者对洛佩斯 · 奥夫拉多尔先生敬而远之。 But the country is faring better than other countries in Latin America, such as Brazil. Its proximity to the United States helps, making it a beneficiary of “friend-shoring”, as do Mexico’s 13 trade agreements with 50 countries. 但巴西的情况要好于拉丁美洲的其他国家，比如巴西。与美国的接近有所帮助，使其成为“朋友支柱”的受益者，墨西哥与50个国家的13项贸易协定也是如此。 Ms Sheinbaum is not the only contender to be Morena’s candidate for the presidency. Her closest rival is Marcelo Ebrard, the foreign minister. He is “the firefighter-in-chief” for Mr López Obrador’s government, says someone close to him. 辛鲍姆女士并不是莫雷纳总统候选人的唯一竞争者。她最亲密的竞争对手是外交部长马塞洛•埃布拉德(Marcelo Ebrard)。一位与他关系密切的人士表示，他是洛佩斯•奥夫拉多尔先生政府的“消防队长”。 His ministry leads talks with the United States on everything from migration to energy. After Mr López Obrador initially refused to condemn Vladimir Putin’s invasion of Ukraine, it was Mr Ebrard who reassured President Joe Biden’s administration that Mexico remained an ally. 他的部门领导着与美国的会谈，从移民到能源。在洛佩斯•奥夫拉多尔(López Obrador)最初拒绝谴责弗拉基米尔•普京(Vladimir Putin)入侵乌克兰之后，正是埃夫拉德向美国总统乔•拜登(Joe Biden)的政府保证，墨西哥仍是美国的盟友。 His diplomacy also ensured that Mexico received vaccines during the pandemic. Until recently, he was better known among Mexicans than Ms Sheinbaum. 他的外交政策还确保墨西哥在大流行期间接种疫苗。直到最近，他在墨西哥人中的知名度还要高于辛鲍姆。 However, Ms Sheinbaum enjoys a widening lead over Mr Ebrard. Opinion polls place her eight percentage points ahead of him. It helps that she is the president’s preferred candidate. 然而，Sheinbaum 女士比 Ebrard 先生的领先优势越来越大。民意调查显示她领先他8个百分点。她是总统首选的候选人，这一点很有帮助。 For several months Morena’s activists have plastered signs declaring “Es Claudia” (“It’s Claudia”) across the country—possibly in violation of Mexico’s strict campaign laws. 几个月来，莫雷纳的活动人士在全国各地张贴标语，宣称“ Es Claudia”(“ It’s Claudia”) ，这可能违反了墨西哥严格的竞选法。 Her nomination seems assured. Mr López Obrador says Morena’s candidate will be decided by two surveys this summer. But concerns abound about their fairness, such as over which company will run them and what questions the public will be asked. 她的提名似乎已经确定。洛佩斯 · 奥夫拉多尔先生说，莫雷纳的候选人将在今年夏天通过两项调查来确定。但人们普遍担心它们的公平性，比如由哪家公司经营，以及公众会被问到什么问题。 Many worry, including some within Morena, that the process will be akin to that under the PRI , when the leader chose his successor, a process known as the dedazo , or big finger. “This is the dedazo in disguise,” says Mariel Ibarra, a journalist. 许多人担心，包括莫雷纳内部的一些人，这个过程将类似于革命制度党，当领导人选择他的继任者，一个过程称为 deazo，或大手指。记者玛丽埃尔•伊巴拉(Mariel Ibarra)表示: “这就是伪装起来的迪亚佐。”。 A tricky inheritance 一笔棘手的遗产 But Ms Sheinbaum is a competitive figure in her own right, too. Mayors of Mexico City enjoy national prominence because they govern 7% of the country’s 126m people, who generate 15% of its GDP . For the most part she has done a good job. On her watch murders almost halved between 2018 and 2022, compared with a national fall of 8%. She has attempted to clean up the state police force by paying them a decent wage. 但是辛鲍姆女士本身也是一个有竞争力的人物。墨西哥城的市长享有全国性的声望，因为他们管理着墨西哥1.26亿人口中的7% ，这些人创造了墨西哥 GDP 的15% 。在大多数情况下，她做得很好。在她任内，谋杀案在2018年至2022年间几乎减少了一半，而在全国范围内则下降了8% 。她试图通过支付他们体面的工资来清理国家警察部队。 In a country where politicians are dogged by allegations of graft, she is seen as clean. 在一个政客们被贪污指控缠身的国家，她被认为是清白的。 Ms Sheinbaum tends to take a rational approach to policymaking. Unlike the president, she surrounds herself with competent people and is willing to take advice. 辛鲍姆倾向于采取理性的决策方法。与总统不同，她身边都是有能力的人，而且愿意听取意见。 During the pandemic she quietly distanced herself from Mr López Obrador, relying on data, masks and free tests to control covid-19 while he declared, mask-free from his pulpit, that there was no need to worry. 在流感大流行期间，她悄悄地与洛佩斯 · 奥夫拉多尔先生保持距离，依靠数据、口罩和免费测试来控制2019冠状病毒疾病，而奥夫拉多尔先生则宣称，没有必要担心。 (Mexico’s absolute death toll was among the highest in the world.) Over the past five years her administration has built centres around the capital to help poor people to study and to access services. (墨西哥的绝对死亡人数位居世界前列。)在过去五年中，她的政府在首都周围建立了中心，帮助穷人学习和获得服务。 Assuming she secures the candidacy, the election looks like Ms Sheinbaum’s to lose. In 2018 the three main opposition parties (the PRI , PAN and PRD ) formed a coalition known as Va por México. The coalition is running again, but no one contender has emerged, while their main message is that they are not Morena. 假设她获得了候选人资格，那么这场选举看起来就像是辛鲍姆要输了一样。2018年，三个主要的反对党(革命革命党、全国行动党和民主革命党)组成了一个名为“墨西哥退伍军人联盟”的联盟。联合政府再次参选，但没有一个竞争者出现，而他们的主要信息是，他们不是莫雷纳。 “We need to do more than be the option that voters hold their nose to vote for,” says Fernando Belaunzarán, a former PRD lawmaker. “我们需要做的不仅仅是成为选民们投票支持的选项,”前民主革命党议员费尔南多•贝朗扎兰(Fernando Belaunzarán)表示。 Citizens’ Movement, a left-wing party that holds the governorships of Jalisco and Nuevo León, two important states, has a well-liked figure in Luis Donaldo Colosio, the son of a popular reformist PRI presidential candidate who was murdered in 1994. However, he has said he will not run. Mr Ebrard would be competitive were he to break with Morena, but few reckon he will. 公民运动是一个左翼政党，拥有哈利斯科和新莱昂这两个重要州的州长职位。公民运动中有一位广受欢迎的人物，他就是路易斯•多纳尔多•科洛西奥(Luis Donaldo Colosio)。科洛西奥的父亲是一位颇受欢迎的改革派总统候选人，他于1994年被谋杀。不过，他已表示不会参选。如果埃布拉德先生和莫雷纳决裂的话，他会很有竞争力的，但是很少有人认为他会这么做。 If Ms Sheinbaum becomes president, how different would she be from her mentor? So far, she appears to be sticking fairly closely to Mr López Obrador’s contradictory ideology. 如果辛鲍姆成为总统，她将与她的导师有何不同？到目前为止，她似乎相当紧密地坚持洛佩斯奥夫拉多尔先生的矛盾的意识形态。 It is hard to imagine that the mayor, who holds a doctorate in energy engineering, agrees with the president’s approach of favouring the national oil and power companies over clean and cheap power. But she has defended this signature part of the president’s platform. 很难想象，拥有能源工程博士学位的市长会同意总统的做法，即支持国有石油和电力公司，而不是清洁廉价的电力公司。但她为总统竞选纲领的这一标志性部分进行了辩护。 Similarly, Ms Sheinbaum has blamed “neoliberal” economic policy for Mexico’s inequality and criticised the electoral body. She may be tempted to follow Mr López Obrador’s lead and boost social handouts that buy loyalty but do not solve poverty. 同样，辛鲍姆将墨西哥的不平等现象归咎于“新自由主义”经济政策，并批评了选举机构。她可能会受到诱惑，效仿洛佩斯 · 奥夫拉多尔先生的做法，增加购买忠诚但不能解决贫困问题的社会救济金。 Some policies would be hard to reverse. Mr López Obrador has deepened the involvement of the armed forces in fighting crime. He has also handed over 70 civilian functions to them, according to a study by Mexico United Against Crime, an NGO . Soldiers now control the ports and customs facilities, and will build, run and keep the profits from airports and a new tourist train. Ms Sheinbaum is unlikely to be able to remove these privileges, even if she wanted to. 有些政策将难以逆转。洛佩斯 · 奥夫拉多尔先生加深了武装部队在打击犯罪方面的参与。根据非政府组织墨西哥联合反犯罪组织的一项研究，他还将70项民事职能移交给了他们。士兵们现在控制着港口和海关设施，并将建造、运营和保留从机场和新的旅游列车获得的利润。辛鲍姆女士不太可能取消这些特权，即使她想这么做。 But she has also used the same playbook: she deployed the National Guard, a notionally civilian organisation that has replaced the federal police, to help run Mexico City’s subway system after several crashes in 2021, to the outrage of subway workers. 但她也使用了同样的策略: 她部署了国民警卫队(National Guard) ，这是一个名义上的民间组织，取代了联邦警察，在2021年发生多起事故后，帮助管理墨西哥城的地铁系统，引起了地铁工作人员的愤怒。 Waiting in the wings 等在后面 It is doubtful, however, that Ms Sheinbaum will continue Mr López Obrador’s attempts to undermine the Supreme Court. Partly that is because she will not enjoy his political strength. 然而，令人怀疑的是，Sheinbaum 女士是否会继续 López Obrador 先生试图破坏最高法院的努力。这在一定程度上是因为她不会享受他的政治实力。 She will find it harder to lead Morena, which is more a fan club for Mr López Obrador than a political party. 她会发现领导莫雷纳更加困难，因为莫雷纳更像是洛佩斯 · 奥夫拉多尔先生的粉丝俱乐部，而不是一个政党。 She is also likely to preside over a more divided Congress: analysts predict 2024 will mirror midterm elections in 2021 when Morena lost ground, including its coalition’s congressional supermajority. 她还可能主持一个更加分裂的国会: 分析人士预测，2024年将重现2021年的中期选举，届时莫雷纳将失去优势，包括联合政府在国会的绝对多数。 Heir today, gone tomorrow 今天是继承人，明天就没了 It is also a matter of personality. Ms Sheinbaum is nowhere near as inflammatory as her boss, who takes aim at critics from hours-long daily press conferences. She is a “very different figure” from the president, says Patricia Mercado, a senator for Citizens’ Movement. 这也是一个个性的问题。Sheinbaum 女士远没有她的老板那么具有煽动性，她的老板通过每天长达数小时的新闻发布会来抨击批评者。公民运动参议员帕特里夏•梅尔卡多(Patricia Mercado)表示，她与总统“截然不同”。 Even so, some worry that Ms Sheinbaum will merely be Mr López Obrador’s proxy. The president says that he will retire to his ranch after stepping down at the end of 2024. But if Morena were to somehow win a supermajority in 2024, he has pledged to use his last month in power, when his successor is president-elect, to pass constitutional reforms that have already been rejected by the Supreme Court. 即便如此，一些人担心 Sheinbaum 女士仅仅是 López Obrador 先生的代理人。总统说，他将在2024年底退休后回到自己的农场。但是，如果莫雷纳在2024年以某种方式赢得绝对多数，他已经承诺利用自己在位的最后一个月，即他的继任者当选总统时，通过已经被最高法院否决的宪法改革。 Whether or not he remains on the political scene, his influence on Mexico will be felt for years to come. ■ 无论他是否留在政治舞台上，他对墨西哥的影响将在未来数年内显现出来。￭ 八点一刻杂志网 www.badianyike.com This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/the-americas/2023/05/23/mexico-could-elect-its-first-female-president-next-year 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/the-americas/2023/05/23/mexico-could-elect-its-first-female-president-next-year 下载的 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 A power struggle 权力斗争 Lula wants to purge Brazil of Jair Bolsonaro’s influence ❗ 🔄 He faces resistance from evangelicals, gun owners and big tech 他面临着来自福音派、枪支拥有者和大型科技公司的抵制 May 25th 2023 | São Paulo 2023年5月25日 | 圣保罗 L UIZ INÁCIO LULA DA SILVA , Brazil’s new left-wing president, has a tricky job ahead of him. The country is more polarised than at any time since its return to democracy in 1985. In January supporters of Jair Bolsonaro, his right-wing populist predecessor, stormed Congress, the presidential palace and the Supreme Court in Brasília, the capital. 路易斯·伊纳西奥·卢拉·达席尔瓦，巴西新任左翼总统，有一个棘手的工作摆在他面前。自1985年回归民主政体以来，两极分化比以往任何时候都严重。今年1月，他的右翼民粹主义前任雅伊尔•博索纳罗(Jair Bolsonaro)的支持者席卷了国会、总统府和首都巴西利亚的最高法院。 They did so to try to overturn the election result, in which Lula (as he is known) won by just 1.8 percentage points. Fully 39% of those polled still think that the election was rigged; younger Brazilians are particularly prone to support the former president. 他们这样做是为了试图推翻卢拉仅以1.8个百分点获胜的选举结果。整整39% 的受访者仍然认为选举被操纵; 年轻的巴西人尤其倾向于支持前总统。 The parallels with the United States are uncanny. 与美国的相似之处是不可思议的。 But Lula wants to ensure that, unlike Mr Bolsonaro’s idol Donald Trump, his predecessor cannot make a comeback. 但卢拉希望确保，与博索纳罗的偶像唐纳德•特朗普(Donald Trump)不同，他的前任不会卷土重来。 In his first few months in office Lula has tried to overturn Mr Bolsonaro’s programme. He prevented ten state-owned enterprises from being privatised. He also repealed policies which pandered to Mr Bolsonaro’s base, including expansive new gun decrees. 在上任的头几个月里，卢拉试图推翻博索纳罗先生的计划。他阻止了十家国有企业私有化。他还废除了迎合博索纳罗先生基础的政策，包括大规模的新枪支法令。 In February he suspended new gun permits and introduced a deadline of May 3rd for gun owners to register their firearms or have them confiscated. 今年2月，他暂停了新的持枪许可证，并规定枪支所有者必须在5月3日前登记枪支或将枪支没收。 On April 30th Lula’s allies in Congress announced they would put forward legislation to curb online disinformation, which Mr Bolsonaro is being investigated for spreading. The law would force tech firms to remove fake news or face fines of up to 10% of their revenues in Brazil. 4月30日，卢拉在国会的盟友宣布，他们将提出立法来遏制网络虚假信息，博索纳罗先生正因传播此类信息而受到调查。该法案将迫使科技公司删除虚假新闻，否则将面临高达其在巴西收入10% 的罚款。 Similarly, on May 3rd Mr Bolsonaro’s home was raided by police over allegations that he falsified his covid-19 vaccination certificate. He is being investigated in another two dozen cases. He denies all wrongdoing. 同样，5月3日，警察突袭了博索纳罗先生的住所，指控他伪造了2019冠状病毒疾病疫苗接种证书。他正在接受另外20多起案件的调查。他否认一切不当行为。 Lula has also dismissed 189 federal-government soldiers for failing to prevent the riots in January. 卢拉还解雇了189名联邦政府士兵，因为他们未能阻止1月份的骚乱。 But his desire to purge Brazil of his predecessor’s Trumpist legacy faces several problems. For a start, Mr Bolsonaro can still count on a band of ardent supporters. An inquiry into the January riot is due to begin soon in Congress. 但他希望清除巴西前任留下的特朗普主义遗产的愿望面临几个问题。首先，博索纳罗先生仍然可以依靠一群热情的支持者。国会很快将开始对1月份的骚乱进行调查。 Two-fifths of Brazilians do not think Mr Bolsonaro played any role in the attack, as Lula suggests. 五分之二的巴西人不认为博索纳罗先生在这次袭击中扮演了任何角色，正如卢拉所说的那样。 Second, Mr Bolsonaro’s supporters are unlikely to respond well to attempts to change the status quo. Take gun owners. The right-wing president passed at least 32 decrees to allow Brazilians to buy more guns. 其次，博索纳罗先生的支持者不太可能对改变现状的努力作出好的反应。以持枪者为例。这位右翼总统至少通过了32项法令，允许巴西人购买更多的枪支。 The number of outstanding permits granted to buy firearms increased from 117,000 to 695,000 during his four-year term. Many gun owners are not going to give them up easily: over 6,000 people failed to register their weapons by the deadline. 在他的四年任期内，未获批准购买火器的数量从117000份增加到695000份。许多枪支拥有者不会轻易放弃他们: 超过6000人没有在截止日期前登记他们的武器。 Marco Aurélio Carvalho, a lawyer who helped formulate Lula’s gun policy, admits that registration is just the first step and more could be done, such as encouraging owners to sell their guns. 曾帮助卢拉制定枪支政策的律师马可•奥雷利奥•卡瓦略(Marco Aurélio Carvalho)承认，注册只是第一步，还可以做更多的事情，比如鼓励枪支所有者出售自己的枪支。 And Lula’s attempts to regulate social media have brought disparate groups together to oppose it. On May 2nd the president’s allies were forced to postpone a vote on the bill, after failing to gather congressional support for it. Many worry that the bill curtails free speech. 卢拉试图规范社交媒体的行为已经引起了不同团体的共同反对。5月2日，由于未能获得国会的支持，总统的盟友被迫推迟对该法案的投票。许多人担心该法案会限制言论自由。 Conservative types are against it, not least as it could incriminate Mr Bolsonaro. Two days after the attack on Congress, Mr Bolsonaro posted and quickly deleted a video claiming the election was fraudulent (he later told police he had uploaded the post by accident). 保守派反对这种做法，尤其是因为它可能使博索纳罗先生入罪。国会遇袭两天后，博索纳罗先生发布并迅速删除了一段声称选举存在欺诈的视频(他后来告诉警方，他上传该视频纯属偶然)。 Tech companies and evangelical Christians also oppose the bill. Google altered its search-engine results so that news articles which opposed the bill would appear at the top of the page, according to a study by the Federal University of Rio de Janeiro. 科技公司和福音派基督徒也反对这项法案。根据里约热内卢联邦大学的一项研究，谷歌修改了其搜索引擎结果，使反对该法案的新闻文章出现在页面顶部。 In response, antitrust regulators opened an investigation against the company at the request of a senator who is a Lula ally. Evangelicals fear that it will prevent them from posting passages of the Bible that could be considered homophobic. 作为回应，反垄断监管机构应卢拉的一位盟友参议员的要求，对该公司展开了调查。福音派担心这会阻止他们张贴可能被认为是恐同的圣经段落。 Even some supporters of stricter regulation rejected certain points of the draft, such as one which would have given immunity to legislators who spread falsehoods online. Critics also feel that the bill could give the courts too much power. 甚至一些支持更严格监管的人也反对草案中的某些要点，比如给予在网上散布谎言的立法者豁免权。批评人士还认为，该法案可能赋予法院太多权力。 Last year the Supreme Court ordered the removal of thousands of social-media posts, mainly by bolsonaristas . It ordered the police to raid the homes of eight businessmen after some joked on WhatsApp that they would support a coup to keep Mr Bolsonaro in power. 去年，最高法院下令删除数以千计的社交媒体帖子，主要是由波索纳里斯塔斯发布的。在一些人在 WhatsApp 上开玩笑说他们将支持一场政变来保持博索纳罗先生的权力之后，政府命令警察突击搜查八名商人的家。 The final problem for Lula is that his predecessor managed to reshape aspects of the Brazilian state. Mr Bolsonaro’s party, which controls nearly a fifth of Congress, will probably water down any version of the fake-news bill. 卢拉面临的最后一个问题是，他的前任成功地重塑了巴西政府的方方面面。博索纳罗先生的政党控制着国会近五分之一的席位，他们可能会淡化任何版本的假新闻法案。 The public security caucus, made up mostly of pro-gun congressmen, has swelled from 25 legislators across both houses in 2018 to 37 today, out of a total of 594 representatives. Mr Bolsonaro’s heirs still wield power. 公共安全核心小组主要由支持枪支的国会议员组成，从2018年的25名议员扩大到今天的37名，共有594名议员。博索纳罗先生的继承人仍然掌权。 In April one of his sons, Flavio, a senator, proposed a bill that would loosen gun restrictions again. 今年4月，他的一个儿子，参议员弗拉维奥，提出了一项法案，将再次放宽枪支限制。 Jair’ll be back. Maybe Jair 会回来的，也许吧 Even if a fake-news bill is eventually passed, it will be hard to control what Brazilians post online. In the United States, viral posts spread on Facebook and other platforms, the content of which can be easily analysed. 即使一项假新闻法案最终获得通过，也很难控制巴西人在网上发布什么内容。在美国，病毒式的帖子在 Facebook 和其他平台上传播，其内容很容易被分析。 By contrast, in Brazil it circulates on encrypted messaging groups like Telegram which are harder to access, says David Nemer of the University of Virginia. 弗吉尼亚大学的 David Nemer 说，相比之下，在巴西，它通过像 Telegram 这样的加密信息群组传播，而这些群组很难访问。 All this means that ridding Brazil of Mr Bolsonaro’s influence will not be easy. With one exception. Under American law, Mr Trump can run for the presidency next year even if he is convicted in one of six inquiries investigating him for alleged wrongdoings. 所有这一切意味着，摆脱博索纳罗先生对巴西的影响并非易事。除了一个例外。根据美国法律，特朗普先生可以参加明年的总统竞选，即使他在六项调查中被判有罪。 In Brazil Mr Bolsonaro would become ineligible for office for eight years if convicted in one of the 15 criminal probes pending against him. ■ 在巴西，博索纳罗先生如果在15项针对他的刑事调查中被判有罪，他将在8年内没有资格担任公职。￭ This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/the-americas/2023/05/25/lula-wants-to-purge-brazil-of-jair-bolsonaros-influence 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/the-americas/2023/05/25/lula-wants-to-purge-brazil-of-jair-bolsonaros-influence 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

The Americas 美国 Sections 部分 China 中国 Asia 亚洲 Australia has faced down China’s trade bans and emerged stronger 澳大利亚取消了中国的贸易禁令，并变得更加强大 Surviving the dragon :: The “lucky country” may be uniquely able to endure Chinese bullying 幸存的龙: : “幸运的国家”可能独一无二地能够忍受中国人的欺凌 Sheikh Hasina is Asia’s iron lady 哈西娜酋长是亚洲的铁娘子 An interview with Bangladesh’s prime minister :: Her tragic past now threatens Bangladesh’s future 接受孟加拉国总理采访: 她的悲惨过去现在威胁着孟加拉国的未来 Japan’s ageing society is finding creative ways to dispose of its dead 日本日益老龄化的社会正在寻找处理死者遗体的创造性方法 Up and away :: A new craze for funeral planning is disrupting the way Japanese think and speak about their demise 一股新的葬礼筹备热潮正在扰乱日本人思考和谈论他们死亡的方式。 America is lavishing attention on Pacific island states 美国把大量的注意力放在太平洋岛国身上 Banyan :: Anthony Blinken is the most significant American official to visit Papua New Guinea since General Douglas MacArthur 安东尼 · 布林肯是自巴布亚新几内亚道格拉斯·麦克阿瑟以来访问美国最重要的官员 The Americas 美国 Sections 部分 China 中国

Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Surviving the dragon 在恶龙面前幸存下来 Australia has faced down China’s trade bans and emerged stronger 澳大利亚取消了中国的贸易禁令，并变得更加强大 The “lucky country” may be uniquely able to endure Chinese bullying 这个“幸运的国家”可能是唯一能够忍受中国欺凌的国家 May 23rd 2023 | BEIJING and SYDNEY 2023年5月23日，北京和悉尼 W HEN CHINA launched a campaign of economic coercion against Australia in 2020, Communist Party bosses thought they had crushing leverage. The economies of the two countries—resource-rich Australia and commodities-hungry China—were complementary and closely connected. 当中国在2020年发起对澳大利亚的经济胁迫运动时，共产党的领导人认为他们拥有压倒性的影响力。资源丰富的澳大利亚和渴求大宗商品的中国这两个国家的经济互补性强，关系密切。 By massively curbing shipments of everything from timber to coal, lobsters, barley and wine, on pretexts including exaggerated concerns about trade practices and pest infestations, China imposed a A$24bn ($16bn) hit on Australia, representing 5.5% of its total annual exports. 通过大规模限制木材、煤炭、龙虾、大麦和葡萄酒等各种商品的出口，借口包括对贸易行为和虫害的过度担忧，中国对澳大利亚实施了240亿澳元(160亿美元)的打击，占其年度出口总额的5.5% 。 Yet it did not succumb. And like a surfer surviving a shark attack with no more than a lightly gnawed board, Australia is now emerging from three years of Chinese bullying in remarkably good shape. 但它没有屈服。就像一个冲浪运动员在鲨鱼袭击中幸存下来，身上只有一块被轻轻咬过的冲浪板一样，澳大利亚现在正以一种非常好的状态摆脱中国三年来的欺凌。 Its exports briefly suffered under the strictures, then surged—culminating last year in Australia’s biggest-ever trade surplus, equivalent to more than 7% of GDP. And the trade blocks, imposed after Australia’s then-conservative government dared call for an inquiry into the origins of Covid-19, are coming off. 澳大利亚的出口在这些限制之下短暂受挫，然后飙升，去年达到高潮，创下澳大利亚有史以来最大的贸易顺差，相当于国内生产总值(GDP)的7% 以上。在当时保守的澳大利亚政府大胆呼吁对2019冠状病毒疾病的起源进行调查之后，澳大利亚实施的贸易封锁正在取消。 On May 18th, after a meeting between the two countries’ trade ministers in Beijing, China lifted a de facto ban on Australian timber. 5月18日，两国贸易部长在北京会晤后，中国解除了对澳大利亚木材的实际禁令。 (“Serious study” by quarantine officials had allayed its concerns about bug infestation, explained Xiao Qian, China’s ambassador in Canberra.) Since January, Chinese importers have been quietly buying Australian coal: in the first quarter of 2023 Australia sent them A$1.2bn-worth of the stuff. (中国驻堪培拉大使肖谦解释说，检疫官员的“严肃研究”减轻了人们对虫害的担忧。)自今年1月以来，中国进口商一直在悄悄购买澳大利亚煤炭: 2023年第一季度，澳大利亚向中国出口了价值12亿澳元的煤炭。 Australia’s centre-left Labor government says cotton and copper exports are also resuming. China is reviewing tariffs on barley, Australia having suspended its case against them at the World Trade Organisation ( WTO ). 澳大利亚中左翼工党政府表示，棉花和铜的出口也正在恢复。中国正在重新考虑对大麦征收关税，澳大利亚已经暂停了在世界贸易组织(WTO)对中国的诉讼。 After years of unchecked Chinese bullying, on Asia’s seas, in its multilateral forums and beyond, Australia’s escape is being widely understood as a significant win. And so it is. Yet it involved, as well as strong nerves, a large dose of luck. 经过多年来中国在亚洲海域、多边论坛及其他地区肆无忌惮的欺凌，澳大利亚的逃脱被广泛理解为一次重大胜利。的确如此。然而，这不仅需要强大的神经，还需要大量的运气。 Chinese leaders were only half-right. As their boycott began, Australia’s economy was indeed reliant on China. Exports of goods and services to China accounted for 11% of outbound trade in 2005. By 2020, the share stood at 37%. 中国领导人只说对了一半。当他们开始抵制时，澳大利亚的经济确实依赖于中国。2005年，对华商品和服务出口占对外贸易的11% 。到2020年，这一比例达到了37% 。 Mitigating its reliance, Australia’s bet on China was chiefly a wager on international trade. Australia has relatively few multinationals that manufacture and sell inside China. But even without vast China-based subsidiaries, Australia was vulnerable. The Economist ’s in-house index of China exposure adds goods exports to China, services exports to China and Hong Kong, and the revenue of foreign multinationals’ affiliates operating in China and Hong Kong. Australia’s exposure, at 8.2% of GDP in 2020, was double that of America, and close to that of Germany. 减轻对中国的依赖，澳大利亚对中国的押注主要是对国际贸易的押注。澳大利亚在中国境内生产和销售的跨国公司相对较少。但即使没有在中国的大型子公司，澳大利亚也很脆弱。《经济学人》的内部中国风险指数包括对中国的商品出口、对中国内地和香港的服务出口，以及在中国内地和香港运营的外国跨国公司子公司的收入。2020年，澳大利亚的风险敞口占 GDP 的8.2% ，是美国的两倍，接近德国。 Luckily for Australia, however, the two economies were such a good fit that China’s firms felt as much pain from the curbs as Australia’s, if not more. And some commodities, such as Australian iron ore, were so hard to replace China chose not to target them. 然而，对澳大利亚来说幸运的是，这两个经济体是如此的契合，以至于中国公司感受到的限制带来的痛苦不亚于澳大利亚公司，甚至更多。澳大利亚铁矿石等一些大宗商品很难取代中国选择不瞄准它们的做法。 Also luckily, many of Australia’s bruised exporters found other markets. 同样幸运的是，澳大利亚许多伤痕累累的出口商找到了其它市场。 After China slapped a tariff of up to 80% on Australian barley, its producers sold it to South-East Asian countries. They also planted other crops. And Chinese beer-makers had to buy other countries’ barley, which was not as good. 在中国对澳大利亚大麦征收高达80% 的关税后，其生产商将其出售给东南亚国家。他们还种植其他作物。而中国啤酒制造商不得不购买其他国家的大麦，而大麦的质量并不好。 When China blocked shipments of Australian coal, similarly, it had to buy more from Russia and Indonesia. That left India and Japan short, so Australia sold to them. Meanwhile, rising world prices made Australian miners lots of money. 同样，当中国阻止澳大利亚煤炭出口时，它不得不从俄罗斯和印度尼西亚购买更多煤炭。这让印度和日本做空，于是澳大利亚卖给了它们。与此同时，不断上涨的全球价格让澳大利亚矿业公司赚了很多钱。 Australian pain, though not insignificant, was concentrated: lobster fishermen struggled; wine exports to China’s middle classes plunged. 澳大利亚的痛苦虽然不是无关紧要，但却是集中的: 龙虾渔民生活艰难; 对中国中产阶级的葡萄酒出口骤减。 Australia’s efforts to manage the crisis politically were assisted by Chinese overreach. China’s propaganda machine fiercely denounced Australia, and almost all official contacts were frozen. 澳大利亚在政治上应对危机的努力，得益于中国的过度干预。中国的宣传机器强烈谴责澳大利亚，几乎所有的官方联系都被冻结。 In November 2020 China’s embassy in Australia made public a list of 14 grievances with the country’s then conservative government. 2020年11月，中国驻澳大利亚大使馆公布了一份清单，列出了中国当时保守派政府的14项不满。 Ranging far beyond economic questions, China moaned that members of Australia’s parliament were allowed to criticise the Communist Party and that the country’s news outlets published “unfriendly or antagonistic” reporting on China. 中国抱怨称，澳大利亚议会成员被允许批评共产党，该国新闻机构发表了“不友好或敌对”的中国报道，这远远超出了经济问题的范围。 The then-prime minister, Scott Morrison, suggested that these complaints were essentially against “Australia just being Australia”. 时任澳大利亚总理斯科特•莫里森(ScottMorrison)表示，这些抱怨基本上是针对“澳大利亚就是澳大利亚”。 Encouraged by such missteps, Australia held its nerve. The election of a new government, led by Anthony Albanese, then gave China an excuse to climb down. 在这些失误的鼓舞下，澳大利亚保持了冷静。由安东尼·阿尔巴尼西领导的新政府的选举，给了中国让步的借口。 Mr Albanese’s government has been careful not to crow. Dispelling talk of a big win for the West, it claims to be steadfastly respectful and pragmatic in its dealing with China. 艾尔巴尼斯先生的政府一直小心翼翼地避免自鸣得意。驱散了西方取得重大胜利的言论，声称在与中国打交道时坚定不移地表现出尊重和务实。 The idea, ministers say, is to “co-operate where we can, disagree where we must”—meaning no gratuitous poking of the dragon. “Disagree behind closed doors, but don’t amplify your differences,” warns another Australian official. “China responds well to this.” 部长们表示，这个想法是“在可能的地方合作，在必须的地方意见不一致”——意思是不要无缘无故地戳龙。另一位澳大利亚官员警告说: “私下里存在分歧，但不要放大分歧。”。“中国对此反应良好。” Alongside such caution, Australia also understood its strengths. China relented because it “saw that we wouldn’t capitulate”, the same official argues. Australia resisted demands to change laws, overturn investment bans and muffle critics. 除了这种谨慎，澳大利亚也明白自己的优势。这位官员认为，中国之所以缓和态度，是因为它“看到我们不会投降”。澳大利亚拒绝了修改法律、推翻投资禁令和压制批评者的要求。 Rather, under its previous and current government, it tied its security policy tighter to America through AUKUS , a trilateral submarine-building pact that also includes Britain, and a policy of deterrence. It is also doing more diplomatically to counter China’s influence in the Pacific. The trade bans have in this sense backfired. 相反，在其前任和现任政府的领导下，它通过 AUKUS，一个包括英国在内的三边潜艇建设协议，以及一项威慑政策，将其安全政策与美国更紧密地联系在一起。同时，它也在采取更加外交的手段来对抗中国在太平洋地区的影响力。从这个意义上说，贸易禁令适得其反。 Australia set another useful example at the WTO. China agreed to review its barley tariffs just as Australia’s case against them was concluding. Rather than force the issue to a judgment, Australia suspended its action. 澳大利亚在世贸组织树立了另一个有用的榜样。中国同意重新评估其大麦关税，而澳大利亚针对中国的诉讼正接近尾声。澳大利亚非但没有将这个问题强加于人，反而中止了自己的行动。 “China does not want to be shamed in the international arena,” says Matthew Goodman of the Center for Strategic and International Studies in Washington. “中国不想在国际舞台上丢脸,”华盛顿战略与国际研究中心(Center for Strategic and International Studies)的马修•古德曼(Matthew Goodman)表示。 Australia was additionally helped by the fact that China wants to join the Comprehensive and Progressive Trans-Pacific Partnership ( CPTPP ), a vast regional trade deal. As a CPTPP member, Australia could block China’s accession. It accordingly made clear there was no possibility of accession talks so long as China was trampling economic rules and its pre-existing free-trade agreements with Australia. 此外，中国希望加入全面进步跨太平洋战略经济伙伴关系协议协定(cptPP)——一项庞大的区域贸易协定——也对澳大利亚有所帮助。作为 CPTPP 成员国，澳大利亚可能会阻止中国加入。因此，它明确表示，只要中国践踏经济规则及其与澳大利亚之前存在的自由贸易协定，就不可能进行入世谈判。 How will Australia guard against the risk of a repeat? Decoupling is not on the agenda—China still accounts for almost a third of its total goods and services exports. But three years of Chinese bullying have left a mark. 澳大利亚将如何防范重蹈覆辙的风险？脱钩不在议程上ーー中国仍占其商品和服务出口总额的近三分之一。但中国三年来的欺凌行为留下了一个印记。 Australia is trying to reduce its reliance on China by signing free-trade agreements with India and Britain. Another, with the EU, is in the works. 澳大利亚正试图通过与印度和英国签署自由贸易协定来减少对中国的依赖。另一项与欧盟合作的计划正在酝酿之中。 China’s aggression has concentrated minds among Australia’s partners and allies, too. At meetings in Japan on May 20th, leaders of the Group of Seven ( G7 ) countries voiced concern at the “disturbing rise in incidents of economic coercion” and promised, for the first time, to collaborate against attempts to “weaponise economic dependencies”. 中国的侵略行为也让澳大利亚的合作伙伴和盟友集中了注意力。在5月20日于日本举行的会议上，七国集团(G7)领导人对“令人不安的经济胁迫事件增多”表示担忧，并首次承诺合作打击“将经济依赖武器化”的企图。 There is more talk of solidarity and co-ordination, so countries do not profit by selling more to a bully when a friend or neighbour is being targeted. 人们更多地谈论团结和协作，因此，当一个朋友或邻居成为攻击目标时，国家不会通过向恃强凌弱者出售更多商品而获利。 America’s ambassador to Japan, Rahm Emanuel, talks of his country offering “support and countervailing opportunities to partners in their times of need”. 美国驻日本大使伊曼纽尔(Rahm Emanuel)表示，美国“在合作伙伴需要帮助的时候，向他们提供支持和反补贴机会”。 China is the largest trade partner of a long and growing list of countries. Very few have the wealth and natural resources that protected Australia, the “lucky” country. Even so, many are studying the lessons of Australia’s escape from China’s grip. ■ 中国是越来越多国家的最大贸易伙伴。很少有人拥有保护澳大利亚这个“幸运”国家的财富和自然资源。即便如此，许多人仍在学习澳大利亚摆脱中国控制的经验教训。￭ This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/asia/2023/05/23/australia-has-faced-down-chinas-trade-bans-and-emerged-stronger 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/asia/2023/05/23/australia-has-faced-down-chinas-trade-bans-and-emerged-stronger 下载的 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 An interview with Bangladesh’s prime minister 采访孟加拉国总理 Sheikh Hasina is Asia’s iron lady 哈西娜酋长是亚洲的铁娘子 Her tragic past now threatens Bangladesh’s future 她悲惨的过去威胁着孟加拉国的未来 May 24th 2023 | TYSONS CORNER, VIRGINIA 2023年5月24日，弗吉尼亚州，泰森角 B ANGLADESH’S PRIME MINISTER , Sheikh Hasina, is the world’s longest-serving female head of government, and one of the most significant of either sex. During two decades in office she has presided over momentous poverty alleviation in her country of 170m, fuelled by average annual GDP growth of 7% for much of that time. The 75-year-old has led her party, the Awami League, to victory in three consecutive polls, and four in all—one more than Indira Gandhi or Margaret Thatcher managed. With an election due early next year, which she is expected to win, The Economist asked her, in an interview in her northern Virginia hotel suite, what ambitions she had left. 孟加拉国总理谢赫 · 哈西娜是世界上任期最长的女性政府首脑，也是两性中最重要的人物之一。在她执政的20年里，她在她的1.7亿人口的国家里主持了重大的脱贫攻坚，在此期间的大部分时间里，国内生产总值平均每年增长7% 。这位75岁的老人领导她的政党人民联盟(Awami League)在连续三次民调中获胜，总共获胜四次，比英迪拉•甘地(Indira Gandhi)或其他撒切尔夫人领导人多一次。明年年初，她有望赢得大选。在她位于弗吉尼亚州北部的酒店套房接受采访时，《经济学人》(The Economist)杂志问她，她还有什么抱负。 “I want to make this country a hunger-free, poverty-free developed country,” she says—then abruptly switches tack. “Can you imagine that they killed my father?” she asks, referring to the grim history that launched and still shadows her career. “我想让这个国家成为一个没有饥饿、没有贫困的发达国家。”。“你能想象他们杀了我父亲吗?”她问道，指的是那段阴暗的历史，那段历史开启了她的职业生涯，至今仍影响着她的职业生涯。 Her father, Bangladesh’s first president, Sheikh Mujibur Rahman, was assassinated in an army coup in 1975, four years after the country’s bloody split from Pakistan. Seventeen of his close relatives—almost all except Sheikh Hasina, in Europe at the time—were murdered too. 她的父亲，孟加拉国第一任总统，谢赫·穆吉布·拉赫曼，在1975年的一次军事政变中被暗杀，四年前该国从巴基斯坦血腥分裂。他的17名近亲(除了当时在欧洲的谢赫•哈西娜(Sheikh Hasina)之外，几乎全部遇害)。 “They killed my brother, my mother, another brother—only ten years old! My two sisters-in-law, my only uncle, a disabled person,” says the prime minister, her eyes wet with tears. “他们杀了我的兄弟，我的母亲，还有另一个兄弟ーー只有十岁！我的两个嫂子，我唯一的叔叔，一个残疾人,”首相说，她的眼泪湿润了。 Her advisers had primed this interviewer to ask about that long-ago tragedy. He had not, but Sheikh Hasina raises it anyway, as she often does. This illustrates the great sense of loss and destiny she exudes—and on which she has built an imposing personality cult. 她的顾问们已经让这位采访者准备好询问那场很久以前的悲剧。他没有，但谢赫哈西娜提出了反正，因为她经常这样做。这说明了她散发出的强烈的失落感和命运感ーー在这种感觉上，她建立了一种令人难忘的个人崇拜。 A huge portrait of Sheikh Mujib, schlepped around the world by her 100-man retinue, leans by her chair. She nods towards it, as if bringing the murdered man into the conversation. 一幅巨大的穆吉布酋长肖像，由她的100人随从拖着环游世界，靠在她的椅子上。她朝它点点头，好像把那个被谋杀的人带进了谈话中。 The question confronting Bangladeshis is whether their leader’s feelings of grievance and dynastic entitlement are becoming a threat to her legacy and their future. The interview provides little reassurance. 孟加拉国人面临的问题是，他们领导人的不满情绪和王朝特权是否正在对她的遗产和他们的未来构成威胁。这次采访没有给我们什么安慰。 No politician likes criticism. But the prime minister bristles at the slightest suggestion that her record is imperfect; and in response offers rapid-fire criticisms of almost every Bangladeshi government except her own. 没有政治家喜欢批评。但是总理对她的记录并不完美的一点点暗示感到愤怒; 作为回应，她对几乎每一个孟加拉国政府都进行了迅速的批评，除了她自己的政府。 Asked about corruption, she blames it on the military government that replaced her father, accuses the World Bank of inventing a recent scandal allegedly involving members of her government, then claims the problem does not exist. 当被问及腐败问题时，她将其归咎于接替她父亲的军政府，指责世界银行(World Bank)最近捏造了一起涉及其政府成员的丑闻，然后声称问题并不存在。 “Maybe in the down level, but not that much nowadays. They dare to do it and I will take action!” “也许在底层，但现在没有那么多。他们敢这样做，我会采取行动!” Bangladesh has long been rated South Asia’s most corrupt country after Afghanistan. Thanks to the Taliban’s steps, it may now be the most corrupt. 长期以来，孟加拉国一直被评为继阿富汗之后南亚最腐败的国家。由于塔利班的步伐，它现在可能是最腐败的。 Some of its graft looks symptomatic of the de facto one-party state that Sheikh Hasina, fulfilling one of her father’s ambitions, has made. 其中一些贪污行为看起来象征着谢赫•哈西娜(Sheikh Hasina)实现了父亲的一个抱负，建立了事实上的一党制国家。 Before she was re-elected in 2008, power switched repeatedly between the League and the Bangladesh Nationalist Party ( BNP ), run by another charismatic dynast, Khaleda Zia. Amid the political tumult, the country’s institutions, including the media, police and courts, had a degree of independence. 在她于2008年再次当选之前，权力在联盟和孟加拉国民族主义党(bNP)之间反复切换，后者由另一位富有魅力的王朝领导人卡莉达•齐亚(Khaleda Zia)执掌。在政治动荡中，包括媒体、警察和法院在内的国家机构有一定程度的独立性。 Now Mrs Zia is under on-off house arrest, her party’s activists are hounded, the media is cowed and the police and courts are suborned to Sheikh Hasina’s party. Not coincidentally, they are two of the country’s most corrupt bodies. 如今，齐亚夫人时不时地遭到软禁，她所在政党的活动人士遭到追捕，媒体受到恐吓，警方和法院都听命于谢赫•哈西娜(Sheikh Hasina)所在的政党。并非巧合的是，他们是这个国家最腐败的两个机构。 The coming election will not offer the BNP a way back. Although Sheikh Hasina claims to be committed to a free vote, she says that only a “real political party” should be permitted to compete—and that her opponents do not fit the criteria. 即将到来的选举不会为 BNP 提供一条退路。尽管谢赫•哈西娜声称致力于自由投票，但她表示，只有“真正的政党”才应被允许参与竞争，而她的对手不符合这一标准。 She accuses the BNP , formed under army rule half a century ago, of being “constituted by a military ruler illegally”. She alleges that the country’s biggest Islamist party, a former ally of Pakistan, is “almost all war criminals”. 她指责半个世纪前在军队统治下成立的 BNP 是“由军事统治者非法组成的”。她宣称，巴基斯坦最大的伊斯兰政党——巴基斯坦的前盟友——“几乎都是战犯”。 “Our point is that there is no such party [apart from the League] who can really contest the election.” “我们的观点是，(除了联盟)没有这样一个政党能够真正参与竞选。” In some ways, Bangladesh has probably benefited from her iron grip. 在某些方面，孟加拉国可能从她的铁腕统治中获益。 It has not obviously boosted the growth rate, which hit a high gear before her extended spell in office and is largely a product of pre-existing factors: such as the country’s garment industry and services provided by its elite NGO s. Yet she has wrought policies, including infrastructure investment, that have helped maintain the boom, which a weaker government might not have sustained. 这并没有明显地提高经济增长率，在她任期延长之前，经济增长率已经达到了一个高速，这在很大程度上是先前存在的因素的产物: 比如该国的服装业和精英非政府组织提供的服务。然而，她制定的政策，包括基础设施投资，帮助维持了经济繁荣，而一个较弱的政府可能无法维持这种繁荣。 But authoritarianism has diminishing returns. Overly reliant on garments, Bangladesh needs to develop new exports, a reality its government is hardly grappling with. 但威权主义有报酬递减。孟加拉国过度依赖服装，需要开发新的出口产品，而政府很难应对这一现实。 (Sheikh Hasina says it is looking to develop handicrafts and food processing—an insufficient solution.) Bangladeshis, a disputatious people, are bridling at her strictures. Several hundred were protesting rowdily outside the hotel. Rights groups say the election could be violent. (谢赫•哈西娜(Sheikh Hasina)表示，该公司正寻求发展手工艺品和食品加工业，这是一个不充分的解决方案。)孟加拉国人，一个有争议的民族，对她的苛责感到愤怒。几百人在旅馆外吵吵嚷嚷地抗议。人权组织说，选举可能是暴力的。 America might once have warned Sheikh Hasina to let Bangladeshi democracy breathe. Now it is mainly concerned that she should not accommodate China, which her government is courting for investment. India, where she has close personal ties, takes a similar view. 美国也许曾经警告过谢赫 · 哈西娜让民主政体喘口气。现在主要关注的是，她不应该容纳中国，而中国正是她的政府寻求投资的对象。印度也持类似观点，她在印度有着密切的个人关系。 Sheikh Hasina appears adept at mollifying all three, a success she attributes to pragmatism. “The relations between America and China are their own matter. Why should I poke my nose there?” Yet she makes a dig at America, because it was once close to Mrs Zia. 谢赫 · 哈西娜似乎善于安抚这三个人，她将这一成功归功于实用主义。“美中关系是他们自己的事情。我为什么要在那里多管闲事?”然而，她挖苦了美国，因为它曾经接近齐亚夫人。 “They say they are the democratic country…But in our country they don’t exercise that. Why don’t they support me?” “他们说他们是民主国家... ... 但在我们国家，他们不这样做。他们为什么不支持我?” Actually they do, but perhaps shouldn’t. Sheikh Hasina’s long career has been a story of courage and the ruthless use of power, with some policy successes that she can lay claim to, and epic national growth that she cannot, but does. It is hard to see how the story will end well. 事实上他们有，但也许不该有。谢赫 · 哈西娜漫长的职业生涯是一个关于勇气和无情使用权力的故事，她可以宣称自己取得了一些政策上的成功，她不能但确实取得了史诗般的国家发展。很难看出这个故事会有什么好结局。 She is growing increasingly authoritarian and resented and, as she confirms, has no plans to retire. 她正变得越来越独裁，越来越被憎恨，而且正如她所证实的那样，她没有退休的计划。 Her government’s latest plan is entitled Vision 2041. She will not see it out, she concedes. But as she broaches a third decade in power, succession planning is not on the agenda. “Because if I’m not there…I don’t know who will come to power.” ■ 她的政府的最新计划名为“愿景2041”。她承认，她将无法看到结果。但在她执政的第三个十年即将到来之际，接班人计划却不在议事日程上。“因为如果我不在那里... ... 我不知道谁会掌权。”￭ This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/asia/2023/05/24/sheikh-hasina-is-asias-iron-lady 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/asia/2023/05/24/sheikh-hasina-is-asias-iron-lady 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Up and away 飞起来 Japan’s ageing society is finding creative ways to dispose of its dead 日本日益老龄化的社会正在寻找处理死者遗体的创造性方法 A new craze for funeral planning is disrupting the way Japanese think and speak about their demise 一股新的葬礼筹备热潮正在扰乱日本人思考和谈论自己死亡的方式 May 25th 2023 | TOCHIGI 2023年5月25日 | TOCHIGI T HE SAKASHITAS gather in front of a gigantic red balloon. Inside it are the ashes of their late father, Haruhiko, and his late dog, Fuu-chan. On a count of three, Saiko, the dead man’s daughter, snips the string tethering the helium-filled orb. 酒鹿们聚集在一个巨大的红气球前。里面是他们已故的父亲，春彦和他已故的狗，福蟾的骨灰。我数到三，死者的女儿 Saiko 剪断了束缚氦气球的绳子。 It shoots into the sky, with a dozen smaller balloons in pursuit. The family members watch—some waving—until the balloons disappear into the clouds. “Our father was a calm man, always travelling the world,” says his oldest son, Kohei. 它冲天而起，十几个小气球紧追不舍。家人们看着气球消失在云端，有些人在挥手致意。“我们的父亲是一个冷静的人，总是周游世界，说:”他的大儿子，高平。 “Whenever we look at the sky, we will think of him.” “每当我们仰望天空，我们都会想起他” In Japan, people are traditionally cremated after death and their ashes buried. But in a crowded, ageing and largely secular society, this is becoming trickier and less desirable. 在日本，人们传统上是死后火化和骨灰埋葬。但在一个拥挤、老龄化、基本上是世俗化的社会，这种做法正变得越来越棘手，也越来越不受欢迎。 Japan’s death rate is soaring—in 2022 the country logged 1.5m deaths, the highest figure since the second world war. Grave sites are running out of space. There are fewer grieving relatives around to perform funeral rites, or to tend graves. 日本的死亡率正在飙升ーー2022年，该国死亡人数达到150万，为二战以来的最高水平。墓地已经没有地方了。周围很少有悲痛欲绝的亲属来举行葬礼或照料坟墓。 As a result, the rituals surrounding death in Japan are changing. 因此，日本围绕死亡的仪式正在发生变化。 The “balloon funeral” chosen by the Sakashitas was invented in 2011 by a Japanese firm called Balloon Kobo, which has performed 300 such funerals to date. Scattering ashes in the sea or mountains used to be frowned upon, but it has become widely accepted in recent years. Sakashitas 选择的“气球葬礼”是2011年由一家名为“气球 Kobo”的日本公司发明的，迄今为止，该公司已经举办了300场这样的葬礼。过去，人们不赞成把骨灰撒在大海或山上，但近年来，这种做法已被广泛接受。 “Tree burials”, whereby buried ashes are marked by a sapling instead of a gravestone, are even more popular. A survey showed that half of those who purchased graves in 2022 chose tree burial sites. 更受欢迎的是“树葬”，即用树苗代替墓碑来标记埋葬的骨灰。一项调查显示，在2022年购买坟墓的人中，有一半选择了树葬地点。 People are increasingly attracted to the idea of “becoming one with nature”, says Inoue Haruyo of Ending Centre, a non-profit that helps put people in touch with cemeteries. 结束中心(Ending Centre)的井上春代(Inoue Haruyo)表示，人们越来越被“与自然融为一体”的想法所吸引。结束中心是一家帮助人们与墓地建立联系的非营利组织。 The new methods also tend to be much cheaper than purchasing tombstones, which typically cost at least a million yen ($7,200). “Most people don’t want to bother their children after death,” says Onodera Yoshihiro, the president of Balloon Kobo. 新方法也比购买墓碑便宜得多，墓碑通常至少要花费100万日元(7200美元)。“大多数人不想在孩子死后打扰他们,”气球公司总裁小野寺义弘说。 Traditionally, Japanese graves are inherited by a family member, usually the eldest son, who is then charged with paying fees to the temples that manage them. Since the 1990s, as Japan’s birth rate has declined, there has been a growing shortage of such custodians. 传统上，日本人的坟墓由家庭成员继承，通常是长子，然后由长子向管理坟墓的寺庙支付费用。自上世纪90年代以来，随着日本出生率的下降，这类监护人日益短缺。 For many city-dwellers, visiting graves in their or their ancestors’ rural home towns (as is expected during certain holidays, such as obon) is also increasingly troublesome. Many ashes have been dug up and transferred as a result. In 2020 nearly 120,000 graves across Japan were thus “closed”. 对于许多城市居民来说，在他们或他们祖先的农村家乡扫墓(正如某些节日期间所预期的那样，比如 obon)也越来越麻烦。因此，许多骨灰被挖出并转移。2020年，日本各地有近12万座坟墓因此被“关闭”。 Japanese families traditionally hold a days-long wake for their dead relative and then the funeral service. But even where the old ways are hewn to, funerals are shrinking for lack of mourners or funds. 日本家庭传统上会为死去的亲人守灵一整天，然后举行葬礼。但是，即使在传统方式习以为常的地方，由于缺乏哀悼者或资金，葬礼也在减少。 In Japan, where life expectancy is over 80, many outlive their friends and close relatives. Partly to save money, it has become more normal to dispense with formalities such as lavish funeral altars and decorations. 在日本，人均寿命超过80岁，许多人比他们的朋友和近亲更长寿。在一定程度上，为了省钱，人们已经变得更加正常，不再需要那些奢华的葬礼祭坛和装饰品之类的礼节。 “The scale of one death has become smaller and smaller,” says Ms Inoue. Funerals and mourning are increasingly being combined into day-long affairs. Simply cremating bodies without any gathering or funeral rite is also becoming more common. 井上女士说: “一个人死亡的规模已经变得越来越小。”。葬礼和哀悼越来越多地被合并为一天的事务。没有任何集会或葬礼仪式的简单火化尸体也变得越来越普遍。 While funerals are traditionally Buddhist (and marriages Shinto), non-religious funerals are also on the rise. 虽然葬礼传统上是佛教徒的(婚姻也是神道教的) ，但是非宗教的葬礼也在增加。 These disruptions are not only easing the burden on bereaved families. They are also changing the way Japanese think and talk about their impending demise. Selecting from a growing number of funeral options necessitates conversations about death, once a taboo. 这些干扰不仅减轻了失去亲人的家庭的负担。他们也在改变日本人思考和谈论他们即将灭亡的方式。从越来越多的葬礼选择中进行选择，需要讨论死亡，这曾经是一个禁忌。 “Shukatsu”, or “death planning”, a term that combines the words for “end” and “activity”, has entered the lexicon. Beyond funeral preparation it often entails taking greater care over bequests and posthumous instructions than was once the norm. “ Shukatsu”，或“死亡计划”，一个结合了“结束”和“活动”的词汇，已经进入词典。除了准备葬礼之外，通常还需要比以往更加注意遗产和遗嘱。 “When he was alive, my husband said he would like to fly in the sky,” says Sakashita Akiko, Haruhiko’s wife, explaining why the family opted for a balloon funeral. Their son Kohei echoes the sentiment. “We knew what our father wanted, and we wanted to respect that.” ■ 春彦的妻子坂下明子(Sakashita Akiko)在解释全家为何选择气球葬礼时表示: “他活着的时候，我丈夫说他想在天上飞。”。他们的儿子高平对此表示赞同。“我们知道父亲想要什么，我们希望尊重这一点。”￭ This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/asia/2023/05/25/japans-ageing-society-is-finding-creative-ways-to-dispose-of-its-dead 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/asia/2023/05/25/japans-ageing-society-is-finding-creative-ways-to-dispose-of-its-dead 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Banyan 榕树 America is lavishing attention on Pacific island states 美国把大量的注意力放在太平洋岛国身上 Anthony Blinken is the most significant American official to visit Papua New Guinea since General Douglas MacArthur 安东尼•布林肯(Anthony Blinken)是自巴布亚新几内亚以来访问道格拉斯·麦克阿瑟最重要的美国官员 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 T HERE ARE two ways of looking at the security pact that Antony Blinken, America’s secretary of state, signed with the prime minister of Papua New Guinea ( PNG ), James Marape, on May 22nd. The obvious one is as further evidence of the great-power contest between America and China that is playing out in ever-expanding ripples across the Pacific Ocean. 5月22日，美国国务卿安东尼 · 布林肯与巴布亚新几内亚巴布亚新几内亚总理詹姆斯 · 马拉佩签署了安全协议。显而易见的一点是，这进一步证明了美国和中国之间的大国竞争正在太平洋上激起不断扩大的涟漪。 In that contest, America had, by its own reckoning, previously neglected the role of Pacific island states, of which PNG , a diverse land with 10m people, is by far the biggest. 根据美国自己的估算，在那场竞赛中，美国此前忽视了太平洋岛国的作用，其中巴布亚新几内亚是迄今为止最大的一个岛国，该国拥有1000万人口，是一个多样化的国家。 America is now making amends. It opened an embassy in the Solomon Islands and Tonga, and has plans to do so in Kiribati and Vanuatu. While in Port Moresby, PNG’ s capital, Mr Blinken also signed renewed “compacts of free association” with Micronesia and Palau, island states between PNG and America’s dependency of Guam. A renewed compact with the Marshall Islands will follow. These states hand over their defence exclusively to America in return for aid guarantees and other benefits. 美国现在正在做出补偿。它在所罗门群岛和汤加群岛开设了大使馆，并计划在基里巴斯和瓦努阿图开设大使馆。在巴布亚新几内亚首都莫尔兹比港，布林肯先生还与密克罗尼西亚和帕劳签署了新的“自由联合协定”。随之而来的是与马绍尔群岛的新契约。这些国家把自己的国防完全交给美国，以换取援助保障和其他利益。 Yet the pact with PNG, the most striking evidence of renewed American engagement with the region, also represents a significant new opportunity for its island states. President Joe Biden had been due to travel to PNG to sign the pact. America’s fraught internal debt-ceiling negotiations nixed that. Even so, Mr Blinken is surely now the most influential American official to have visited PNG since General Douglas MacArthur. 然而，美国与巴布亚新几内亚签署的协议，是美国重新参与该地区事务的最明显证据，也为巴布亚新几内亚岛国提供了一个重要的新机遇。美国总统乔•拜登(Joe Biden)原定前往巴布亚新几内亚签署该协议。美国令人担忧的国内债务上限谈判否决了这一提议。即便如此，布林肯先生无疑是自道格拉斯·麦克阿瑟以来访问巴布亚新几内亚的最有影响力的美国官员。 The strategic thinking is this: if America is to deter China from invading Taiwan (or if it is to defend Taiwan if China invades), Guam will be a crucial American military base. It is outside the range of many Chinese missiles. 战略思维是这样的: 如果美国要阻止中国入侵台湾(或者如果中国入侵台湾，美国要保卫台湾) ，关岛将是一个关键的美国军事基地。它在许多中国导弹的射程之外。 But Guam’s resilience rests on safe seas and skies around it. Hence the importance of these other island states. In addition, were China to control the waters around PNG, Australia, an important American ally, could be bottled up. Some of the bitterest fighting of the second world war took place in PNG , where MacArthur was supreme allied commander, precisely because of its strategic importance. 但是关岛的恢复能力取决于它周围的安全海域和天空。因此，这些其他岛国的重要性。此外，如果中国控制了巴布亚新几内亚附近的水域，美国的重要盟友澳大利亚可能会受到压制。第二次世界大战中一些最惨烈的战斗发生在巴布亚新几内亚，麦克阿瑟的盟军统帅正是因为其战略重要性。 America’s view of a titanic, superpower contest is officially deplored by China—but shared by it. Its envoy to the Pacific, Qian Bo, accuses America of possessing a “cold-war mentality”, of trying to sabotage China’s relationships, and of being blinded by “ideological prejudice”. 美国认为这是一场巨大的、超级大国之间的较量，中国官方对此表示遗憾，但中国也有同感。中国驻太平洋大使钱波指责美国持有“冷战思维”，试图破坏中美关系，并被“意识形态偏见”蒙蔽了双眼。 Perhaps. But China has a cold-war mentality, too. It vies unbendingly with Taiwan for influence with the Pacific’s smaller states. In 2019 its financial inducements, some of them underhand, persuaded the Solomon Islands and Kiribati to switch diplomatic allegiance. 也许吧。但中国也有冷战思维。它不屈不挠地与台湾争夺太平洋小国的影响力。2019年，它的金融诱惑(其中一些是暗中的)说服所罗门群岛和基里巴斯改变了外交立场。 A security pact that China signed with the Solomons last year led a panicked America to realise how much ground in the region it had lost. 去年，中国与所罗门群岛签署了一项安全协议，这让惊慌失措的美国意识到，自己在该地区失去了多少地盘。 Untrammelled competition between the two big powers is met by some islanders with foreboding (Port Moresby saw protests against the new pact). 两个大国之间不受约束的竞争使得一些岛民有不祥的预感(莫尔兹比港出现了反对新协议的抗议活动)。 The Pacific islands’ history as hapless victims is seared into the collective memory; they were objects of colonial exploitation, then bloody battlefields and finally post-war sites for nuclear tests. 这些太平洋岛屿作为不幸受害者的历史深深烙印在人们的集体记忆中; 它们曾经是殖民剥削的对象，然后是血腥的战场，最后是战后的核试验场。 But for those minded to see the upside for the Pacific states, as well as the risks, in their renewed geopolitical relevance, there are many opportunities to point to. 但是，对于那些希望看到太平洋国家重新获得地缘政治重要性的好处和风险的人来说，有很多机会可以指出。 The island states are no longer victims. It has been years, says John Blaxland of the Australian National University in Canberra, since they enjoyed such favours from outside powers, in terms of high-level visits, offers of investment and other commitments (the $45m Mr Blinken promised impoverished PNG will help it fight transnational crime and climate change). 岛国不再是受害者。堪培拉澳大利亚国立大学(Australian National University)的约翰•布莱克斯兰德(John Blaxland)表示，多年来，他们一直享受着外部势力在高层访问、投资和其它承诺(布林肯先生承诺的4500万美元将帮助贫困的巴布亚新几内亚打击跨国犯罪和气候变化)方面的好处。 Great-power competition can mean growing options. The PNG deal underscores a level of comfort among Pacific states with outsiders that have English, Christianity and history in common with them. Last year they flatly rejected China’s attempts to create a new geopolitical bloc. Yet they are happy to take Chinese investment. 大国之间的竞争可能意味着更多的选择。巴布亚新几内亚的协议强调了太平洋国家对与他们有共同英语、基督教和历史的外来者的一定程度的安慰。去年，他们断然拒绝了中国创建一个新的地缘政治集团的企图。然而，他们乐于接受中国的投资。 Mr Marape says PNG will not stop working with China; indeed it is negotiating a free-trade deal with it. PNG, says Mihai Sora of the Lowy Institute, a Sydney think-tank, is “very deft at compartmentalising relationships”—security and development with America, trade and investment with China. 马拉佩先生说，巴布亚新几内亚不会停止与中国的合作; 事实上，它正在与中国谈判一项自由贸易协定。悉尼智库罗伊研究所(Lowy Institute)的米海•索拉(Mihai Sora)表示，巴布亚新几内亚“非常善于划分关系”，即与美国的安全与发展、与中国的贸易与投资。 Other Pacific states are, too. Seen from a local perspective, the island countries are increasingly binding America and China into a web of Pacific obligations. It could make the region stronger—and, by restraining the adventurism of both powers, perhaps even safer. ■ 其他太平洋国家也是如此。从当地的角度来看，这些岛国正日益将美国和中国捆绑在一个太平洋义务网络中。这可能会让该地区变得更加强大ーー而且，通过抑制这两个大国的冒险主义，或许会让该地区变得更加安全。￭ Read more from Banyan, our columnist on Asia: 阅读更多亚洲专栏作家 Banyan 的文章: Myanmar’s conflict is dividing South-East Asia (May 18th) 缅甸的冲突正在分裂东南亚(5月18日) A winner has emerged in the old rivalry between Singapore and Hong Kong (May 11th) 新加坡和香港(5月11日)之间的老对手已经出现了一个赢家 America’s closest Indo-Pacific allies are cosying up (May 4th) 美国最亲密的印度洋-太平洋盟友正在讨好(5月4日) Also: How the Banyan column got its name 还有: 榕树一栏是如何得名的 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/asia/2023/05/25/america-is-lavishing-attention-on-pacific-island-states 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/asia/2023/05/25/america-is-lavishing-attention-on-pacific-island-states 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 A toe-hold in Europe 在欧洲站稳脚跟 Hungary is becoming more important to China 匈牙利对中国越来越重要 Viktor Orban and Xi Jinping bond over their anti-Americanism 维克托•欧尔班(Viktor Orban)和习近平因反美而建立了友好关系 May 24th 2023 | BEIJING AND BUDAPEST 2023年5月24日 | 北京和布达佩斯 T O EARS ACCUSTOMED to a swelling chorus of China-scepticism in the European Union, the language of Hungarian diplomats is striking. Not for them the common talk of European officials about the need to “de-risk” relations with China and to treat it as a “systemic rival”. 对于欧盟内部日益高涨的对中国的怀疑声音，匈牙利外交官的语言已经习以为常。对他们来说，欧洲官员的共同话题不是有必要“降低”与中国关系的风险，并将中国视为“系统性竞争对手”。 Co-operation between Hungary and China presents “opportunities rather than risks”, said Hungary’s foreign minister, Peter Szijjarto, in Beijing on May 15th. 匈牙利外交部长彼得 · 西贾托5月15日在北京表示，匈牙利与中国的合作带来的是“机遇而非风险”。 Wang Yi, China’s foreign-affairs overlord, told him that relations between the countries had entered their “best period in history”. 中国外交大臣王毅告诉他，两国关系已进入“历史上最好的时期”。 As China surveys the grim landscape of its foreign policy in Europe, battered by differences over matters ranging from the war in Ukraine to human rights abuses in Xinjiang and China’s muscle-flexing around Taiwan, Hungary’s dogged friendliness towards China—and Russia—stands out. 从乌克兰战争到新疆侵犯人权，再到中国在台湾问题上炫耀武力，中国在欧洲的外交政策面临严峻考验，而匈牙利对中国和俄罗斯的顽固友好态度尤为突出。 Hungary echoes China’s view that Western support for Ukraine is merely fuelling the conflict at Europe’s expense. Speaking to Bloomberg on May 23rd, Hungary’s prime minister, Viktor Orban, defended his blocking of a $540m package of EU financial aid to Ukraine. “There’s no chance to win this war,” he said. 匈牙利赞同中国的观点，认为西方对乌克兰的支持只是以牺牲欧洲利益为代价来加剧冲突。5月23日，匈牙利总理维克多 · 欧尔班在接受彭博社采访时为自己阻止欧盟向乌克兰提供5.4亿美元的财政援助进行了辩护。“没有机会赢得这场战争,”他说。 Other countries in eastern Europe, such as Poland, had shared Hungary’s fondness for close ties with China. 其他东欧国家，如波兰，也和匈牙利一样喜欢与中国保持密切关系。 But their enthusiasm has been dented by China’s support for Russia during the Ukrainian war, and a sense that the business rewards of embracing China politically have not been as great as expected. 但乌克兰战争期间中国对俄罗斯的支持，以及在政治上拥抱中国的商业回报并不像预期的那么高的感觉，削弱了他们的热情。 China sees good uses in friendship with Europe. The EU is a vital trading partner. Moreover the bloc could, if it wished, soften the impact of America’s rivalry with China by taking a less security-focused approach. Officials in Beijing are alarmed by a recent trend—seen not least in critical joint statements at a G 7 meeting in Tokyo on May 21st—towards greater transatlantic co-operation on China, notwithstanding differences among EU members over how far this should go. During a trip to China last month France’s president, Emmanuel Macron, warned that European countries should not become “just America’s followers” regarding Taiwan. 中国认为与欧洲的友谊很有用。欧盟是一个重要的贸易伙伴。此外，如果欧盟愿意，它可以采取不那么注重安全的做法，从而减轻美国与中国竞争的影响。北京的官员们对最近的趋势感到震惊，尤其是在5月21日于东京举行的七国集团(G7)会议上发表的批评性联合声明中，尽管欧盟成员国在这方面应走多远存在分歧。法国总统埃马纽埃尔•马克龙(Emmanuel Macron)上月访华期间警告称，欧洲国家不应成为台湾问题上“美国的追随者”。 But Hungary is special. The worldviews of Mr Orban and of China’s leader, Xi Jinping, have much in common. They are both authoritarians (albeit to different degrees), who bridle at American power but believe the West is in decline. They are both fond of Russia. 但匈牙利很特别。欧尔班和中国领导人习近平的世界观有很多共同点。他们都是威权主义者(尽管程度不同) ，对美国的实力不满，但认为西方正在衰落。他们都喜欢俄罗斯。 Mr Orban’s friends in the business world stand to benefit from deals with authoritarian regimes. 欧尔班先生在商界的朋友们将从与专制政权的交易中获益。 Hungary is well-placed to serve China’s foreign-policy interests. As a member of the EU it has a veto over foreign and security policy. It has used it to dilute or scupper EU statements critical of China. It is also a member of NATO , an alliance that China views with growing misgivings. Hungary has shown occasional willingness to thumb its nose there, too, for instance in its recent support for Turkey in blocking Sweden’s accession. 匈牙利完全有能力为中国的外交政策利益服务。作为欧盟成员国，它对外交和安全政策拥有否决权。它用它来淡化或破坏欧盟批评中国的声明。中国也是北约成员国，中国对北约的看法越来越疑虑重重。匈牙利也偶尔表现出对此嗤之以鼻的意愿，例如最近它支持土耳其阻止瑞典加入欧盟。 So, as China sees it, a country of fewer than 10m people and one of the poorest in the EU has an outsize role to play. This was evident in February when Mr Wang went on his first overseas tour since taking over as China’s foreign-affairs chief (state media confirmed his promotion in January; it means he outranks Qin Gang, the foreign minister). 因此，在中国看来，一个人口不到1000万、是欧盟最贫穷国家之一的国家，需要发挥巨大的作用。今年2月，王先生出任中国外交部长以来的首次海外访问(官方媒体在1月份证实了他的晋升; 这意味着他的级别高于外交部长秦刚) ，这一点就很明显。 He visited France, Italy and then Germany for the Munich Security Conference of global foreign-policy elites. Before heading to Moscow, he stopped in Hungary where he praised the country’s “China-friendly policy”, according to Hungarian media. 他先后访问了法国、意大利和德国，参加由全球外交政策精英组成的慕尼黑安全会议。据匈牙利媒体报道，在前往莫斯科之前，他访问了匈牙利，赞扬了匈牙利的“对华友好政策”。 Mr Szijjarto thanked him for China’s “absolutely indispensable” support during the covid-19 pandemic, including the supply of Chinese-made vaccines (which Hungary used in defiance of an EU consensus that covid vaccines deployed in the bloc should first gain EU approval). Szijjarto 先生感谢中国在2019冠状病毒疾病大流行期间提供的“绝对不可或缺”的支持，包括中国制造的疫苗(匈牙利无视欧盟关于在欧盟部署的冠状病毒疾病疫苗应首先获得欧盟批准的共识，使用了中国制造的疫苗)。 In the past couple of years, it might have been expected that events in Hungary would give China pause for thought. Mr Orban, who has ruled the country since 2010 and emerged even stronger after another victory in general elections last year, remains a dependable friend. 在过去几年里，人们或许曾预料到，匈牙利发生的事件会让中国停下来思考一下。欧尔班先生自2010年以来一直统治着这个国家，并在去年的大选中再次获胜后变得更加强大，他仍然是一位可靠的朋友。 Polls suggest that Hungarians are among the people most supportive of China within the EU (it helps that the country’s mostly pro-government media stifle China-sceptic views). But China-related protests in Budapest in 2021 and recently in Debrecen, the country’s second-largest city, have raised questions about how enduring this sentiment will prove. 民意调查显示，匈牙利是欧盟内部最支持中国的国家之一(这有助于该国主要亲政府的媒体压制对中国持怀疑态度的观点)。但是，2021年在布达佩斯以及最近在中国第二大城市德布勒森发生的与中国有关的抗议活动，引发了人们对这种情绪将持续多久的质疑。 A survey published last year by Pew Research Centre, an American polling outfit, found that 52% of respondents in Hungary held negative views of China, a rise of 15 points since 2019. 美国民调机构皮尤研究中心(Pew Research Centre)去年发布的一项调查显示，匈牙利52% 的受访者对中国持负面看法，比2019年上升了15个百分点。 South of Budapest’s city centre, in the semi-wasteland of an abandoned logistics hub, street signs recall the unrest in the capital two years ago that brought anxieties about China—at least briefly—into public debate. 在布达佩斯市中心以南，一个废弃物流中心的半荒地上，街道上的路标让人想起两年前首都发生的骚乱，那场骚乱使人们对中国的担忧(至少短暂地)引发了公开辩论。 The names on them are pokes at China: “Uyghur Martyrs Street” and “Bishop Hszie Si-kuang Street” (after the late Xie Shiguang, a Catholic, imprisoned for 30 years). 上面的名字是对中国的讽刺: “维吾尔烈士街”和“谢主教四光街”(以已故天主教徒谢世光入狱三十年而命名)。 In 2021 the mayor of Budapest, Gergely Karacsony, gave the roads these new labels (also a Dalai Lama Street and a Free Hong Kong Street) in protest against a plan to build a campus of Shanghai’s Fudan University on the nearby land. 2021年，布达佩斯市长格尔吉利 · 卡拉克索尼(Gergely Karacsony)给道路贴上了这些新标签(还有达赖喇嘛街和自由香港街) ，以抗议在附近土地上修建上海复旦大学校园的计划。 Leaked official documents showed that construction would cost $1.8bn, with 80% of the money coming from a Chinese loan. 泄露的官方文件显示，建设将耗资18亿美元，其中80% 来自中国贷款。 Opposition position 反对派 The Fudan project helped to galvanise the government’s critics in Budapest, an opposition stronghold. Three days after the renaming of the streets, thousands joined a rally to denounce the campus plan. 复旦工程有助于激起反对派据点布达佩斯对政府的批评。街道重新命名三天后，数千人参加了一个集会，谴责校园计划。 Complaints about it ranged from possible stifling of academic debate to the loss of land that had been earmarked for subsidised student housing. 有关这一政策的抱怨从可能扼杀学术辩论到失去原本用于资助学生住房的土地不一而足。 Mr Orban appeared to back down, agreeing to a referendum on the plan. But after his re-election in 2022 the Constitutional Court said such a ballot could not be held because it would involve an international agreement. It is unclear, however, whether the project will proceed. 欧尔班先生似乎做出了让步，同意就该计划举行全民公决。但在2022年他再次当选后，宪法法院表示，这样的投票不能举行，因为它将涉及一项国际协议。然而，目前还不清楚该项目是否会继续进行。 Some analysts in Budapest say China, alarmed by public anger, may have got cold feet. 布达佩斯的一些分析人士表示，中国可能因为公众的愤怒而临阵退缩。 Fractured after last year’s election, the opposition shows little sign of trying to revive public interest. But in the past few months in Debrecen, the government’s own supporters have joined a campaign that still simmers against another Chinese project. 去年大选后，反对党四分五裂，几乎没有显示出试图恢复公众利益的迹象。但过去几个月，在德布勒森，政府自己的支持者加入了一场反对另一个中国项目的运动。 It involves a plan, announced in August by CATL , a Chinese battery giant, to build a factory just outside the city in the village of Mikepercs at a cost of around $7.7bn. It would make batteries for electric vehicles ( EV s), the largest such facility in Europe. Hungary’s efforts to develop its EV industry have attracted others, too. Last year BMW , a German firm, began building a $2bn EV and battery factory in Debrecen. This month another Chinese firm, EVE Energy, said it would set up a $1.2bn battery factory to supply BMW ’s plant. 中国电池巨头宁德时代科技集团(CATL)今年8月宣布，计划斥资约77亿美元，在 Mikepercs 郊外建设一家工厂。该公司将为电动汽车制造电池，这是欧洲最大的电动汽车制造厂。匈牙利发展电动汽车工业的努力也吸引了其他国家。去年，德国公司宝马(BMW)开始在德布勒森建造一家价值20亿美元的电动汽车和电池工厂。本月，另一家中国公司 EVE Energy 表示，将投资12亿美元建立一家电池厂，为宝马工厂供货。 Some residents worry about the potential impact of the CATL project on the local environment and the area’s scarce water resources. They have staged protests and heckled local officials at public hearings. 一些居民担心 CATL 项目对当地环境和该地区稀缺水资源的潜在影响。他们举行了抗议活动，并在公开听证会上质问当地官员。 “Opening up to the East is a mistake,” says one activist, a 60-year-old retired medic, referring to Mr Orban’s policy of promoting business with China and other Asian countries. “Because Hungarian culture is European culture.” “向东方开放是一个错误,”一位60岁的退休医生说，他指的是欧尔班先生促进与中国和其他亚洲国家商业往来的政策。“因为匈牙利文化就是欧洲文化” In Budapest, however, opposition supporters say they doubt the protests in Debrecen will pose a challenge to Mr Orban or to China’s government. The media give little coverage, they say. Any difficulty for his party, Fidesz, will remain largely local. 然而，在布达佩斯，反对派支持者表示，他们怀疑德布勒森的抗议活动会对欧尔班先生或中国政府构成挑战。他们说，媒体很少报道。他的政党青年民主联盟(Fidesz)面临的任何困难都将主要局限于本地。 Opposition politicians have attacked another multi-billion-dollar endeavour involving Chinese firms: a new rail link between Budapest and Belgrade in neighbouring Serbia (work on the Hungarian side began in 2021). 反对派政治家抨击了另一项涉及中国公司的数十亿美元计划: 一条连接布达佩斯和邻国塞尔维亚的贝尔格莱德的新铁路(匈牙利方面的工作于2021年开始)。 They say it is overpriced and worry about favouritism in awarding contracts. But China regards the project as a highlight of its belt-and-road building in Europe, for which Mr Orban is a cheerleader. 他们表示，该公司定价过高，并担心在授予合同时存在偏袒。但中国将该项目视为其在欧洲“一带一路”建设的一大亮点，而欧尔班先生是这方面的啦啦队长。 For all the shifts in European attitudes to China, it is likely that Mr Xi will make few adjustments. Despite Mr Orban’s political isolation in Europe, he shows no sign of distancing himself from China. As long as he remains in power, Hungary will be a useful ally. 尽管欧洲对中国的态度发生了种种转变，但习可能不会做出什么调整。尽管欧尔班先生在欧洲受到政治孤立，但他没有表现出与中国疏远的迹象。只要他继续掌权，匈牙利将是一个有用的盟友。 “Studying its position on China could offer lessons for developing good relations between China and other European countries,” wrote two Chinese scholars in a paper published this year in a Communist Party journal. 两名中国学者在今年发表于中共党刊的一篇论文中写道: “研究中国的立场，可以为发展中国与其他欧洲国家的良好关系提供借鉴。”。 One obvious lesson would be that pro-Russian, illiberal governments make solid friends. China’s options in Europe are narrowing. ■ 一个显而易见的教训是，亲俄、不自由的政府会结交可靠的朋友。中国在欧洲的选择范围正在缩小。 ￭ Subscribers can sign up to Drum Tower , our new weekly newsletter, to understand what the world makes of China—and what China makes of the world. 订阅者可以在我们新的每周通讯《鼓楼》(Drum Tower)上注册，了解世界如何看待中国ーー以及中国如何看待世界。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/china/2023/05/24/hungary-is-becoming-more-important-to-china 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/china/2023/05/24/hungary-is-becoming-more-important-to-china 下载的 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Surfin’ again 再次冲浪 Small-town Chinese officials are making money with music festivals 中国小城镇的官员通过音乐节赚钱 And exhausted urban youngsters are flocking to them 疲惫不堪的城市年轻人蜂拥而至 May 25th 2023 | BEIJING 2023年5月25日 | 北京 L OCKDOWN WAS brutal. But nature is healing. And, it turns out, nature is also part of the healing. As frazzled urbanites emerge from their pandemic hibernaculum into a grim economy, some want to venture to the great outdoors. 封锁很残酷。但大自然在治愈。事实证明，自然也是治愈的一部分。当疲惫不堪的城市居民从他们的大流行的冬眠地进入一个严峻的经济时，一些人想要冒险到大户外去。 Small cities and rural areas are obliging by putting on funky new music festivals where the youth can dance and hug and relax once again. 小城市和农村地区正在举办时髦的新音乐节，让年轻人可以再次跳舞、拥抱和放松。 Music festivals were a growing business when covid struck. China’s Woodstock moment had happened back in 2000, when the first festival emerged at Midi, a renowned music school in a Beijing suburb. 当冠状病毒疾病来袭时，音乐节的生意越来越红火。中国的伍德斯托克音乐节要追溯到2000年，当时第一届音乐节在北京郊区的著名音乐学校迷笛举行。 Covering a range of musical tastes from grunge to metal to punk, they grew in number to more than 250 in 2019. So far this year alone, 150 have been announced. 涵盖了从垃圾摇滚到金属摇滚到朋克的音乐品味，2019年这一数字增长到了250多种。仅今年迄今为止，已经宣布了150个。 A perfect example took place in mid-April at a remote wetland park in the southwestern province of Sichuan. Some 5,000 revellers gathered outside the city of Meishan for the Chunyou Festival ( chun you means “spring outing”). Sparkling lakes dot the landscape between green hills and idyllic forests not far from Sichuan’s famous panda sanctuaries. A fifth of those attending came from outside Sichuan, many from Shanghai and Beijing. 一个完美的例子发生在四月中旬的四川省西南部的一个偏远的湿地公园。大约5000名狂欢者聚集在梅山乡城外庆祝春游(春游的意思是春游)。绿色的小山和田园诗般的森林之间点缀着波光粼粼的湖泊，距离四川著名的熊猫保护区不远。五分之一的与会者来自四川以外地区，其中许多人来自上海和北京。 Many discounted tickets also went to locals, some of whom just came to watch the city folks boogie. With everyone dressed up in boho chic, it felt like a small-scale Coachella, without the weed. 许多打折票也流向了当地人，他们中的一些人只是来看城市居民跳舞。每个人都穿着波西米亚风格的衣服，感觉就像一个没有大麻的小型科切拉。 Many urban hipsters have been to such festivals this spring. 今年春天，许多都市潮人都参加了这样的节日。 Others have friends who have just returned from a forest near a town no one had ever heard of before, where they talked about love and peace and forgot about parental pressure to find a spouse and the ”996” grind (9am till 9pm, six days a week). 还有些人的朋友刚从一个从未听说过的小镇附近的森林里回来，在那里他们谈论着爱与和平，忘记了父母寻找配偶的压力和“996”的折磨(每周六天，上午9点到晚上9点)。 Seeing their popularity, another struggling tribe has looked to cash in. Local officials also had a tough lockdown, exhausting their budgets chasing down every single case of covid, while watching their income slip away. 看到他们的受欢迎程度，另一个苦苦挣扎的部落已经开始赚钱了。地方官员也面临严格的封锁，他们在追查每一宗冠状病毒疾病的同时，眼睁睁地看着自己的收入流失，耗尽了自己的预算。 Now, realising that anxious urban youths will pay good money to loiter near a yoga tent drinking craft beer or mingle at a Tibetan tea area, officials are scrambling to compete for tourists, and looking to build their small-town brand by getting the cool kids posting online. 现在，意识到焦虑的城市年轻人会花大价钱在瑜伽帐篷附近闲逛，喝着精酿啤酒，或者在藏族茶区混在一起，官员们争先恐后地争夺游客，希望通过让酷孩子们在网上发帖来打造他们的小镇品牌。 Type #musicfestivals into Xiaohongshu, a social-media app, and you get 2m hits. Chunyou was part of a series of culture- and tourism-awareness programmes for Renshou county, where the wetland park is located. 在社交媒体应用“ Xiaohongshu 音乐节”上输入 # ，点击量就会达到200万。春游是湿地公园所在地仁寿县一系列文化和旅游宣传活动的一部分。 A local newspaper said the number of tourists visiting the park increased 50% from the last festival two years ago. 当地一家报纸说，参观公园的游客人数比两年前的上一个节日增加了50% 。 During the May Day holiday this year, more than 40 music festivals were held across 19 provinces and regions, with many of those in lower-tier cities. 今年的五一假期，全国19个省区共举办了40多个音乐节，其中许多都是在中小城市举办的。 Zhuji, a small city two hours from Shanghai, reported that tourists spent 450m yuan ($64m) the weekend it launched its first-ever music festival, including an increase of 365% on hotels and restaurants from the previous weekend. 距离上海两小时车程的小城市诸暨市报告称，该市首次举办音乐节，周末游客消费4.5亿元人民币(合6400万美元) ，其中酒店和餐厅消费较上周末增长了365% 。 Yantai, a coastal city in Shandong province where this year’s Midi festival was held, said 340,000 tourists visited and spent 200m yuan. In the midst of a national economic slowdown, Zhuji and Yantai have both been struggling with debts. 烟台是山东省的一个沿海城市，今年的迷笛节就是在这里举行的。在国民经济放缓之际，诸暨市和烟台都在艰难应对债务问题。 “Music festivals have ‘brought fire’ to us,” claimed the Zhuji government on WeChat, a social-media platform, using the Chinese phrase for “going viral”. “音乐节给我们带来了火灾,”诸暨市政府在社交媒体平台微信上表示。 Chunyou “is a much-needed trip for me after living in Shanghai for a while,” says Wency Chen, who works in communications. Several of her friends had flown to Sichuan too. A woman in her 30s, who calls herself Peach, drove two hours with her brother to get there. 在上海生活了一段时间后，春友“对我来说是一次非常必要的旅行,”从事传播工作的温西 · 陈(WencyChen)说。她的几个朋友也飞到四川去了。一个自称 Peach 的30多岁的女人，和她的哥哥开了两个小时的车到了那里。 The whole scene made her feel “happy and liberated”, she said. 她说，整个场景让她感到“快乐和自由”。 The music is not bad, either. China’s live music scene has evolved a lot, and Chunyou featured a lot of ethnic-minority tunes, played by artists such as Kawa, a reggae group from Yunnan province, and Ts.Bayandalai, an ethnic Mongolian musician. And, in spite of a crackdown on many areas of urban cultural life (see next story), there is still a sense of freedom in the music world. It may not equal the hedonism of some Western gigs, but the festivals are among the few places where rainbow flags can be waved. The organisers are keen to keep a small-town vibe. “We don’t want to become a giant festival,” says one. “We just want people to have fun.” ■ 音乐也不错。中国的现场音乐演出已经发生了很大的变化，春游以很多少数民族的曲调为特色，由来自云南的雷鬼乐队 Kawa 和 Ts 等艺术家演奏。巴音达莱，蒙古族音乐家。而且，尽管对城市文化生活的许多领域进行了打压(见下一个故事) ，音乐世界仍然有一种自由的感觉。它可能不等于一些西方音乐会的享乐主义，但是这些节日是少数几个可以挥舞彩虹旗的地方。组织者希望保持小镇的氛围。“我们不想成为一个盛大的节日,”其中一人表示。“我们只是想让人们玩得开心。”￭ Subscribers can sign up to Drum Tower , our new weekly newsletter, to understand what the world makes of China—and what China makes of the world. 订阅者可以在我们新的每周通讯《鼓楼》(Drum Tower)上注册，了解世界如何看待中国ーー以及中国如何看待世界。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/china/2023/05/25/small-town-chinese-officials-are-making-money-with-music-festivals 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/china/2023/05/25/small-town-chinese-officials-are-making-money-with-music-festivals 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Dog’s dinner 狗的晚餐 China’s cancel culture is nationalist, not woke 中国的取消文化是民族主义的，而不是觉醒的 One comedian finds himself unwittingly on the end of it 一个喜剧演员发现自己不知不觉地走到了最后 May 25th 2023 | BEIJING 2023年5月25日 | 北京 D OG JOKES are usually fairly inoffensive. The audience laughed when Li Haoshi, a Beijing-based comedian, wisecracked at a show on May 13th about seeing his two dogs chasing a squirrel. The dogs’ zeal, he said, reminded him of military slogans associated with the People’s Liberation Army ( PLA ): “Forge exemplary conduct! Fight to win!” The slogans have become something of a motto for the armed forces (and are also favourites of President Xi Jinping). The incident may have ended Mr Li ‘s career. 狗的笑话通常是相当无害的。5月13日，北京喜剧演员李浩石在一场演出中开玩笑说，他的两只狗在追一只松鼠，观众们都笑了。他说，这些狗的热情让他想起了与中国人民解放军(PLA)有关的军事口号: “树立模范行为！为胜利而战!”这些口号已成为军队的座右铭(也是习近平主席的最爱)。这一事件可能结束了李先生的职业生涯。 His joke was recorded and posted on Weibo, a social-media platform. Nationalists claimed he was comparing the PLA to dogs. Outrage built. Hashtags about the joke received over a billion views. Within days Beijing’s police had started an investigation. They have not said what Mr Li will be charged with, but he has already been banned from performing. 他的笑话被记录下来并发布在社交媒体平台微博上。民族主义者声称他把解放军比作狗。愤怒建立。关于这个笑话的标签有超过十亿的点击量。几天之内，北京警方就展开了调查。他们没有说李先生将被指控什么罪名，但是他已经被禁止表演了。 The media company he worked for was fined $2m for “wantonly slandering the glorious image of the PLA ”. Its line-up of shows was cancelled. 他供职的这家媒体公司因“肆意诋毁中国人民解放军的光荣形象”而被罚款200万美元。它的演出阵容被取消了。 Since the incident, several other events have also been called off with little explanation. On May 20th, an open-air concert in Beijing (aptly named “What the Folkstival”) was about to begin when police arrived to shut it down. 事件发生后，几个其他事件也被取消，几乎没有任何解释。5月20日，一场在北京举行的露天音乐会(恰如其分地命名为“ What the Folkstival”)即将开始，警方赶到将其关闭。 A Japanese band’s concert in the southern city of Guangzhou was also cancelled. 一支日本乐队在南方城市广州举行的音乐会也被取消。 The uproar around Mr Li’s joke sounds absurd. But it is just the latest example of a toxic loop between China’s angry nationalists and its paranoid authorities. Someone unwittingly insults China. Bloggers denounce the mistake. State-run media weigh in to attack the offender. 围绕李先生笑话的喧嚣听起来很荒谬。但这只是中国愤怒的民族主义者与偏执的当局之间一个有毒循环的最新例子。有人无意中侮辱了中国。博主们谴责这个错误。国营媒体介入攻击罪犯。 Authorities punish them. Tougher standards are set for what is politically acceptable , squeezing China’s already cramped space for self-expression. 当局会惩罚他们。对于政治上可以接受的东西，中国设定了更严格的标准，从而挤压了中国本已狭窄的自我表达空间。 Several well-known figures have fallen victim to this cycle in recent years. In 2022 Luo Changping, a former investigative reporter, was sentenced to seven months in jail for insulting “heroes and martyrs”. 近年来，一些知名人士已经成为这种恶性循环的牺牲品。2022年，前调查记者罗昌平因侮辱“英雄烈士”被判入狱7个月。 He had used a pun on social media to suggest that Chinese soldiers who fought in the Korean war were stupid. 他在社交媒体上用了一个双关语，暗示参加过朝鲜战争的中国士兵是愚蠢的。 The year before, Zhang Zhehan, an actor, was purged from China’s film industry after a photo emerged of him standing outside the Yasukuni shrine in Tokyo, where war criminals involved with Japan’s invasion of China are commemorated. 此前一年，演员张哲汉在东京的靖国神社外站立的照片曝光后，他被从中国电影行业开除。靖国神社供奉着参与日本侵华战争的战犯。 Mr Zhang said he was just there to see cherry blossoms. 张先生说他只是去看樱花的。 The chilling effect is particularly visible in the entertainment industry. Comedians urge audience members not to record their shows. Performing abroad is not safe either. 这种寒蝉效应在娱乐业尤为明显。喜剧演员呼吁观众不要录制他们的表演。出国演出也不安全。 Wang Yuechi, a comedian who toured North America this year, has been deleted from Chinese social-media platforms because of politically sensitive material in his shows there. Wary venues ask performers to submit their jokes or songs in advance for approval. 今年曾在北美巡回演出的喜剧演员王月池，由于在中国的演出中涉及政治敏感内容，已被从中国的社交媒体平台上删除。谨慎的场所要求表演者提前提交他们的笑话或歌曲以供审批。 Industry associations urge actors and musicians to be more patriotic. 行业协会敦促演员和音乐家更爱国。 In response, performers self-censor and pledge to spread “positive energy”. But the government still keeps a close eye. One Shanghai district sends volunteers to theatres.
