Sam Zell 接下来会投资什么地产？ Zell-side analysis :: One of America’s mightiest property tycoons died on May 19th, aged 81 Zell-side analysis: 美国最有权势的地产大亨之一于5月19日去世，享年81岁 Meta gets whacked with a €1.2bn penalty Meta 被罚款12亿欧元 Fine and dandy :: It is the biggest punishment yet for breaching the EU’s privacy rules 这是迄今为止对违反欧盟隐私规定的最大惩罚。 Why are corporate retreats so extravagant? 为什么公司的休假如此奢侈？ Bartleby :: Companies’ summer camps get unnecessarily creative 巴特比: 公司的夏令营变得毫无必要地富有创造性 The tech giants have an interest in AI regulation 科技巨头对人工智能监管很感兴趣 Schumpeter :: It is a way of holding back open-source proliferation 熊彼特: 这是阻止开放源码扩散的一种方式 International 国际的 Sections 部分 Finance & economics 金融与经济

Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 A giant sucking sound 巨大的吸吮声 Can carbon removal become a trillion-dollar business? 碳移除能否成为一项价值万亿美元的业务？ Quite possibly—and not before time 很有可能，而且不是在时间到之前 May 21st 2023 | Notrees 2023年5月21日 “T ODAY WE SEE the birth of a new species,” declared Julio Friedmann, gazing across the bleak landscape. Along with several hundred grandees, the energy technologist had travelled to Notrees, a remote corner of the Texas oil patch, in late April. “今天我们看到了一个新物种的诞生,”胡里奥 · 弗里德曼凝视着这片荒凉的土地宣布。4月下旬，这位能源技术专家与数百名贵族一起前往德克萨斯州石油开采区的偏远角落诺特里什(Notree)。 He was invited by 1PointFive, an arm of Occidental Petroleum, an American oil firm, and of Carbon Engineering, a Canadian startup backed by Bill Gates. The species in question is in some ways akin to a tree—but not the botanical sort, nowhere to be seen on the barren terrain. 他受到了美国石油公司西方石油公司公司1Pointfive 和比尔•盖茨支持的加拿大初创企业 Carbon Engineering 的邀请。这个物种在某些方面类似于一棵树，但不是植物学上的那种，在这片贫瘠的土地上是看不到的。 Rather, it is an arboreal artifice: the world’s first commercial-scale “direct air capture” ( DAC ) plant. 更确切地说，这是一个树上的策略: 世界上第一个商业规模的“直接空气捕捉”(DAC)工厂。 Like a tree, DAC sucks carbon dioxide from the air, concentrates it and makes it available for some use. In the natural case, that use is creating organic molecules through photosynthesis. For DAC , it can be things for which humans already use CO 2 , like adding fizz to drinks, spurring plant growth in greenhouses or, in Occidental’s case, injecting it into oilfields to squeeze more drops of crude from the deposits. DAC 就像一棵树，从空气中吸收二氧化碳，并将其浓缩起来，供人们使用。在自然情况下，这种用途是通过光合作用产生有机分子。对于 DAC 来说，它可以是人类已经使用 CO 2 的东西，比如在饮料中加入汽水，刺激温室中植物的生长，或者，在 Occidental 的例子中，将其注入油田以从沉积物中榨取更多的原油。 Yet some of the 500,000 tonnes of CO 2 that the Notrees plant will capture annually, once fully operational in 2025, will be pumped beneath the plains in the service of a grander goal: fighting climate change. For unlike the carbon stored in biological plants, which can be released when they are cut down or burned, CO 2 artificially sequestered may well stay sequestered indefinitely. Companies that want to net out some of their own carbon emissions but do not trust biology-based offsets will pay the project’s managers per stashed tonne. 然而，诺特里斯核电站每年将捕获50万吨二氧化碳，到2025年全面投入使用后，其中一部分将被抽到平原地下，以实现一个更宏伟的目标: 应对气候变化。因为与生物植物中储存的碳不同，生物植物在被砍伐或燃烧时会释放出来，人工隔离的 CO 2 很可能会无限期地保持隔离状态。那些希望减少部分自身碳排放量、但不相信基于生物学的碳补偿的公司，将按每储存一吨碳向项目经理支付报酬。 That makes the Notrees launch the green shoot of something else, too: a real industry. 这也使得诺特里夫妇开启了另一个新的绿芽: 一个真正的产业。 Carbon Engineering and its rivals, like Climeworks , a Swiss firm, Global Thermostat, a Californian one, and myriad startups worldwide, are attracting capital. Occidental plans to build 100 large-scale DAC facilities by 2035. Others are trying to mop up CO 2 produced by power plants and industrial processes before it enters the atmosphere, an approach known as carbon capture and storage ( CCS ). In April ExxonMobil unveiled plans for its newish low-carbon division, whose long-term goal is to offer such decarbonisation as a service for industrial customers in sectors, like steel and cement, where emissions are otherwise hard to abate. 碳工程公司及其竞争对手，如瑞士的 Climeworks 公司、加州的 Global Thermostat 公司以及世界各地的众多初创公司，正在吸引资本。西方计划到2035年建造100个大型发援会设施。其他人则试图在发电厂和工业生产过程产生的 CO 2 进入大气之前将其清除，这种方法被称为碳捕获和储存(CCS)。今年4月，埃克森美孚(ExxonMobil)公布了其新成立的低碳部门的计划，该部门的长期目标是为钢铁和水泥等行业的工业客户提供此类脱碳服务，否则这些行业的排放将难以减少。 The oil giant thinks this sector could be raking in annual revenues of $6trn globally by 2050. 这家石油巨头认为，到2050年，该行业在全球的年收入可能达到6万亿美元。 The boom in carbon removal, whether from the air or from industrial point sources, cannot come fast enough. The UN -backed Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change assumes that if Earth is to have a chance of warming by less than 2°C above pre-industrial levels, renewables, electric vehicles and other emissions reductions are not enough. CCS and sources of “negative emissions” such as DAC must play a part. The Department of Energy calculates that America’s climate targets require capturing and storing between 400m and 1.8bn tonnes of CO 2 annually by 2050, up from 20m tonnes today. Wood Mackenzie, an energy consultancy, reckons various forms of carbon removal account for a fifth of the global emissions reductions needed to emit no net greenhouse gases by 2050. If Wood Mackenzie is right, this would be equivalent to sucking up more than 8bn tonnes of CO 2 annually. That means an awful lot of industrial-scale carbon-removal ventures (see chart 1). 无论是从空气中还是从工业点源中，碳去除的繁荣都来得不够快。联合国支持的政府间气候变化专门委员会认为，如果地球有机会比工业革命前的水平升温不到2摄氏度，可再生能源、电动汽车和其他减排措施是不够的。CCS 和 DAC 等“负排放”来源必须发挥作用。根据美国能源部(Department of Energy)的计算，到2050年，美国的气候目标要求每年捕获和储存4亿至18亿吨二氧化碳，高于目前的2000万吨。能源咨询公司 Wood Mackenzie 认为，各种形式的碳清除占到到2050年实现无温室气体净排放所需全球减排量的五分之一。如果 Wood Mackenzie 是对的，这相当于每年吸收逾80亿吨 CO 2 。这意味着大量工业规模的碳清除风险投资(见表1)。 For years such projects were regarded as technically plausible, perhaps, but uneconomical. An influential estimate by the American Physical Society in 2011 put the cost of DAC at $600 per tonne of CO 2 captured. By comparison, permits to emit one tonne trade at around $100 in the EU ’s emissions-trading system. CCS has been a perennial disappointment. Simon Flowers of Wood Mackenzie says the power sector has spent some $10bn over the years trying to get it to work, without much success. 多年来，这样的项目被认为在技术上是可行的，也许，但不经济。美国物理学会在2011年的一项有影响力的估计显示，DAC 的成本为每吨捕获的 CO 2 600美元。相比之下，在欧盟的排放交易体系中，每吨排放量的许可价格在100欧元左右。CCS 一直令人失望。Wood Mackenzie 的西蒙•弗劳尔斯(Simon Flowers)表示，多年来，电力行业花费了大约100亿美元，试图让它运转起来，但没有取得多大成功。 Backers of the new crop of carbon-removal projects think this time is different. One reason for their optimism is better and, crucially, cheaper technology (see chart 2). The cost of sequestering a tonne of CO 2 beneath Notrees has not been disclosed, but a paper from 2018 published in the journal Joule put the price tag for Carbon Engineering’s DAC system at between $94 and $232 per tonne when operating at scale. That is much less than $600, and not a world away from the EU ’s carbon price. 新一轮碳移除项目的支持者认为这次情况有所不同。他们乐观的一个原因是技术更好，更重要的是更便宜(见表2)。将一吨二氧化碳封存在诺特里斯之下的成本尚未披露，但2018年发表在《焦耳》(Joule)杂志上的一篇论文将碳工程公司 DAC 系统的规模运营成本定在每吨94美元至232美元之间。这远远低于600美元，与欧盟的碳排放价格相去甚远。 CCS , which should be cheaper than DAC , is also showing a bit more promise. Svante, a Canadian startup, uses inexpensive materials to capture CO 2 from dirty industrial flue gas for around $50 a tonne (though that excludes transport and storage). Other companies are converting the captured carbon into products which they then hope to sell at a profit. CarbonFree, which works with US Steel and BP , a British oil-and-gas company, takes CO 2 from industrial processes and turns it into speciality chemicals. 应该比 DAC 便宜的 CCS 技术也显示出更大的前景。Svante，一家加拿大的初创公司，使用廉价的材料从肮脏的工业废气中捕获 CO 2 ，成本约为每吨50美元(尽管这还不包括运输和储存)。其他公司正在将捕获的碳转化为产品，然后希望出售获利。与美国钢铁公司(US Steel)和英国石油天然气公司英国石油(BP)合作的 Carbon free 公司，从工业流程中提取 CO 2 ，并将其转化为特用化学品。 LanzaTech, which has a commercial-scale partnership with ArcelorMittal, a European steel giant, and several Chinese industrial firms, builds bioreactors that convert industrial carbon emissions into useful materials. LanzaTech 与欧洲钢铁巨头安赛乐米塔尔和几家中国工业企业建立了商业规模的合作伙伴关系，建造生物反应器，将工业碳排放转化为有用的材料。 Some make their way into portable carbon stores, such as Lululemon yoga pants. 其中一些进入了便携式碳储存设施，比如露露柠檬瑜伽裤。 All told, carbon capture, utilisation and storage ( CCUS in the field’s acronym-rich jargon) may attract $150bn in investments globally this decade, predicts Wood Mackenzie. Assessing current and proposed projects, the consultancy reckons that global CCUS capacity—which on its definition includes CCS, the sundry ways to put the captured carbon to use, as well as DAC —will rise more than sevenfold by 2030. Wood Mackenzie 预测，总的来说，碳捕获、利用和封存(CCUS，该领域缩写丰富的术语)在未来十年可能在全球吸引1500亿美元的投资。在评估当前和拟议中的项目时，该咨询公司估计，到2030年，全球 CCUS 产能(根据其定义，包括 CCS、利用捕获的碳的各种方式以及 DAC)将增长7倍以上。 The second—possibly bigger—factor behind the recent flurry of carbon-removal activity is government action. 第二个可能更大的因素是政府的行动。 One obvious way to promote the industry would be to make carbon polluters pay a high enough fee for every tonne of carbon they emit that it would be in their interest to pay carbon removers to mop it all up, either at the source or from the atmosphere. 促进该行业发展的一个显而易见的办法是，让碳污染企业为其排放的每吨碳支付足够高的费用，这样，无论是从源头还是从大气中，支付清除碳的费用都符合它们的利益。 A reasonable carbon price like the EU ’s current one may, just about, make CCS viable. For DAC to be a profitable enterprise, though, the tax would probably need to be a fair bit higher, which could smother economies still dependent on hydrocarbons. 像欧盟目前这样合理的碳排放价格可能会让 CCS 变得可行。不过，要想让发展援助委员会成为一家盈利企业，税率可能需要提高一些，这可能会扼杀仍然依赖碳氢化合物的经济体。 That, plus the dim prospects for a global carbon tax, means that state support is needed to bridge the gap between the current price of carbon and the cost of extracting it. 再加上全球碳税前景黯淡，这意味着需要国家支持，以弥合当前碳价格与碳开采成本之间的差距。 The emerging view among technologists, investors and buyers is that carbon removal will develop like waste management did decades ago—as an initially costly endeavour that needs public support to get off the ground but can in time turn profitable. 技术专家、投资者和买家之间正在形成的观点是，碳移除将像几十年前的废弃物管理那样发展ーー这是一项最初代价高昂的努力，需要公众支持才能启动，但最终可能会变得有利可图。 Policymakers are coming over to this view. Some of the hundreds of billions of dollars in America’s recently approved climate handouts are aimed at bootstrapping the industry into existence. 政策制定者正逐渐接受这种观点。美国最近批准的数千亿美元气候补贴中的一部分旨在促进该行业的发展。 An enhanced tax credit included in one of the laws, the Inflation Reduction Act, provides up to $85 per tonne of CO 2 permanently stored, and $60 per tonne of CO 2 used for enhanced oil recovery, which also sequesters CO 2 (albeit in order to produce more hydrocarbons). Clio Crespy of Guggenheim Securities, an investment firm, calculates that this credit increases the volume of emissions in America that are “in the money” for carbon removal more than tenfold. The EU ’s response to America’s climate bonanza is likely to promote carbon removal, too. Earlier this year the EU and Norway announced a “green alliance” to boost regional carbon-capture plans. 其中一项增强的税收抵免法案——《通货膨胀减少法案》规定，每吨永久储存的二氧化碳最高可达85美元，每吨用于提高原油采收率的二氧化碳最高可达60美元，这也可以隔离二氧化碳(尽管是为了生产更多的碳氢化合物)。投资公司古根海姆证券(Guggenheim Securities)的克里奥 · 克雷斯皮(Clio Crepy)计算出，这种信用增加了美国的碳排放量，而这些排放量是用于碳清除的“现金”的十倍以上。欧盟对美国气候变化的回应也可能促进碳移除。今年早些时候，欧盟和挪威宣布了一个“绿色联盟”，以促进区域碳捕获计划。 Buyers of carbon credits are starting to line up. Tech firms, keen to burnish their progressive credentials, are leading the way. 购买碳信用额度的人开始排起长队。热衷于提升自己进步资历的科技公司正在引领潮流。 On May 15th Microsoft said it would purchase (for an undisclosed sum) 2.7m tonnes of carbon captured over a decade from biomass-burning power plants run by Orsted, a Danish clean-energy firm, and pumped underneath the North Sea by a consortium involving Equinor, Shell and TotalEnergies, three European oil giants. 5月15日，微软宣布将购买(数额不详)270万吨碳，这些碳是从丹麦清洁能源公司奥斯特德(Orsted)运营的生物质燃烧发电厂十年来捕获的，由欧洲三大石油巨头 Equinor、壳牌(Shell)和 TotalEnergy 组成的财团抽取到北海海底。 On May 18th Frontier, a buyers’ club with a $1bn carbon-removal pot bankrolled mainly by Alphabet, Meta, Stripe and Shopify, announced a $53m deal with Charm Industrial. The firm will remove 112,000 tonnes of CO 2 between 2024 and 2030 by converting agricultural waste, which would otherwise emit carbon as it decomposes, into an oil that can be stored underground. 5月18日，一个拥有10亿美元碳清除资金的买家俱乐部 Frontier 宣布与 Charm Industrial 达成一项5300万美元的交易，该资金主要由 Alphabet、 Meta、 Stripe 和 Shopify 提供。该公司将在2024年至2030年间，通过将农业废弃物转化为可以储存在地下的石油，从而去除11.2万吨二氧化碳。 Big tech is not alone. NextGen, a joint venture between Mitsubishi Corporation, a Japanese conglomerate, and South Pole, a Swiss developer of carbon-removal projects, intends to acquire over 1m tonnes in certified CO 2 -removal credits by 2025, and sell them on to others. It has just announced the purchase of nearly 200,000 tonnes’ worth of such credits from 1PointFive and two other ventures. The end-buyers include SwissRe and UBS , two Swiss financial giants, Mitsui OSK Lines, a Japanese shipping firm, and Boston Consulting Group. On May 23rd JPMorgan Chase, America’s biggest bank, said it would spend over $200m in the coming years on buying credits from carbon-removal firms. 大型科技公司并不孤单。日本三菱商事集团 nextGen 与瑞士碳清除项目开发商 South Pole 组建的合资企业，计划到2025年获得100多万吨经认证的 CO 2 清除信用额度，并将其出售给其他企业。它刚刚宣布从1PointFive 和另外两家合资企业购买价值近20万吨的此类信贷。最终买家包括瑞士两大金融巨头瑞士再保险(SwissRe)和瑞银集团(UBS)、日本船运公司三井 OSK 航运(Mitsui OSK Lines)和波士顿咨询集团(Boston Consulting Group)。5月23日，美国最大的银行摩根大通表示，它将在未来几年花费2亿美元从碳清除公司购买信用额度。 Maybe the biggest sign that the carbon-removal business has legs is its embrace by the oil industry. Occidental is keen on DAC . ExxonMobil says it will spend $17bn from 2022 to 2027 on “lower-emissions investments”, with a slug going to CCS . Its main American rival, Chevron, is hosting Svante at one of its Californian oilfields. As the Microsoft deal shows, their European peers want to convert parts of the North Sea floor into a giant carbon sink. 或许，碳清除行业有长足发展的最大迹象，就是石油行业对其的拥抱。西方人很喜欢 DAC。埃克森美孚(ExxonMobil)表示，从2022年到2027年，该公司将投入170亿美元用于“低排放投资”，其中一部分将用于 CCS 技术。它在美国的主要竞争对手雪佛龙(Chevron)正在其位于加利福尼亚的一个油田招待斯万特。正如微软的交易所显示的那样，他们的欧洲同行希望将北海海底的部分地区转变成一个巨大的碳汇。 Equinor and Wintershall, a German oil-and-gas firm, have already secured licences to stash carbon captured from German industry in North Sea sites. Hugo Dijkgraaf, Wintershall’s technology chief, thinks his firm can abate up to 30m tonnes of CO 2 per year by 2040. The idea, he says, is to turn “from an oil-and-gas company into a gas-and-carbon-management company”. Equinor 和德国油气公司 Wintershall 已经获得许可证，可以将从德国工业捕获的碳储存在北海地区。Wintershall 公司的技术总监 Hugo Dijkgraaf 认为，到2040年，他的公司每年可以减少3000万吨 CO 2 。他表示，这个想法是要“从一家石油和天然气公司转变为一家天然气和碳管理公司”。 Saudi Arabia, home to Saudi Aramco, the world’s oil colossus, has a goal of increasing CCS capacity fivefold in the next 12 years. Its mega-storage facility at Jubail Industrial City is expected to be operational by 2027. ADNOC , Aramco’s Emirati counterpart, wants to increase its capacity sixfold by 2030, to 5m tonnes a year. 全球石油巨头沙特阿美(Saudi Aramco)的总部所在地沙特阿拉伯有一个目标，即在未来12年内将 CCS 产能提高5倍。其位于 Jubail Industrial City 的巨型储存设施预计将于2027年投入使用。阿联酋石油公司(Aramco)的阿联酋石油天然气公司(ADNOC)希望到2030年将产能提高6倍，达到每年500万吨。 The oilmen’s critics allege that their enthusiasm for carbon removal is mainly about improving their reputations in the eyes of increasingly climate-conscious consumers, while pumping more crude for longer. There is surely some truth to this. 批评石油商的人士声称，他们对碳去除的热情主要是为了提高他们在日益关注气候变化的消费者眼中的声誉，同时延长更多原油的开采时间。这肯定有一些道理。 But given the urgent need to both capture carbon at source and achieve voluminous negative emissions, the willing involvement of giant oil firms, with their vast capital budgets and useful expertise in engineering and geology, is to be welcomed. ■ 但是，考虑到从源头上捕捉碳并实现大量负排放的迫切需要，大型石油公司的积极参与是值得欢迎的，因为它们拥有庞大的资本预算和在工程和地质方面的有用专业知识。￭ To stay on top of the biggest stories in business and technology, sign up to the Bottom Line , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter. Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Deutsche Bahn 德国铁路 It will take years to get Deutsche Bahn back on track 让 Deutsche Bahn 重回正轨需要数年时间 Europe’s biggest rail operator has gone off the rails 欧洲最大的铁路运营商已经脱轨 May 25th 2023 | BERLIN 2023年5月25日 | 柏林 I N MID-MAY GERMANS were bracing for the third, and longest, national rail strike this year. Deutsche Bahn ( DB ) was locked in a dispute over pay with EVG , the union representing most German railway workers, including 180,000 at the state-run behemoth. At the last minute union leaders called off a 50-hour stoppage that was going to begin on the evening of May 14th. German travellers breathed a sigh of relief—and then gasped as DB failed to reinstate all of the 50,000 cancelled services. The next day roads were clogged by commuters who, worried about getting stuck at a train station, took the car instead. 五月中旬，德国人正在为今年第三次也是最长的一次全国铁路罢工做准备。德国联邦铁路公司(Deutsche Bahn)与代表大多数德国铁路工人的工会 EVG 陷入了薪酬纠纷，其中包括这家国有铁路巨头的18万名工人。在最后一刻，工会领导人取消了原定于5月14日晚上开始的50个小时的罢工。德国游客松了一口气ーー随后，当 DB 未能恢复全部被取消的5万条航线时，他们倒吸了一口凉气。第二天，道路被上班族堵塞，他们担心被困在火车站，于是改乘汽车。 On May 23rd DB and the union met for a fourth round of wage talks, with no long-term resolution in sight. And labour unrest is only one of many fronts on which DB is fighting. Once a source of national pride, it has become the butt of bad jokes (“We have one about DB but we don’t know whether it will work”). In April just 70% of its long-distance trains were on time. And even that was an improvement on the whole of last year, when only 60% were punctual; the company’s (unambitious) goal is at least 80%. DB services are “too crowded, too old and too kaputt ”, Berthold Huber, who sits on DB ’s board, told the Süddeutsche Zeitung, a daily, this month. 5月23日，DB 和工会举行了第四轮工资谈判，但没有达成长期解决方案。而劳工骚乱只是 DB 正在抗争的众多战线之一。它曾经是民族自豪感的来源，现在却成了冷笑话的笑柄(“我们有一个关于 DB 的，但是我们不知道它是否会起作用”)。四月份，只有70% 的长途列车准点。甚至这也是去年全年的一个进步，当时只有60% 的人是准时的; 公司(没有雄心壮志)的目标是至少80% 。DB 董事会成员贝托尔德•胡贝尔(Berthold Huber)本月对《南德意志报》(a daily)表示，DB 的服务“过于拥挤、过于陈旧、过于糟糕”。 DB ’s woes are the result of poor management, a bloated bureaucracy, political interference and years of underinvestment. In 2004 DB ’s annual budget for the construction and upgrading of railway lines was cut from €4bn ($5bn) a year to €1.5bn, notes Christian Böttger of the University of Applied Sciences in Berlin. It has edged up since but last year was still only €1.9bn. This year it will be €2bn. DB 的困境是管理不善、官僚机构臃肿、政治干预和多年投资不足的结果。柏林应用科学大学(University of Applied Sciences)的克里斯蒂安•博特格尔(Christian Böttger)指出，2004年，德国开发银行用于铁路建设和改造的年度预算从每年40亿欧元(合50亿美元)削减至15亿欧元。自那以来，这一数字有所上升，但去年仍只有19亿欧元。今年将达到20亿欧元。 The railway business has been bleeding money for years; in 2022 it made an operating loss of €500m. DB ’s overall operating profit of €1.3bn, on revenues of €56bn, was all down to its logistics arm, DB Schenker, which has benefited from the uptick in e-commerce and now contributes nearly half of sales. 多年来，铁路业务一直在亏损; 2022年，铁路业务亏损5亿欧元。DB 的总营业利润为13亿欧元，营业收入为560亿欧元，这一切都归功于其物流部门 DB Schenker。该部门受益于电子商务的增长，目前贡献了近一半的销售额。 Vowing to make up for the failings of its predecessors, Olaf Scholz’s newish cabinet has ambitious plans for DB . It wants to pump an extra €45bn into the network, hoping almost to double the number of passenger journeys by 2030 to around 4bn, and to increase freight volumes by 25%. This year DB is beginning to renovate and modernise 650 train stations, as well as upgrading 2,000km of tracks, 1,800 switch points and 200 bridges. 奥拉夫•肖尔茨(Olaf Scholz)的新内阁誓言要弥补前任的失败，对 DB 制定了雄心勃勃的计划。它希望向铁路网注入额外的450亿欧元，希望到2030年将客运量增加一倍，达到40亿欧元左右，并将货运量增加25% 。今年，DB 开始对650个火车站进行翻新和现代化改造，并对2000公里的轨道、1800个转换点和200座桥梁进行升级改造。 It will also add another 500 people to its 4,300-strong team of security personnel, who are tasked with protecting train tracks against mischief-makers (last October DB was hit by suspected sabotage, causing the suspension of all services in northern Germany). And it is accelerating the digitisation of railway traffic, from signals and switches to digital “twins” of wagons ferrying goods. 它还将在其4300多名安全人员的团队中再增加500人，这些人的任务是保护铁轨免受恶作剧者的破坏(去年10月德国铁路局被怀疑遭到破坏，导致德国北部的所有服务暂停)。它正在加速铁路交通的数字化，从信号和交换机到运输货物的货车的数字“双胞胎”。 In time this may improve passengers’ lot. But not soon. This year carriages may get more packed: since May 1st Germans can buy a monthly Deutschland ticket, valid on all regional and local trains, for just €49. Delays and missed connections are forecast to be worse than in 2022, in part owing to all those upgrade works. And DB may slip into the red. It is forecasting a loss of €1bn from operations in 2023, because of the investments, as well as high cost inflation that is politically tough to pass on to travellers (who, in a further drain on DB cash, can demand compensation for all the delays). “The pretence of running an economically viable business was abandoned …long ago,” says Mr Böttger. It is all about getting more cash from the state. ■ 随着时间的推移，这可能会改善乘客的命运。但不是很快。今年的车厢可能会变得更加拥挤: 自5月1日起，德国人可以购买每月的德国车票，有效的所有地区和当地的火车，只需 something 49。预计延误和错过连接的情况将比2022年更严重，部分原因是所有这些升级工程。数据库可能会出现赤字。德意志银行预计，由于这些投资以及高成本通胀，德意志银行2023年的运营将亏损10亿欧元，这在政治上很难转嫁给旅行者(旅行者可以要求对所有延误进行赔偿，这将进一步消耗德意志银行的现金)。伯特格先生说: “经营一家经济上可行的企业的幌子早就被抛弃了。”。这一切都是为了从国家获得更多的现金。￭ To stay on top of the biggest stories in business and technology, sign up to the Bottom Line , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter. 要了解商业和技术领域最重要的新闻，请注册我们的每周订阅时事通讯“底线”。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/it-will-take-years-to-get-deutsche-bahn-back-on-track 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/it-will-take-years-to-get-deutsche-bahn-back-on-track 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Muscle and memory 肌肉和记忆 Asian businesses are being dragged into the chip war 亚洲企业正被卷入芯片战争 China’s decision to ban Micron puts South Korea’s semiconductor manufacturers in an awkward spot 中国禁止美光的决定让韩国半导体制造商陷入尴尬境地 May 25th 2023 | Singapore 2023年5月25日 | 新加坡 U NLIKE LOGIC chips, which process information, memory chips, which store it, looked less vulnerable to the Sino-American techno-tussle. Such semiconductors are commodities, less high-tech than microprocessors and so less central to the great-power struggle for technological supremacy. 与处理信息的 LOGIC 芯片不同，存储信息的内存芯片在中美技术角力中显得不那么脆弱。这些半导体是大宗商品，不如微处理器高科技，因此在大国争夺技术霸权的斗争中不那么重要。 That changed on May 21st, when the Chinese government banned memory chips made by Micron from critical-infrastructure projects. The restriction hurts the American chipmaker, which last year derived 11% of its revenue from mainland China. 5月21日，当中国政府禁止美光公司生产的存储芯片进入关键基础设施项目时，情况发生了变化。这一限制措施损害了这家美国芯片制造商的利益，该公司去年11% 的收入来自中国大陆。 It also opens up a new front in the transpacific chip war—one which the countries that are near China but allies of America are being roped into. 它还在跨太平洋芯片战争中开辟了一条新战线，那些靠近中国但是美国盟友的国家正在被拉入这场战争。 By opening a gap in the market, the ban creates an opportunity for the world’s two biggest memory-chip makers, Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix, both from South Korea. They made 16% and 44% of their respective sales in China in 2021. With Micron frozen out of critical infrastructure—and looking riskier to other Chinese buyers—the South Korean duo can pick up more contracts. 通过打开市场缺口，该禁令为全球两大存储芯片制造商——三星电子(Samsung Electronics)和 SK 海力士(SK Hynix)——创造了机会。2021年，他们在中国的销售额分别占到总销售额的16% 和44% 。由于美光被排除在关键基础设施之外，而且在其他中国买家看来风险更大，这两家韩国公司可以获得更多合同。 That is what investors seem to have concluded: Samsung’s share price is at its highest since April 2022; SK Hynix’s was last this lofty in August. 这似乎是投资者得出的结论: 三星股价处于2022年4月以来的最高水平; SK 海力士(SK Hynix)的股价上一次达到这一水平是在去年8月。 The prospect of a windfall for South Korea’s chipmakers is, however, complicated by the country’s blend of American and Chinese interests. 然而，韩国芯片制造商获得意外之财的前景，由于美国和中国的利益交织在一起而变得更加复杂。 President Joe Biden’s administration has made no official demand asking its counterpart in Seoul to prevent the South Korean firms from jumping in to fill orders for which Micron will no longer be able to compete. But it is said to be lobbying for this behind closed doors. 美国总统乔•拜登(Joe Biden)的政府没有正式要求首尔的美国政府阻止韩国公司参与美光再也无法竞争的订单。但据说，该公司正在私下为此进行游说。 The 28,500 American troops stationed in South Korea to protect it against its despotic nuclear-armed neighbour to the north lend weight to such requests. 驻扎在韩国的28,500名美国士兵保护韩国免受朝鲜这个拥有核武器的专制邻国的威胁，这一要求也很有说服力。 At the same time, Seoul has little interest in damaging its commercial ties with China. South Korea exported $156bn-worth of goods, equivalent to 9% of its GDP , to the country in 2022, and imported roughly the same amount. That makes China its largest trading partner by some distance. 与此同时，韩国无意破坏与中国的商业关系。2022年，韩国向朝鲜出口了价值1560亿美元的商品，相当于其国内生产总值(GDP)的9% ，进口量也大致相当。这使得中国在一定程度上成为其最大的贸易伙伴。 On May 24th the South Korean government formally asked America to review the rules which limit American subsidies to chipmakers if the recipient expands its Chinese production capacity of advanced chips by more than 5% over ten years. 5月24日，韩国政府正式要求美国重新审查限制美国对芯片制造商补贴的规定，如果接受国在十年内将中国先进芯片的生产能力扩大5% 以上。 South Korea is, in other words, treading carefully. Any public attempt to guide Samsung and SK Hynix risks upsetting Beijing, which is already fuming: on May 22nd a spokesperson for China’s foreign ministry said America had “coerced other countries into imposing export restrictions on China for the sole purpose of maintaining its hegemonic interests”. 换句话说，韩国正在谨慎行事。任何公开指导三星和 SK 海力士的尝试都有可能激怒已经怒火中烧的北京: 5月22日，中国外交部发言人表示，美国“胁迫其他国家对中国实施出口限制，唯一目的就是维护其霸权利益”。 The official line from Seoul is, for the time being, that it is up to the companies how they respond. That strategic ambiguity may not be viable for much longer. ■ 韩国政府的官方说法是，就目前而言，这取决于企业如何回应。这种战略模糊性可能不会持续太久。￭ To stay on top of the biggest stories in business and technology, sign up to the Bottom Line , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter. 要了解商业和技术领域最重要的新闻，请注册我们的每周订阅时事通讯“底线”。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/asian-businesses-are-being-dragged-into-the-chip-war 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/asian-businesses-are-being-dragged-into-the-chip-war 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Reactivated 重新激活 Why activist investors are going to have a busy year 为什么维权投资者今年会很忙 If you think they have gone soft, think again 如果你认为他们已经软化，再想想 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 C ARL ICAHN owns 1.4% of Illumina, a genomics giant with a market value of $34bn. Mr Icahn’s modest stake belies his ambition. As the elder statesman of activist investing, the 87-year-old aims to convert small shareholdings into considerable influence. 卡尔 ICAHN 拥有 Illumina 公司1.4% 的股份，Illumina 是一家市值340亿美元的基因组巨头。伊坎先生的微薄利益掩盖了他的雄心壮志。作为维权投资领域的资深政治家，这位87岁的老人的目标是将小额股权转化为相当大的影响力。 Mr Icahn has nominated three directors to the board of Illumina, whose share price has fallen by 60% since its peak in 2021, in part owing to hubristic dealmaking. On May 25th, after The Economist was published, Mr Icahn’s colourful campaign against the firm’s bosses (16 letters, drawing on Shakespeare and Lincoln) will reach a climax at its annual general meeting. 伊坎先生已经提名三名董事进入 Illumina 公司的董事会，该公司的股价自2021年达到顶峰以来已经下跌了60% ，部分原因是由于傲慢的交易。5月25日，在《经济学人》出版之后，伊坎先生针对公司老板的活动(16封信，引用了莎士比亚和林肯)将在年度股东大会上达到高潮。 It is one of the hottest tickets in this year’s “proxy season”, when most American firms elect their boards of directors. 这是今年“代理季”中最热门的门票之一，大多数美国公司都是在这个季节选举董事会成员。 After a dismal year, when activist funds lost 16% of their value as stockmarkets slumped, many observers were expecting a spring offensive. Fund managers had a busy end to 2022, taking advantage of those sunken stockmarket valuations. 在经历了惨淡的一年之后，由于股市暴跌，维权基金市值缩水了16% ，许多观察人士原本预计会出现皇帝会战。2022年底，基金经理们利用股市估值下跌的机会，忙碌地度过了年底。 New corporate-governance rules introduced last September make it easier for dissident investors to obtain board seats, by compelling firms to include all nominees on proxy ballots and allowing shareholders to mix and match those proposed by the company and by its detractors, rather than pick alternative slates. 去年9月出台的新的公司治理规则使得持不同政见的投资者更容易获得董事会席位，通过强制公司将所有提名人包括在代理投票中，允许股东混合并匹配公司及其批评者提出的人选，而不是选择其他人选。 “Asked in December, I would have said this is going to be a proxy season for the ages,” says Kai Liekefett of Sidley Austin, a law firm. 盛德国际律师事务所(Sidley Austin)的凯•利克菲特(Kai Liekefett)表示: “在12月份被问及这个问题时，我会说，这将是一个长久以来的代理季节。”。 Five months on things are, at first blush, looking less epochal. In the first quarter of 2023 the number of new activist campaigns in America was a third lower than a year earlier, according to Lazard, an investment bank. 五个月的时间，乍一看，似乎不那么划时代。投资银行 Lazard 的数据显示，2023年第一季度，美国新激进主义运动的数量比去年同期减少了三分之一。 With dealmaking at its most subdued in a decade, “transactional” funds, shorter-term investors which agitate for anything from spin-offs of units to the sale of the whole company, are finding little traction. Few big-ticket fights made it to contested votes for board spots. 随着交易撮合处于10年来最低迷的时期，“交易型”基金——鼓吹从剥离部门到出售整个公司等任何事情的短期投资者——几乎找不到什么吸引力。很少有大赌注的比赛能进入争夺董事会席位的竞争性投票。 Yet the quietude may be deceiving. For one thing, many targets, worried that the new rules favour activists, have sought a truce rather than risk a painful proxy brawl. 然而，这种平静可能具有欺骗性。一方面，许多目标担心新规定有利于维权人士，因此寻求休战，而不是冒险进行痛苦的代理权争斗。 In February Trian, an activist outfit run by Nelson Peltz, ended its battle against Disney after the entertainment behemoth presented a restructuring plan. 今年2月，由纳尔逊•佩尔茨(Nelson Peltz)管理的维权组织 Trian 在迪士尼提交重组计划后，结束了与迪士尼的竞争。 Elliott Management, another activist giant, called off plans to nominate directors to the board of Salesforce in March, two months after the software firm appointed the boss of ValueAct, another well-known fund, to its board. 今年3月，另一家维权巨头埃利奥特管理公司(Elliott Management)取消了向 Salesforce 董事会提名董事的计划。两个月前，这家软件公司任命了另一家知名基金 ValueAct 的老板进入其董事会。 This month Shake Shack, a fast-food retailer, announced a settlement agreement with Engaged Capital, a smaller activist fund. Such truces may be less entertaining for outside observers than Mr Icahn’s antics, but for the activists they are wins nonetheless. 本月，快餐零售商 Shake Shack 宣布与规模较小的维权基金 EngagedCapital 达成和解协议。这样的休战对于外部观察家来说可能没有伊坎先生的滑稽动作那么有趣，但是对于活动家来说，他们还是赢了。 Fifteen years of rock-bottom interest rates and cheap money have created a target-rich environment. As the cost of capital rises, activists spy plenty of management teams that could do with more discipline. 15年的低利率和廉价资金创造了一个富有目标的环境。随着资金成本的上升，活动人士发现，许多管理团队需要更严格的纪律。 On May 11th Elliott revealed it owned 10% of Goodyear, a tyremaker with a market value of around $4bn, along with a plan to sell retail stores and bolster margins. Four days later the hedge fund announced a 13% interest in NRG , an $8bn energy company, calling its recent acquisition of Vivint, a home-security firm, “the single worst deal” the power-and-utilities sector has seen in the past decade. Both campaigns attempt to focus managers’ minds and streamline the companies’ operations. 5月11日，埃利奥特透露，该公司持有市值约40亿美元的轮胎制造商固特异(Goodyear)10% 的股份，并计划出售零售店以提高利润率。四天后，对冲基金宣布对价值80亿美元的能源公司 NRG 拥有13% 的股份，并称其最近对 Vivint 家庭安全公司的收购是电力和公用事业部门在过去十年中“最糟糕的一笔交易”。这两场运动都试图集中管理者的注意力，并精简公司的运营。 The share prices of Goodyear and NRG jumped by 21% and 3%, respectively, on the news of Elliott’s stakes. 埃利奥特入股的消息传出后，固特异和 NRG 的股价分别上涨了21% 和3% 。 Activists are also pursuing larger prey. Campaigns involving companies with market capitalisations above $50bn made up a record share of activity during the first quarter of 2023. Some funds are taking aim at technology firms, which accounted for a quarter of campaigns in America last year. 活动人士也在追逐更大的猎物。2023年第一季度，市值超过500亿美元的公司参与的宣传活动，在活动中所占份额达到创纪录水平。一些基金瞄准了科技公司，这些公司去年在美国占据了四分之一的市场份额。 Tech’s retreat from the pandemic-induced boom in all things digital presents activists with an opportunity to force cost-cutting or shedding unprofitable businesses. 科技从流行病引发的所有数字产业的繁荣中退出，为活动人士提供了一个机会，迫使他们削减成本，或者剥离无利可图的业务。 Technology giants’ vast market capitalisations—Salesforce is worth $200bn—allow activists to deploy large sums without passing ownership thresholds that would trigger disclosures of their stakes before they are ready to launch the public-facing part of their campaigns. 科技巨头庞大的市值(Salesforce 市值达2000亿美元)使维权人士能够在不超过所有权门槛的情况下部署大笔资金，这将导致他们在准备发起面向公众的部分运动之前披露所持股份。 Not even the biggest of big tech, hitherto largely spared the activists’ rod, is safe. This month Pershing Square and Third Point, two hedge funds, revealed investments in Alphabet, Google’s $1.5trn parent company. Third Point insists its stake is not an activist campaign. 即使是迄今基本上没有受到激进分子抨击的最大型科技公司，也不是安全的。本月，两家对冲基金 Pershing Square 和 Third Point 披露了对谷歌1.5万亿美元母公司 Alphabet 的投资。Third Point 坚称，它所持股份不是一场激进运动。 It and Pershing may still benefit from the earlier efforts of TCI , another fund already on the warpath against Alphabet’s high costs and cash-burning moonshots. 它和潘兴仍可能受益于 TCI 早些时候的努力。 TCI 是另一家已经开始反对 Alphabet 高成本和烧钱的登月计划的基金。 American activists will also increasingly export their version of shareholder capitalism abroad. 美国活动人士也将越来越多地向海外输出他们版本的股东资本主义。 In addition to large regional players, such as Cevian Capital in Europe, high-profile American fund managers have become familiar faces in the world’s business circles, making it easier for them to engage with other shareholders. 除了欧洲的 Cevian Capital 等大型地区性基金公司外，知名的美国基金管理公司已成为全球商界熟悉的面孔，这使它们更容易与其它股东打交道。 Even as new campaigns in America languished at the start of the year, activity in Europe and Asia has surged. 尽管今年年初美国的新竞选活动停滞不前，但欧洲和亚洲的竞选活动却激增。 Take Japan. Many of those who picked fights there in the past, like T. Boone Pickens, a corporate raider who took on Koito Manufacturing in the 1980s, or TCI , which in 2008 dropped its investment in J -Power, an electric utility, came back bruised. Now, thanks to corporate-governance reforms over the past decade, Japan Inc has had no choice but to become more receptive. 以日本为例。许多过去在那里挑起争端的人，比如上世纪80年代收购了小东制造(Koito Manufacturing)的企业掠夺者托·布恩·皮肯斯，以及2008年放弃对电力公用事业公司日本电力(J-Power)投资的 TCI，回来时都受了伤。现在，由于过去十年的公司治理改革，日本公司别无选择，只能变得更容易接受。 New stockmarket guidelines will go so far as to ask companies worth less than the book value of their equity to disclose their initiatives for improvement. 新的股票市场指导方针甚至要求市值低于其股本账面价值的公司披露其改进举措。 Activists do not always prevail. On May 25th ValueAct lost a proxy battle to elect four directors to the board of Seven & i, the Japanese conglomerate which owns the 7-Eleven chain of convenience stores. This month Mr Icahn himself became the target of an activist attack. 活动人士并不总是占上风。5月25日，ValueAct 在选举 Seven & i 董事会四名董事的代理权争夺战中败北。 Seven & i 是日本企业集团，旗下拥有7-11连锁便利店。这个月，伊坎先生自己成为了激进分子攻击的目标。 A short-seller, Hindenburg Research, claims that his listed vehicle, Icahn Enterprises, is overvalued. In response to the assault, Mr Icahn denied Hindenburg’s allegations and defended the style of investing he pioneered. 一个卖空者，兴登堡研究，声称他的上市工具，伊坎企业，被高估。作为对袭击的回应，伊坎先生否认了兴登堡的指控，并为他开创的投资风格进行了辩护。 Activists, he argued, “breach the walls” of badly run companies. Whether or not Mr Icahn’s own firm deserves activist treatment, plenty of others certainly do. ■ 他认为，活动人士“打破了”经营不善的公司的“围墙”。无论伊坎先生自己的公司是否值得激进主义者的对待，很多其他公司当然值得。￭ To stay on top of the biggest stories in business and technology, sign up to the Bottom Line , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter. 要了解商业和技术领域最重要的新闻，请注册我们的每周订阅时事通讯“底线”。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/why-activist-investors-are-going-to-have-a-busy-year 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/why-activist-investors-are-going-to-have-a-busy-year 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Zell-side analysis Zell 侧分析 What properties would Sam Zell invest in next? Sam Zell 接下来会投资什么地产？ One of America’s mightiest property tycoons died on May 19th, aged 81 美国最有权势的地产大亨之一于5月19日去世，享年81岁 May 25th 2023 | New York 2023年5月25日 | 纽约 S AM ZELL called himself “the Grave Dancer”, even though, as he explained, his penchant for buying distressed assets “wasn’t so much dancing on graves as …raising the dead”. In the mid-1970s, when he coined this nickname, America’s property market was struggling. 萨姆•泽尔(SAM ZELL)称自己为“坟墓舞者”，尽管正如他解释的那样，他购买不良资产的嗜好“与其说是在坟墓上跳舞，不如说是在... ... 唤醒死者”。20世纪70年代中期，当他创造这个绰号时，美国的房地产市场正在苦苦挣扎。 Mr Zell, who died on May 18th at the age of 81 and with a fortune of more than $5bn, had already made good money erecting and managing apartment buildings in small but growing cities. But after others cottoned on to the same opportunity, the market became saturated. 泽尔先生于5月18日去世，享年81岁，拥有超过50亿美元的财富，他已经在小城市建造和管理公寓大楼中赚了不少钱。但在其他人意识到同样的机会之后，市场变得饱和。 Supply exceeded demand; property prices crashed. Undeterred, he bought flats, offices and retail space, often for pennies. As America’s economy boomed in the 1980s, their value soared. 供过于求，房地产价格暴跌。但他没有被吓倒，他买了公寓、写字楼和零售店，往往只花了几分钱。20世纪80年代，随着美国经济的蓬勃发展，它们的价值飙升。 He danced on more graves after Black Monday in October 1987. With rents and occupancy rates falling, indebted property owners needed money, and turned to capital markets. 1987年10月黑色星期一之后，他在更多的坟墓上跳舞。随着租金和入住率的下降，负债累累的业主需要资金，于是转向了资本市场。 He created a fund with Merrill Lynch, an investment bank, that raised capital from investors to buy distressed properties. Such real-estate investment trusts ( REIT s), which own, run or finance properties, date back to the 1960s. But it was Mr Zell who helped usher in their modern version, says Michael Knott of Green Street, a firm of real-estate analysts. 他与投资银行美林(Merrill Lynch)共同创建了一只基金，从投资者那里筹集资金，用于购买不良资产。这种房地产投资信托基金(REITs)拥有、运营或融资房地产，其历史可以追溯到上世纪60年代。但是，正是泽尔先生帮助开创了他们的现代版本，房地产分析公司 Green Street 的迈克尔•诺特(Michael Knott)说。 Mr Zell was born to Polish-Jewish parents who narrowly escaped the Holocaust. He got his start in business early, buying Playboy magazines in downtown Chicago, where he went to Hebrew school, for 50 cents and selling them to classmates in the suburb where he lived for $3. He wore jeans to work long before office-casual was a thing, and took motorcycle-riding trips around the world with a group of friends, “Zell’s Angels”. 泽尔先生的父母是波兰犹太人，他们侥幸逃过了大屠杀。他很早就开始做生意，在芝加哥市中心以50美分的价格买下《花花公子》杂志，然后以3美元的价格卖给住在郊区的同学。早在上班之前，他就穿着牛仔裤上班——休闲装很流行，还和一群朋友“泽尔的天使”(Zell’s Angels)一起骑摩托车环游世界。 He explained his business philosophy as “If it ain’t fun, we don’t do it.” His timing was impeccable. In 2007 he sold his office-landlord business to Blackstone, a private-equity giant, for $39bn. A year later Lehman Brothers collapsed—and the commercial-property market with it. 他解释自己的商业哲学是“如果不好玩，我们就不做。”他来得正是时候。2007年，他以390亿美元的价格将自己的办公室业主业务出售给了私募股权巨头黑石集团(Blackstone)。一年后，雷曼兄弟(Lehman Brothers)破产，商业地产市场也随之崩溃。 Where would a young Mr Zell look to build his fortune today? Stephanie Wright of New York University thinks that, given his preference for easy-to-understand markets with limited competition, outdoor storage facilities could pique his interest. 年轻的泽尔先生今天会去哪里发财呢？纽约大学(New York University)的斯蒂芬妮•赖特(Stephanie Wright)认为，鉴于他偏爱有限竞争、易于理解的市场，户外存储设施可能会激起他的兴趣。 They are big but employ few people, meaning cities dislike them and limit their growth, even though demand remains robust. The same goes for parks of prefabricated homes, the business of the first company Mr Zell ever took public, in 1993. People still buy them, yet zoning laws restrict them. 它们规模很大，但雇佣的人却很少，这意味着城市不喜欢它们，限制了它们的增长，尽管需求依然强劲。1993年，泽尔先生的第一家上市公司——预制房屋公园也是如此。人们仍然购买它们，但是分区法律限制了它们。 Once a house is installed on the property, homeowners rarely move it. On the rare occasions when one does, the developer still has the land and can put another home in its place. 一旦房子安装在房产上，业主很少搬家。在极少数情况下，当一个人这样做，开发商仍然有土地，可以把另一个家在它的地方。 Conventional wisdom argues for staying away from the office and retail assets that helped make Mr Zell a billionaire. American malls have long been written off for dead, bricks and mortar deemed to be no match for e-commerce. 传统观点认为，要远离让泽尔先生成为亿万富翁的办公室和零售资产。长期以来，美国的商场一直被认为是无法与电子商务相抗衡的。 In the era of remote and hybrid work, leasing activity is slowing across most big American cities even as employment continues to rise, according to CBRE , a property manager. Office-vacancy rates are increasing—to 17.8% across America in the first three months of 2023, the highest level in 30 years. Even though some upscale malls and office buildings are thriving, many older and shabbier properties look destined for obsolescence. 物业管理公司世邦魏理仕(CBRE)表示，在远程和混合工作的时代，尽管就业率持续上升，但美国大多数大城市的租赁活动都在放缓。办公室空置率正在上升ーー2023年前三个月，全美办公室空置率达到17.8% ，为30年来的最高水平。尽管一些高档购物中心和写字楼正在蓬勃发展，但许多老旧破旧的房产看起来注定要被淘汰。 Yet to Mr Zell those failing assets may have looked the most attractive of all. For in failure, as any grave dancer knows, lies opportunity. ■ 然而，对于泽尔先生来说，这些破产的资产可能看起来是最具吸引力的。因为任何舞者都知道，失败就意味着机会。￭ To stay on top of the biggest stories in business and technology, sign up to the Bottom Line , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter. 要了解商业和技术领域最重要的新闻，请注册我们的每周订阅时事通讯“底线”。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/what-properties-would-sam-zell-invest-in-next 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/what-properties-would-sam-zell-invest-in-next 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Fine and dandy 好极了 Meta gets whacked with a €1.2bn penalty Meta 被罚款12亿欧元 It is the biggest punishment yet for breaching the EU’s privacy rules 这是迄今为止对违反欧盟隐私规定的最大惩罚 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 On May 22nd the EU whacked Meta with a €1.2bn ($1.3bn) fine for transferring users’ data to America, in breach of European privacy rules (which turn five this week). The social-media giant is a repeat offender when it comes to privacy breaches. But the EU has reserved the biggest penalties for other sins. Between 2017 and 2019 Google was fined a total of over €8bn for abusing its dominance in search and advertising. Both Google and Meta can afford it; the fines represent a fraction of their profits. ■ 5月22日，欧盟对 Meta 处以12亿欧元(合13亿美元)的罚款，原因是该公司将用户数据传输到美国，违反了欧洲隐私规定(本周该规定将生效)。这家社交媒体巨头在侵犯隐私方面屡试不爽。但是欧盟保留了对其他罪行的最大惩罚。2017年至2019年间，谷歌因滥用其在搜索和广告领域的主导地位，共被处以逾80亿英镑的罚款。谷歌和梅塔都负担得起，罚款只是他们利润的一小部分。￭ To stay on top of the biggest stories in business and technology, sign up to the Bottom Line , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter. 要了解商业和技术领域最重要的新闻，请注册我们的每周订阅时事通讯“底线”。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/meta-gets-whacked-with-a-eu12bn-penalty 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/meta-gets-whacked-with-a-eu12bn-penalty 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Bartleby 巴特比 Why are corporate retreats so extravagant? 为什么公司的休假如此奢侈？ Companies’ summer camps get unnecessarily creative 企业的夏令营不必要地富有创造性 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 I CE BATHS , infrared saunas, white-water rafting, fly-fishing, archery workshops, whisky tastings, yoga at sunrise, shooting clay pigeons, go-kart races, mountain-biking in Norway, falconry in Ireland, climbing up a glacier in Alberta, singing “Kumbaya” around a campfire. 冰浴、红外线桑拿、激流漂流、飞钓、射箭工作坊、威士忌品尝会、日出时的瑜伽、射陶鸽、卡丁车比赛、挪威的山地自行车、爱尔兰的猎鹰活动、阿尔伯塔的冰川攀登、围着篝火唱“ Kumbaya”。 These seemingly disparate activities have one thing in common: all of them are real examples of the contemporary corporate off-site. 这些看似完全不同的活动有一个共同点: 它们都是当代企业场外活动的真实例子。 Your columnist, a guest Bartleby, cringes at the idea of PowerPoint presentations followed by role-playing exercises and mandatory games. She prefers to let the ties with her colleagues deepen in organic ways. Still, the executive retreat has become an annual business tradition. 你的专栏作家，客人 Bartleby，对于 PPT 演示，然后是角色扮演练习和强制性游戏的想法感到畏缩。她更喜欢以有机的方式加深与同事之间的联系。尽管如此，高管度假已成为一年一度的商业传统。 The idea is that, by disconnecting employees from their day-to-day routine, companies can build camaraderie and foster creativity. And it has grown in importance. 他们的想法是，通过让员工脱离日常工作，公司可以建立友谊，培养创造力。而且越来越重要了。 A splashy, exciting getaway once a year may help retain executives in a tight labour market (and is cheaper than fatter monthly pay cheques). 每年一次引人注目、令人兴奋的度假，可能有助于在紧张的劳动力市场中留住高管(而且比每月更丰厚的薪水更便宜)。 In the era of remote work—without the thousands of micro-interactions that happen in the office—team-building trips have also gained a structural role. Suddenly, off-sites are no longer an afterthought but lodged near the heart of corporate HR strategy. Not participating is not an option; so what if co-workers end up meeting in person for the first time wearing flip-flops? 在远程工作的时代ーー没有办公室里发生的成千上万的微型互动ーー团队建设之旅也获得了一种结构性作用。突然之间，外部网站不再是事后才考虑的问题，而是成为了企业人力资源战略的核心。不参与是不可能的; 那么，如果同事们最终穿着人字拖第一次面对面地见面，又该怎么办呢？ It used to be barbecues and softball games. Retreats moved things a notch higher in style and expense. Just three months after Steve Jobs left Apple and started another company in 1985, he whisked his employees to Pebble Beach for their first off-site. 以前是烧烤和垒球比赛。务虚会在风格和费用上都提高了一个档次。1985年，史蒂夫•乔布斯(Steve Jobs)离开苹果(Apple) ，创办了另一家公司，仅仅三个月后，他就带着员工们来到圆石滩(Pebble Beach) ，开始了他们的第一次离职。 As corporate psychology boomed in the 1990s, team-building retreats became entrenched. 随着上世纪90年代企业心理学的蓬勃发展，团队建设务虚会变得根深蒂固。 By 2015 Uber was reportedly offering Beyoncé $6m to perform for its employees (no, not the drivers) at a corporate event in Las Vegas (the pop star was apparently paid in the then-hot startup’s stock rather than cash). 据报道，到2015年，优步向碧昂斯支付了600万美元，让她在拉斯维加斯的一个公司活动中为其员工(不，不是司机)表演(这位流行歌星显然是以当时炙手可热的初创公司的股票而不是现金支付的)。 WeWork, an office-rental firm with tech pretensions, used to host raucous summer retreats around the world; employees were encouraged to dance the night away to electronic music. WeWork 是一家自诩为科技公司的办公室租赁公司，过去常常在世界各地举办喧闹的夏季静修活动，鼓励员工在电子音乐的伴奏下彻夜跳舞。 Given Uber’s lacklustre ride since its initial public offering in 2019, current management has gone easy on A -listers. WeWork revised its staff-entertainment policies after its party-loving founder and CEO , Adam Neumann, was forced out in the wake of its abortive IPO later that year. But the trend for the corporate getaway has, if anything, intensified. 考虑到优步自2019年首次公开发行(IPO)以来表现平平，目前的管理层对一线明星相当宽容。WeWork 在其热爱派对的创始人兼首席执行官亚当•诺依曼(Adam Neumann)在当年晚些时候首次公开发行(IPO)流产后被迫离职后，修改了员工娱乐政策。但是，如果说有什么变化的话，那就是企业逃离的趋势加剧了。 To stand out, companies try to make their retreats as bespoke and exotic as possible. 为了脱颖而出，公司试图使他们的静修尽可能定制和异国情调。 Those firms that cannot afford pop stars can have an astronaut regale executives with tales of life in space—not Queen Bey, exactly, but potentially enthralling to the nerdier elements of the workforce. 那些请不起流行歌星的公司，可以请来一位宇航员，让高管们讲述太空生活的故事ーー准确地说，不是贝女王(Queen Bey) ，但可能会吸引职场中较为书呆子的人。 Many organisers opt for the great outdoors, perhaps in the belief that the sublime will unleash authenticity. Wineries around the world are now expanding to accommodate retreats featuring winemaking lessons; employees stomp grapes. 许多组织者选择户外活动，也许是因为他们相信这种崇高的活动将释放出真实性。世界各地的葡萄酒厂现在正在扩大，以适应以酿酒课程为特色的静修活动; 员工们跺着葡萄。 A Montana ranch offers corporate clients paintball, flag-capturing and dummy-cattle-roping. Butchershop, a brand-strategy agency, held its second summit in Costa Rica; activities included zip-lining, horseback riding through the jungle and jumping off a cliff into the water. 蒙大拿州的一个牧场为企业客户提供彩弹射击、旗帜捕捉和假牛绳。品牌战略机构 Butchershop 在哥斯达黎加举行了第二次峰会，活动包括滑索、马术穿越丛林和从悬崖跳入水中。 A sure-fire way for a business to make its retreat memorable is to thrust participants into adversity. Battling the elements together is supposed to foster team spirit, but zealous organisers have occasionally been known to overdo it. 一家企业要想让自己的撤退令人难忘，一个万无一失的办法就是让参与者陷入逆境。人们认为，一起与各种因素作斗争可以培养团队精神，但热心的组织者偶尔也会做得过火。 One large European company sent executives to the Arctic Circle in midwinter. They endured frigid temperatures for days, without a fresh change of clothes. 一家大型欧洲公司在仲冬派遣高管到北极圈。他们忍受了好几天的严寒，没有换新衣服。 Walking on hot coals—an ancient ritual recast as a team-building exercise—led to the injury of 25 employees of a Swiss ad agency in Zurich. 在滚烫的煤炭上行走导致苏黎世一家瑞士广告公司25名员工受伤。 It is unclear what many days away achieves, except for straining the expense budget and consuming valuable time. Returning to your desk with frostbite or burnt feet is unlikely to boost your productivity. 目前还不清楚多天的时间能达到什么效果，除了紧张的开支预算和消耗宝贵的时间。带着冻伤或者烧伤的脚回到办公桌前不太可能提高你的工作效率。 Even if you escape injury you may have lost esteem for the co-worker who drank too much and delivered a maudlin monologue. Walking on fire with colleagues may be meant to encourage spiritual healing and to put employees and bosses on equal—and equally uncomfortable—footing. 即使你避免了受伤，你可能也会对那个喝得太多、说着伤感独白的同事失去尊重。与同事一起火上浇油可能是为了鼓励精神疗伤，让员工和老板处于平等的地位，同样也让他们感到不舒服。 Yet it is walking through metaphorical fire which actually causes teams to bond. That happens not at a corporate retreat but after years of working together. ■ 然而，正是这种穿越隐喻性火焰的方式，实际上促使团队建立了联系。这种情况不会发生在公司度假村，而是在多年的合作之后。￭ Read more from Bartleby, our columnist on management and work: 阅读更多来自 Bartleby 的文章，我们的管理与工作专栏作家: Businesses’ bottleneck bane (May 18th) 商业瓶颈的祸根(5月18日) How to recruit with softer skills in mind (May 11th) 如何用软技能招聘(5月11日) A short guide to corporate rituals (May 4th) 企业仪式简介(5月4日) Also: How the Bartleby column got its name 还有: 巴特比专栏是如何得名的 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/why-are-corporate-retreats-so-extravagant 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/why-are-corporate-retreats-so-extravagant 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Schumpeter 熊彼特 The tech giants have an interest in AI regulation 科技巨头对人工智能监管很感兴趣 It is a way of holding back open-source proliferation 这是阻止开放源码扩散的一种方式 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 O NE OF THE joys of writing about business is that rare moment when you realise conventions are shifting in front of you. It brings a shiver down the spine. Vaingloriously, you start scribbling down every detail of your surroundings, as if you are drafting the opening lines of a bestseller. 写商业故事的乐趣之一就是当你意识到传统在你面前正在改变的时候。让人脊背发凉。你开始自负地草草记下周围环境的每一个细节，就好像你在起草一本畅销书的开篇。 It happened to your columnist recently in San Francisco, sitting in the pristine offices of Anthropic, a darling of the artificial-intelligence ( AI ) scene. When Jack Clark, one of Anthropic’s co-founders, drew an analogy between the Baruch Plan, a (failed) effort in 1946 to put the world’s atomic weapons under UN control, and the need for global co-ordination to prevent the proliferation of harmful AI , there was that old familiar tingle. When entrepreneurs compare their creations, even tangentially, to nuclear bombs, it feels like a turning point. 你的专栏作家最近在旧金山，坐在人工智能(AI)领域的宠儿 Anthroic 的原始办公室里，就发生了这样的事情。当“人类学”联合创始人之一杰克•克拉克(Jack Clark)将1946年旨在将全球核武器置于联合国控制之下的巴鲁克计划(Baruch Plan)(失败了)与防止有害人工智能扩散的全球协作需求相提并论时，我们听到了那种熟悉的老调重弹。当企业家们将他们的创造物与核弹相比较时，哪怕只是零星的比较，这感觉就像是一个转折点。 Since Chat GPT burst onto the scene late last year there has been no shortage of angst about the existential risks posed by AI. But this is different. Listen to some of the field’s pioneers and they are less worried about a dystopian future when machines outthink humans, and more about the dangers lurking within the stuff they are making now. Chat GPT is an example of “generative” AI , which creates humanlike content based on its analysis of texts, images and sounds on the internet. Sam Altman, CEO of Open AI , the startup that built it, told a congressional hearing this month that regulatory intervention is critical to manage the risks of the increasingly powerful “large language models” ( LLM s) behind the bots. 自从去年年底 ChatGPT 出现以来，人们一直对人工智能带来的生存风险感到不安。但这次不一样。听听这个领域的一些先驱者，他们不那么担心机器智力超过人类的反乌托邦未来，而更担心他们现在制造的东西中潜藏的危险。ChatGPT 是“生成性”人工智能的一个例子，它基于对互联网上的文本、图像和声音的分析，创建类似人类的内容。OpenAI 的首席执行官 Sam Altman 本月在国会听证会上表示，监管干预对于管理机器人背后日益强大的“大型语言模型”(LLM)的风险至关重要。 In the absence of rules, some of his counterparts in San Francisco say they have already set up back channels with government officials in Washington, DC , to discuss the potential harms discovered while examining their chatbots. These include toxic material, such as racism, and dangerous capabilities, like child-grooming or bomb-making. Mustafa Suleyman, co-founder of Inflection AI (and board member of The Economist ’s parent company), plans in coming weeks to offer generous bounties to hackers who can discover vulnerabilities in his firm’s digital talking companion, Pi. 由于缺乏相关规定，他在旧金山的一些同行表示，他们已经与华盛顿特区的政府官员建立了秘密渠道，讨论在检查聊天机器人时发现的潜在危害。这些包括有毒物质，如种族主义，和危险的能力，如儿童美容或制造炸弹。穆斯塔法·苏莱曼是善变人工智能的联合创始人(也是《经济学人》母公司的董事会成员) ，他计划在未来几周向能够发现他公司数字谈话伙伴派(Pi)漏洞的黑客提供慷慨的奖金。 Such caution makes this incipient tech boom look different from the past—at least on the surface. As usual, venture capital is rolling in. But unlike the “move fast and break things” approach of yesteryear, many of the startup pitches now are first and foremost about safety. 这种谨慎态度使得这场初露端倪的科技热潮与过去有所不同ーー至少表面上是这样。像往常一样，风险资本滚滚而来。但是不同于过去“快速行动，打破常规”的方法，现在许多初创公司的宣传首先是关于安全的。 The old Silicon Valley adage about regulation—that it is better to ask for forgiveness than permission—has been jettisoned. Startups such as Open AI , Anthropic and Inflection are so keen to convey the idea that they won’t sacrifice safety just to make money that they have put in place corporate structures that constrain profit-maximisation. 硅谷关于监管的古老格言ーー请求宽恕胜于许可ーー已被抛弃。诸如 OpenAI，Anthropico 和拒绝转变这样的初创公司非常热衷于传达这样一个理念: 他们不会为了赚钱而牺牲安全，他们已经建立了限制利润最大化的公司结构。 Another way in which this boom looks different is that the startups building their proprietary LLM s aren’t aiming to overturn the existing big-tech hierarchy. In fact they may help consolidate it. That is because their relationships with the tech giants leading in the race for generative AI are symbiotic. Open AI is joined at the hip to Microsoft, a big investor that uses the former’s technology to improve its software and search products. 这种繁荣看起来与众不同的另一个方面是，创建自有 LLM 的初创公司并不打算颠覆现有的大型科技公司的等级制度。事实上，他们可能有助于巩固它。这是因为它们与在生成性人工智能领域处于领先地位的科技巨头之间是共生关系。OpenAI 与微软(Microsoft)紧随其后，后者是一家大型投资机构，利用 OpenAI 的技术改进其软件和搜索产品。 Alphabet’s Google has a sizeable stake in Anthropic; on May 23rd the startup announced its latest funding round of $450m, which included more investment from the tech giant. Alphabet 旗下的谷歌在 Anthroic 公司持有相当大的股份; 5月23日，这家初创公司宣布了最新一轮4.5亿美元的融资，其中包括来自这家科技巨头的更多投资。 Making their business ties even tighter, the young firms rely on big tech’s cloud-computing platforms to train their models on oceans of data, which enable the chatbots to behave like human interlocutors. 让他们的业务联系更加紧密，这些年轻的公司依靠大型科技公司的云计算平台来训练他们的模型在海量的数据，这使得聊天机器人的行为像人类对话者。 Like the startups, Microsoft and Google are keen to show they take safety seriously—even as they battle each other fiercely in the chatbot race. They, too, argue that new rules are needed and that international co-operation on overseeing LLM s is essential. As Alphabet’s CEO , Sundar Pichai, put it, “ AI is too important not to regulate, and too important not to regulate well.” 与这些初创企业一样，微软和谷歌也热衷于展示自己对安全的重视ーー尽管它们在聊天机器人(chat bot)竞赛中彼此激烈竞争。他们也认为，需要制定新的规则，监督 LLM 的国际合作至关重要。正如 Alphabet 的首席执行官 Sundar Pichai 所说，“人工智能太重要了，不能不加以监管，也太重要了，不能监管得不好。” Such overtures may be perfectly justified by the risks of misinformation, electoral manipulation, terrorism, job disruption and other potential hazards that increasingly powerful AI models may spawn. Yet it is worth bearing in mind that regulation will also bring benefits to the tech giants. That is because it tends to reinforce existing market structures, creating costs that incumbents find easiest to bear, and raising barriers to entry. 越来越强大的人工智能模型可能产生错误信息、操纵选举、恐怖主义、工作中断以及其他潜在危险，这些风险可能完全证明这种提议是合理的。然而，值得记住的是，监管也会给科技巨头带来好处。这是因为它往往会加强现有的市场结构，创造现有企业认为最容易承受的成本，并提高进入壁垒。 This is important. If big tech uses regulation to fortify its position at the commanding heights of generative AI , there is a trade-off. The giants are more likely to deploy the technology to make their existing products better than to replace them altogether. They will seek to protect their core businesses (enterprise software in Microsoft’s case and search in Google’s). 这很重要。如果大型科技公司利用监管来巩固自己在生成性人工智能领域的制高点地位，那么就存在一种权衡。这些巨头更有可能利用这项技术来改进他们现有的产品，而不是完全取代它们。他们将寻求保护他们的核心业务(微软的企业软件和谷歌的搜索)。 Instead of ushering in an era of Schumpeterian creative destruction, it will serve as a reminder that large incumbents currently control the innovation process—what some call “creative accumulation”. The technology may end up being less revolutionary than it could be. 它不会迎来熊彼特式的创造性破坏时代，而是会提醒人们，大型现有企业目前控制着创新过程ーー一些人称之为“创造性积累”。这项技术的革命性可能不如它本来可能的那么强。 LLaMA on the loose LLama 在逃 Such an outcome is not a foregone conclusion. One of the wild cards is open-source AI , which has proliferated since March when LL a MA , the LLM developed by Meta, leaked online. Already the buzz in Silicon Valley is that open-source developers are able to build generative- AI models that are almost as good as the existing proprietary ones, and hundredths of the cost. 这样的结果并不是预料之中的。其中一个不确定因素是开源人工智能，自从今年3月份 Meta 开发的 LLM LLaMA 在网上泄露以来，这种技术已经大量增加。在硅谷，已经有一种传言称，开源开发人员能够构建几乎与现有专有模型一样好的生成式人工智能模型，而且成本只有现有模型的百分之一。 Anthropic’s Mr Clark describes open-source AI as a “very troubling concept”. Though it is a good way of speeding up innovation, it is also inherently hard to control, whether in the hands of a hostile state or a 17-year-old ransomware-maker. 人类学的克拉克先生将开源人工智能描述为一个“非常令人不安的概念”。尽管这是一种加速创新的好方法，但无论是在敌对国家手中，还是在一个17岁的勒索软件制造商手中，它本质上都难以控制。 Such concerns will be thrashed out as the world’s regulatory bodies grapple with generative AI . Microsoft and Google—and, by extension, their startup charges—have much deeper pockets than open-source developers to handle whatever the regulators come up with. 随着全球监管机构努力应对生成性人工智能问题，这些担忧将得到解决。微软(Microsoft)和谷歌(Google)ーー进一步说，还有它们的创业费用ーー在应对监管机构提出的任何建议方面，它们比开源开发人员拥有更为雄厚的资金。 They also have more at stake in preserving the stability of the information-technology system that has turned them into titans. For once, the desire for safety and for profits may be aligned. ■ 他们还面临着更大的利害关系，即维护信息技术系统的稳定性，正是这个系统让他们成为了巨人。这一次，对安全和利润的渴望可能是一致的。￭ Read more from Schumpeter, our columnist on global business: 阅读更多来自我们的全球商业专栏作家熊彼特的文章: America’s culture wars threaten its single market (May 18th) 美国的文化战争威胁着它的单一市场(5月18日) Writers on strike beware: Hollywood has changed for ever (May 10th) 罢工的作家们要当心: 好莱坞已经永远改变了(5月10日) America needs a jab in its corporate backside (May 3rd) 美国需要在其企业背后打一拳(5月3日) Also: If you want to write directly to Schumpeter, email him at schumpeter@economist.com . And here is an explanation of how the Schumpeter column got its name. 另外: 如果你想直接写信给熊彼特，请发电子邮件至 Schumpeter@economist.com。这里有一个熊彼特专栏如何得名的解释。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/the-tech-giants-have-an-interest-in-ai-regulation 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/business/2023/05/25/the-tech-giants-have-an-interest-in-ai-regulation 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Business 生意 Sections 部分 Science & ... 科学..。 Finance & economics 金融与经济 What happens if America defaults on its debt? 如果美国拖欠债务会发生什么？ The nightmare scenario :: An unimaginable eventuality becomes all too imaginable 噩梦般的场景: 无法想象的可能性变得无法想象 China and Russia compete for Central Asia’s favour 中国和俄罗斯争夺中亚的青睐 Xi v Putin :: The problem is that the former Soviet republics are feeling increasingly flush 习和普京: 问题是前苏联加盟共和国感觉越来越富裕 China’s state capitalists celebrate their soaring shares 中国国有资本家庆祝股价飙升 Quite a coup :: Government-owned firms have enjoyed a renaissance this year 今年，国有企业迎来了复兴 The American credit cycle is at a dangerous point 美国信贷周期正处于危险之中 Buttonwood :: Welcome to a bad time for big debts 梧桐树: 欢迎来到负债累累的坏时代。 What would humans do in a world of super-AI? 在一个超级人工智能的世界里，人类会做什么？ Love and conflict :: A thought experiment based on economic principles 爱与冲突: 基于经济学原理的思维实验 What performance-enhancing stimulants mean for economic growth 提高绩效的兴奋剂对经济增长意味着什么 Free exchange :: Could America’s Adderall shortage have harmed its productivity? 自由交流: 美国的阿得拉短缺会不会损害其生产力？ Business 生意 Sections 部分 Science & ... 科学..。

Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 The nightmare scenario 噩梦般的场景 What happens if America defaults on its debt? 如果美国拖欠债务会发生什么？ An unimaginable eventuality becomes all too imaginable 一个难以想象的可能性变得难以想象 May 22nd 2023 | Washington, DC 2023年5月22日 | 华盛顿特区 T HE AMERICAN CONSTITUTION vests legislative power in Congress. Over the coming days the political body may arrogate to itself a metaphysical power: transforming the utterly unthinkable into hard reality. By failing to raise America’s debt ceiling in time, Congress could drive the country into its first sovereign default in modern history. A collapse in stockmarkets, a surge in unemployment, panic throughout the global economy—all are firmly within the realm of possibility. 美国宪法赋予国会立法权。在接下来的日子里，这个政治机构可能会自诩为一种形而上学的力量: 将完全不可想象的事物转变为严酷的现实。由于未能及时提高美国的债务上限，国会可能会使美国陷入现代历史上的首次主权违约。股市崩盘、失业率飙升、全球经济陷入恐慌ーー所有这些都有可能发生。 How this would happen is straightforward . 这种情况将如何发生很简单。 America has until roughly June 1st to raise its debt limit—a politically determined ceiling on total gross federal borrowing, currently $31.4trn—or it will run out of cash to cover all its obligations, from military salaries to sending cheques to pensioners and making bond payments. 美国必须在大约6月1日之前提高债务上限，这是政治上决定的联邦总借款上限，目前为31.4万亿美元，否则美国将用尽现金来支付所有债务，从军人薪水到给养老金领取者寄支票和支付债券。 The country has faced such deadlines in the past, lulling observers into the belief that it will, once again, raise its debt limit at the last minute. But its politicians are more fractious than during past standoffs. 过去也曾面临过这样的最后期限，让观察人士相信，它将在最后一刻再次提高债务上限。但它的政客们比过去的僵局时期更加暴躁。 Kevin McCarthy, the Republican speaker of the House of Representatives, is pushing for swingeing spending cuts, as he must to keep his narrow, quarrelsome majority together. 众议院议长、共和党人凯文•麦卡锡(Kevin McCarthy)正在推动大幅削减开支，因为他必须维持自己那狭窄、争吵不休的多数党地位。 The Republican proposal—to reset some federal spending in 2024 at the level it was at in 2022—would equate to a roughly 25% cut in real terms, all but demolishing President Joe Biden’s legislative achievements in green-energy investment, and perhaps throwing the economy into a deep recession. 共和党提议在2024年将部分联邦支出重置至2022年的水平，这相当于实际削减约25% ，几乎完全摧毁了乔 · 拜登(Joe Biden)总统在绿色能源投资方面的立法成就，并可能使经济陷入严重衰退。 That, predictably, is a non-starter for Democrats, who are also resisting Republican demands to expand work requirements for welfare recipients. Negotiations have broken down several times over the past week. 可以预见的是，这对民主党来说是不可能的，他们也在抵制共和党提出的扩大福利受益者工作要求的要求。过去一周，谈判多次破裂。 Adding to the complexity is an intervention from Donald Trump, who remarked that the consequences of an American default “could be maybe nothing”—an insouciance that has emboldened the Republican fringe. 唐纳德•特朗普(Donald Trump)的干预增加了问题的复杂性，他表示，美国违约的后果“可能什么都不是”，这种漫不经心的态度让共和党边缘人士更加大胆。 The stockmarket, basically flat over the past month, reflects the common view among investors that a compromise will ultimately be reached. That could well turn out to be complacent. 股市在过去一个月基本持平，反映了投资者的普遍看法，即最终将达成妥协。这很可能会被证明是自满。 Moreover, any agreement may merely extend the debt ceiling by just another year or two, meaning that America is likely once again to find itself in the same mess. 此外，任何协议都可能只是将债务上限再延长一两年，这意味着美国很可能再次发现自己陷入同样的困境。 The Treasury, working with the Federal Reserve, has a fallback plan if Congress does not raise the debt limit. Known as “payment prioritisation”, this would stave off a default by paying interest on bonds and cutting back even more from other obligations. 如果国会不提高债务上限，财政部将与美联储(Fed)合作，制定一项后备计划。这被称为“支付优先权”，通过支付债券利息和削减更多其它债务，将避免违约。 Yet putting bondholders ahead of pensioners and soldiers would be unpalatable, and may prove unsustainable. What’s more, prioritisation would rely on the continued success of regular auctions to replace maturing Treasury bonds. 然而，将债券持有人置于养老金领取者和士兵之前，这种做法令人不快，而且可能被证明是不可持续的。更重要的是，优先顺序将依赖于定期拍卖的持续成功，以取代到期的美国国债。 There is no guarantee that investors would trust such a dysfunctional government. With each passing day, an American default would loom as an ever more serious risk. 不能保证投资者会信任这样一个运转不良的政府。随着时间一天天过去，美国违约将成为一个越来越严重的风险。 Default could come in two flavours: a short crunch or a longer crisis. Although the consequences of both would be baleful, the latter would be much worse. 违约可能有两种形式: 短期危机或长期危机。尽管两者的后果都是有害的，但后者的后果会更糟糕。 Either way, the Fed would have a crucial role to play in containing the fallout; this crucial role would, however, be one of damage-limitation. Every market and economy around the world would feel the pain, regardless of the central bank’s actions. 无论哪种方式，美联储都将在遏制冲击波方面发挥关键作用; 然而，这一关键作用将是损害限制之一。无论央行采取什么行动，世界各地的每个市场和经济体都会感受到痛苦。 America is home to the world’s biggest sovereign-debt market: with $25trn of bonds in public hands, it accounts for about one-third of the global total (see chart 1). 美国拥有世界上最大的主权债务市场: 公众持有25万亿美元的债券，约占全球总量的三分之一(见图表1)。 Treasuries are viewed as the ultimate risk-free asset—offering a guaranteed return for corporate cash managers, governments elsewhere and investors big and small—and as a baseline for pricing other financial instruments. They are the bedrock of daily cash flows. 美国国债被视为最终的无风险资产，为企业现金管理公司、其它地区的政府以及大大小小的投资者提供有保证的回报，也是为其它金融工具定价的基准。它们是每日现金流的基石。 Short-term “repo” lending in America, worth about $4trn a day and a lifeblood for global financial markets, largely runs by using Treasuries as collateral. All this infrastructure would be thrown into doubt. 美国的短期“回购”贷款，每天价值约4万亿美元，是全球金融市场的命脉，主要是以美国国债作为抵押品。所有这些基础设施都会受到质疑。 X-asperation X 射线喷射 By definition, a default would initially be a short-term disruption. An official at the Fed says that it would resemble a liquidity crisis. 按照定义，违约最初将是短期的中断。美联储的一位官员表示，这将类似于一场流动性危机。 Assume that the government defaults on bills and bonds coming due after the “x-date” when it runs out of cash (this is estimated by the Treasury to be June 1st, if not perhaps a little after that, depending on tax receipts). 假设政府在现金耗尽的“ X 日期”之后对到期的票据和债券违约(根据税收收入，财政部估计是6月1日，如果不是稍晚一点的话)。 Demand may still remain firm for debt with later maturities on the assumption that Congress would come to its senses before too long. Indeed, a preview of the divergence can already be seen. 假设国会不久就会恢复理智，那么对于较晚到期的债务，需求可能仍将保持坚挺。实际上，我们已经可以看到分歧的预兆。 Treasury bills due in June currently have annualised yields of about 5.5%; those in August are closer to 5%. This gap would probably widen precipitously in the event of a default. 6月份到期的国债年收益率约为5.5% ，而8月份的国债年收益率更接近5% 。如果发生违约，这一差距可能会急剧扩大。 The cost of insuring against a default on American government debt, as measured by credit-default swaps, has rocketed over the past few months to a record high (see chart 2). 以信用违约互换(CDS)衡量，美国政府债务违约的保险成本在过去几个月里飙升至历史新高(见图表2)。 To start with, the Fed would treat defaulted securities much as it treats normal securities, accepting them as collateral for central-bank loans and potentially even buying them outright. 首先，美联储将像对待普通证券一样对待违约证券，接受它们作为央行贷款的抵押品，甚至有可能直接购买它们。 In effect, the Fed would replace impaired debt with good debt, working on the assumption that the government would make payment on the defaulted securities, just with some delay. 实际上，美联储将以良好债务取代不良债务，假设政府将支付违约证券，只需稍微推迟一些时间。 Although Jerome Powell, chairman of the Fed, described such steps as “loathsome” in 2013, he also said that he would accept them “under certain circumstances”. 尽管美联储主席杰罗姆•鲍威尔(Jerome Powell)在2013年称这些举措“令人厌恶”，但他也表示，“在某些情况下”他会接受这些举措。 The Fed is wary of both inserting itself at the centre of a political dispute and taking actions that seem to directly backstop the federal deficit—in the end, though, its desire to prevent financial chaos would almost certainly override these concerns. 美联储不愿让自己置身于一场政治争端的中心，也不愿采取似乎直接支持联邦赤字的行动ーー不过，最终防止金融混乱的愿望几乎肯定会压倒这些担忧。 The Fed’s response would create a paradox. To the extent that its actions succeed in stabilising markets, they would reduce the need for politicians to compromise. Moreover, running a financial system based, in part, on defaulted securities would pose challenges. 美联储的回应将制造一个悖论。在某种程度上，如果其行动能够成功稳定市场，那么它们将降低政治家妥协的必要性。此外，部分基于违约证券的金融体系的运行将带来挑战。 Fedwire, the settlement system for Treasuries, is programmed to make bills disappear once they pass their maturity date. The Treasury has said that it will intervene to extend the operational maturities of defaulted bills to ensure they remain transferable. 美国国债的结算系统 Fedwire 的程序是，一旦票据过了到期日，就让它们消失。美国财政部表示，将进行干预，延长违约票据的运营期限，以确保它们仍然可以转让。 Yet it is easy to imagine this kind of jerry-rigged system eventually breaking down. At a minimum, investors would demand higher interest to compensate for the risk, leading to a tightening of credit conditions throughout global markets. 然而，人们很容易想象这种偷工减料的体系最终会崩溃。至少，投资者会要求提高利率以补偿风险，从而导致全球市场信贷环境收紧。 However this process works out, America would already be in the throes of extreme fiscal austerity. 无论这个过程如何进行，美国都已经处于极端财政紧缩的阵痛之中。 The government would be unable to borrow more money, meaning it would have to cut spending by the gap between current tax revenues and expenditures—an overnight reduction of around 25%, according to analysts at the Brookings Institution, a think-tank. 政府将无法借到更多的钱，这意味着它将不得不通过当前税收收入与支出之间的差距来削减开支——据布鲁金斯学会分析师称，这一差距将在一夜之间缩减约25% 。 Moody’s Analytics, a research outfit, estimates that in the immediate aftermath of a default, America’s economy would shrink by nearly 1% and its unemployment rate would rise from 3.4% to 5%, putting about 1.5m people out of work. 研究机构穆迪分析(Moody’s Analytics)估计，一旦发生违约，美国经济将收缩近1% ，失业率将从3.4% 上升至5% ，导致大约150万人失业。 In the short-term scenario, Congress responds by raising the debt ceiling, allowing markets to recover. A default that lasts for a few days would be a black eye for America’s reputation and probably induce a recession. 在短期情况下，国会的回应是提高债务上限，让市场得以复苏。如果违约持续几天，美国的声誉将蒙上一层黑眼圈，并可能引发经济衰退。 Yet with deft management, it would not be the stuff of nightmares. 然而，有了灵活的管理，这将不会是噩梦的东西。 A longer default would be more dangerous. Mark Zandi of Moody’s calls it a potential “ TARP moment”, referring to the autumn of 2008 when Congress initially failed to pass the Troubled Asset Relief Programme to bail out the banks, prompting global markets to crater. Continued failure to lift the debt ceiling, even after a default occurs, could have a similar impact. 延长违约期将更加危险。穆迪(Moody’s)的马克•赞迪(Mark Zandi)称之为潜在的“不良资产救助计划时刻”，他指的是2008年秋天，当时国会最初未能通过问题资产救助计划(TARP)来纾困银行，导致全球市场崩盘。即使在违约发生之后，仍然无法提高债务上限，也可能产生类似的影响。 The Council of Economic Advisers, an agency in the White House, estimates that in the first few months of a breach, the stockmarket would slump by 45%. 白宫经济顾问委员会(Council of Economic Advisers)估计，在违约的头几个月，股市将暴跌45% 。 Moody’s reckons it would fall by about 20%, and that unemployment would shoot up by five percentage points, which would mean somewhere in the region of 8m Americans losing their jobs. 穆迪估计，失业率将下降20% 左右，失业率将上升5个百分点，这意味着大约800万美国人将失去工作。 The government, constrained by the debt ceiling, would be unable to respond to the downturn with fiscal stimulus, making for a deeper recession. The political dynamics are difficult to anticipate—but they would not be pleasant. 由于债务上限的限制，政府将无法通过财政刺激来应对经济低迷，从而导致更严重的经济衰退。政治动态很难预料，但它们不会令人愉快。 An avalanche of credit downgrades would add to these troubles. In 2011, during a previous debt-ceiling standoff, Standard & Poor’s, a rating agency, downgraded America to a notch below its top AAA rating. After a default, rating agencies would be under immense pressure to do likewise. This could lead to a nasty chain reaction. 信用评级大幅下调将加剧这些麻烦。2011年，在上一次债务上限僵局期间，评级机构标准普尔(Standard & Poor’s)将美国的信用评级下调至低于其最高 AAA 评级的一级。在违约之后，评级机构将面临巨大压力，要求它们采取同样的做法。这可能会导致严重的连锁反应。 Institutions backstopped by the American government, including Fannie Mae, a crucial source of mortgage finance, would also be downgraded, translating into higher mortgage rates and undercutting the all-important property sector. 得到美国政府支持的机构，包括抵押贷款融资的重要来源房利美(Fannie Mae) ，也将被降级，从而导致抵押贷款利率上升，并削弱至关重要的房地产行业。 Yields on corporate bonds would spike as investors scrambled for cash. Banks would pull back their lending. Panic would spread. 随着投资者争抢现金，公司债券收益率将飙升。银行将收回放贷。恐慌将蔓延。 There would be bizarre, unpredictable twists. Normally, the currencies of defaulting countries suffer. In the case of an American breach, investors might flock at first to the dollar, viewing it as a haven in crisis, as is normally the case. 会有奇怪的，不可预测的曲折。通常情况下，违约国家的货币会受到影响。在美国违约的情况下，投资者可能会首先涌向美元，将其视为危机中的避风港，这种情况通常都会发生。 Within America, people might turn to deposits at too-big-to-fail banks, believing the Fed would stand behind them whatever happened. But any signs of resilience would carry a huge caveat: America would have violated the trust that the world has long placed in it. 在美国国内，人们可能会转向“大到不能倒”的银行存款，认为无论发生什么，美联储都会支持他们。但是，任何复苏的迹象都会带来一个巨大的警告: 美国将违背世界长期以来对它的信任。 Questions about alternatives to the dollar and to the American financial system would gain urgency. Faith, once destroyed, cannot easily be restored. ■ 有关替代美元和美国金融体系的问题将变得更加紧迫。信仰一旦被摧毁，就无法轻易恢复。￭ 八点一刻杂志网 www.badianyike.com For more expert analysis of the biggest stories in economics, finance and markets, sign up to Money Talks , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter. 如果你想了解更多关于经济、金融和市场方面的重大新闻的专家分析，请注册我们的每周订阅时事通讯 Money Talks。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/finance-and-economics/2023/05/22/what-happens-if-america-defaults-on-its-debt 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/finance-and-economics/2023/05/22/what-happens-if-america-defaults-on-its-debt 下载的 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Xi v Putin 习 VS 普京 China and Russia compete for Central Asia’s favour 中国和俄罗斯争夺中亚的青睐 The problem is that the former Soviet republics are feeling increasingly flush 问题在于，前苏联加盟共和国感觉越来越富裕 May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 C ENTRAL ASIA’S top brass is on tour. On May 9th presidents of the region’s former Soviet republics were in Moscow, having been roped in as spectators for Vladimir Putin’s annual military parade. Ten days later, as G 7 leaders gathered in Japan, it was Xi Jinping’s turn to play host, in Xi’an. At the first in-person meeting of a new group that China has dubbed the C + C 5, he wooed Central’s Asia’s five leaders with a bonanza of investments, trade deals and knowledge transfers, to the tune of $3.8bn. 中亚高层正在巡回演出。5月9日，该地区前苏联加盟共和国的总统在莫斯科，作为弗拉基米尔 · 普京一年一度的阅兵式的观众。10天后，七国集团(G7)领导人齐聚日本，轮到习近平在西安主持会议。在一个被中国称为“ C + C 5”的新集团的首次面对面会议上，他通过投资、贸易协议和知识转移等丰富多彩的手段，争取到了中亚五国领导人的支持，金额高达38亿美元。 Central Asia is home to untapped natural gas and oil reserves, supplies of copper and uranium, and 10,300km of land borders with China and Russia. Outreach to the region has occupied Beijing and Moscow since the fall of the Soviet Union. 中亚拥有尚未开发的天然气和石油储量、铜和铀的供应，以及与中国和俄罗斯长达1.03万公里的陆地边界。自苏联解体以来，对该地区的外联活动一直占据着北京和莫斯科。 But owing to uncertainty in Afghanistan and war in Ukraine, Chinese officials have taken a particular interest of late. 但由于阿富汗的不确定性和乌克兰的战争，中国官员最近对此特别感兴趣。 Security was top of the agenda in Xi’an, upsetting a long-term compromise in which the region’s armed forces move in lockstep with Moscow while Beijing pulls the region’s pursestrings by bankrolling colossal projects. 安全问题是西安会议的首要议题，这打破了一项长期妥协: 该地区的武装力量与莫斯科步调一致，而北京方面通过资助大型项目来控制该地区的资金。 The prizes offered by Mr Xi add to a push already under way to strengthen China’s economic ties with Central Asia. Beijing is involved in more than 90 industrial projects across the region. Turkmen gas alone accounts for more than 70% of the country’s annual gas imports. 习提供的这些奖项进一步推动了中国加强与中亚经济联系的努力。中国政府参与了该地区90多个工业项目。仅土库曼斯坦的天然气就占该国年度天然气进口量的70% 以上。 In 2022 China was Central Asia’s largest trading partner, as the value of imports and exports between the two parties rose to $70bn—up more than 40% from the year before, according to official Chinese figures. 中国官方数据显示，2022年，中国是中亚最大的贸易伙伴，双方的进出口额增至700亿美元，比前一年增长逾40% 。 By contrast, trade between Russia and Central Asia amounted to less than $40bn. 相比之下，俄罗斯与中亚之间的贸易额不到400亿美元。 Mr Putin has stepped up his overtures, partly to counter Mr Xi. Russia has promised a slew of infrastructure investments. Competition is particularly fierce to build transport for the region’s natural resources, many of which are trapped underground. 普京加大了示好的力度，部分原因是为了对抗习。俄罗斯已承诺进行大量基础设施投资。在为该地区的自然资源建设交通运输方面，竞争尤其激烈，其中许多资源被困在地下。 In Xi’an Mr Xi offered to construct a pipeline, the fourth of its kind, to ferry gas eastward from Kazakhstan, Turkmenistan and Uzbekistan. 在西安，习主席提出建设一条管道，从哈萨克斯坦、土库曼斯坦和乌兹别克斯坦向东输送天然气，这是此类管道中的第四条。 Russia wants as much fuel as possible flowing through its borders in the Caspian and Central pipeline systems, which it has the power to turn on and off. In the words of an academic in Moscow, “there is a silent stand-off.” 俄罗斯希望尽可能多的燃料通过里海和中央管道系统的边界流入俄罗斯，俄罗斯有能力开启和关闭这些管道系统。用莫斯科一位学者的话来说，“这是一场无声的对峙。” Mr Putin’s diplomacy also has other motivations. Necessity has drawn Russia closer to its southern neighbours. War in Ukraine has left Mr Putin desperate to hold on to remaining allies, even though politicians in Central Asia have avoided openly supporting his invasion. 普京先生的外交还有其他动机。必要性使俄罗斯更加接近其南部邻国。乌克兰战争使得普京先生不顾一切地想要留住他的盟友，尽管中亚的政治家们一直避免公开支持他的入侵。 Moscow is newly reliant on Central Asia to circumvent sanctions. Central bankers in Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan help settle payments, using gold and roubles, for transactions that before the war would have been made using SWIFT , a Western system. Logistics bosses and customs officials receive Russian exports before passing them on to Europe. Such intermediated trade counted for 4-6% of Kyrgyzstan’s GDP last year. 莫斯科最近依赖中亚来规避制裁。哈萨克斯坦和乌兹别克斯坦的中央银行家帮助用黄金和卢布结算战前本应使用西方系统 SWIFT 进行的交易。物流主管和海关官员接收俄罗斯出口商品，然后再转运到欧洲。这种中间贸易占去年吉尔吉斯斯坦国内生产总值的4-6% 。 The result is that Central Asia’s economies are booming. Currencies in Kazakhstan, Tajikistan and Uzbekistan have risen against the dollar since the war began, bucking an emerging-market trend. 其结果是，中亚地区的经济正在蓬勃发展。自战争开始以来，哈萨克斯坦、塔吉克斯坦和乌兹别克斯坦的货币兑美元汇率一直在上升，这与新兴市场的趋势背道而驰。 The European Bank for Reconstruction and Development, a multilateral outfit, predicts that industries thriving on transporting Russian exports will contribute to GDP growth of 5.2% in the region this year, comfortably outstripping expectations. Capital, firms and people fleeing Russia, many of whom are highly educated, may well produce an even healthier 2024. 欧洲复兴开发银行是一家多边机构，它预测，依靠运输俄罗斯出口产品而蓬勃发展的工业今年将为该地区贡献5.2% 的 GDP 增长，远远超出预期。逃离俄罗斯的资本、企业和人员(其中许多人受过高等教育)很可能创造出一个更加健康的2024年。 Frenetic business has sent pay soaring, too. In November, annual real wage growth reached 10% in Uzbekistan and 7% in Kazakhstan, meaning the region’s workers have seen more of a lift than those in any G 7 country. Newly flush households will spend more on food and energy during Central Asia’s freezing winters. Strong domestic demand has left the region’s governments struggling to keep up. 狂热的生意也导致了工资的飙升。去年11月，乌兹别克斯坦和哈萨克斯坦的实际工资年增长率分别达到10% 和7% ，这意味着该地区的工人比七国集团中任何一个国家的工人都有更大的提升。在中亚寒冷的冬季，新富家庭将在食品和能源上花费更多。强劲的国内需求让该地区各国政府难以跟上。 Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan both paused gas exports in January, with a promise to bring them to a complete end by 2025. Whoever wins the charm offensive to build Central Asia’s new pipelines may find there is no fuel available to fill them. ■ 哈萨克斯坦和乌兹别克斯坦均在今年1月暂停天然气出口，并承诺在2025年前完全停止出口。无论谁在建设中亚新管道的魅力攻势中获胜，都可能发现没有可用的燃料来填充这些管道。￭ For more expert analysis of the biggest stories in economics, finance and markets, sign up to Money Talks , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter. 如果你想了解更多关于经济、金融和市场方面的重大新闻的专家分析，请注册我们的每周订阅时事通讯 Money Talks。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/finance-and-economics/2023/05/25/china-and-russia-compete-for-central-asias-favour 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/finance-and-economics/2023/05/25/china-and-russia-compete-for-central-asias-favour 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Quite a coup 真是惊天动地 China’s state capitalists celebrate their soaring shares 中国国有资本家庆祝股价飙升 Government-owned firms have enjoyed a renaissance this year 国有企业今年迎来了复兴 May 25th 2023 | Shanghai 2023年5月25日 | 上海 R EMEMBER THE name : China Science Publishing & Media ( CSPM ) might be the hottest state-owned textbook stock, ever. Shares in the company, which publishes titles such as “Gaseous Detonation Physics and its Universal Framework Theory”, are up 230% since the start of the year. It is not the content, riveting though it is, that has lifted CSPM ’s market capitalisation to nearly 30bn yuan ($4.3bn). Across the board, China’s state-owned companies have enjoyed a renaissance—at the request of regulators.
