Stop focusing on profits, authorities have insisted. 记住这个名字: 中国科学出版传媒(CSPM)可能是有史以来最热门的国有教科书股票。自今年年初以来，该公司股价上涨了230% 。该公司出版了《气体爆炸物理学及其宇宙框架理论》等书籍。尽管内容引人入胜，但并不是它将 CSPM 的市值提升至近300亿元人民币(合43亿美元)。在监管机构的要求下，中国国有企业总体上实现了复兴。当局坚称，不要只关注利润。 Instead, think about firms’ social contributions and their broader impact on the economy. All told, the “reconsideration” of China’s clunkiest firms has been worth 3trn yuan in the first five months of the year. 相反，考虑一下企业的社会贡献及其对经济的广泛影响。总而言之，今年前五个月对中国最笨拙的公司的“重新考虑”价值3万亿元。 The success of the concept—called the “valuation system with Chinese characteristics”—is quite the coup for the country’s state capitalists. It suggests an ability to guide investment flows. 这个被称为“中国特色价值体系”的概念获得成功，对中国的国有资本家来说是一个相当大的成功。它表明了引导投资流动的能力。 First mentioned in November by Yi Huiman, head of the China Securities Regulatory Commission, the system’s principles continue to evolve. 去年11月，中国证监会(csrc)主席易会满首次提到，该体系的原则仍在不断演变。 At their core is the idea that standard valuation methods are poor at assessing state-owned companies because such firms adhere to central-government policies which seek to improve overall economic prosperity, not simply a firm’s bottom line. 其核心观点是，标准估值方法在评估国有企业方面很差，因为这些企业遵循的是中央政府寻求改善整体经济繁荣的政策，而不仅仅是企业的底线。 China’s state-owned enterprises ( SOE s) have a reputation for poor performance and stingy dividends. Their market valuations have reflected this. Part of the problem, say proponents of the new system, is that companies have communicated poorly with investors. Some have even asked analysts not to cover them. 中国国有企业(SOE)素有业绩不佳和股息吝啬的名声。它们的市场估值反映了这一点。新体系的支持者表示，部分问题在于，企业与投资者沟通不畅。一些人甚至要求分析师不要报道这些问题。 Now the firms should help investors understand their “intrinsic values”, Mr Yi has said. No method for doing this has been divulged, but investors speculate that these values include local employment and the hesitancy at many SOE s to lay off unproductive staff. 易先生说，现在这些公司应该帮助投资者理解他们的“内在价值”。目前尚未披露具体方法，但投资者猜测，这些价值观包括当地就业，以及许多国有企业不愿裁减效率低下的员工。 This sounds strikingly similar to what investors grudgingly call “national service”, or sacrificing profits to boost economic growth. 这听起来与投资者不情愿地称之为“国民服务”的做法惊人地相似，即牺牲利润来促进经济增长。 China’s largest banks are often asked to do this by lowering interest rates on loans to risky borrowers—defying all commercial logic—in the hopes of spurring growth. 中国大型银行经常被要求降低面向高风险借款人的贷款利率，以期刺激经济增长。这种做法违背了所有商业逻辑。 Kweichow Moutai, a state-owned liquor-maker and one of China’s most valuable listed firms, frequently spends on public works and has even started bailing out local governments in its province. By classic valuation models, this is all detrimental to shareholder value. 贵州茅台是一家国有白酒生产商，也是中国市值最高的上市公司之一。该公司经常投资于公共工程，甚至已经开始为该省的地方政府纾困。根据传统的估值模型，这些都不利于股东价值。 The biggest beneficiary of the new system may be pensioners. China faces a gaping shortfall in its ability to support the elderly. Poor demographics and an early retirement age are exacerbating the problem. 新制度的最大受益者可能是退休人员。中国养老能力严重不足。糟糕的人口结构和提前退休年龄加剧了这一问题。 Officials are transferring trillions of yuan in state-owned capital to the national pension fund as part of a reform process. A boost in the valuation of the firms could eventually help the state meet its pension obligations. The new valuation system, says Meng Lei of UBS , a bank, is an attempt to help investors understand the broader implications of this transfer of state-owned capital to pensioners. 作为改革进程的一部分，官员们正在将数万亿元的国有资本转移到国家养老基金。这些公司估值的提高最终可能有助于国家履行其养老金义务。瑞银(UBS)的孟磊表示，新的估值体系旨在帮助投资者理解国有资本向养老金领取者转移的更广泛影响。 Is there a more traditional case for investment in companies such as CSPM ? After a surge of excitement this year, state firms’ share prices are falling again. A new index tracking them is down by 11% since its peak on May 8th. The 291 state-owned shares that are included in the China coverage of MSCI , an index operator, trade at around six times forward earnings, or close to all-time lows, according to Goldman Sachs, a bank. This is about half the ratio for non-state companies. 对于投资于 CSPM 这样的公司，是否存在更为传统的情况？在经历了今年的高涨之后，国有企业的股价再次下跌。一个跟踪他们的新指数比5月8日的峰值下降了11% 。根据高盛(Goldman Sachs)的数据，纳入 MSCI 明晟中国指数覆盖范围的291只国有股的预期市盈率约为6倍，接近历史低点。这一比例约为非国有企业的一半。 Even before the recent fall, SOE s were trading at a hefty discount. Officials now hope to match their fine words with actions. The regulator that oversees state assets recently switched its main gauge for evaluating company performance from net profits to return on equity. Analysts at CICC , a Chinese investment bank, reckon this might help lift capital returns and operating results. In which case, the interests of state capitalists and private investors would be better aligned than at present. ■ 甚至在最近的下跌之前，国有企业的股价就已大幅下跌。官员们现在希望言行一致。负责监管国有资产的监管机构最近将评估公司业绩的主要指标从净利润转向了股本回报率。中国投资银行中金公司(CICC)的分析师认为，这可能有助于提高资本回报率和经营业绩。在这种情况下，国家资本家和私人投资者的利益将比目前更加一致。￭ For more expert analysis of the biggest stories in economics, finance and markets, sign up to Money Talks , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter. 如果你想了解更多关于经济、金融和市场方面的重大新闻的专家分析，请注册我们的每周订阅时事通讯 Money Talks。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/finance-and-economics/2023/05/25/chinas-state-capitalists-celebrate-their-soaring-shares 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/finance-and-economics/2023/05/25/chinas-state-capitalists-celebrate-their-soaring-shares 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Buttonwood 梧桐树 The American credit cycle is at a dangerous point 美国信贷周期正处于危险之中 Welcome to a bad time for big debts 欢迎来到负债累累的时代 May 24th 2023 | 2023年5月24日 T HE CELEBRATED tome “Capital in the Twenty-First Century”, by Thomas Piketty, a French economist, runs to 204,000 words—longer even than Homer’s “Odyssey”. But the book’s central argument can be distilled to a single, three-character expression: r > g. As long as “r”, the real rate of return to capital, exceeds “g”, the real rate of economic growth—as Mr Piketty calculated it did over the course of the 20th century—then inequality will supposedly widen. 法国经济学家托马斯 · 皮凯蒂的著作《二十一世纪资本论》长达20.4万字，比荷马的《奥德赛》还长。但是，这本书的核心论点可以浓缩为一个单一的三个字符的表达式: r > g。只要“ r”(实际资本回报率)超过“ g”(实际经济增长率)(正如皮凯蒂先生在20世纪所计算的那样) ，那么不平等就会扩大。 The simplicity of the message won Mr Piketty widespread acclaim. It also spawned a resurgence in the popularity of economic expressions. An influential one, i > g, is a variation on the Piketty rule. 信息的简单性赢得了皮凯蒂先生的广泛赞誉。它还催生了经济表达方式的复兴。一个有影响力的，i > g，是 Piketty 规则的一个变体。 It applies when nominal interest rates (or risk-free returns) exceed nominal growth. The troubling conclusion from this expression applies to debt. 当名义利率(或无风险收益率)超过名义增长率时，它就适用。从这个表达式得出的令人不安的结论适用于债务。 In an i > g world, growth in the revenues, wages or tax receipts that a debtor earns will be slower than the interest accumulating on their borrowing, meaning debt levels have the potential to explode. 在一个 i > g 的世界里，债务人收入、工资或税收收入的增长速度将低于其借款利息的累积速度，这意味着债务水平有爆炸性增长的潜力。 An i > g world is unfamiliar to America and most of the West. Since the end of 2009 nominal growth has been higher than nominal rates (aside from the first half of 2020, when the covid-19 pandemic crashed the economy). Now America is about to cross the threshold. 对于美国和大多数西方国家来说，这是一个陌生的世界。自2009年底以来，名义增长率一直高于名义增长率(2020年上半年除外，当时2019冠状病毒疾病疫情导致经济崩溃)。现在美国即将跨过这道门槛。 In the first quarter of 2023, despite annualised real economic growth of only 1.1%, troublesomely high inflation meant that nominal GDP rose at an annualised rate of 5.1%, roughly in line with today’s federal funds rate. A panel of economists surveyed by Bloomberg, a data firm, anticipate that in the second quarter of the year growth will slip to just 0.4% and inflation to 3.3%. 2023年第一季度，尽管实际经济年增长率仅为1.1% ，但令人头疼的高通胀率意味着名义 GDP 年增长率为5.1% ，与今天的联邦基金利率大致相当。接受彭博社(Bloomberg)调查的一组经济学家预计，今年第二季度的经济增长率将下滑至0.4% ，通胀率将降至3.3% 。 Nominal growth is forecast to be just 3.7%—well below nominal rates of around 5.2%. 预计名义增长率仅为3.7% ，远低于5.2% 左右的名义增长率。 “This is when the rubber really meets the road for the economic cycle,” notes Carl Riccadonna of BNP Paribas, a bank. “This is the point at which, if you’re a business, your revenues are now growing more slowly than your cost of financing.” Wage growth will lag debt growth. Government debts will grow faster than tax receipts. A single quarter of this might be bearable. 法国巴黎银行(BNP Paribas)的卡尔•里卡多纳(Carl Riccadonna)指出: “这是经济周期真正到来的时候。”。“如果你是一家企业，现在就是这个时候，你的收入增长慢于融资成本。”工资增长将滞后于债务增长。政府债务的增长速度将超过税收收入的增长速度。这其中的四分之一也许是可以忍受的。 Unfortunately, economists expect the situation to last a year or more. 不幸的是，经济学家预计这种情况将持续一年或更长时间。 The precise impact depends on the extent to which debt reprices as interest rates rise. The vast majority of American homeowners have 30-year fixed-rate mortgages. 确切的影响取决于随着利率上升，债务重新定价的程度。绝大多数美国房主都有30年期的固定利率抵押贷款。 This generous financing will protect them against a pincer-like combo of slowing wage growth and rising interest expenses. Nevertheless, consumers carrying other kinds of debt—including revolving credit-card balances and private student loans—will feel the pinch. 这种慷慨的融资将保护他们免受工资增长放缓和利息支出上升的夹击。然而，背负着其他种类债务(包括信用卡周转余额和私人助学贷款)的消费者将感到手头拮据。 Many companies hold a mix of fixed- and floating-rate debt, meaning they will also be somewhat insulated. But the maturities of their debts tend to be much shorter than those of mortgages. A large portion of corporate fixed-rate debt is due to roll over in 2024. Companies that are preparing to refinance are getting nervous. 许多公司持有固定利率债务和浮动利率债务的组合，这意味着它们也将在一定程度上得到隔离。但它们的债务期限往往比抵押贷款期限短得多。很大一部分企业固定利率债务将于2024年展期。准备进行再融资的公司正变得紧张起来。 Raphael Bejarano of Jefferies, an investment bank, points out that many corporate treasurers have been spooked by just how difficult it has been to issue debt over the past year. 投资银行杰富瑞(Jefferies)的拉斐尔•贝贾拉诺(Raphael Bejarano)指出，过去一年发行债券的难度之大，令许多企业财务主管感到恐慌。 “Many of them are looking at their big maturities in 2024 and trying to roll some of that debt a little earlier, even at higher rates,” he says. “他们中的许多人正在考虑2024年的大期限，并试图提前一点滚动部分债务，即便利率更高,”他表示。 What they really fear is being unable to roll their debt over at all. 他们真正担心的是根本无法展期债务。 The most-exposed companies include many that have been recently snapped up by private-equity barons. Private-credit loans taken on by their firms’ portfolio companies tend to have floating rates. 风险敞口最大的公司包括许多最近被私募股权巨头收购的公司。由公司投资组合公司承担的私人信贷贷款往往有浮动利率。 During the last major credit cycle, in 2008, many private-equity firms were able to hang on to their overleveraged acquisitions by negotiating with lenders, which were mostly banks. 在2008年的上一个主要信贷周期中，许多私募股权公司通过与贷款机构(主要是银行)谈判，得以保住他们过度杠杆化的收购。 This time around they will be going toe-to-toe with private-credit lenders, many of which also employ hefty private-equity teams and will be more than happy to take on overleveraged firms. In a sign of what may be to come, on May 16th KKR , a giant private-assets firm, allowed Envision Healthcare, a portfolio company in which it invested $3.5bn at a $10bn valuation in 2018, to fall into bankruptcy and be seized by its lenders. 这一次，他们将与私人信贷贷款机构展开直接对抗，其中许多机构还雇佣了大量私人股本团队，并且非常乐意与负债过高的公司展开较量。5月16日，KKR，一家大型私人资产公司，允许 Envision Healthcare，一家投资组合公司，在2018年以100亿美元的估值投资了35亿美元，陷入破产并被债权人接管。 When surveying this scene, it is reassuring to note that interest rates have been high for a while, the American economy has fared pretty well and even bank failures seem to have represented a flesh wound rather than a fatal one. 在调查这一现象时，我们可以放心地注意到，利率已经高企了一段时间，美国经济表现相当不错，甚至银行倒闭似乎只是皮肉伤，而不是致命伤。 But all of this has happened in a different context. It is far easier to swallow a high cost of capital when it is matched by high returns on said capital. And that will not be the case for much longer. ■ 但这一切都发生在不同的背景下。当资本成本与资本的高回报率相匹配时，就更容易接受高成本的资本。这种情况不会持续太久。￭ 八点一刻杂志网 www.badianyike.com Read more from Buttonwood, our columnist on financial markets: 阅读更多来自我们的金融市场专栏作家 Buttonwood 的文章: How to invest in artificial intelligence (May 17th) 如何投资人工智能(5月17日) Investors brace for a painful crash into America’s debt ceiling (May 10th) 投资者准备好迎接美国债务上限的痛苦崩溃(5月10日) What the First Republic deal means for America’s banks (May 3rd) 第一共和国协议对美国银行意味着什么(5月3日) Also: How the Buttonwood column got its name 还有: Buttonwood 栏目是如何得名的 Correction (May 25th 2023) : This article originally misstated the title of Mr Piketty’s book and America’s economic growth. Sorry. 更正(2023年5月25日) : 本文最初错误地标题皮凯蒂先生的书和美国的经济增长。对不起。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/finance-and-economics/2023/05/24/the-american-credit-cycle-is-at-a-dangerous-point 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/finance-and-economics/2023/05/24/the-american-credit-cycle-is-at-a-dangerous-point 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Love and conflict 爱与冲突 What would humans do in a world of super-AI? 在一个超级人工智能的世界里，人类会做什么？ A thought experiment based on economic principles 基于经济学原理的思维实验 May 23rd 2023 | 2023年5月23日 I N “WALL-E” , a film released in 2008, humans live in what could be described as a world of fully automated luxury communism. Artificially intelligent robots, which take wonderfully diverse forms, are responsible for all productive labour. 在2008年上映的电影《机器人总动员》中，人类生活在一个可以被描述为完全自动化的奢侈共产主义世界。人工智能机器人形态多样，负责所有的生产劳动。 People get fat, hover in armchairs and watch television. The “Culture” series by Iain M. Banks , a Scottish novelist, goes further, considering a world in which AI has grown sufficiently powerful as to be superintelligent—operating far beyond anything now foreseeable. The books are favourites of Jeff Bezos and Elon Musk, the bosses of Amazon and Tesla, respectively. In the world spun by Banks, scarcity is a thing of the past and AI “minds” direct most production. Humans turn to art, explore the cultures of the vast universe and indulge in straightforwardly hedonistic pleasures. 人们变胖，坐在扶手椅上，看电视。苏格兰小说家伊恩 · 班克斯(Iain M. Banks)的《文化》(Culture)系列更进一步，认为在这个世界上，人工智能已经变得足够强大，可以成为超级智能，其运行速度远远超出了目前可预见的范围。这两本书分别是亚马逊(Amazon)和特斯拉(Tesla)的老板杰夫•贝佐斯(Jeff Bezos)和埃隆•马斯克(Elon Musk)的最爱。在银行主导的世界里，稀缺已经成为过去，人工智能“思想”指导着大多数生产。人类求助于艺术，探索浩瀚宇宙的文化，沉溺于直截了当的享乐之中。 Such stories may seem far-fetched. But rapid progress in generative AI —the sort that underpins Open AI ’s popular chatbot, Chat GPT —has caused many to take them more seriously. On May 22nd Open AI ’s founders published a blog post saying that “it’s conceivable that within the next ten years, AI systems will exceed expert skill level in most domains, and carry out as much productive activity as one of today’s largest corporations.” Last summer forecasters on Metaculus, an online prediction platform that is a favourite of many techies, thought it would take until the early 2040s to produce an AI capable of tricking humans into thinking that it was human after a two-hour chat, had good enough robotic capabilities to assemble a model car and could pass various other challenging cognitive tests. After a year of astonishing AI breakthroughs, Metaculus forecasters now think that this will happen by the early 2030s. There is no shortage of money for research, either. Five new generative- AI unicorns (startups valued at $1bn or more) have already been minted this year. 这样的故事似乎有些牵强。但是，生成性人工智能的快速进步(OpenAI 广受欢迎的聊天机器人 ChatGPT 的基础就是这种进步)已经让许多人更加认真地对待它们。5月22日，OpenAI 的创始人发表了一篇博客文章，称“可以想象，在未来十年内，人工智能系统将超越大多数领域的专家技能水平，进行与当今最大的公司之一一样多的生产活动。”去年夏天，许多技术人士最喜欢的在线预测平台 Metaculus 的预测者们认为，要到本世纪40年代初，才能研发出一种人工智能，能够在两小时的聊天之后让人类误以为自己是人类，拥有足够好的机器人能力来组装一辆模型汽车，并能通过其它各种具有挑战性的认知测试。在人工智能取得了一年的惊人突破之后，Metaculus 的预测者们现在认为，这种情况将在本世纪30年代初出现。用于研究的资金也不短缺。今年已经有5家新的生成型人工智能独角兽公司(估值在10亿美元以上的初创公司)诞生。 The road to a general AI —one better than the very best of humanity at everything—could take longer than expected . Nevertheless, the rising possibility of ultra-powerful AI raises the question of what would be left for humans when it arrives. Would they become couch potatoes as in “Wall-E”? Here is a thought experiment, guided by the principles of economics, to provide something of an answer. 通往普通人工智能的道路可能比预期的更漫长。普通人工智能比人类最优秀的人类在任何方面都做得更好。尽管如此，超级强大的人工智能的可能性日益增加，这提出了一个问题: 当它到来时，人类还剩下什么。他们会像《机器人瓦力》里那样变成电视迷吗？在经济学原理的指导下，这里有一个思想实验，可以提供一些答案。 AI is your oyster 人工智能就是你的牡蛎 Inevitably, such a thought experiment involves some fairly heroic assumptions. For a start, we suppose that AI will be benevolent, controllable and distinguishable from humans. We also suppose that human culture will not be radically altered by technological progress to the point that people begin to love or even worship AI s. Instead, we imagine AI as a tool: a virtual, super-smart, dirt-cheap bot. We assume that constraints on the widespread use of AI , such as energy limits, will be resolved. None of this is guaranteed, but it helps make an exercise like this possible. 不可避免地，这样一个思想实验涉及到一些相当英勇的假设。首先，我们假设人工智能将是仁慈的、可控的，并且可以与人类区分开来。我们还认为，技术进步不会从根本上改变人类文化，以至于人们开始喜欢甚至崇拜人工智能。相反，我们把人工智能想象成一个工具: 一个虚拟的、超级聪明的、非常便宜的机器人。我们假设，对人工智能广泛应用的限制，如能源限制，将得到解决。这些都不能保证，但它有助于使这样的练习成为可能。 In 2019 Philippe Aghion, Ben Jones and Chad Jones, three economists, modelled the impact of AI . They found that explosive economic growth was plausible if AI could be used to automate all production, including the process of research itself—and thus self-improve. A nearly unlimited number of AI s could work together on any given problem, opening up vast scientific possibilities. Yet their modelling carried an important caveat. If AI automated most but not all production, or most but not all of the research process, growth would not take off. As the economists put it: “Economic growth may be constrained not by what we do well but rather by what is essential and yet hard to improve.” 2019年，三位经济学家菲利普•阿格翁(Philippe Aghon)、本•琼斯(Ben Jones)和查德•琼斯(Chad Jones)对人工智能的影响进行了建模。他们发现，如果人工智能可以用来实现所有生产自动化，包括研究过程本身，从而实现自我完善，那么经济爆炸式增长是可能的。几乎无限数量的人工智能可以在任何给定的问题上进行合作，开辟了巨大的科学可能性。然而，他们的模型带有一个重要的警告。如果人工智能实现了大部分(但不是全部)生产的自动化，或者实现了大部分(但不是全部)研究过程的自动化，增长就不会起飞。正如经济学家所言: “经济增长可能受到制约的不是我们做得好的方面，而是必不可少但又难以改善的方面。” An idea put forward by William Baumol, a late economist, offers an explanation for this. In a paper published in 1965, he and William Bowen, a colleague, examined wages in the performing arts. 已故经济学家威廉•鲍莫尔(William Baumol)提出的一个观点对此做出了解释。在1965年发表的一篇论文中，他和同事威廉•鲍恩(William Bowen)研究了表演艺术领域的工资水平。 They noted that the “output per man-hour of the violinist playing a Schubert quartet in a standard concert hall is relatively fixed”. Even as technological progress made other industries more productive, the performing arts remained unaffected. 他们指出，“在标准音乐厅演奏舒伯特四重奏的小提琴手每工时的产量是相对固定的”。即使技术进步使其他产业更具生产力，表演艺术仍未受到影响。 Because humans were still willing to spend on the arts even as prices rose—demand was “inelastic”—the arts took up more of GDP and therefore weighed on overall growth. 由于人类仍然愿意在艺术品价格上涨的情况下进行消费(需求是“无弹性的”) ，艺术品在 GDP 中所占比重更大，因此对整体增长构成了压力。 Baumol’s example points to a broader principle. If the domains that AI is able to fully automate are only imperfect substitutes for those which it cannot, and the demand for non-automatable industries is hard to budge, then the unproductive sectors will grow as a share of GDP , reducing overall growth. Messrs Aghion, Jones and Jones note that this is in fact what has happened across much of the past century. Technology has automated swathes of agriculture and manufacturing, driving down the relative price of their outputs. 鲍莫尔的例子指出了一个更广泛的原则。如果人工智能能够完全自动化的领域只是那些它不能完全自动化的领域的不完美替代品，而且对非自动化行业的需求难以调整，那么非生产性行业占 GDP 的比重将会增长，从而降低整体增长。阿基翁、琼斯和琼斯先生指出，这实际上是过去一个世纪大部分时间里发生的情况。技术使大量农业和制造业实现了自动化，降低了它们产出的相对价格。 As a result, people have spent a greater share of their incomes on industries such as education, health care and recreation, which have not seen the same productivity gains. 其结果是，人们在教育、医疗保健和娱乐等行业上的支出占收入的比重更大，而这些行业的生产率却没有得到同样的提高。 Will Baumol’s story matter in a world in which AI is more capable than the most talented humans? If the AI is not embodied—maybe because progress in robotics lags that in computing—then the answer is surely yes. Much of the economy, including construction and manufacturing, is decidedly physical. 在一个人工智能比最有天赋的人类更有能力的世界里，鲍莫尔的故事还重要吗？如果人工智能不具体化(也许是因为机器人技术的进步落后于计算机技术) ，那么答案肯定是肯定的。经济的很大一部分，包括建筑业和制造业，显然是实体经济。 There are countless forms of employment, including many in health care, that require a combination of braininess and an ability to traverse the physical world. These jobs would only increase in importance in a scenario where AI began to dominate cognitive labour. Humans would work in the physical world, perhaps under the guidance of AI “chief executives” or “professors”. 有无数种就业形式，包括许多在医疗保健领域的就业形式，需要智力和穿越物质世界的能力相结合。在人工智能开始主导认知劳动的情况下，这些工作的重要性只会增加。人类将在物质世界中工作，或许是在人工智能“首席执行官”或“教授”的指导下。 But what if ultra-powerful AI develops super-humanoid robots, too? Material needs would almost certainly be met by machine hands. One might then expect humanity to give up on toil, much like in “Wall-E”. 但是如果超级强大的人工智能也开发出超级人形机器人呢？几乎可以肯定，机械手能够满足物质需求。人们可能会期望人类放弃辛劳，就像《机器人瓦力》里那样。 Indeed, in 1930 John Maynard Keynes, another economist, penned an essay entitled “Economic Possibilities for our Grandchildren”, in which he speculated that a century in the future people would work for less than 15 hours a week. 实际上，1930年，另一位经济学家约翰·梅纳德·凯恩斯写了一篇题为《我们子孙后代的经济可能性》的文章，他在文中推测，未来一个世纪，人们每周的工作时间将不到15小时。 The growth generated by technology would solve the “economic problem”, he predicted, and allow people to turn their attention to activities that are intrinsically pleasurable. 他预测，技术带来的增长将解决“经济问题”，并让人们将注意力转向本质上令人愉悦的活动。 Admittedly, Keynes’s 15-hour work week has not arrived—but higher levels of wealth, which may increase the appeal of leisure, have cut working hours much as he expected. 诚然，凯恩斯的每周15小时工作制尚未实现，但财富水平的提高(这可能会增加休闲的吸引力)大大减少了工作时间，这与他的预期相去甚远。 The average number of hours worked a week in the rich world has fallen from around 60 in the late 20th century to under 40 today. 在富裕国家，每周平均工作时间已从20世纪末的60小时左右，下降到今天的40小时以下。 There are, nevertheless, some wants that perhaps only humans can satisfy even in a world of supercharged, embodied AI. It is also worth noting that what is intrinsically pleasurable may include work. Consider three areas where humans may still have a role: work that is blurred with play, play itself and work where humans retain some kind of an advantage. 尽管如此，仍然有一些需求，也许只有人类才能满足，即使是在一个机械增压，具体化的人工智能的世界。同样值得注意的是，本质上令人愉悦的事情可能包括工作。考虑三个人类仍然可以发挥作用的领域: 工作与游戏相混淆，游戏本身，以及人类保持某种优势的工作。 Fun and games 有趣的游戏 Start with the blurring boundary between work and play. Although working hours have fallen over the past century, most of the drop was before the 1980s. Increasingly, rich people labour for longer than poorer people. Keynes’s essay hints at an explanation for this odd development. 从工作和娱乐之间模糊的界限开始。尽管工作时间在过去一个世纪里有所下降，但大部分下降发生在上世纪80年代之前。富人比穷人劳动的时间越来越长。凯恩斯的文章暗示了对这种奇怪发展的解释。 He divided human desires in two: “Those needs which are absolute in the sense that we feel them whatever the situation of our fellow human beings may be, and those which are relative in the sense that we feel them only if their satisfaction lifts us above, makes us feel superior to, our fellows.” 他把人类的欲望一分为二: “无论我们的人类同胞处于什么情况下，我们都能感受到那些绝对的需求，而那些相对的需求，只有在他们的满足使我们凌驾于我们之上时，我们才能感受到它们，使我们觉得自己比我们的同胞更优越。” Keynes perhaps underestimated the size of this second class of wants. A cynic might suggest that entire academic disciplines fall into it: existing with no apparent value to the world, with academics nevertheless competing furiously for status based on their braininess. 凯恩斯可能低估了这第二类需求的规模。愤世嫉俗者可能会认为，整个学术学科都陷入了这种状态: 对世界没有明显价值的存在，而学者们却依靠自己的聪明才智激烈地争夺地位。 Economists would say that, for many, work has become a “consumption good”, offering far more utility than the income it generates. 经济学家会说，对许多人来说，工作已经成为一种“消费品”，提供的效用远远超过其产生的收入。 Games offer another hint as to why people may not stop working altogether. Millions of people are employed in entertainment and sports, competing for clout in activities that some consider immaterial. Perhaps when AI s overtake humans, interest in watching such games will wane. But evidence from sports where humans are already second-rate suggests otherwise. Since IBM ’s DeepBlue defeated Garry Kasparov, the world grandmaster, in chess in 1997, interest in the game has only increased. Other games that have been “solved” by AI , including Go, an ancient Chinese board game, and competitive video games, have witnessed a similar pattern. Across the world the number of video-game players has nearly doubled in the past decade, reaching 3.2bn last year. 游戏为人们为什么不能完全停止工作提供了另一个线索。数百万人受雇于娱乐和体育行业，在一些人认为无关紧要的活动中争夺影响力。也许当人工智能超越人类时，人们对观看这类游戏的兴趣就会减弱。但是，来自人类已经是二流的体育运动的证据表明，情况并非如此。自从 IBM 的“深蓝”(DeepBlue)在1997年的国际象棋比赛中击败世界特级大师加里•卡斯帕罗夫(GarryKasparov)以来，人们对这项运动的兴趣不断增加。其他被人工智能“解决”的游戏，包括围棋(一种古老的中国棋类游戏)和竞技视频游戏，也经历了类似的模式。过去10年，全球视频游戏玩家数量几乎翻了一番，去年达到32亿人。 Nowadays a growing class of gamers compete or stream for a living. 如今，越来越多的游戏玩家以竞争或流为生。 AI might supercharge this interest. 人工智能可能会增加这种利息。 As Banks speculated, humans might specialise in “the things that really [matter] in life, such as sport, games, romance, studying dead languages, barbarian societies and impossible problems, and climbing high mountains without the aid of a safety harness.” Other humans would presumably want to watch them, too. 正如班克斯推测的那样，人类可能专注于“生活中真正(重要)的事情，比如体育、游戏、浪漫、学习已经消亡的语言、野蛮社会和不可能解决的问题，以及在没有安全带帮助的情况下攀登高山。”其他人大概也会想要观察它们。 It seems unlikely that people will give up control of politics to robots. Once AI s surpass humans, people will presumably pay even closer attention to them. Some political tasks might be delegated: humans could, for instance, put their preferences into an AI model that produces proposals for how to balance them. Yet as a number of political philosophers, including John Locke in the 17th century and John Rawls in the 20th, have argued, participation in political procedures gives outcomes legitimacy in the eyes of fellow citizens. 人们似乎不太可能把政治控制权交给机器人。一旦人工智能超越人类，人们大概会更加关注它们。一些政治任务可能会被委派下去: 例如，人类可以将自己的偏好放入人工智能模型中，该模型会为如何平衡这些偏好提供建议。然而，正如许多政治哲学家(包括17世纪的约翰•洛克(John Locke)和20世纪的约翰•罗尔斯(John Rawls))所主张的那样，参与政治程序使结果在同胞眼中具有合法性。 There would also be more cynical considerations at play. Humans like to have influence over one another. This would be true even in a world in which everyone’s basic needs and wants are met by machines. 此外，还有更多愤世嫉俗的考虑因素在起作用。人类喜欢相互影响。即使在一个每个人的基本需求和愿望都由机器满足的世界里，这也是真实的。 Indeed, the wealthiest 1% of Americans participate politically at two to three times the rate of the general public on a range of measures from voting to time spent on politics. 事实上，最富有的1% 的美国人参与政治的比率是普通大众的2到3倍，他们参与政治的程度从投票到花在政治上的时间不等。 Last, consider areas where humans have an advantage in providing a good or service—call it a “human premium”. This premium would preserve demand for labour even in an age of superadvanced AI . One place where this might be true is in making private information public. 最后，考虑一下人类在提供商品或服务方面具有优势的领域ーー称之为“人类溢价”。即使在超级先进的人工智能时代，这种溢价也将维持对劳动力的需求。有一个地方可能是正确的，那就是公开私人信息。 So long as people are more willing to share their secrets with other people than machines, there will be a role for those who are trusted to reveal that information to the world selectively, ready for it then to be ingested by machines. 只要人们比机器更愿意与他人分享自己的秘密，那么那些被信任的人就会扮演一个角色，他们会有选择性地向世界透露这些信息，随时准备好让机器接受它们。 Your correspondent would like to think that investigative journalists will still have jobs. 你们的记者希望调查记者仍然有工作。 The human premium might appear elsewhere, too. People value history, myths and meaning. Non-fungible tokens, for which provenance can be verified on a blockchain, are typically valued at many multiples more than images with identical pixels but a different history. 人类的溢价可能也会出现在其它地方。人们重视历史、神话和意义。不可替代的标记，其来源可以在区块链上验证，通常比具有相同像素但历史不同的图像的价值高出许多倍。 In areas such as caregiving and therapy, humans derive value from others spending their scarce time with them, which adds feeling to an interaction. 在护理和治疗等领域，人类从其他人与他们共度的宝贵时间中获得价值，这增加了互动的感觉。 Artificial diamonds, which have the same molecular structure as those from the ground, trade at an enormous discount—around 70% by one estimate. In the future, items with a “made by a human” tag might be especially desirable. 人造钻石的分子结构与地面钻石相同，其交易价格有很大的折扣，据估计折扣约为70% 。将来，带有“由人类制造”标签的物品可能会特别受欢迎。 People problems 人的问题 If this premium is big enough, it could even weigh on growth. Divide the sectors of the economy into those with a large human premium and those without. 如果这种溢价足够大，甚至可能拖累经济增长。将经济部门划分为人力资本溢价较高的部门和人力资本没有溢价的部门。 If humans do not substitute machine-produced goods and services for those made by fellow humans, the Baumol effect would only deepen. Measured economic growth could even hit zero. Indeed, if extremely powerful AI failed to supercharge growth, it would suggest that the economy had already moved beyond materiality towards play, politics and areas where what people value most of all is interacting with others. 如果人类不用机器生产的产品和服务来替代人类生产的产品和服务，鲍莫尔效应只会加深。经济增长甚至可能达到零。事实上，如果非常强大的人工智能未能加速增长，这将表明，经济已经超越了物质性，转向了游戏、政治以及人们最看重的领域，即与他人互动。 Perhaps one day AI s will produce entirely new goods and services that will outcompete the desire to please and interact with other humans. The manner in which such a contest played out would reveal something profound: just how much of a “social animal” is a human? ■ 也许有一天，人工智能将生产出全新的商品和服务，这些商品和服务将超越取悦他人和与他人互动的欲望。这样一场竞赛的进行方式将揭示一些深刻的东西: 究竟“社会性动物”在多大程度上是一个人？￭ For more expert analysis of the biggest stories in economics, finance and markets, sign up to Money Talks , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter. 如果你想了解更多关于经济、金融和市场方面的重大新闻的专家分析，请注册我们的每周订阅时事通讯 Money Talks。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/finance-and-economics/2023/05/23/what-would-humans-do-in-a-world-of-super-ai 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/finance-and-economics/2023/05/23/what-would-humans-do-in-a-world-of-super-ai 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Free exchange 自由交换 What performance-enhancing stimulants mean for economic growth 提高绩效的兴奋剂对经济增长意味着什么 Could America’s Adderall shortage have harmed its productivity? 美国阿得拉(Adderall)的短缺会损害其生产率吗？ May 25th 2023 | 2023年5月25日 T OWARDS THE end of last year America began running short of medicines used to treat attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder ( ADHD ), including Adderall (an amphetamine) and Ritalin (a central-nervous-system stimulant). Nine in ten pharmacies reported shortages of the medication, which tens of millions of Americans use to help improve focus and concentration. 去年年底，美国开始短缺治疗注意力缺陷多动障碍(ADHD)的药物，包括阿得拉(一种安非他明)和利他林(一种中枢神经系统兴奋剂)。十分之九的药店报告药物短缺，数以千万计的美国人使用这种药物来帮助提高注意力和集中力。 Around the same time, something intriguing happened: American productivity, a measure of efficiency at work, dropped. In the first quarter of 2023, output per hour fell by 3%. 大约在同一时间，一些有趣的事情发生了: 美国的生产率——衡量工作效率的指标——下降了。2023年第一季度，每小时产量下降了3% 。 Coincidence? Probably. Lots of other things could have explained the productivity dip. Equally, though, many of America’s most productive people rely on Adderall to get the job done. It often seems like half of Silicon Valley, the most innovative place on Earth, is on the stuff. 巧合吗？也许吧。很多其他因素可以解释生产力的下降。同样，尽管，许多美国最有生产力的人依赖阿得拉来完成工作。硅谷是地球上最具创新精神的地方，它似乎有一半的人都在使用这种东西。 And surprising things can cause GDP to rise and fall, including holidays, strikes and the weather. What’s more, the economic history is clear: without things that give people a buzz, the world would still be in the economic dark ages. 令人惊讶的事情可以导致 GDP 的上升和下降，包括假期、罢工和天气。更重要的是，经济史很清楚: 如果没有那些能让人兴奋的事情，世界仍将处于经济黑暗时代。 Not all drug consumption helps people work better, of course. Don Draper from “Mad Men”, a TV series about advertising executives in the 1960s, came across many of his finest ideas three Scotches deep. But contrary to popular belief Ernest Hemingway, one of America’s greatest authors, never advised “write drunk, edit sober”, preferring to write liquor-free. 当然，并非所有的药物消费都能帮助人们更好地工作。《广告狂人》(Mad Men)是一部关于上世纪60年代广告业高管的电视连续剧。但与流行的观点相反，作为美国最伟大的作家之一，欧内斯特·海明威从来不建议“醉酒写作，清醒编辑”，而是宁愿不喝酒。 In a book published in 1983 David Ogilvy, perhaps the most famous real-life mad man, warned of the dangers of drunks in the office. Use of cocaine, common on Wall Street and in Hollywood, can give people a short-term boost. It also causes grave long-term problems. 在1983年出版的一本书中，也许是现实生活中最著名的疯子大卫 · 奥格威警告人们在办公室里喝醉酒的危险。可卡因的使用，在华尔街和好莱坞很常见，可以给人们一个短期的刺激。它还会带来严重的长期问题。 Indeed, economists normally think of mood-altering substances as a drag on prosperity. One estimate in 2007 put the cost of drug abuse in America at $193bn, or around 1.3% of GDP . More recently economists have looked at “deaths of despair”, which many link to abuse of opioids. In 2021 more than 80,000 Americans died from opioid overdoses. 事实上，经济学家通常认为情绪改变物质会拖累经济繁荣。据2007年的一项估计，美国的药物滥用成本为1930亿美元，约占 GDP 的1.3% 。最近，经济学家研究了“绝望之死”，其中许多与阿片类药物滥用有关。2021年，超过8万美国人死于阿片类药物过量。 But stimulants can play a positive role, too. Consider two of them: sugar and coffee. The first allowed people to work harder; the second allowed them to work smarter. 但兴奋剂也可以起到积极的作用。考虑其中的两个: 糖和咖啡。前者让人们更努力地工作，后者让人们更聪明地工作。 Until the start of the 18th century calories were a significant constraint on Western economic growth. In 1700 total food supply per person in Britain was equivalent to around 2,000 calories a day—enough for the average man to survive, but not to do a great deal more. 直到18世纪初，卡路里一直是制约西方经济增长的重要因素。1700年，英国人均食物供应量相当于每天大约2000卡路里的热量，足够一个普通人生存，但不能做更多。 Workers were therefore inefficient. Many of the poor, who survived on even more meagre diets, barely had the energy to move, let alone do anything useful. 因此，工人效率低下。许多穷人靠着更为微薄的食物生存下来，几乎没有精力搬家，更不用说做任何有用的事情了。 This changed when sugar imports from Britain’s colonies increased. Annual sugar consumption per person rose from around 5lbs a year in 1700 to 20lbs by 1800—several times higher than in continental Europe. 当从英国殖民地进口的糖增加时，这种情况发生了变化。人均年糖消费量从1700年的5磅左右上升到1800年的20磅，是欧洲大陆的几倍。 After 1800 imports then soared as Britons developed a taste for sweet tea and cakes. The change from a fibre-heavy to a sugar-heavy diet, noted Robert Fogel, a Nobel prize-winning economist, “raised the proportion of ingested energy that [could] be metabolised”. 1800年以后，随着英国人对甜茶和蛋糕的喜爱，进口量猛增。诺贝尔经济学奖得主罗伯特•福格尔(Robert Fogel)指出，从高纤维饮食向高糖饮食的转变，“提高了(可能)代谢的摄入能量的比例”。 Some observed that a growing share of Englishmen were getting fat. But the imports also gave Britain’s economy a sugar high. In France in the late 18th century about 10% of people could not work for lack of nourishment. In Britain, by contrast, only the bottom 3% were incapable. 一些人注意到越来越多的英国人变胖了。但是进口也给英国经济带来了甜蜜的快感。在18世纪末的法国，大约有10% 的人由于缺乏营养而无法工作。相比之下，在英国，只有最底层的3% 的人无能为力。 In the 18th century British GDP growth was seven times as fast as France’s. Fogel reckoned that “bringing the ultra-poor into the labour force [and] raising the energy available for work by those in the labour force” explains about one-third of Britain’s economic growth in the 19th and 20th centuries. 在18世纪，英国的 GDP 增长速度是法国的7倍。福格尔认为，“将极端贫困人口纳入劳动力大军，并提高劳动力可用于工作的能源”解释了19世纪和20世纪英国经济增长的三分之一左右。 Coffee, meanwhile, pushed the middle classes to do bigger and better things. Joel Mokyr of Northwestern University has stressed the importance of a “culture of growth”, the title of a book he published in 2016, in explaining Europe’s industrialisation. 与此同时，咖啡促使中产阶级做更大、更好的事情。西北大学的乔尔•莫基尔(Joel Mokyr)强调了“增长文化”(他2016年出版的一本书的书名)在解释欧洲工业化方面的重要性。 During this period science became less academic, and more focused on solving real-world problems. Over time it became the handmaiden of inventions, such as the internal-combustion engine, which massively lifted living standards. 在这一时期，科学变得不那么学术化，而更多地关注于解决现实世界的问题。随着时间的推移，它成为发明的仆人，如内燃机，这大大提高了生活水平。 Coffee houses, which some at the time called “penny universities”, played a crucial role. 一些当时被称为“便士大学”的咖啡馆起到了至关重要的作用。 By the early 18th century central London was home to as many as 600 coffee shops. The Marine Coffee House in London was an early location for a set of lectures on mathematics, Mr Mokyr points out. 到18世纪早期，伦敦市中心有多达600家咖啡店。莫基尔先生指出，位于伦敦的海洋咖啡馆是举办一系列数学讲座的早期场所。 The London Chapter Coffee House was the favourite of the fellows of the Royal Society, the intellectual godfathers of the scientific revolution, and was where people gathered to discuss how science could be applied. 伦敦分会咖啡馆是英国皇家学会(Royal Society)研究员们的最爱，他们是科学革命的知识分子教父，人们聚集在这里讨论如何应用科学。 Caffeine lubricated the discussion in a way that alcohol—a depressant—never could. The chemical increases both selective attention (focusing on the relevant stimulus) and sustained attention (maintaining it). 咖啡因以一种酒精(一种镇静剂)永远无法做到的方式润滑了这场讨论。这种化学物质增加了选择性注意力(关注相关刺激)和持续性注意力(保持)。 This was not the only way in which coffee fuelled growth. In the 18th century Europe came to rely more heavily on clocks to organise the timing of economic activity, and less on the natural rhythms of the human body, as was common in agricultural societies. 这不是咖啡促进经济增长的唯一途径。在18世纪，欧洲开始更多地依靠时钟来安排经济活动的时间，而不像农业社会那样依赖人体的自然节奏。 Factories cannot function unless everyone is there at the same time. Yet if people now had to get up at unnatural hours, they needed something to pep them up. 除非所有人同时在场，否则工厂无法运作。然而，如果人们现在不得不在不正常的时间起床，他们需要一些东西来鼓舞他们。 “Caffeine became instrumental to the regimented time of the urban industrialised societies,” according to Steven Topik of the University of California, Irvine. 加州大学欧文分校(University of California，Irvine)的史蒂文•托皮克(Steven Topik)表示: “咖啡因在城市工业化社会的严格管制时期发挥了重要作用。”。 Flying high 飞得很高 The prolonged shortage of ADHD medication has imposed real pain on those who need it to function. Fortunately, though, the shortage does now appear to be easing. Some pharmacies are finally getting drugs back in stock and regulators have removed some medications from their official-shortages list. ADHD 药物的长期短缺给那些需要它来维持机能的人带来了真正的痛苦。不过，幸运的是，短缺现在似乎正在缓解。一些药店终于恢复了药品库存，监管机构已经将一些药品从官方短缺名单中删除。 Silicon Valley types have been experimenting with other stimulants, such as nootropics, which are not in shortage. American productivity appears, once again, to be rising. Coincidence? ■ 硅谷人一直在试验其他兴奋剂，如益智药，这些药物并不短缺。美国的生产率似乎又一次在上升。巧合吗？￭ Read more from Free exchange, our column on economics: 阅读更多我们关于经济学的专栏《自由交换》 : Robert Lucas was a giant of macroeconomics (May 18th) 罗伯特 · 卢卡斯是一位宏观经济学巨人(5月18日) A new world order seeks to prioritise security and climate change (May 11th) 一个新的世界秩序寻求优先考虑安全和气候变化(5月11日) How Japanese policymakers ended up in a very deep hole (May 4th) 日本的政策制定者是如何最终陷入一个非常深的困境的(5月4日) For more expert analysis of the biggest stories in economics, finance and markets, sign up to Money Talks , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter. 如果你想了解更多关于经济、金融和市场方面的重大新闻的专家分析，请注册我们的每周订阅时事通讯 Money Talks。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/finance-and-economics/2023/05/25/what-performance-enhancing-stimulants-mean-for-economic-growth 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/finance-and-economics/2023/05/25/what-performance-enhancing-stimulants-mean-for-economic-growth 下载的 Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Finance & economics 金融与经济 Sections 部分 Culture 文化 Science & technology 科技 Artificial brains are helping scientists study the real thing 人工大脑正在帮助科学家研究真实的东西 Neuroscience and AI :: No model is perfect. But that doesn’t stop them being useful 神经科学与人工智能: 没有模型是完美的，但这并不妨碍它们的实用性 Old tyres can become a climate-friendly fuel 旧轮胎可以成为气候友好型燃料 Recycling old tyres :: Getting fuel from your wheels 回收旧轮胎: : 从车轮中获取燃料 Parenting can be bad for the kids 为人父母对孩子不好 Helicopter beetles :: At least, from a genetic point of view 直升机甲虫: 至少从遗传学的角度来看是这样的 Why Venetians are pondering raising their entire city 为什么威尼斯人正在考虑提高他们的整个城市 Saving Venice :: A €5.5bn flood barrier has bought only a temporary reprieve ❗ 🔄 Finance & economics 金融与经济 Sections 部分 Culture 文化

Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Neuroscience and AI 神经科学和人工智能 Artificial brains are helping scientists study the real thing 人工大脑正在帮助科学家研究真实的东西 No model is perfect. But that doesn’t stop them being useful 没有模型是完美的，但这并不妨碍它们的实用性 May 24th 2023 | 2023年5月24日 T HE STRIKING progress in artificial intelligence over the past decade is mostly down to advances in machine learning, whereby computers teach themselves complicated tasks by crunching large quantities of data, rather than having to be programmed directly by humans. 人工智能在过去十年取得的显著进步主要归功于机器学习的进步，计算机通过处理大量数据自学复杂的任务，而不必直接由人类编程。 This approach has driven rapid progress in computer vision, language translation and, most recently, the human-like conversational skills of chatbots such as GPT -4. 这种方法推动了计算机视觉、语言翻译以及最近的类人聊天机器人(如 GPT-4)会话技能的快速发展。 The learning is done by software models called “artificial neural networks” ( ANNS ). The standard description of an ANN is that it is loosely inspired by the networks of neurons in the human brain. It is de rigueur to follow that description with an immediate disclaimer, in which both computer scientists and neuroscientists jump in nervously to point out that the analogy is very rough, that ANN s are mere cartoons of real brains (if even that) and that they fail to capture the complexity of the biological organ. 学习是通过称为“人工神经网络”(ANNS)的软件模型完成的。人工神经网络的标准描述是，它是由人类大脑中的神经元网络松散地激发出来的。按照这样的描述立即做出免责声明是必要的，计算机科学家和神经科学家都紧张地跳出来指出这个类比非常粗糙，神经网络仅仅是真实大脑的卡通(如果真是这样的话) ，并且它们无法捕捉到生物器官的复杂性。 All that is true. But some neuroscientists are beginning to find that even cartoons can be useful. The inner workings of the best ANN s—those that are closest to matching human performance on tasks like identifying objects, or responding to text prompts—appear to have some remarkable similarities to the workings of brains. 这些都是真的。但是一些神经科学家开始发现即使是卡通也是有用的。最好的人工神经网络(ANN)的内部工作原理似乎与大脑的工作原理有一些惊人的相似之处。 ANN 与人类在识别物体或响应文本提示等任务上的表现最为接近。 Having taken inspiration from biology, in other words, programmers are now returning the favour, with their creations telling neuroscientists useful things about biological brains. 换句话说，在从生物学中获得灵感之后，程序员们现在正在回报这一恩惠，他们的发明告诉神经科学家有关生物大脑的有用信息。 The seminal study comparing brains and ANN s was published in Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences in 2014. Daniel Yamins, a neuroscientist at the Massachusetts Institute of Technology ( MIT ), and his colleagues trained an ANN to pick out objects from photographs—a cat, for instance. The researchers compared what was going on inside the electronic network to what was happening inside the brains of macaque monkeys that had been set the same task, and whose brains had been wired with electrodes. 这项比较大脑和神经网络的开创性研究于2014年在美国国家科学院院刊上发表。麻省理工学院(MIT)的神经系统科学家丹尼尔 · 维生斯(Daniel Yamin)和他的同事们训练了一个人工神经网络(ANN) ，让它从照片(比如一只猫)中挑出物体。研究人员将电子网络中发生的事情与猕猴大脑中发生的事情进行了比较，猕猴的大脑被安排了相同的任务，并且它们的大脑被连接了电极。 ANN s are built up from large numbers of artificial neurons that, just like their natural counterparts, can be on or off; firing or silent. These neurons are linked together in layered, interconnected networks. Activity in lower layers can affect how neurons in the higher layers fire. 人工神经网络是由大量的人工神经元组成的，就像它们的天然对应物一样，它们可以开启或关闭，可以发射或沉默。这些神经元以分层互连的网络连接在一起。低层神经元的活动可以影响高层神经元的放电方式。 Inside the black box 在黑盒子里 Dr Yamins’s test involves image recognition, which in natural brains proceeds hierarchically. One layer of neurons will detect simple features such as patches of light or dark. A higher layer organises those into edges; a still higher layer combines the edges into shapes. 维生素博士的测试涉及图像识别，在自然大脑中，图像识别是分层次进行的。一层神经元将检测到一些简单的特征，例如光斑或黑斑。更高的层将这些边组织成边; 更高的层将边组合成形状。 That process of increasing abstraction continues until, eventually, the brain decides whether it is looking at a cat, a dog or a banana. 这个增加抽象的过程一直持续到最后，大脑决定它是在看一只猫，一只狗还是一根香蕉。 Images that share some characteristics provoke similar clusters of neurons to fire. If a certain set of neurons fires when looking at a cat, another, partially overlapping set is likely to fire in response to a picture of a dog. 共享某些特征的图像会激发类似的神经元群。如果一组特定的神经元在看猫的时候被激活，那么另一组部分重叠的神经元可能会在看到狗的图片时被激活。 The neurons that respond to both images are thought to be representing features—fur, four legs and a tail, say—that are present in both pictures. 人们认为，对两幅图像都有反应的神经元代表着两幅图像中都存在的特征ーー毛发、四条腿和一条尾巴。 When Dr Yamins and his colleagues compared what was going on inside the macaque brains to the silicon ones, they found arresting parallels between how the monkeys represented images and how the computers did. 当亚米斯博士和他的同事们将猕猴大脑内部的活动与硅大脑进行比较时，他们发现猴子表现图像的方式与计算机表现图像的方式有着惊人的相似之处。 “The paper was a game-changer,” says Nancy Kanwisher, another professor at MIT who has spent much of her career studying the human visual system, and who now uses ANN s in some of her research. “The [artificial] network was not in any way designed to fit the brain. It was just designed to solve the problem and yet we see this incredible fit.” “这篇论文改变了游戏规则,”麻省理工学院的另一位教授南希 · 坎维舍(Nancy Kanwisher)说，她的大部分职业生涯都在研究人类视觉系统，现在她的一些研究中使用了人工神经网络。“(人工)网络在设计上绝不是为了适应大脑。它只是为了解决这个问题而设计的，然而我们看到了这种令人难以置信的契合。” Since then, whenever an ANN model has close to human performance on a task, neuroscientists have been eager to compare it with natural brains. They have found similarities between ANN s trained to recognise speech and process language, such as those used in transcription software, and the human auditory cortex. 从那时起，每当一个人工神经网络模型在一项任务中的表现接近于人类时，神经科学家就迫不及待地将其与自然大脑进行比较。他们发现了被训练来识别语音和处理语言的神经网络(如转录软件中使用的神经网络)与人类听觉皮层之间的相似之处。 The pattern holds for written language too. One paper published in 2021 compared human brain activity against many different commercial language models. It found that the most sophisticated ANN —at the time Open AI ’s GPT -2—was the closest match for human brain activity. The better models get at solving certain tasks, the more similar they seem to get to the human brain doing the same. 这种模式也适用于书面语言。2021年发表的一篇论文将人脑活动与许多不同的商业语言模型进行了比较。研究发现，最复杂的人工神经网络ーー当时 OpenAI 的 GPT-2ーー与人类大脑活动最接近。解决特定任务的模型越好，它们对人类大脑做同样事情的效果就越相似。 Another indication that the analogy between artificial neural networks and natural ones is useful is that the study of the former can make testable predictions about the latter. A paper published in 2022, by researchers at Columbia University and MIT , found that an ANN trained on image-recognition tasks produced a group of artificial neurons devoted to classifying foodstuffs specifically. When the paper was published there was, as far as anyone knew, no analogous area of the human visual system. 人工神经网络和自然神经网络之间的类比有用的另一个迹象是，对前者的研究可以对后者做出可检验的预测。哥伦比亚大学和麻省理工学院的研究人员在2022年发表的一篇论文中发现，一个接受过图像识别任务训练的人工神经网络产生了一组专门用于对食品进行分类的人工神经元。当这篇论文发表的时候，据大家所知，人类的视觉系统中没有类似的区域。 But the following year researchers from the same laboratory announced that they had discovered a region of the human brain that does indeed contain neurons that fire more often when a person is shown pictures of food. 但是第二年，来自同一实验室的研究人员宣布，他们发现人类大脑的一个区域确实含有神经元，当人们看到食物的图片时，这些神经元会更频繁地发出信号。 Perhaps the strongest evidence for the claim that artificial brains can reveal useful things about biological ones is the apparent ability for software and wetware to interact with each other directly. 人工大脑能够揭示生物学上有用的东西，也许最有力的证据就是软件和湿件之间直接相互作用的明显能力。 Nicholas Sexton and Bradley Love, a pair of neuroscientists of University College London, started out rather sceptical about the supposed resemblance between natural and artificial neural networks. Nicholas Sexton 和 Bradley Love 是伦敦大学学院的两位神经科学家，他们一开始对自然神经网络和人工神经网络之间的相似性持怀疑态度。 Simply seeing similar patterns of activity, they argued, was not enough to claim that ANN s and brains were solving problems in the same way. To prove that the correspondence was meaningful, they suggested investigating whether it was possible to feed brain activity into an ANN . 他们认为，仅仅看到类似的活动模式并不足以断言人工神经网络和大脑是以同样的方式解决问题的。为了证明这种对应是有意义的，他们建议研究是否有可能将大脑活动输入人工神经网络。 In 2022 they published a paper in Science Advances that did just that. The researchers fed an ANN trained to recognise images data recorded by an MRI scanner examining human brains. The idea was to try to let the ANN “see” through human eyes. Sure enough, the hotwired ANN was able to interpret data from any of the hierarchical layers of the biological visual system—though it did best with data from the higher levels, which had already been partly processed by the brain in question. 2022年，他们在《科学进展》上发表了一篇论文，正是这样做的。研究人员给一个人工神经网络喂食，该网络经过训练，能够识别通过核磁共振扫描仪检查人脑所记录的图像数据。这个想法是试图让人工神经网络通过人类的眼睛“看”。毫无疑问，热线神经网络能够解释生物视觉系统中任何一个层级的数据ーー尽管它最适合解释来自更高层级的数据，因为这些数据已经部分地被有问题的大脑处理过了。 If the computer model was shown brain activity from a human that was looking at a picture of a greyhound, for example, then it would say that it was looking at a greyhound—as opposed to some other object—almost 70% of the time. 例如，如果计算机模型显示的是一个人在看一张灰狗的图片时的大脑活动，那么它会说，几乎70% 的时间它在看一只灰狗，而不是其他物体。 The fact that a silicon brain can happily accept half-chewed data from a biological one suggests that, on some level, the two systems are performing the same sort of cognitive task. 硅大脑可以愉快地接受来自生物大脑的半咀嚼的数据，这一事实表明，在某种程度上，两个系统正在执行相同类型的认知任务。 That insight might prove useful for brain-computer interfaces, which are devices that aim to allow biological brains to talk directly to machines. An ANN linked up to a camera, for instance, might be used to feed partly processed visual information into the brain. That might help treat some forms of blindness caused by damage to the brain’s visual system. 这种见解可能会被证明对大脑-计算机接口有用，这种设备的目的是让生物大脑直接与机器对话。例如，与摄像机相连的人工神经网络可以用来将部分经过处理的视觉信息输入大脑。这可能有助于治疗由大脑视觉系统损伤引起的某些形式的失明。 Several different research groups in Europe and America are already testing that idea in experiments on macaques. 欧洲和美国的几个不同的研究小组已经在猕猴身上试验了这个想法。 Models of the mind 心智模型 Even those most enthusiastic about ANN s do not argue they are perfect analogues of the human brain. Some make mistakes that humans never would—give an ANN a picture of a cat but with the skin of an elephant, for example, and the model is more likely to identify it as an elephant. But no scientific model is ever perfect. The question is whether it is useful. 即使是那些最热衷于神经网络的人也不认为它们是人类大脑的完美类似物。有些人会犯人类永远不会犯的错误ーー例如，如果给神经网络一张猫的照片，但是有大象的皮肤，该模型就更有可能将其识别为大象。但是没有一个科学模型是完美的。问题是它是否有用。 One of neuroscience’s problems is that experiments are difficult to run, for both ethical and practical reasons. Poking and prodding ANN s could offer a useful alternative. 神经科学的一个问题是，由于伦理和实际原因，实验很难进行。戳戳人工神经网络可以提供一个有用的替代方案。 In any case, comparing biology and silicon continues to produce intriguing results. In a paper published in May researchers from the University of Texas at Austin used a neural network to monitor brain signals from participants in an MRI scanner. Using just data from the MRI , the ANN could produce a rough summary of a story that the test subject was listening to, a description of a film they were watching, or the gist of a sentence they were imagining. “When I was in graduate school I would dream about something like this existing,” says Dr Love. 无论如何，比较生物学和硅继续产生有趣的结果。在五月份发表的一篇论文中，德克萨斯州大学奥斯汀分校的研究人员使用神经网络来监测核磁共振扫描仪参与者的大脑信号。利用核磁共振的数据，人工神经网络可以对测试对象正在听的故事、他们正在看的电影的描述或者他们正在想象的句子的主旨做出一个粗略的概括。“当我在研究生院的时候，我会梦想有这样的东西存在,”拉夫博士说。 “I thought it would be hundreds of years until we had something that works this well.” ■ “我原以为要过几百年我们才能有这么好的东西。”￭ Curious about the world? To enjoy our mind-expanding science coverage, sign up to Simply Science , our weekly subscriber-only newsletter. 对世界感到好奇? 为了享受我们开阔视野的科学报道，请注册“简单科学”，我们的每周订阅时事通讯。 This article was downloaded by calibre from https://www.economist.com/science-and-technology/2023/05/24/artificial-brains-are-helping-scientists-study-the-real-thing 本文是通过 Calibre 从 https://www.economist.com/science-and-technology/2023/05/24/artificial-brains-are-helping-scientists-study-the-real-thing 下载的 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个

Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Recycling old tyres 回收旧轮胎 Old tyres can become a climate-friendly fuel 旧轮胎可以成为气候友好型燃料 Getting fuel from your wheels 从你的轮子里获取燃料 May 24th 2023 | 2023年5月24日 G ETTING RID of old tyres has long been a problem. Every year more than a billion reach the end of the road. Until recently, most were thrown into landfills or piled up in storage yards, which occasionally caught fire. 摆脱旧轮胎一直是个问题。每年有超过十亿人到达路的尽头。直到最近，大多数垃圾还被扔进垃圾填埋场或堆放在储藏场，偶尔还会着火。 Tougher environmental laws mean many countries now insist tyres are recycled. And they are, sort of. Some of the methods might be better than dumping them, but they are not especially green. 更严格的环保法律意味着许多国家现在坚持轮胎是可回收的。算是吧。有些方法可能比抛弃它们更好，但它们并不是特别环保。 Energy recovery is one common method. This involves burning tyres in an incinerator to generate electricity, or as a supplementary fuel to provide heat for cement kilns and other industrial processes. But that produces planet-warming pollution. 能量回收是一种常用的方法。这包括在焚化炉中焚烧轮胎以发电，或作为辅助燃料为水泥窑和其他工业过程提供热量。但是，这会产生导致全球变暖的污染。 Tyres can be used whole or shredded in construction projects, such as building embankments or repairing roads. There are, however, concerns about chemicals from the tyres leaching out and contaminating the ground. 轮胎可以在建筑工程中使用，例如修筑路堤或修理道路。然而，人们担心轮胎中的化学物质会渗出并污染地面。 Some firms, therefore, have begun exploring an alternative, pleasingly symmetrical idea. Since tyres are mostly made from hydrocarbons, it should be possible in principle to turn old tyres into low-carbon fuel which can be used to run the vehicles they came from. 因此，一些公司已经开始探索另一种令人愉快的对称想法。由于轮胎主要由碳氢化合物制成，原则上应该可以将旧轮胎转化为低碳燃料，用于驱动来自旧轮胎的车辆。 One of the most ambitious firms pursuing the idea is Wastefront, which is based in Oslo, in Norway. Later this year the company will start building a giant tyre-recycling plant in Sunderland in north-east England. 追求这一理念的最雄心勃勃的公司之一是总部位于挪威奥斯陆的 Wastefront。今年晚些时候，该公司将开始在桑德兰东北英格兰建造一个巨大的轮胎回收厂。 In a couple of years, when the plant is fully operational, it will be able to turn 8m old tyres into new products, including some 25,000 tonnes of a gooey black liquid called tyre derived oil ( TDO ). 几年后，当该工厂全面投入运营时，它将能够将800万个旧轮胎转化为新产品，其中包括约2.5万吨名为轮胎衍生油(TDO)的粘稠黑色液体。 The process works by deconstructing a tyre into its three main components. One is steel, which is used to brace the structure of a tyre and which can be readily recycled. The second is carbon black, a powdery, soot-like form of carbon used to improve the durability of the tyre. 这个过程的工作原理是将轮胎分解成三个主要组成部分。一种是钢，用来支撑轮胎的结构，可以很容易地回收利用。第二种是碳黑，一种粉末状的类似煤烟的碳，用来提高轮胎的耐久性。 The third is rubber. Some of that will be natural rubber obtained from the sap of rubber trees. Some will be the synthetic sort, which is made in factories from crude oil. 第三种是橡胶。其中一些将是从橡胶树的树液中获得的天然橡胶。其中一些将是由原油工厂生产的合成油。 In order to do the deconstructing, the tyres are first shredded and the steel bracing removed. The remaining material then goes through a process called pyrolysis. This involves exposing a material to high temperatures in the absence of air. 为了进行解构，首先要将轮胎粉碎，并拆除钢支撑。剩余的物质然后经过一个叫做热解的过程。这包括在没有空气的情况下将材料暴露在高温下。 That causes the rubber to decompose into a mix of hydrocarbon gases, which are drawn off. What is left behind is pure carbon black. 这导致橡胶分解成碳氢化合物气体的混合物，这些气体被抽走。留下的是纯炭黑。 Once the drawn-off gas has cooled down, a proportion of it liquefies into TDO . The remaining gases, which include methane, are funnelled back around to be burned, fuelling the reactor. This, says Vianney Valès, Wastefront’s boss, creates a closed-loop system that prevents emissions. The overall output of the process by weight is 40% TDO , 30% carbon black, 20% steel and 10% gas. 一旦抽出的气体冷却下来，其中一部分液化成 TDO。剩余的气体，包括甲烷，通过漏斗回流燃烧，为反应堆提供燃料。Wastefront 的老板维安尼•瓦莱斯(Vianney Valès)表示，这创造了一个防止排放的闭环系统。该工艺按重量计算的总产量为40% TDO，30% 炭黑，20% 钢和10% 气体。 The carbon black can be re-used to make new tyres. That is of interest to tyre-makers because it helps efforts to become carbon neutral. Producing new carbon black requires the partial burning of heavy oil residues or coal, which produces plenty of greenhouse-gas emissions. 这种炭黑可以再用来制造新轮胎。轮胎制造商对此很感兴趣，因为这有助于实现碳中和。生产新的炭黑需要部分燃烧重油残渣或煤炭，这会产生大量的温室气体排放。 The recovered TDO is similar to crude oil fresh from the ground, and is well-suited for making diesel. To do that, Wastefront is working with Vitol, a Swiss company that is the world’s largest independent oil trader, and which operates a number of refineries around the world. 回收的 TDO 类似于新鲜地面原油，非常适合制造柴油。为了做到这一点，Wastefront 正与瑞士公司维多(Vitol)合作。维多是全球最大的独立石油交易商，在世界各地经营着许多炼油厂。 While not completely carbon-neutral, diesel made from TDO does produce an 80-90% reduction in emissions of carbon dioxide, the main greenhouse gas, compared with the conventional fuel. The future market for cleaner fuels is likely to remain substantial, even though electric vehicles are steadily replacing those with combustion engines. 虽然不是完全碳中和，但由 TDO 制造的柴油与传统燃料相比，确实可以减少主要温室气体二氧化碳排放80-90% 。尽管电动汽车正在稳步用内燃机取代电动汽车，但未来的清洁燃料市场可能仍然很大。 Fossil-fuelled vehicles will remain on the road for decades to come, particularly large commercial vehicles like lorries, which are harder to electrify and which are big burners of diesel. The fuel will also be needed by trains and ships. 未来几十年，化石燃料汽车仍将在道路上行驶，特别是像卡车这样的大型商用车辆，它们更难通电，而且燃烧大量柴油。火车和轮船也需要这些燃料。 So, anything that helps to clean up overall emissions during what will be a long transition to the electrification of transport is useful—especially if it also shrinks a mountainous waste problem. ■ 因此，在交通运输向电气化的漫长过渡期间，任何有助于清理总排放量的措施都是有益的，尤其是如果它还能减少山区垃圾问题的话。￭ 八点一刻杂志网 www.badianyike.com Curious about the world? Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Helicopter beetles 直升机甲虫 Parenting can be bad for the kids 为人父母对孩子不好 At least, from a genetic point of view 至少，从遗传学的角度来看 May 24th 2023 | 2023年5月24日 P ARENTS OFTEN fret about whether they are doing their jobs properly. Take too hands-off an approach and the kids will grow up feral. Smother them with attention, though, and the risk is of raising offspring unable to function on their own. 父母经常为他们是否正确地完成了自己的工作而烦恼。如果不采取措施，孩子们长大后会变得野蛮。尽管如此，用心地闷死它们，风险在于养育无法独立生活的后代。 Evolution, it seems, faces similar trade-offs. In a paper published in Proceedings of the Royal Society B on May 24th, Rahia Mashoodh and Rebecca Kilner, a pair of biologists at the University of Cambridge, offer an explanation for a curious fact: once a species has evolved to care for its young, the trait is almost never lost again. 进化似乎也面临着类似的权衡。在5月24日发表于皇家学会报告 b 的一篇论文中，剑桥大学的两位生物学家 Rahia Mashoodh 和 Rebecca Kilner 对一个奇怪的事实给出了一个解释: 一旦一个物种进化到可以照顾它的幼崽，这种特性就几乎再也不会丢失了。 They argue that parental pampering comes with a genetic price that makes returning to the status quo ante difficult. 他们认为，父母的溺爱伴随着基因的代价，这使得恢复原状变得困难。 The price in question is mutations—random changes in an organism’s genome. Some are seriously harmful, and quickly weeded out by natural selection. But many are only mildly deleterious, and do not much hamper an individual’s chances of reproducing as long as conditions are good. 有问题的代价是突变ーー生物体基因组的随机变化。有些是非常有害的，很快就会被自然选择淘汰。但是许多只是轻微的有害，并且只要条件良好，并不会阻碍个体的繁殖机会。 With that in mind, researchers have long speculated that doting parents provide exactly the sorts of pleasant environment that allow offspring with mildly damaging mutations to survive when they otherwise would not. 考虑到这一点，研究人员长期以来一直推测，溺爱孩子的父母恰恰提供了一种愉快的环境，这种环境允许具有轻度破坏性突变的后代在其他情况下无法存活下来。 Testing the theory requires tracking species over many generations. That is tricky, for many species that provide parental care are irritatingly long-lived. Dr Mashoodh and Dr Kilner turned to the burying beetle, which combines parental care with a relatively short lifespan. 检验这一理论需要对物种进行多代追踪。这是棘手的，因为许多物种，提供父母照顾是令人恼火的长寿。Mashoodh 博士和 Kilner 博士转向了埋葬甲虫，它结合了父母的照顾和相对较短的寿命。 The beetles breed in the corpses of small mammals and birds, turning them into carefully tended nests and regularly spraying them with disinfectant chemicals to keep them clear of bothersome pathogens. 这些甲虫在小型哺乳动物和鸟类的尸体上繁殖，把它们变成精心照料的巢穴，并定期向它们喷洒消毒剂，使它们远离烦人的病原体。 When their young hatch, the parents protect them from predators and feed them bits of the carcass. 当它们的幼崽孵化时，父母会保护它们免受捕食者的伤害，并给它们喂食一些尸体。 The researchers set up two populations. In one, beetles were raised by doting parents. In the other, beetles hatched out of eggs near a dead mouse but had no parents to make life easier. This split was maintained for 20 generations. 研究人员建立了两个种群。在其中一个项目中，甲虫是由溺爱它们的父母抚养长大的。在另一个实验中，甲虫在一只死老鼠附近从卵中孵化出来，但是没有父母来使生活变得更容易。这种分裂持续了20代。 Drs Mashoodh and Kilner then inbred the resulting descendants. Inbreeding amplifies damaging mutations, and often results in extinction. 然后，Mashoodh 博士和 Kilner 博士将由此产生的后代近亲繁殖。近亲繁殖放大了破坏性的突变，并经常导致灭绝。 But the rate at which inbreeding drives a species off the evolutionary cliff varies with how many damaging mutations are present in the first place. 但是，近亲繁殖将一个物种推下进化悬崖的速度，取决于首先存在多少破坏性突变。 The population of beetles whose ancestors had been pampered by their parents died out in just two generations of inbreeding. By contrast, beetles that had a history of surviving alone endured for four. 甲虫的祖先曾经被它们的父母溺爱过的种群在仅仅两代的近亲交配中就灭绝了。相比之下，有独自生存历史的甲虫能存活四年。 That strongly suggests that the hatchlings denied parental care over the course of many generations ended up with considerably healthier genomes than those that had been looked after by mum and dad. 这有力地表明，在许多代的过程中，这些被剥夺了父母照顾的幼体最终获得了比父母照顾过的幼体更健康的基因组。 Parental care, in other words, is a one-way evolutionary street because it generates a problem that only it can solve. That makes going backwards hard. But it is not all bad news. Although most mutations are harmful, the researchers point out that a few are helpful. 换句话说，父母照顾是一条单行道，因为它产生了一个只有它才能解决的问题。这让倒退变得困难。但也不全是坏消息。尽管大多数突变是有害的，研究人员指出，少数是有益的。 They speculate that a species full of helicopter parents might expose its young to more of the evolutionary bad—but also more of the good. ■ 他们推测，一个物种中充满了“直升机父母”，可能会让它的幼崽面临更多进化上的坏处ーー但也会面临更多的好处。￭ Curious about the world? Previous 上一页 Articles 文章 Sections 部分 Next 下一个 Saving Venice 拯救威尼斯 Why Venetians are pondering raising their entire city 为什么威尼斯人正在考虑提高他们的整个城市 A €5.5bn flood barrier has bought only a temporary reprieve 一道55亿英镑的防洪堤只换来了暂时的喘息之机 May 24th 2023 | Venice 2023年5月24日 | 威尼斯 T HE RELIEF in the City of Canals is palpable. For centuries, regular high tides— acqua alta to the locals—have flowed through Venice , submerging walkways, flooding buildings, and stopping boats from passing under its many bridges. 运河之城(City of Canal)的松了一口气是显而易见的。几个世纪以来，常规的高潮ーー当地人称之为水母ーー一直流经威尼斯，淹没人行道，淹没建筑物，阻止船只从许多桥下通过。 For most of the city, at least, that is no longer the case. In operation since last year, after nearly two decades under construction, a giant piece of hydraulic engineering called the Experimental Electromechanical Module—known by its Italian acronym MOSE —now protects Venice and its lagoon . In a city where waders were as easy to buy as postcards and ice cream, most people can now go about their business without consulting tide charts. Property prices are up, especially for flats and shops on the ground floor. 至少对大多数城市来说，情况已经不是这样了。经过近20年的建设，一个名为“实验机电模块”的巨型水利工程从去年开始投入使用，如今保护着威尼斯及其泻湖。在这个城市，防水靴就像明信片和冰淇淋一样容易买到，现在大多数人不用查看潮汐图就可以做自己的生意。房地产价格上涨，尤其是一楼的公寓和商店。 Or at least, they are for now. Although MOSE is up and running, there are questions about how long the barrier will last. The flood-defence scheme was designed to serve for a century. But Hermes Redi, the director-general of Consorzio Venezia Nuova ( CVN ), the Venetian engineering consortium that built it, fears that, thanks to a combination of climate change and the gradual sinking of the city itself, its useful lifespan might be just half as long. 至少目前是这样。尽管 MOSE 已经启动并运行，但关于这一障碍将持续多久仍存在疑问。这个防洪计划的设计寿命为一个世纪。但是，建造这座城市的威尼斯工程财团 Consorzio Venezia Nuova (CVN)的总干事赫尔墨斯 · 雷迪(Hermes Redi)担心，由于气候变化和城市自身的逐渐下沉，它的使用寿命可能只有原来的一半。 MOSE is made up of 78 hinged steel floodgates that run for 1.6km along the sea floor beneath the three inlets to the Venetian lagoon. MOSE 由78个铰链钢制防洪闸组成，沿着通往威尼斯泻湖的三个入口下方的海床延伸1.6公里。 When a high tide begins, machines that consume enough electricity to power “a small town”, as a technician puts it, compress air that is blasted into each floodgate. As seawater is forced out, the floodgates rise into nearly vertical positions. 当涨潮开始时，正如一位技术人员所说的那样，消耗足够电力为“一个小镇”供电的机器会压缩喷入每个防洪闸的空气。随着海水被挤出，防洪闸上升到几乎垂直的位置。 The resulting barrier holds back the Adriatic until the tide retreats. 由此形成的屏障阻挡了亚得里亚海，直到潮水退去。 That sinking feeling 那种下沉的感觉 For all its technological whizzyness, the system has downsides. Cost is one. Mr Redi reckons each raising of the barrier costs about €150,000 (other estimates are higher). Maintenance costs add up, too. Sand must be cleared from the machinery. 尽管这套系统技术先进，但它也有缺点。代价是一。雷迪先生估计，每提高一个门槛的成本约为15万英镑(其他估计更高)。维护费用也增加了。必须把机器上的沙子清理干净。 Each floodgate is designed to be removed every five years for defouling. Last year, when MOSE was used 36 times, the operating cost was more than €70m ($76m). 每个防洪闸的设计是每五年拆除一次，以便进行除污。去年，当 MOSE 被使用36次时，其运营成本超过7000万欧元(合7600万美元)。 The barrier also disrupts maritime traffic, causing protests from both fishermen and the big container ships that call at Marghera, a busy port in the lagoon. 这道屏障还扰乱了海上交通，引发了渔民和停靠在环礁湖繁忙港口 Marghera 的大型集装箱船的抗议。 Antonio Revedin of the North Adriatic Sea Port Authority says a delay can cost an individual cargo ship €80,000 a day—though a system of locks, due to come online later this year, should help. 北亚得里亚海港务局(North Adriatic Sea Port Authority)的安东尼奥•雷韦丁(Antonio Revedin)表示，延误可能导致一艘货轮每天损失8万英镑，不过一套定于今年晚些时候上线的船闸系统应该会有所帮助。 There are environmental issues, too. Most of Venice’s sewage flows into its canals. As Luigi Tosi, a geologist with Italy’s National Research Council ( CNR ), puts it, a lagoon that is sealed off too often would become “first a bathtub, then a sewer”. All that means that MOSE is only used when tides exceed 110cm. That means that some low-lying parts of the city, including St Mark’s square, still flood. 还有环境问题。威尼斯的大部分污水流入运河。正如意大利国家研究委员会(CNR)的地质学家路易吉 · 托西(Luigi Tosi)所说，一个经常被封闭的泻湖“先是一个浴缸，然后是一个下水道”。所有这一切意味着 MOSE 仅在潮汐超过110厘米时使用。这意味着城市的一些低洼地区，包括圣马可广场，仍然会发生洪水。 Rising sea levels will make those downsides more apparent as the barrier is raised more and more frequently. 随着屏障越来越频繁地升高，海平面上升将使这些不利因素更加明显。 One paper, published in 2021, predicted a rise in water levels of between 32 and 110cm in the Venetian lagoon by 2100, depending on how sharply the world cuts its carbon emissions. 2021年发表的一篇论文预测，到2100年，威尼斯泻湖的水位将上升32至110厘米，这取决于世界削减碳排放的力度。 What is needed, then, is a plan to extend the system’s life. Dr Tosi is among those who think that seawater could be the answer as well as the problem. They propose to pump seawater underground, and in doing so to raise the land. 因此，我们需要的是一个延长金融体系寿命的计划。托西博士是认为海水既可以解决问题，也可以解决问题的人之一。他们提议将海水泵入地下，这样做是为了提高土地。 That may seem outlandish but, in principle, it is merely the reversal of something that has already happened. Between the 1940s and 1970s the extraction of groundwater for use by industry caused Venice to sink by about 15cm. 这似乎有些奇怪，但从原则上讲，这只不过是对已经发生的事情的逆转。在20世纪40年代到70年代之间，为工业开采地下水导致威尼斯下沉了约15厘米。 Pietro Teatini, a hydraulic engineer at Padua University, points out that there is precedent from the oil-and-gas industry, which has shown that storing gas in underground reservoirs can lift the land above. 帕多瓦大学(Padua University)的水力工程师彼得罗•蒂蒂尼(Pietro Teatini)指出，石油和天然气行业有先例，这些先例表明，在地下储存天然气可以抬高地面。 The area’s geology is promising. Its sandy subsoils should be relatively expandable. Those sandy layers are capped with watertight clay which would prevent injected seawater seeping upwards to contaminate freshwater aquifers. 这个地区的地质条件很好。它的砂质底土应该是相对可膨胀的。这些沙层上覆盖着不透水的粘土，防止注入的海水向上渗透，污染淡水含水层。 Giuseppe Gambolati, a semi-retired hydraulic engineer at Padua University, thinks it should be possible to achieve a rise of 25cm across the entire city within a decade. His proposal calls for drilling three test wells. 帕多瓦大学半退休的液压工程师朱塞佩 · 甘博拉蒂(Giuseppe Gambolati)认为，应该有可能在10年内使整个城市上升25厘米。他的建议要求钻三口试井。 If those reveal no show-stopping problems, then the full job would involve a dozen wells 600 to 800 metres deep around the city. Dr Gambolati reckons the city could be raised for something like 2% of MOSE ’s €5.5bn construction cost. Maintaining the uplift, by continual pumping of water, might cost 5% as much as the flood barrier’s operating expenses. 如果这些都没有显示出任何值得炫耀的问题，那么整个工作将涉及到城市周围600到800米深的十几口井。Gambolati 博士估计，这座城市的建设成本大约可以达到摩斯建筑工程55亿欧元建设成本的2% 。通过持续不断的抽水来维持抬升，可能要花费防洪堤运行费用的5% 。 For now seawater injection remains just an idea. But if something is not done, then the rising waters may eventually force more drastic changes. Dario Camuffo, who studies both the environment and Venice’s cultural heritage at CNR , says one option would be simply to abandon the city’s ground floors. Raised pavements could allow people to enter buildings on the storey above. Another, he says, is that prized structures might be taken apart for reassembly elsewhere. 目前，海水注入仍然只是一个想法。但如果不采取措施，那么上升的水位最终可能迫使更剧烈的变化。在中国北车公司(CNR)研究环境和威尼斯文化遗产的达里奥•卡穆福(Dario Camuffo)表示，一个选择就是放弃城市的底层。加高的人行道可以让人们进入楼上的建筑物。他说，另一个原因是，有价值的建筑物可能会被拆开，在其他地方重新组装。 Mr Redi worries that Venice’s lagoon may need to be permanently cordoned off from the sea with a dyke. For a city proud of its maritime heritage, that might be seen more as a humiliation than an adaptation. ■ 雷迪先生担心，威尼斯的泻湖可能需要用堤坝永久性地与海洋隔开。对于一个为自己的海洋遗产感到自豪的城市来说，这可能更像是一种羞辱，而不是一种适应。￭ Curious about the world?
